Enchanter's Touch You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Identify Magic and to Craft magical items. Additionally, you can use the Read Aura action on magic items without needing to be trained in the appropriate skill.  
Baraqel's Student  
Your lineage has studied the astrological teachings of Baraqel, learning to read the stars and understand their influence on fate and fortune.  
Star Reader You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about astronomy, astrology, and divination. Additionally, you can use the Cast a Divination Spell activity once per day without needing to prepare or have the spell in your repertoire. The GM determines the specific divination effect based on your request and the situation.  
Penemue's Scribe  
Your ancestors were taught the arts of writing and record-keeping by Penemue, allowing them to preserve forbidden knowledge across generations.  
Keeper of Secrets You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Decipher Writing and to Recall Knowledge about history, lore, and ancient texts. Additionally, you can perfectly recall any written information you have read, as if you had a photographic memory for text.  
Sorcerer Clan Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Sorcerer Clan Feats  
Blood Magic \[Feat 1\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
You can sacrifice your own vitality to fuel your magical abilities. As a single action, you can take 1d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 1 Focus Point. You can use this ability only if you have a focus pool. The bleed damage can be stopped by a successful DC 15 flat check or by receiving healing that restores Hit Points.  
Corruption Resistance \[Feat 1\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry  
You have learned to resist the corrupting influence of forbidden knowledge. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from using forbidden magic, you gain 1 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0).  
Watcher's Mark \[Feat 1\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry  
Your Watcher's Mark is more pronounced than that of most Sorcerer Clan members. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks when dealing with creatures that can see your mark. Additionally, you can use your mark to identify other Sorcerer Clan members or creatures associated with the Watchers—you gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about such creatures.  
Forbidden Lore \[Feat 1\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry  
You gain the Occultism skill as trained, even if you don't meet the normal prerequisites. If you are already trained in Occultism, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Occultism checks. Additionally, you can use Occultism checks instead of Religion checks to Recall Knowledge about the Watchers, their teachings, and related topics.  
5th Level Sorcerer Clan Feats  
Advanced Blood Magic \[Feat 5\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Blood Magic feat  
Frequency Once per day  
You can sacrifice more of your vitality for greater power. As a single action, you can take 2d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 2 Focus Points. Alternatively, you can take 1d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 1 Focus Point and increase the DC of your next spell cast before the end of your turn by 1\.  
Watcher's Gift \[Feat 5\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Forbidden Lore feat  
Frequency Once per day  
You can call upon the power of your patron Watcher for assistance. Choose one of the following effects:  
Gain a \+2 status bonus to one attack roll, skill check, or saving throw  
Restore 1 Focus Point  
Gain resistance 5 to one damage type (acid, cold, electricity, fire, or sonic) for 1 minute  
Gain a \+2 status bonus to your spell DCs for 1 minute  
Corruption Mastery \[Feat 5\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Corruption Resistance feat  
You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from using forbidden magic, you gain 2 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 1 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation and prayer (this does not require any specific faith—only focused willpower).  
Dark Revelation \[Feat 5\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Watcher's Mark feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
Your Watcher's Mark glows with supernatural intensity, revealing hidden truths. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You can see invisible creatures and objects  
You can see through illusions and disguises (though you still need to attempt the normal checks to disbelieve or see through them)  
You gain a \+2 status bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures, objects, or dangers  
You can use the Seek action as a single action instead of a three-action activity  
9th Level Sorcerer Clan Feats  
Blood Sorcery \[Feat 9\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Advanced Blood Magic feat  
Frequency Once per day  
You can sacrifice a significant portion of your vitality for immense power. As a single action, you can take 4d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 3 Focus Points. Alternatively, you can take 2d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 2 Focus Points and increase the DC of your next spell cast before the end of your turn by 2\. The bleed damage from this ability can be stopped only by magical healing or by succeeding at two consecutive DC 15 flat checks.  
Watcher's Power \[Feat 9\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Watcher's Gift feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You channel the full power of your patron Watcher. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+3 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You restore 2 Focus Points  
You gain resistance 10 to one damage type (acid, cold, electricity, fire, or sonic)  
You gain a \+3 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast one additional spell of up to 4th level before needing to Refocus  
Corruption Immunity \[Feat 9\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Corruption Mastery feat  
You gain a \+3 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from using forbidden magic, you gain 3 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 2 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation and prayer.  
Forbidden Mastery \[Feat 9\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Dark Revelation feat  
You have mastered the forbidden arts to such a degree that you can use them with minimal risk. You can cast spells from the Azazel, Semyaza, Baraqel, Kokabiel, Penemue, or Kasdeja traditions (see Chapter 9\) without gaining Corruption Points, provided the spell is of 3rd level or lower. For spells of 4th level or higher, you gain 1 fewer CP than normal.  
13th Level Sorcerer Clan Feats  
Life Essence \[Feat 13\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Blood Sorcery feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You sacrifice a portion of your life essence for ultimate power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You restore 5 Focus Points  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You gain resistance 15 to all damage except force  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast spells without needing to Refocus  
However, when the duration ends, you take 10d6 persistent bleed damage that can be stopped only by magical healing of 5th level or higher, or by succeeding at three consecutive DC 20 flat checks. Additionally, your maximum Hit Points are reduced by 20 until you complete a full week of downtime.  
Watcher Lord \[Feat 13\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Watcher's Power feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 10 minutes  
You become a living conduit for your patron Watcher's power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You restore 4 Focus Points  
You gain resistance 20 to one damage type (acid, cold, electricity, fire, or sonic) and resistance 10 to all other damage types except force  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast spells of up to 7th level without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire (you still need the appropriate spell slots)  
You can cast one additional spell of up to 7th level before needing to Refocus  
Corruption Lord \[Feat 13\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Corruption Immunity feat  
You gain a \+4 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from using forbidden magic, you gain 4 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 3 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation and prayer. You also gain the ability to sense the Corruption Points of other creatures within 30 feet—you can determine whether a creature has 0, 1–2, 3–5, 6–10, or 11+ Corruption Points.  
Arch-Sorcerer \[Feat 13\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Forbidden Mastery feat  
You have achieved mastery over the forbidden arts. You can cast spells from the Azazel, Semyaza, Baraqel, Kokabiel, Penemue, or Kasdeja traditions without gaining Corruption Points, provided the spell is of 5th level or lower. For spells of 6th level or higher, you gain 1 fewer CP than normal. Additionally, you can teach the forbidden arts to others—any character who studies under you for at least 1 month gains the Sorcerer Clan ancestry and the Forbidden Lore feat.  
17th Level Sorcerer Clan Feats  
Sacrifice of Power \[Feat 17\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Life Essence feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You sacrifice a significant portion of your life force for godlike power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You restore 10 Focus Points  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You gain resistance 30 to all damage except force  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast spells without needing to Refocus  
You can cast spells of up to 10th level without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire (you still need the appropriate spell slots)  
However, when the duration ends, you take 20d6 persistent bleed damage that can be stopped only by magical healing of 9th level or higher, or by succeeding at five consecutive DC 25 flat checks. Additionally, your maximum Hit Points are reduced by 50 until you complete a full month of downtime, and you permanently lose 1 point of Constitution (this reduction can be restored only by a Wish spell or similar miracle).  
Watcher Avatar \[Feat 17\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Watcher Lord feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You temporarily become an avatar of your patron Watcher. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You restore 8 Focus Points  
You gain resistance 30 to all damage except force  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast spells of up to 10th level without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire  
You can cast one additional spell of up to 10th level before needing to Refocus  
You gain the ability to fly at a Speed of 60 feet  
You gain the ability to teleport up to 120 feet as a single action (this does not trigger reactions)  
You can use the Wish spell once during the duration (this does not cost a spell slot or Focus Point, but it still has the normal risks and limitations of the Wish spell)  
Corruption God \[Feat 17\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Corruption Lord feat  
You are virtually immune to corruption. You gain a \+6 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from using forbidden magic, you gain 6 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 5 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation and prayer. You can also sense the exact number of Corruption Points possessed by any creature within 60 feet, and you can attempt to drain Corruption Points from others—once per day, you can target a creature within 30 feet and attempt a DC 25 spell attack roll. On a success, the target loses 1d4 Corruption Points and you gain half that amount (rounded down).  
Sorcerer King \[Feat 17\]  
Sorcerer Clan  
Prerequisites Sorcerer Clan ancestry, Arch-Sorcerer feat  
You have transcended mortal limitations through forbidden knowledge. You can cast spells from the Azazel, Semyaza, Baraqel, Kokabiel, Penemue, or Kasdeja traditions without gaining Corruption Points, regardless of spell level. Additionally, you can create new spells based on the forbidden arts—once per week, you can spend 8 hours in research and meditation to create a new spell of up to 8th level. The GM determines the exact effects and limitations of this spell based on your concept and the traditions you follow. Finally, you can establish a Sorcerer Clan enclave—any settlement where you reside for at least 1 year becomes a center of forbidden learning, attracting students and scholars from across the antediluvian world.

Giant Lineages  
Nephilim (1st Generation)  
"The Nephilim were on the earth in those days—and also afterward—when the sons of God went to the daughters of humans and had children by them. They were the heroes of old, men of renown." — Genesis 6:The Nephilim are the first and greatest of the giant lineages—the direct offspring of the fallen Watchers and human women. Standing 12 to 15 feet tall, these titans represent the pinnacle of antediluvian power and corruption. Born from forbidden unions on Mount Hermon, the first generation of Nephilim inherited both the celestial might of their angelic fathers and the mortal hunger of their human mothers.  
In the early days, the Nephilim were revered as demigods and heroes. Their strength was unmatched, their wisdom profound, and their presence commanded awe. But as their numbers grew and their appetites expanded, they became tyrants who consumed the resources of the earth at an unsustainable rate. The Book of Enoch describes their descent into brutality: "And the giants, who were produced from them, devoured the labor of men, and when men could no longer sustain them, the giants turned against them and devoured mankind" (1 Enoch 7:3).  
Nephilim society is organized around bloodlines and strength. The most powerful Nephilim establish themselves as warlords and kings, ruling over human populations through fear and force. They build massive fortifications, forge weapons of bronze that would be too heavy for humans to wield, and demand tribute in food, wealth, and sometimes human lives. Some Nephilim seek to establish lasting dynasties, marrying human women to produce second-generation giants like the Rephaim and Anakim. Others embrace their monstrous nature, becoming raiders and destroyers who live only for conquest and consumption.  
Physical Description: Nephilim are truly titanic in stature, with males standing 12 to 15 feet tall and females 11 to 14 feet. Their bodies are proportionally massive, with broad shoulders, thick limbs, and features that blend human and angelic characteristics. Many have unusual eye colors (gold, silver, or deep crimson), hair that shimmers with unnatural hues, or skin that bears faint celestial markings. Their voices are deep and resonant, capable of shaking the ground when they speak in anger. They require clothing and equipment scaled to their immense size—bronze armor plates the size of human shields, weapons that function as siege engines in human hands.  
Society: Nephilim society is hierarchical and brutal, based on the principle that strength determines worth. The strongest Nephilim rule as kings and warlords, while weaker individuals serve as lieutenants, enforcers, or outcasts. Bloodlines matter—those who can trace their ancestry directly to powerful Watchers like Azazel or Semyaza hold higher status. Nephilim settlements are fortified strongholds built to their scale, with walls thick enough to withstand siege engines and halls large enough to accommodate their massive forms. They keep human thralls to serve their needs, from cooking and cleaning to more sinister purposes.  
Relations: Nephilim view humans as inferior beings—useful as servants, soldiers, or food, but never as equals. They respect strength wherever they find it, so a particularly powerful human warrior might earn a Nephilim's grudging acknowledgment. They have complex relationships with other giant lineages: the Rephaim and Anakim are seen as lesser cousins, the Gibborim as useful half-breeds, and the Horim as primitive cave-dwellers. They fear the Sethites' connection to the divine but dismiss their power as insignificant compared to their own might. The Sorcerer Clans intrigue them—these humans have tapped into Watcher knowledge without giant blood, making them valuable allies or dangerous rivals.  
Nephilim Ancestry
Hit Points 12 Size Large
Speed 25 feet  
Ability Boosts Strength, Constitution, one free  
Ability Flaw Dexterity  
Languages Common, Giant, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Abyssal, Celestial, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Celestial or Abyssal as languages when adding additional languages, reflecting your celestial heritage and fallen nature.  
Traits Giant, Nephilim  
Angelic Resistance You gain resistance 5 to radiant damage.  
Insatiable Hunger You require four times the normal amount of food and water to avoid the effects of starvation or dehydration.  
Powerful Build You count as one size larger when determining your carrying capacity and the amount you can push, drag, or lift.  
Large Size You take up a 10-foot square and have reach 10 feet (instead of 5 feet) when making melee attacks. Your equipment is sized for Large creatures and costs twice as much as normal equipment. You can wield weapons meant for Huge creatures, but they require two hands to use and take a –2 item penalty to attack rolls.  
Nephilim Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific bloodline among the Nephilim people.  
Son/Daughter of Azazel  
Your father was Azazel, the Watcher who taught humanity the arts of warfare, metallurgy, and vanity. You have inherited his martial prowess and affinity for weapons and armor.  
Warrior's Blood You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip. Additionally, you are trained in the Crafting skill and can Craft weapons and armor of up to uncommon rarity without needing a formula, provided you have the appropriate raw materials and tools.  
Son/Daughter of Semyaza  
Your father was Semyaza, the leader of the 200 Watchers who first suggested the oath on Mount Hermon. You carry the weight of his leadership and his knowledge of enchantments and root-cutting.  
Leader's Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks. Additionally, you can cast Charm as an occult innate spell once per day. This is a 1st-level spell.  
Son/Daughter of Baraqel  
Your father was Baraqel, the Watcher who revealed the secrets of astrology and the observation of stars. You have inherited his celestial insight and connection to the heavens.  
Star-Touched You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about astronomy, astrology, and divination. Additionally, you can cast Guidance as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a cantrip that targets yourself only.  
Son/Daughter of Kokabiel  
Your father was Kokabiel, the Watcher who taught the constellations and their hidden meanings. You bear his knowledge of celestial patterns and cosmic mysteries.  
Constellation Reader You gain low-light vision if you did not already have it. Additionally, you can use the Cast a Divination Spell activity once per day without needing to prepare or have the spell in your repertoire. The GM determines the specific divination effect based on your request and the situation.  
Nephilim Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Nephilim Feats  
Crushing Blow \[Feat 1\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Requirements You are wielding a melee weapon  
When you successfully Strike a creature with a melee weapon, you can deal an additional 1d6 bludgeoning damage. This represents your immense strength crushing your opponent's defenses.  
Giant's Reach \[Feat 1\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Your natural reach increases to 15 feet (instead of 10 feet) when making melee attacks. This represents your exceptionally long limbs and ability to strike at distant foes.  
Titanic Appetite \[Feat 1\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You fail a saving throw against a disease, poison, or other effect that would cause you to become sickened or nauseated  
Your massive constitution allows you to overcome toxins and illnesses that would fell lesser creatures. You can immediately reroll the failed saving throw. You must use the result of the second roll, even if it is worse.  
Watcher's Legacy \[Feat 1\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
You begin with 3 Corruption Points (CP) instead of the normal starting amount for your class. These points represent your tainted bloodline and connection to the fallen Watchers. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points beyond your starting amount.  
5th Level Nephilim Feats  
Earth-Shaking Step \[Feat 5\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Requirements You are standing on solid ground  
You stomp the ground with such force that it shakes violently. Each creature within a 15-foot burst centered on you must attempt a DC 18 Reflex save. On a failure, the creature is knocked prone. On a critical failure, the creature is knocked prone and takes 2d6 bludgeoning damage.  
Giant's Grip \[Feat 5\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
You can wield weapons meant for Huge creatures with only one hand, though they still take a –2 item penalty to attack rolls. Additionally, when you wield a two-handed weapon in one hand, you can use your free hand to wield a shield or perform other actions that require a free hand.  
Titanic Resilience \[Feat 5\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
You gain resistance 5 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. This represents your thick hide and massive frame absorbing blows that would severely wound smaller creatures.  
Watcher's Blood \[Feat 5\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Watcher's Legacy feat  
Frequency Once per day  
You can call upon your celestial heritage to enhance your physical abilities. For 1 minute, you gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. Additionally, you can ignore the effects of difficult terrain caused by natural features (rocks, mud, snow, etc.).  
9th Level Nephilim Feats  
Colossal Presence \[Feat 9\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Your mere presence is intimidating to smaller creatures. Enemies that are smaller than Large take a –1 status penalty to attack rolls against you. Additionally, when you use the Demoralize action, you can target all enemies within 30 feet of you instead of just one enemy.  
Giant's Leap \[Feat 9\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry  
Action \[A\]  
You leap across the battlefield with incredible force. You can High Jump or Long Jump up to 60 feet without needing to attempt an Athletics check. If you land on a creature, that creature takes 3d6 bludgeoning damage and must attempt a DC 22 Reflex save. On a failure, the creature is knocked prone. On a critical failure, the creature is knocked prone and stunned until the end of its next turn.  
Titanic Might \[Feat 9\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Crushing Blow feat  
The additional damage from your Crushing Blow feat increases to 2d6 bludgeoning damage. Additionally, when you use Crushing Blow, you can choose to deal the additional damage as force damage instead of bludgeoning damage, representing the supernatural power of your strikes.  
Watcher's Fury \[Feat 9\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Watcher's Blood feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You channel the full fury of your Watcher heritage. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+3 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+3 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks  
You gain resistance 10 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks  
Your reach increases by an additional 5 feet (for a total of 20 feet when making melee attacks)  
You can use the Earth-Shaking Step feat without expending its daily use  
13th Level Nephilim Feats  
Giant's Dominion \[Feat 13\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Colossal Presence feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You assert your dominance over the battlefield. For the duration, all enemies within 60 feet of you that are smaller than Large take a –2 status penalty to attack rolls against you and a –2 status penalty to saving throws against your fear effects. Additionally, when an enemy within 60 feet of you rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll against you, that enemy becomes frightened 1\.  
Titanic Form \[Feat 13\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Titanic Might feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You temporarily grow to truly colossal proportions. For the duration, you increase to Huge size (taking up a 15-foot square), your reach increases to 20 feet, and you gain a \+4 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+4 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. Your equipment grows with you. When the duration ends, you return to your normal size.  
Unstoppable \[Feat 13\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Titanic Resilience feat  
You gain resistance 10 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from all sources (including magical attacks). Additionally, you are immune to the clumsy, enfeebled, and slowed conditions. This represents your titanic resilience and inability to be hindered by conventional means.  
Watcher Lord \[Feat 13\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Watcher's Fury feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 10 minutes  
You become a living embodiment of your Watcher father's power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+4 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks  
You gain resistance 15 to all damage except force  
Your reach increases by an additional 10 feet (for a total of 30 feet when making melee attacks)  
You can use any of your Nephilim ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can cast one spell of up to 6th level from your father's tradition (Azazel's warfare magic, Semyaza's enchantments, Baraqel's divinations, or Kokabiel's celestial magic) without needing to prepare it or have it in your repertoire  
17th Level Nephilim Feats  
Colossus \[Feat 17\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Giant's Dominion feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You become an unstoppable force of nature. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You increase to Gargantuan size (taking up a 20-foot square), and your reach increases to 30 feet  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+6 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks  
You gain resistance 20 to all damage except force  
You are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious  
When you use the Earth-Shaking Step feat, the burst radius increases to 30 feet and the damage increases to 4d6 bludgeoning damage  
You can move through spaces occupied by creatures smaller than Large without needing to attempt checks to Tumble Through  
Titan King \[Feat 17\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Titanic Form feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You temporarily achieve the full power of the first-generation Nephilim warlords. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You increase to Gargantuan size (taking up a 20-foot square), and your reach increases to 30 feet  
You gain a \+8 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+8 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks  
You gain resistance 30 to all damage except force  
You are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious  
You can use the Giant's Leap feat to jump up to 120 feet, and creatures you land on take 6d6 bludgeoning damage  
You can use any of your Nephilim ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can command lesser creatures—any creature smaller than Large within 120 feet of you must attempt a DC 30 Will save or become frightened 2 and unable to attack you or your allies for 1 round  
Unbreakable \[Feat 17\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Unstoppable feat  
You gain resistance 20 to all damage except force. Additionally, you are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious. When you would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level. You can use this ability once per day.  
Watcher Avatar \[Feat 17\]  
Nephilim  
Prerequisites Nephilim ancestry, Watcher Lord feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You temporarily become an avatar of your Watcher father. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You increase to Gargantuan size (taking up a 20-foot square), and your reach increases to 30 feet  
You gain a \+10 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+10 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks  
You gain resistance 40 to all damage except force  
You are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious  
You gain the ability to fly at a Speed of 60 feet  
You can cast spells of up to 10th level from your father's tradition without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire  
You can use any of your Nephilim ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can use the Wish spell once during the duration (this does not cost a spell slot or Focus Point, but it still has the normal risks and limitations of the Wish spell)  
Rephaim  
"And the shades (Rephaim) shall be brought low." — Isaiah 14:9 (paraphrased)  
The Rephaim are the second generation of giants, born from the unions of first-generation Nephilim with human women. Standing 9 to 11 feet tall, they are smaller than their Nephilim parents but still tower over ordinary humans. The name "Rephaim" means "shades" or "ghosts" in Hebrew, reflecting their association with death and the underworld—a connection that became literal after the Flood, when the disembodied spirits of dead Nephilim and Rephaim became the wandering demons known as Refa'im.  
Unlike the Nephilim, who were direct offspring of Watchers and humans, the Rephaim inherited their power through diluted bloodlines. This made them less inherently powerful than their parents, but also more numerous and adaptable. Many Rephaim established themselves as regional warlords, ruling over human populations from fortified strongholds. Others became mercenaries, selling their strength to the highest bidder. Some Rephaim developed a fascination with death and the afterlife, perhaps sensing their own eventual fate as disembodied spirits.  
The Rephaim are known for their grim demeanor and connection to the dead. They often dwell in places associated with mortality—burial grounds, battlefields, or regions where many have died. Some Rephaim practice necromancy or communicate with spirits, seeking knowledge from beyond the grave. Others simply embrace their role as bringers of death, becoming feared warriors who leave trails of corpses in their wake.  
Physical Description: Rephaim stand 9 to 11 feet tall (males) or 8½ to 10½ feet tall (females), with gaunt, angular features that give them a skeletal appearance. Their skin is often pale or ashen, their eyes deep-set and shadowed. Many have hair the color of dust or bone, and their voices carry an echo that seems to come from beyond the grave. They favor dark clothing and armor, often incorporating bones, skulls, or other death-related motifs into their appearance.  
Society: Rephaim society is less structured than Nephilim society, with power based more on individual strength and reputation than bloodline. They often live in isolated fortresses or nomadic warbands, preying on human settlements or serving as enforcers for more powerful entities. Some Rephaim gather in "death cults" that worship mortality itself or seek to commune with the spirits of the dead. These cults perform dark rituals and sometimes practice human sacrifice.  
Relations: Rephaim view humans as prey or tools, though they respect particularly strong or cunning individuals. They have a complex relationship with Nephilim—respecting their power but resenting their dominance. They get along reasonably well with Anakim and Gibborim, seeing them as fellow giant-kin. They are fascinated by the Horim's connection to the underworld and sometimes form alliances with them. Sethites and other righteous humans are particular targets for Rephaim, as their faith represents everything the Rephaim have rejected.  
Rephaim Ancestry
Hit Points 10 Size Large
Speed 25 feet  
Ability Boosts Strength, Constitution, one free  
Ability Flaw Charisma  
Languages Common, Giant, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Abyssal, Necril, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Abyssal or Necril as languages when adding additional languages, reflecting your people's connection to death and the underworld.  
Traits Giant, Rephaim  
Spirit Sight You can see invisible creatures and objects, and you can see into the Ethereal Plane. This functions as the See Invisibility spell at will.  
Deathly Aura Enemies within 10 feet of you take a –1 status penalty to saving throws against fear effects.  
Intimidating Presence You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks.  
Rephaim Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific bloodline among the Rephaim people.  
Death Speaker  
Your family has long practiced the arts of necromancy and spirit communication, learning to speak with the dead and command undead servants.  
Necromantic Heritage You can cast Command Undead as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a 2nd-level spell. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about death, undeath, and the afterlife.  
Grave Digger  
Your bloodline has served as caretakers of burial grounds and sacred death sites, developing an intimate knowledge of mortality and the transition between life and death.  
Grave Keeper You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Medicine checks and to checks to Recall Knowledge about burial customs, funeral rites, and death-related traditions. Additionally, you can use the Treat Wounds action without needing to be trained in Medicine, though you still take the normal penalty for not being trained.  
Battle Reaper  
Your ancestors were warriors who specialized in harvesting lives on the battlefield, becoming feared for their ability to bring death to their enemies.  
Reaper's Touch You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to attack rolls against creatures that are below half their maximum Hit Points. Additionally, when you reduce a creature to 0 Hit Points, you gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level.  
Shadow Walker  
Your lineage has learned to move between the world of the living and the realm of shadows, becoming comfortable in darkness and death.  
Shadow Heritage You gain darkvision with a range of 60 feet. Additionally, you can use the Darkness spell as an occult innate spell once per day. This is a 2nd-level spell.  
Rephaim Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Rephaim Feats  
Death Gaze \[Feat 1\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\]  
Requirements You can see a living creature within 30 feet of you  
You fix your gaze upon a creature, causing them to feel the chill of death. The target must attempt a DC 15 Will save. On a failure, the creature is frightened 1 until the end of your next turn. On a critical failure, the creature is frightened 2 until the end of your next turn.  
Grave Robber \[Feat 1\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Subsist in wilderness environments and to checks to Recall Knowledge about burial sites, tombs, and other death-related locations. Additionally, you can use the Subsist action to find food and water in environments that would normally be inhospitable (graveyards, battlefields, etc.) without taking the normal penalty.  
Spirit Hunter \[Feat 1\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to attack rolls against undead creatures and creatures with the incorporeal trait. Additionally, you can deal damage to incorporeal creatures with your unarmed attacks and non-magical weapons as if they were magical.  
Undead Resistance \[Feat 1\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry  
You gain resistance 5 to negative damage and a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would create undead creatures or control undead.  
5th Level Rephaim Feats  
Death's Door \[Feat 5\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Death Gaze feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You reduce a living creature to 0 Hit Points  
You can immediately use your Death Gaze ability on another creature within 30 feet of you as a free action. The DC of the Will save increases to 18\.  
Grave Lord \[Feat 5\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Grave Digger heritage  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You command the power of death itself. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+2 status bonus to Intimidation checks  
Undead creatures within 30 feet of you cannot attack you or your allies  
You can use the Command Undead spell as a 3rd-level spell once during the duration  
Living creatures within 10 feet of you take a –1 status penalty to attack rolls and saving throws  
Reaper's Harvest \[Feat 5\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Battle Reaper heritage  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You reduce a living creature to 0 Hit Points  
You harvest the life force of your fallen enemy. You regain Hit Points equal to twice your level, and you gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level. These temporary Hit Points last for 1 minute.  
Shadow Step \[Feat 5\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Shadow Walker heritage  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\]  
You step through the shadows, teleporting up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. This movement does not trigger reactions. If you end your movement in dim light or darkness, you gain the concealed condition until the start of your next turn.  
9th Level Rephaim Feats  
Death Master \[Feat 9\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Death's Door feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You become a master of death magic. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You can cast Animate Dead as a 4th-level spell once during the duration  
You gain a \+3 status bonus to Intimidation checks  
Undead creatures within 60 feet of you cannot attack you or your allies, and they treat you as a friendly creature  
Living creatures within 15 feet of you take a –2 status penalty to attack rolls and saving throws  
You gain resistance 10 to negative damage  
Grave Warden \[Feat 9\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Grave Lord feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 10 minutes  
You become the warden of death itself. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You can cast Animate Dead as a 5th-level spell once during the duration, and any undead you create remain under your control for the duration  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to Intimidation checks  
Undead creatures within 120 feet of you cannot attack you or your allies, and they treat you as a friendly creature  
Living creatures within 30 feet of you take a –2 status penalty to attack rolls and saving throws, and they are sickened You gain resistance 15 to negative damage and immunity to the sickened condition  
Soul Reaper \[Feat 9\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Reaper's Harvest feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You reduce a living creature to 0 Hit Points  
You don't just harvest life force—you claim the soul itself. You regain Hit Points equal to three times your level, and you gain temporary Hit Points equal to twice your level. These temporary Hit Points last for 1 minute. Additionally, the slain creature's soul cannot be restored to life by any means short of a Wish spell or divine intervention for 24 hours.  
Umbral Form \[Feat 9\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Shadow Step feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You transform into a being of pure shadow. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain the incorporeal trait (you can move through solid objects, but not force effects; you take half damage from non-magical attacks and double damage from force attacks)  
You can teleport up to 60 feet as a single action, and this movement does not trigger reactions  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to Stealth checks  
You are immune to precision damage and critical hits  
You can see in magical darkness and are not blinded by darkness spells  
13th Level Rephaim Feats  
Death Incarnate \[Feat 13\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Death Master feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)

You temporarily become the living embodiment of death. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You can cast Animate Dead as a 7th-level spell once during the duration, and any undead you create remain under your control for the duration

Living creatures within 60 feet of you take a –4 status penalty to attack rolls and saving throws, and they are sickened You gain resistance 20 to negative damage and immunity to the sickened condition

Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Grave Warden feat

You can cast Animate Dead as an 8th-level spell once during the duration, and any undead you create remain under your control for the duration. Additionally, you can have up to 200 Hit Points worth of undead under your control at once

Undead creatures within 1 mile of you cannot attack you or your allies, and they treat you as a friendly creature. Additionally, you can command any undead creature within range as a free action (Will save negates, DC 30\)

Harvester of Souls \[Feat 13\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Soul Reaper feat

Shadow Lord \[Feat 13\]  
Rephaim  
Prerequisites Rephaim ancestry, Umbral Form feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You become the master of shadows and darkness. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain the incorporeal trait  
You can teleport up to 120 feet as a single action, and this movement does not trigger reactions  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to Stealth checks  
You are immune to precision damage and critical hits  
You can see in magical darkness and are not blinded by darkness spells  
You can cast Darkness as a 6th-level spell once during the duration, and the area of darkness moves with you  
You can use the Shadow Step ability twice per day instead of once per day  
Creatures that start their turn in darkness within 60 feet of you take 4d6 negative damage (Reflex save for half)

Anakim  
"And there we saw the Nephilim, the sons of Anak, which come of the Nephilim: and we were in our own sight as grasshoppers, and so we were in their sight." — Numbers 13:The Anakim are the noble giants of the antediluvian world, distinguished by their imposing stature, regal bearing, and the heavy bronze chains they wear as symbols of status and power. Known as the "Long-Necked Ones" for their distinctive physical characteristic, the Anakim trace their lineage back to the early unions between Watchers and human women, though their bloodline has been diluted through generations of intermarriage with both Nephilim and humans.  
Unlike the brutal Nephilim or the death-obsessed Rephaim, the Anakim cultivated a sophisticated warrior culture centered around honor, lineage, and martial prowess. They established fortified city-states where they ruled as noble houses, each family distinguished by the unique patterns and weights of the chains they wore. These chains were not merely decorative—they served as weapons, symbols of office, and markers of social rank. The heaviest chains belonged to the ruling families, and to wear them required immense strength and endurance.  
Anakim society was hierarchical and deeply concerned with bloodlines and honor. Noble houses competed for prestige through martial tournaments, territorial expansion, and strategic marriages. They kept detailed genealogies tracing their ancestry back to specific Watchers, and these lineages determined social standing. Anakim nobles often took human wives or concubines, producing offspring like the Gibborim who served as elite warriors and administrators in their courts.  
Despite their noble pretensions, the Anakim were still giants in a corrupted world. Their appetites were vast, their demands on human populations were heavy, and their sense of superiority often led to cruelty toward those they deemed inferior. Yet they maintained a code of honor that distinguished them from the more savage Nephilim—broken promises were rare among Anakim nobles, and they valued loyalty and courage even in their enemies.  
Physical Description: Anakim stand 10 to 13 feet tall (males) or 9½ to 12 feet tall (females), with elongated necks that give them a distinctive, regal appearance. Their features are refined and angular, with high cheekbones, sharp jawlines, and piercing eyes that seem to look through mortals rather than at them. Their hair is typically long and elaborately braided, often woven with bronze rings or beads. The most striking feature of any Anakim is their chains—massive bronze links worn across the shoulders, around the waist, or draped over the chest. These chains can weigh hundreds of pounds, yet Anakim wear them with apparent ease, a testament to their supernatural strength.  
Society: Anakim society revolves around noble houses, each ruling a fortified city or region. Leadership passes through bloodlines, with the strongest and most charismatic offspring inheriting titles and chains of office. Anakim cities are architectural marvels for their time, with massive stone walls, towering gates, and central keeps where the ruling family resides. They maintain standing armies of both Anakim warriors and human soldiers, and their courts are filled with human servants, artisans, and advisors.  
Relations: Anakim view humans as useful but inferior—valuable as soldiers, craftsmen, and servants, but never as equals. They respect the Nephilim for their raw power but consider them uncultured brutes. They have cordial relations with other giant lineages, particularly the Gibborim who often serve in their courts. They view the Sethites with disdain, seeing their righteousness as weakness. The Sorcerer Clans intrigue them, as these humans have achieved power without giant blood, though they are wary of the spiritual corruption such knowledge brings.  
Anakim Ancestry
Hit Points 12 Size Large
Speed 25 feet
Ability Boosts Strength, Charisma, one free
Ability Flaw Dexterity  
Languages Common, Giant, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Abyssal, Celestial, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Celestial or Abyssal as languages when adding additional languages, reflecting your people's connection to the Watchers and their celestial heritage.  
Traits Giant, Anakim  
Chain Master You are trained in the use of chain weapons. Additionally, when you wield a chain weapon with the reach trait, you can use it to Grapple creatures up to 15 feet away instead of the normal 10-foot reach.  
Noble Bearing You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when dealing with creatures that have the giant trait or are of Large size or larger.  
Long-Necked Your elongated neck gives you enhanced vision. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures or objects.  
Anakim Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific noble house among the Anakim people.  
Chain-Bearer of Azazel  
Your noble house traces its lineage to Azazel, the Watcher who taught warfare and metallurgy. Your family's chains are forged from the finest bronze, etched with patterns representing weapons and armor.  
Warrior's Chains You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip. Additionally, when you successfully Grapple a creature using a chain weapon, the creature takes 1d6 bludgeoning damage at the start of each of its turns while grappled.  
Chain-Bearer of Semyaza  
Your bloodline descends from Semyaza, the leader of the 200 Watchers. Your family's chains bear intricate enchantments and symbols of leadership.  
Leader's Chains You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks. Additionally, you can cast Command as an occult innate spell once per day. This is a 1st-level spell.  
Chain-Bearer of Baraqel  
Your noble house was founded by a descendant of Baraqel, the Watcher of astrology. Your family's chains are inscribed with celestial patterns and star charts.  
Star-Touched Chains You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about astronomy, astrology, and divination. Additionally, you can cast Guidance as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a cantrip that targets yourself only.  
Chain-Bearer of the First City  
Your lineage traces back to the founders of the first Anakim city-state. Your family's chains are ancient heirlooms passed down through generations, each link representing a century of rule.  
Ancient Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about history, politics, and noble lineages. Additionally, you begin play with a set of ancestral chains worth 50 gp as a trade good, but invaluable as a symbol of your noble status.  
Anakim Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Anakim Feats  
Chain Strike \[Feat 1\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry  
Requirements You are wielding a chain weapon  
When you successfully Strike a creature with a chain weapon, you can attempt to Trip the target as a free action. The target must be your size or smaller.  
Heavy Chains \[Feat 1\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry  
You can wear heavier chains than normal without penalty. When you wear chains as armor (using the rules for improvised armor), you gain a \+1 item bonus to AC instead of the normal \+0. Additionally, you gain resistance 2 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks while wearing chains.  
Noble Command \[Feat 1\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry  
You can use your Charisma modifier instead of your Strength modifier for Intimidation checks. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when attempting to Make an Impression on creatures that have the humanoid or giant trait.  
Watcher's Blood \[Feat 1\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry  
You begin with 2 Corruption Points (CP) instead of the normal starting amount for your class. These points represent your diluted but still-present connection to the fallen Watchers. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points beyond your starting amount.  
5th Level Anakim Feats  
Chain Mastery \[Feat 5\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry, Chain Strike feat  
Requirements You are wielding a chain weapon  
When you use Chain Strike to Trip a creature, you can also attempt to Shove the target 5 feet in any direction as part of the same free action. Additionally, the DC of the Trip attempt increases by 2\.  
Crushing Chains \[Feat 5\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry, Heavy Chains feat  
The resistance from your Heavy Chains feat increases to 5 against bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, when you successfully Grapple a creature using a chain weapon, you can deal 1d6 bludgeoning damage to the creature as a free action.  
Noble Presence \[Feat 5\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry, Noble Command feat  
Your noble bearing commands respect. Enemies that are smaller than Large take a –1 status penalty to attack rolls against you. Additionally, when you use the Demoralize action, you can target all enemies within 30 feet of you instead of just one enemy.  
Chain of Office \[Feat 5\]  
Anakim  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (manipulate)  
Requirements You are wearing your ancestral chains  
You invoke the authority of your noble house. For 1 minute, you gain a \+2 status bonus to Diplomacy and Intimidation checks, and creatures with the humanoid trait that are not your enemies treat you as friendly. This ability does not work on creatures that are already hostile or on creatures that are not influenced by social status.  
9th Level Anakim Feats

Anakim

Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\]

Anakim

You can use the Chain of Office ability without expending its daily use  
Allies within 30 feet of you gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws  
Enemies within 30 feet of you take a –1 status penalty to attack rolls and saving throws

Anakim

Gibborim  
"These were the mighty men who were of old, the men of renown." — Genesis 6:The Gibborim are the "Mighty Men of Old"—human-giant hybrids who bridge the gap between mortal humanity and the titanic Nephilim. Standing 6½ to 8 feet tall, they possess the strength and martial prowess of their giant ancestors tempered by the adaptability and cunning of their human heritage. The name "Gibborim" means "mighty ones" or "heroes" in Hebrew, and these warriors lived up to their title, becoming legendary champions, elite soldiers, and feared mercenaries throughout the antediluvian world.  
Unlike the pure-blooded giants who often viewed humans with contempt, the Gibborim understood both worlds. They could move freely in human settlements without causing panic, yet they possessed enough giant blood to command respect among their larger kin. This unique position made them valuable as intermediaries, enforcers, and military leaders. Many Gibborim served as captains in Nephilim armies, commanders of Anakim household guards, or champions in Rephaim warbands. Others carved out their own paths as independent mercenaries, selling their services to the highest bidder regardless of bloodline or allegiance.  
The Gibborim were renowned for their martial skill and tactical intelligence. While they lacked the raw power of first-generation Nephilim, they compensated with superior technique, discipline, and battlefield awareness. They mastered weapons that were too heavy for ordinary humans but too small for true giants, becoming experts with bronze greatswords, war spears, and heavy shields. Their hybrid nature also granted them enhanced endurance—they could fight longer and harder than pure humans, yet they didn't suffer from the insatiable appetites that plagued their giant kin.  
Physical Description: Gibborim stand 6½ to 8 feet tall (males) or 6 to 7½ feet tall (females), with powerful, well-proportioned builds that combine human agility with giant strength. Their features are striking—strong jaws, broad shoulders, and intense eyes that seem to miss nothing. Their skin tones vary widely depending on their specific ancestry, ranging from the pale complexions of Rephaim descendants to the darker hues of human lineages. Gibborim typically wear their hair long and braided, often incorporating bronze rings or beads as tokens of their victories. Their clothing and armor are practical but well-made, reflecting their status as elite warriors.  
Society: Gibborim society is meritocratic and martial. Status is earned through combat prowess, leadership ability, and battlefield achievements rather than birthright alone. While bloodlines matter—those with stronger giant ancestry command more respect—individual skill can elevate even the most diluted Gibborim to positions of power. They often form warrior brotherhoods or mercenary companies, bound by oaths of loyalty and shared combat experience. These groups operate with military precision, each member knowing their role and trusting their comrades implicitly.  
Relations: Gibborim get along well with all giant lineages, serving as respected intermediaries between the various factions. They have complex relationships with humans—some Gibborim protect human communities from giant oppression, while others exploit their smaller kin for personal gain. They respect the Sethites' courage but view their righteousness as impractical. They admire the Cainites' ingenuity but see their city-building as a distraction from true martial virtue. The Sorcerer Clans intrigue them, though they are wary of the spiritual corruption that accompanies forbidden knowledge.  
Gibborim Ancestry
Hit Points 10 Size Medium
Speed 25 feet
Ability Boosts Strength, Constitution, one free
Ability Flaw None  
Languages Common, Giant, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Abyssal, Celestial, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Giant as an additional language even if you don't have enough Intelligence modifier to normally gain additional languages.  
Traits Human, Gibborim  
Powerful Build You count as one size larger when determining your carrying capacity and the amount you can push, drag, or lift.  
Martial Training You gain the Athletics skill as trained, even if you don't meet the normal prerequisites. If you are already trained in Athletics, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks.  
Heroic Blood You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against fear effects.  
Gibborim Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific bloodline among the Gibborim people.  
Son/Daughter of Nephilim  
Your giant ancestry is strong, with a direct Nephilim parent or grandparent. You have inherited significant physical power and a connection to your titanic heritage.  
Titan's Blood You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip. Additionally, when you make a melee Strike, you can deal an additional 1d4 bludgeoning damage.  
Son/Daughter of Rephaim  
Your bloodline traces back to the death-touched Rephaim. You have inherited their grim demeanor and connection to mortality.  
Death-Touched You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks. Additionally, you can cast Doom as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a 1st-level spell.  
Son/Daughter of Anakim  
Your ancestry connects you to the noble Anakim houses. You have inherited their regal bearing and social grace.  
Noble Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks. Additionally, you can use your Charisma modifier instead of your Strength modifier for Intimidation checks.  
Son/Daughter of Wanderer  
Your human side comes from the nomadic Wanderer peoples. You have inherited their survival skills and connection to the wilderness.  
Wilderness Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Survival checks. Additionally, you can use the Subsist action in wilderness environments without taking the normal penalty for not being trained in Survival.  
Gibborim Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

Gibborim

You inspire your allies with your tactical expertise. For the duration, you and all allies within 30 feet gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and a \+1 status bonus to AC. Additionally, you can use the Aid action as a single action instead of a two-action activity.  
13th Level Gibborim Feats  
Colossal Grip \[Feat 13\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Titanic Grip feat  
You can wield weapons meant for Gargantuan creatures without taking the –2 item penalty to attack rolls. Additionally, when you wield a weapon meant for a creature larger than Gargantuan, you can use it with only one hand, though it still takes a –2 item penalty to attack rolls.  
Heroic Legend \[Feat 13\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Heroic Endurance feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Trigger You or an ally within 30 feet is reduced to 0 Hit Points  
Your legend inspires miraculous recovery. The triggering creature is restored to half their maximum Hit Points and gains temporary Hit Points equal to your level. Additionally, they are healed of one condition of the GM's choice.  
Devastating Blow \[Feat 13\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Mighty Critical feat  
When you score a critical hit with a melee weapon, you can deal an additional 5d6 damage of the weapon's type. Additionally, when you use Mighty Blow on a critical hit, the additional damage is tripled instead of doubled. Finally, creatures hit by your critical hits must attempt a DC 25 Fortitude save or be stunned until the end of their next turn.  
Master Tactician \[Feat 13\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, War Leader feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 10 minutes  
You become a master of battlefield tactics. For the duration, you and all allies within 60 feet gain the following benefits:  
A \+3 status bonus to attack rolls  
A \+2 status bonus to AC  
A \+2 status bonus to saving throws  
The ability to use the Aid action as a single action instead of a two-action activity  
When an ally within 60 feet rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll, they can immediately reroll it  
17th Level Gibborim Feats  
Titan's Grip \[Feat 17\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Colossal Grip feat  
You can wield weapons of any size without penalty. Additionally, when you wield a weapon meant for a creature larger than yourself, you deal an additional 1d6 damage of the weapon's type with each Strike.  
Heroic Immortality \[Feat 17\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Heroic Legend feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Trigger You are reduced to 0 Hit Points  
Your heroic legend transcends mortality. You are restored to full Hit Points and gain temporary Hit Points equal to twice your level. Additionally, you gain a \+4 status bonus to all saving throws for 1 minute. This ability cannot prevent you from dying if you are subject to an effect that specifically prevents resurrection or revival.  
Legendary Blow \[Feat 17\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Devastating Blow feat  
When you score a critical hit with a melee weapon, you can deal an additional 8d6 damage of the weapon's type. Additionally, when you use Mighty Blow on a critical hit, the additional damage is quadrupled instead of tripled. Finally, creatures hit by your critical hits must attempt a DC 30 Fortitude save or be stunned for 1 minute (basic Fortitude save).  
Legendary Commander \[Feat 17\]  
Gibborim  
Prerequisites Gibborim ancestry, Master Tactician feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You achieve legendary status as a battlefield commander. For the duration, you and all allies within 120 feet gain the following benefits:  
A \+4 status bonus to attack rolls  
A \+3 status bonus to AC  
A \+3 status bonus to saving throws  
The ability to use the Aid action as a free action instead of a single action  
When an ally within 120 feet rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll, they can immediately reroll it  
When an ally within 120 feet scores a critical hit, they deal an additional 2d6 damage of the weapon's type  
You can use the Master Tactician ability twice per day instead of once per day

Horim  
"And the Horites also dwelt in Seir beforetime..." — Genesis 14:6 (paraphrased)  
The Horim are the cave-dwelling giant-kin of the antediluvian world, adapted to life in the subterranean darkness and the hidden places of the earth. Standing 6 to 7 feet tall, they are the smallest of the giant lineages, but their adaptation to underground life makes them formidable opponents in their native terrain. The name "Horim" derives from the Hebrew word for "cave-dwellers" or "troglodytes," reflecting their preference for living in natural caverns, abandoned mines, and underground complexes.  
Unlike the surface-dwelling Nephilim and Anakim who built fortified cities and ruled over human populations, the Horim retreated into the earth, seeking refuge from the corruption and violence that plagued the world above. They established extensive cave networks and underground settlements, carving their homes from living rock and developing sophisticated systems of tunnels, chambers, and ventilation shafts. Their society was built around the principles of survival, secrecy, and mastery of stonework.  
The Horim developed unique physical adaptations to their subterranean lifestyle. Their eyes evolved to see in complete darkness, their skin became pale from lack of sunlight, and their senses of hearing and touch became exceptionally acute. They learned to navigate by sound and vibration, reading the subtle signs of stone and earth as clearly as surface dwellers read the stars and weather patterns. Their connection to the earth granted them an almost supernatural understanding of stone, allowing them to detect hidden passages, structural weaknesses, and mineral deposits with uncanny accuracy.  
Horim culture revolves around the earth itself. They worship the stone and the darkness, seeing the underground world as a sanctuary from the corruption above. They are master stoneworkers, carving intricate patterns and symbols into cave walls, creating beautiful crystalline formations, and crafting weapons and tools from the finest stone and bronze. Their art is geometric and angular, reflecting the natural patterns of crystal growth and rock strata. They keep extensive records carved into stone tablets, preserving their history and knowledge for future generations.  
Physical Description: Horim stand 6 to 6'10" tall (males) or 5'5" to 6'5" tall (females), with lean, wiry builds adapted for navigating narrow tunnels and climbing rock faces. Their skin is pale, almost translucent in some cases, with visible blue veins tracing patterns across their bodies. Their hair is typically white, silver, or pale blonde, and their eyes are large and luminous, capable of seeing in absolute darkness. They dress in simple garments made of leather and fur, often incorporating stones, crystals, and bronze ornaments into their clothing and jewelry.  
Society: Horim society is organized around extended family clans, each controlling a specific cave system or underground region. Leadership is based on age, wisdom, and skill in stoneworking and survival. Elders are highly respected for their knowledge of the underground world and their ability to guide the clan through difficult times. Horim settlements are hidden and well-defended, with multiple entrances and escape routes known only to clan members.  
Relations: Horim are generally reclusive and distrustful of surface dwellers, viewing them as corrupted by the world above. They have cordial relations with other giant lineages, particularly the Rephaim who share their affinity for death and darkness. They respect the Gibborim for their strength but see them as too influenced by surface culture. They view humans with suspicion but may trade with them for goods they cannot produce underground. Sethites and other righteous humans are viewed with particular wariness, as their connection to the divine represents everything the Horim have rejected.  
Horim Ancestry
Hit Points 8 Size Medium
Speed 25 feet
Ability Boosts Dexterity, Wisdom, one free
Ability Flaw Charisma  
Languages Common, Giant, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Terran, Undercommon, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Terran or Undercommon as languages when adding additional languages, reflecting your people's connection to the earth and underground realms.  
Traits Giant, Horim  
Superior Darkvision You can see in darkness and dim light up to 120 feet as if it were bright light, though you can't discern color in darkness, only shades of gray.  
Stone Cunning You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about stonework, geology, and underground environments. Additionally, you can sense the presence of stone and earth within 30 feet of you, allowing you to detect hidden passages, structural weaknesses, and mineral deposits.  
Sunlight Sensitivity You are dazzled while in sunlight. Additionally, you take a –1 status penalty to attack rolls and Perception checks that require sight while in direct sunlight.  
Horim Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific clan among the Horim people.  
Stone-Shaper  
Your family has been master stoneworkers for generations, carving intricate patterns and structures from living rock.  
Stone Heritage You gain the Crafting skill as trained, even if you don't meet the normal prerequisites. Additionally, you can Craft items made of stone or earth (weapons, tools, structures) of up to uncommon rarity without needing a formula, provided you have the appropriate raw materials and tools.  
Crystal-Seer  
Your bloodline has developed a unique connection to crystalline formations, using them for divination and spiritual insight.  
Crystal Sight You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about crystals, minerals, and divination. Additionally, you can cast Augury as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a 2nd-level spell.  
Tunnel-Rat  
Your clan specializes in navigating and surviving in the most treacherous underground environments, from narrow passages to deep chasms.  
Tunnel Master You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Acrobatics checks to Balance and Tumble Through, and to Athletics checks to Climb. Additionally, you can move through spaces that are too narrow for your size (such as tunnels meant for Small creatures) without needing to attempt checks to Squeeze Through.  
Earth-Walker  
Your lineage has learned to move through solid earth as if it were air, a rare ability granted by your deep connection to the stone.  
Earth Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about earth, stone, and underground environments. Additionally, you can cast Pass without Trace as a primal innate spell once per day. This is a 2nd-level spell.  
Horim Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Horim Feats  
Cave Stealth \[Feat 1\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks in underground environments, caves, and areas with natural stone. Additionally, you can use the Hide action even when you are only partially concealed by rock formations, stalagmites, or other natural stone features.  
Earth Sense \[Feat 1\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry  
Your connection to the earth allows you to sense vibrations and movements through stone. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures or objects in underground environments. Additionally, you can detect the presence of creatures within 30 feet of you that are in contact with the ground, even if they are invisible or hidden.  
Stone Skin \[Feat 1\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry  
Your skin has the toughness of stone. You gain resistance 2 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. This represents your natural resilience to impacts and falls.  
Underground Survivor \[Feat 1\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Subsist in underground environments and to checks to Recall Knowledge about survival in caves and tunnels. Additionally, you can use the Subsist action in underground environments without taking the normal penalty for not being trained in Survival.  
5th Level Horim Feats  
Crystal Focus \[Feat 5\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Crystal-Seer heritage  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You focus your mind through a crystalline lens. For the duration, you gain a \+2 status bonus to Perception checks and a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against mental effects. Additionally, you can see invisible creatures and objects within 30 feet of you.  
Earth Glide \[Feat 5\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Earth-Walker heritage  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\]  
You move through solid earth or stone as if it were air. You can travel up to 30 feet through natural, unworked earth or stone. You cannot end your movement inside solid material, and you emerge at the nearest open space. This movement does not trigger reactions.  
Stone Mastery \[Feat 5\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Stone-Shaper heritage  
You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about stonework, geology, and underground environments. Additionally, you can use the Stone Shape spell as a primal innate spell once per day. This is a 3rd-level spell.  
Tunnel Fighter \[Feat 5\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Tunnel-Rat heritage  
You excel at combat in confined spaces. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to attack rolls when fighting in narrow tunnels, caves, or other confined environments. Additionally, you can use the High Ground action without needing to be on higher elevation, provided you are fighting in an underground environment with natural features that provide tactical advantages.  
9th Level Horim Feats  
Crystal Ward \[Feat 9\]  
Horim

Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You are about to take damage  
You create a protective barrier of crystalline energy. You gain resistance 10 to all damage from the triggering attack. If the attack is a critical hit, this resistance increases to 20\.

Horim

You gain resistance 10 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks  
You can sense the presence of all creatures within 60 feet of you that are in contact with the ground

Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Stone Mastery feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)

Your body transforms into living stone. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:

You are immune to precision damage and critical hits

Your unarmed attacks deal an additional 1d6 bludgeoning damage  
However, your Speed is reduced by 10 feet, and you cannot use abilities that require flexibility or fine manipulation.  
Underground Master \[Feat 9\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Tunnel Fighter feat  
You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to attack rolls when fighting in underground environments. Additionally, you can use the Cover action without needing to be behind physical cover, provided you are in an underground environment with natural features. You also gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks in underground environments.

Horim

You gain resistance 20 to all damage except force  
You can see invisible creatures and objects within 120 feet of you  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to Perception checks and saving throws against mental effects  
Your unarmed attacks deal an additional 2d6 force damage  
You can cast Wall of Stone as a 6th-level spell once during the duration

Horim

Horim

Master of the Depths \[Feat 13\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Underground Master feat  
You gain a \+4 circumstance bonus to attack rolls when fighting in underground environments. Additionally, you can use the Cover action as a free action, and you gain a \+4 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks in underground environments. You can also use the Subsist action in underground environments to find food, water, and other necessities for yourself and up to five allies without taking any penalty.  
17th Level Horim Feats  
Crystal Deity \[Feat 17\]  
Horim  
Prerequisites Horim ancestry, Crystal Avatar feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You temporarily ascend to deity status over crystalline power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:

You can see invisible creatures and objects within 240 feet of you

Earth Deity \[Feat 17\]  
Horim

You temporarily ascend to deity status over the earth itself. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:

You can use the Earth Glide ability three times per day instead of once per day  
You gain immunity to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious

Horim

Elioud  
"And the Elioud were born from the Nephilim..." — Book of Giants (paraphrased)  
The Elioud are the third generation of giant offspring, born from the unions of second-generation giants (Rephaim, Anakim) with human women. Standing 6'2" to 7 feet tall for males and 5'10" to 6'8" for females, the Elioud represent the final dilution of giant blood before it becomes indistinguishable from pure humanity.
