The name "Elioud" means "mighty ones" or "heroes" in some ancient texts, though they are less mighty than their Nephilim and Rephaim ancestors.  
Unlike the towering Nephilim or the noble Anakim, the Elioud are often indistinguishable from exceptionally large and strong humans. This allows them to move freely in human society without causing alarm or attracting unwanted attention. Many Elioud choose to pass as humans, hiding their giant heritage to avoid persecution or to gain advantages in human communities. Others embrace their ancestry, serving as intermediaries between giant and human societies or using their unique position to manipulate both sides for personal gain.  
The Elioud occupy a unique social position in the antediluvian world. They are not powerful enough to rule as Nephilim warlords, nor are they numerous enough to form their own distinct society like the Anakim nobility. Instead, they exist on the margins, serving as mercenaries, bodyguards, enforcers, and sometimes outlaws. Their diluted blood grants them enhanced strength and endurance compared to pure humans, but they lack the supernatural abilities and immense size of their giant ancestors.  
Elioud culture is diverse and fragmented, reflecting their scattered existence across human and giant societies. Some Elioud maintain traditions passed down from their giant ancestors, preserving knowledge of ancient bloodlines and forbidden practices. Others fully assimilate into human culture, abandoning their heritage in favor of acceptance and security. A few Elioud form small communities or brotherhoods, banding together for mutual protection and support in a world that often views them with suspicion.  
Physical Description: Elioud stand 6'2" to 7 feet tall (males) or 5'10" to 6'8" tall (females), with powerful, well-proportioned builds that combine human agility with residual giant strength. Their features are typically human-like but with subtle giant characteristics—broader shoulders, thicker bones, or unusually intense eyes. Their skin tones, hair colors, and facial features vary widely depending on their specific ancestry and human heritage. Elioud often dress in practical clothing that helps them blend into human society, though some proudly display symbols of their giant heritage.  
Society: Elioud society is informal and decentralized, with no unified culture or leadership. They exist as individuals or small groups scattered across the antediluvian world, adapting to whatever society they find themselves in. Some Elioud serve powerful Nephilim or Anakim as elite guards or trusted lieutenants. Others work as mercenaries for human settlements, offering their enhanced strength in exchange for payment and protection. A few Elioud become outlaws, using their abilities to prey on travelers and isolated communities.  
Relations: Elioud have complex relationships with both humans and giants. They can pass as humans when necessary, allowing them to move freely in human settlements and gain the trust of human communities. However, they are often viewed with suspicion once their heritage is revealed. They have cordial relations with other giant lineages, particularly the Gibborim who share their hybrid nature. They respect the Nephilim and Anakim for their power but recognize that they can never achieve the same status. They view pure humans with a mixture of pity and envy—pity for their weakness, envy for their freedom from the burdens of giant blood.  
Elioud Ancestry
Hit Points 8 Size Medium
Speed 25 feet
Ability Boosts Strength, one free
Ability Flaw None  
Languages Common, plus a number of additional languages equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum 1). Choose from Giant, Ancient, or any other languages to which you have access.  
Additional Languages You can select Giant as an additional language even if you don't have enough Intelligence modifier to normally gain additional languages.  
Traits Human, Elioud  
Diluted Blood You can attempt to pass as a human of exceptional size and strength. When you do so, creatures that would normally be hostile to giants or giant-kin must succeed at a DC 15 Perception check to notice your true heritage.  
Ancestral Echo Once per day, you can channel the strength of your giant ancestors. For 1 minute, you gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks.  
Mighty Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip.  
Elioud Heritages  
At 1st level, choose one of the following heritages representing your specific bloodline among the Elioud people.  
Nephilim-Blooded  
Your giant ancestry is strong, with a direct Nephilim or Rephaim parent or grandparent. You have inherited significant physical power and a connection to your titanic heritage.  
Titan's Echo You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Climb and Swim. Additionally, when you make a melee Strike, you can deal an additional 1d4 bludgeoning damage.  
Anakim-Blooded  
Your bloodline traces back to the noble Anakim houses. You have inherited their regal bearing and social grace, along with their physical strength.  
Noble Echo You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks. Additionally, you can use your Charisma modifier instead of your Strength modifier for Intimidation checks.  
Rephaim-Blooded  
Your ancestry connects you to the death-touched Rephaim. You have inherited their grim demeanor and connection to mortality.  
Death-Touched Echo You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Intimidation checks. Additionally, you can cast Doom as a divine innate spell once per day. This is a 1st-level spell.  
Human-Passing  
Your giant blood is so diluted that you can easily pass as a pure human. You have learned to hide your heritage and adapt to human society.  
Human Heritage You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human. Additionally, you gain the Society skill as trained, even if you don't meet the normal prerequisites.  
Elioud Ancestry Feats  
At 1st level and every 4 levels thereafter, you gain an ancestry feat.  
1st Level Elioud Feats  
Ancestral Strength \[Feat 1\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry  
When you make a melee Strike, you can deal an additional 1d6 damage of the weapon's type. This represents the enhanced striking power from your giant blood.  
Blending In \[Feat 1\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, when you are in a crowd of humans, you can use the Hide action even when you are observed.  
Giant's Resilience \[Feat 1\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry  
You gain resistance 2 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. This represents your enhanced durability from your giant heritage.  
Heritage Channeling \[Feat 1\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You fail an attack roll, skill check, or saving throw  
You call upon your ancestral heritage to overcome failure. Reroll the failed roll. You must use the result of the second roll, even if it is worse.  
5th Level Elioud Feats  
Enhanced Echo \[Feat 5\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Ancestral Echo feat  
The duration of your Ancestral Echo ability increases to 10 minutes. Additionally, when you use Ancestral Echo, you gain a \+3 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+3 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks.  
Master of Disguise \[Feat 5\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Blending In feat  
You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, you can use the Impersonate action without needing to be trained in Deception, though you still take the normal penalty for not being trained.  
Titanic Resilience \[Feat 5\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Giant's Resilience feat  
The resistance from your Giant's Resilience feat increases to 5 against bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, you gain resistance 2 to piercing and slashing damage from non-magical attacks.  
Ancestral Memory \[Feat 5\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Heritage Channeling feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Trigger You or an ally within 30 feet is reduced to 0 Hit Points  
Your ancestral memory awakens in a moment of crisis. The triggering creature is restored to 1 Hit Point and gains temporary Hit Points equal to your level. Additionally, you can immediately use the Heritage Channeling ability.  
9th Level Elioud Feats  
Powerful Echo \[Feat 9\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Enhanced Echo feat  
The duration of your Ancestral Echo ability increases to 1 hour. Additionally, when you use Ancestral Echo, you gain a \+4 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+4 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. Finally, you can use Ancestral Echo twice per day instead of once per day.  
Perfect Disguise \[Feat 9\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Master of Disguise feat  
You gain a \+3 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, you can use the Impersonate action without penalty, even if you are not trained in Deception. You can also change your appearance to match a specific human individual you have observed, though this requires 10 minutes of preparation.  
Colossal Resilience \[Feat 9\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Titanic Resilience feat  
You gain resistance 10 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, you are immune to the clumsy and enfeebled conditions. This represents your mastery over your giant heritage and your inability to be easily hindered.  
Ancestral Awakening \[Feat 9\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Ancestral Memory feat  
Frequency Once per day  
Action \[A\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 minute  
You fully awaken the power of your giant ancestors. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You gain resistance 10 to all damage except force  
You can use any of your Elioud ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can cast one spell of up to 4th level from your ancestral tradition (Nephilim warfare magic, Anakim enchantments, or Rephaim necromancy) without needing to prepare it or have it in your repertoire  
13th Level Elioud Feats  
Titanic Echo \[Feat 13\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Powerful Echo feat  
The duration of your Ancestral Echo ability increases to 8 hours. Additionally, when you use Ancestral Echo, you gain a \+6 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+6 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. Finally, you can use Ancestral Echo three times per day instead of once per day.  
Master of Shadows \[Feat 13\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Perfect Disguise feat  
You gain a \+4 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, you can use the Impersonate action as a single action instead of a three-action activity. You can also change your appearance to match any humanoid creature you have observed, regardless of size or species, though this requires 1 minute of preparation.  
Unbreakable \[Feat 13\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Colossal Resilience feat  
You gain resistance 15 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from all sources (including magical attacks). Additionally, you are immune to the clumsy, enfeebled, and slowed conditions. When you would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level. You can use this ability once per day.  
Ancestral Ascension \[Feat 13\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Ancestral Awakening feat  
Frequency Once per week  
Action \[AA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 10 minutes  
You temporarily ascend to the full power of your giant ancestors. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+6 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You gain resistance 20 to all damage except force  
You can use any of your Elioud ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can cast spells of up to 7th level from your ancestral tradition without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire  
You gain the ability to increase to Large size as a free action, though you return to Medium size when the duration ends  
17th Level Elioud Feats  
Colossal Echo \[Feat 17\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Titanic Echo feat  
The duration of your Ancestral Echo ability increases to 24 hours. Additionally, when you use Ancestral Echo, you gain a \+8 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+8 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. Finally, you can use Ancestral Echo at will, with no daily limit.  
Phantom of the Crowd \[Feat 17\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Master of Shadows feat  
You gain a \+6 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, you can use the Impersonate action as a free action. You can change your appearance to match any creature you have observed, regardless of size, species, or type, and this change requires no preparation time. You can maintain multiple disguises simultaneously, switching between them as a free action.  
Indestructible \[Feat 17\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Unbreakable feat  
You gain resistance 25 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from all sources. Additionally, you are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious. When you would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to twice your level. You can use this ability twice per day.  
Ancestral Godhood \[Feat 17\]  
Elioud  
Prerequisites Elioud ancestry, Ancestral Ascension feat  
Frequency Once per month  
Action \[AAA\] (concentrate)  
Duration 1 hour  
You temporarily achieve godhood through your ancestral power. For the duration, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+10 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You gain resistance 40 to all damage except force  
You can use any of your Elioud ancestry feats without expending their daily uses  
You can cast spells of up to 10th level from your ancestral tradition without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire  
You gain the ability to increase to Large size at will, and you can choose to remain Large even after the duration ends (though this reveals your true heritage)  
You can use the Wish spell once during the duration (this does not cost a spell slot or Focus Point, but it still has the normal risks and limitations of the Wish spell)  
Chapter 3: Backgrounds  
Introduction to Backgrounds  
In the antediluvian world, your character's past shapes who they are in the present. Unlike modern fantasy settings where backgrounds might represent medieval professions or guild affiliations, the backgrounds of Nephilim Wars reflect the dawn of civilization itself—a time when humanity was just beginning to build cities, develop crafts, and establish social structures under the shadow of giants and fallen angels.  
Your background represents the life you led before the events that brought you into the campaign. It encompasses your upbringing, your training, your social status, and the experiences that shaped your worldview. A Tribal Elder carries the wisdom of generations and the respect of their community. A Watcher's Apprentice bears the dangerous knowledge of forbidden arts. A Giant's Thrall knows the brutal reality of Nephilim oppression firsthand.  
Backgrounds in Nephilim Wars provide more than just mechanical benefits—they establish your character's place in the world and suggest connections to the setting's key themes and conflicts. They answer questions like: Where did you come from? What skills did you develop? Who do you know? What experiences prepared you for the challenges ahead?  
Each background grants specific mechanical advantages that reflect your character's history and training. These benefits complement your ancestry and class, helping to create a unique and well-rounded character. More importantly, they provide roleplaying hooks and narrative opportunities that can enrich your character's story and connect them to the broader world.  
This chapter presents twenty distinct backgrounds, each designed to fit the bronze-age aesthetic and thematic concerns of the Nephilim Wars setting. Whether you are a righteous Sethite preserving ancient truths, a cunning Cainite navigating urban politics, or a desperate survivor trying to escape Nephilim tyranny, these backgrounds provide the foundation for your character's journey in the age before the Flood.  
Background Mechanics  
Each background in Nephilim Wars provides the following benefits:  
Ability Boosts: Two ability boosts—one is a fixed boost to a specific ability score that reflects your background's focus, and the other is a free boost that you can assign to any ability score (including the same one as the fixed boost, if you wish).  
Skill Training: You become trained in one specific skill that represents your background's area of expertise. This skill reflects the practical knowledge and abilities you developed through your life experiences.  
Lore Training: You become trained in one specific Lore skill that represents your specialized knowledge of a particular subject, culture, or field of study. Lore skills in Nephilim Wars include both standard PF2e Lore categories and setting-specific options like Star Reading Lore, Root Cutting Lore, and Nephilim Courts Lore.  
Skill Feat: You gain one skill feat of 1st level or lower that is thematically appropriate to your background. This feat represents a specialized technique or ability you developed through your experiences.  
Additional Languages: Some backgrounds grant additional languages beyond those provided by your ancestry. These languages reflect the cultures, regions, or groups you were exposed to during your formative years.  
Equipment: Each background includes a set of starting equipment appropriate to your character's history and social status. This equipment is in addition to the gear provided by your class.  
The Backgrounds  
Watcher's Apprentice  
"And the angels, the children of the heaven, saw and lusted after them, and they said to one another: 'Come, let us choose us wives from among the children of men and beget us children.'" — 1 Enoch 6:You were initiated into the forbidden mysteries taught by the fallen Watchers. Perhaps you were born into a Sorcerer Clan family and trained from childhood in the arts of Azazel, Semyaza, or Baraqel. Maybe you sought out forbidden knowledge on your own, drawn by the promise of power that the righteous path could not offer. Or perhaps you were chosen by a lesser Watcher or a powerful sorcerer to serve as their apprentice, learning secrets that were never meant for mortal minds.  
Whatever the circumstances of your initiation, you now bear the Watcher's Mark—a visible brand on your skin that identifies you as one who has tasted forbidden knowledge. This mark may glow faintly when you use your powers, and those with spiritual sight can recognize it immediately. Some view you with fear and suspicion, while others seek you out for the power you command.  
Your training has given you insight into the celestial arts that the Watchers taught humanity. You understand the principles of enchantment, the patterns of the stars, the properties of herbs and poisons, or the techniques of warfare and metallurgy—depending on which Watcher's tradition you follow. This knowledge is dangerous, for every use of forbidden magic risks further corrupting your soul, but it is also immensely powerful.  
Ability Boosts Intelligence, free  
Skill Training Occultism  
Lore Training Forbidden Lore  
Skill Feat Quick Identification  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from Celestial, Abyssal, or Ancient  
Equipment A set of scholar's tools, a clay tablet inscribed with forbidden symbols, a bronze dagger, and a pouch containing 5 gp  
Roleplaying the Watcher's Apprentice  
As a Watcher's Apprentice, you walk a dangerous path between power and corruption. You have access to knowledge that most mortals can only dream of, but every use of that knowledge carries a spiritual cost. Your fellow adventurers may view you with suspicion, especially if they come from righteous lineages like the Sethites. Even those who benefit from your powers may fear what you might become if you continue down this path.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Watcher's Apprentice:  
Which Watcher's tradition do you follow, and why did you choose (or were chosen for) this path?  
How do you feel about the corruption that comes with using forbidden magic? Do you embrace it, resist it, or try to ignore it?  
What is your relationship with your mentor (if you still have one)? Are they a source of guidance, a rival, or a threat?  
How do you interact with righteous characters who reject forbidden knowledge? Do you try to convert them, keep your powers hidden, or openly challenge their beliefs?  
What do you hope to achieve with your forbidden knowledge? Power? Understanding? Survival? Something else?  
Tribal Elder  
"And to Seth, to him also there was born a son; and he called his name Enos: then began men to call upon the name of the LORD." — Genesis 4:You are a respected elder of your tribe, a keeper of wisdom and tradition in a world that grows increasingly chaotic. Your people look to you for guidance in matters of faith, law, and survival. You have lived for many decades (perhaps even a century or more, for lifespans in the antediluvian age are long), and your experience has taught you much about the ways of the world and the will of the Most High.  
As an elder, you are responsible for preserving your tribe's history, mediating disputes, leading rituals and sacrifices, and making decisions that affect the entire community. You may be a spiritual leader, a council member, a judge, or a combination of these roles. Your authority comes not from force or wealth but from the respect and trust of your people.  
Your position has given you deep insight into the righteous path and the dangers of corruption. You have seen the effects of Nephilim oppression on neighboring tribes. You have witnessed the spread of Watcher-taught arts and the moral decay that follows. You have guided your people through times of plenty and times of scarcity, always striving to keep them faithful to the Creator's commandments.  
Now, for reasons known only to you and the Most High, you have left your position of authority to embark on a journey into the wider world. Perhaps you received a vision calling you to a greater purpose. Maybe your tribe was destroyed by Nephilim raiders, and you seek justice or a new home for the survivors. Or perhaps you believe that the wisdom of the elders is needed beyond the borders of your tribe, in places where corruption runs rampant and the righteous are few.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Society  
Lore Training Tribal Customs Lore  
Skill Feat Group Impression  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from any regional language appropriate to your tribe's location  
Equipment An elder's staff (functions as a quarterstaff), a set of fine robes, a tribal amulet of office, and a pouch containing 10 gp  
Roleplaying the Tribal Elder  
As a Tribal Elder, you carry the weight of responsibility and the wisdom of years. You are accustomed to being respected and listened to, and you may find it difficult to adapt to situations where your authority is not recognized. Your perspective is shaped by tradition and faith, and you may be skeptical of new ideas or practices that conflict with your tribe's customs.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Tribal Elder:  
What tribe do you come from, and what are its customs and beliefs? How do these differ from other tribes you encounter?  
What specific role did you hold in your tribe (spiritual leader, judge, council member, etc.)? How does this background influence your approach to problems?  
Why did you leave your position of authority? Do you plan to return someday, or is your journey a permanent departure?  
How do you respond to corruption and forbidden knowledge? Do you try to reform those who have strayed, condemn them, or avoid them entirely?  
What wisdom or teachings do you hope to share with your companions and the wider world?  
Bronze Smith  
"And Zillah, she also bare Tubal-cain, an instructor of every artificer in brass and iron." — Genesis 4:You are a master of the metallurgical arts, a craftsman who transforms raw ore into weapons, tools, and works of beauty. Your skills were passed down through generations, originating with Tubal-Cain himself, the first smith and the instructor of all who work with brass and iron. In a world where bronze weapons determine the balance of power between tribes and where Nephilim warlords demand ever-finer arms and armor, your craft is both valuable and dangerous.  
Your forge is your sanctuary and your workshop. There, amidst the heat and smoke, you shape metal with hammer and tongs, creating blades that can pierce giant hide, armor that can turn aside Nephilim blows, and tools that make survival in the wilderness possible. You understand the properties of different metals and alloys, the techniques of tempering and quenching, and the secrets of crafting weapons that are both beautiful and deadly.  
Your reputation as a smith has likely brought you into contact with powerful figures—tribal chieftains who commission weapons for their warriors, Nephilim lords who demand tribute in the form of fine arms, or righteous leaders who seek tools for building rather than destruction. You may have used your skills to serve these patrons, or you may have refused certain commissions on moral grounds. Either way, your craft has shaped your worldview and your place in society.  
Ability Boosts Intelligence or Strength, free  
Skill Training Crafting  
Lore Training Metallurgy Lore  
Skill Feat Specialty Crafting (weapons or armor)  
Additional Languages Dwarven (reflecting your people's expertise in crafts and engineering)  
Equipment A smith's toolkit, a bronze hammer, a set of tongs, a whetstone, and a pouch containing 15 gp  
Roleplaying the Bronze Smith  
As a Bronze Smith, you value skill, precision, and the tangible results of hard work. You are practical and resourceful, with a deep understanding of how things are made and how they can be broken. Your craft has taught you patience and attention to detail, qualities that serve you well in many situations beyond the forge.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Bronze Smith:  
Who taught you your craft? Was it a family tradition, an apprenticeship with a master smith, or self-taught experimentation?  
What is your philosophy about the weapons and tools you create? Do you see them as neutral instruments, or do you believe they carry some responsibility for how they are used?  
Have you ever been asked to create something you found morally objectionable? How did you respond?  
How do you feel about the Nephilim and their demand for ever-more-powerful weapons? Do you see them as just another customer, or do you resist serving them?  
What is your relationship with other craftsmen and artisans? Are you part of a guild or brotherhood, or do you work alone?  
Temple Servant  
"And the LORD God took the man, and put him into the garden of Eden to dress it and to keep it." — Genesis 2:You have dedicated your life to the service of the Most High, working as a caretaker, attendant, or acolyte in a sacred place of worship. This might be a temple built by righteous Sethites to honor the Creator, a sacred grove where your tribe gathers to offer sacrifices, or even a hidden shrine where the faithful meet in secret to avoid persecution by Nephilim overlords or Cainite authorities.  
Your duties have varied depending on the nature of your sacred place. You may have been responsible for maintaining the temple grounds, preparing sacrifices, lighting incense, chanting prayers, or assisting priests and prophets in their rituals. You have learned the proper forms of worship, the significance of various symbols and ceremonies, and the stories of the Creator's dealings with humanity from the beginning.  
Your time in sacred service has given you a deep connection to the divine and a strong sense of right and wrong. You have witnessed miracles and heard prophecies. You have felt the presence of the Most High in moments of prayer and sacrifice. You have also seen the effects of corruption on those who turn away from the righteous path. This experience has shaped your character and your understanding of the world.  
Now, for reasons known only to you and the Most High, you have left your sacred duties to journey into the wider world. Perhaps you received a divine calling to spread the truth to those who have not heard it. Maybe your temple was destroyed by enemies of the faith, and you seek to rebuild or avenge it. Or perhaps you believe that the service of the Most High requires action beyond the temple walls, in places where corruption is strongest and the need for righteousness is greatest.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Religion  
Lore Training Sacred Texts Lore  
Skill Feat Recognize Spell  
Additional Languages Celestial (reflecting your connection to the divine)  
Equipment A set of vestments, a holy symbol of the Most High, a censer, a prayer book written on clay tablets, and a pouch containing 8 gp  
Roleplaying the Temple Servant  
As a Temple Servant, your faith is central to your identity and your actions. You are accustomed to a life of discipline, ritual, and devotion, and you may find the chaos of the outside world disorienting at times. Your perspective is shaped by your religious training, and you may be quick to see divine significance in everyday events.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Temple Servant:  
What specific temple or sacred place did you serve in, and what were your duties there? How did these duties shape your understanding of the divine?  
What is your relationship with the priests, prophets, or other religious leaders you served under? Do you still maintain contact with them?  
How do you respond to places of worship dedicated to false gods or corrupted practices? Do you try to purify them, avoid them, or confront them?  
What specific teachings or scriptures have most influenced your faith? How do you apply these teachings to the challenges you face?  
How do you balance your devotion to the Most High with the practical demands of survival in a dangerous world?  
Giant's Thrall  
"And the giants, who were produced from them, devoured the labor of men, and when men could no longer sustain them, the giants turned against them and devoured mankind." — 1 Enoch 7:You were once a slave or servant in the household of a Nephilim or other giant lord. Perhaps you were born into servitude, your family having been subjugated by giants for generations. Maybe you were captured in a raid on your village and forced to serve your giant masters. Or perhaps you voluntarily entered into service, seeing it as the only way to survive in a world dominated by titans.  
Your life as a thrall was one of hardship and danger. You performed menial tasks—cooking, cleaning, carrying heavy loads, tending to the giant's needs—while living in constant fear of your master's wrath. You learned to read the moods of your giant overlords, to anticipate their demands, and to avoid their notice when possible. You witnessed the brutality of giant rule firsthand, seeing fellow thralls punished or killed for minor offenses, and you developed a deep understanding of giant psychology and behavior.  
Despite the oppression, or perhaps because of it, you managed to escape. The circumstances of your escape vary—you may have fled during a moment of chaos, been rescued by rebels, or even killed your master in a desperate bid for freedom. Whatever the case, you now carry the scars (physical and emotional) of your servitude, along with valuable knowledge about your former masters and their weaknesses.  
Ability Boosts Constitution, free  
Skill Training Stealth  
Lore Training Nephilim Courts Lore  
Skill Feat Quick Escape  
Additional Languages Giant (learned while serving your masters)  
Equipment A set of worn but serviceable clothing, a small knife hidden in your boot, a token taken from your former master's household, and a pouch containing 3 gp  
Roleplaying the Giant's Thrall  
As a Giant's Thrall, you carry the trauma of servitude and the determination of someone who has escaped oppression. You are wary of authority figures, especially those who remind you of your former masters. You are resourceful and resilient, having learned to survive in harsh conditions. You may have a deep-seated hatred for giants, or you may have developed a more complex understanding of them through your close contact.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Giant's Thrall:  
How long were you in servitude, and at what age did you escape? How has this experience shaped your worldview?  
What specific giant or giants did you serve, and what were they like? Did any of them show you kindness, or were they all cruel?  
How did you escape? Was it a solo effort, or did you have help? Do you have unfinished business related to your escape (rescuing others, retrieving something you left behind, etc.)?  
How do you feel about other giants you encounter? Do you see them all as enemies, or do you recognize that some may be different from your former masters?  
What skills or knowledge did you gain during your servitude that now serve you well? Are there any habits or behaviors from your time as a thrall that you still struggle to overcome?  
Wandering Prophet  
"And Enoch walked with God: and he was not; for God took him." — Genesis 5:24
You are a prophet of the Most High, called to speak truth to a world that has turned away from righteousness. Like Enoch before you, you have received visions and revelations that guide your path and inform your message. You wander from place to place, bringing warnings of judgment to the corrupt, comfort to the oppressed, and calls to repentance to all who will listen.  
Your prophetic gift manifests in various ways. You may receive dreams that reveal future events. You might hear the voice of the Most High speaking directly to you. You could have visions that show you hidden truths about people and places. Or you might simply possess an uncanny insight into the hearts of others and the consequences of their actions. Whatever the form of your gift, it has set you apart from ordinary people and drawn both followers and enemies to you.  
Your message is not always welcome. The righteous may embrace your words, but the corrupt often reject them—sometimes violently. You have likely faced persecution, ridicule, and threats as you proclaim the coming judgment and call people to turn from their wicked ways. Yet you persist, driven by the conviction that your message is necessary, however unwelcome it may be.  
You travel light, carrying only what you need to survive and to deliver your message. You rely on the hospitality of those who accept your words, and you are prepared to move on quickly when your presence becomes dangerous. Your life is one of uncertainty and risk, but also of purpose and divine guidance.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Diplomacy  
Lore Training Prophecy Lore  
Skill Feat Fascinating Performance (using your prophetic speeches)  
Additional Languages Celestial (reflecting your connection to divine revelation)  
Equipment A prophet's staff, a set of simple robes, a scroll containing your prophecies, a waterskin, and a pouch containing 5 gp  
Roleplaying the Wandering Prophet  
As a Wandering Prophet, your identity is defined by your divine calling and your message. You are passionate about your mission and may sometimes seem single-minded or uncompromising in your pursuit of righteousness. You are accustomed to being an outsider, someone who speaks uncomfortable truths to those in power. You may struggle with doubt or fear at times, but your faith in your calling sustains you.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Wandering Prophet:  
What is the primary message or theme of your prophecies? Is it focused on judgment, repentance, hope, or something else?  
How do you receive your revelations? Through dreams, visions, direct communication, or some other means?  
Have you ever had a prophecy that you didn't want to deliver? How did you handle that situation?  
What is your relationship with other prophets or religious figures? Do you see them as allies, rivals, or something else?  
How do you balance the urgency of your message with the need to survive in a dangerous world? Are there times when you hold back your words for safety's sake?  
Merchant of Enoch  
"And Cain knew his wife; and she conceived, and bare Enoch: and he builded a city, and called the name of the city, after the name of his son, Enoch." — Genesis 4:You are a merchant from the great city of Enoch, the first metropolis built by Cainite civilization. Your family has been involved in trade for generations, establishing networks of contacts and developing expertise in the movement of goods across vast distances. You understand the value of commodities, the art of negotiation, and the importance of maintaining good relationships with suppliers and customers.  
The city of Enoch is a marvel of antediluvian engineering and culture. Its bronze-reinforced walls protect a bustling population of artisans, musicians, metallurgists, and merchants. Within its gates, goods from across the known world are bought and sold—bronze weapons from Tubal-Cain's forges, musical instruments from Jubal's workshops, livestock from Jabal's herds, and exotic items traded from distant lands.  
Your merchant background has given you practical skills in business and finance, as well as a broad knowledge of the world beyond your home city. You have traveled to other settlements, both Cainite and independent, establishing trade routes and learning about different cultures and customs. You understand the political landscape of the antediluvian world and know how to navigate it to your advantage.  
You may have left the city of Enoch for various reasons. Perhaps you were exiled for some transgression. Maybe you sought new markets or opportunities beyond the city's walls. Or perhaps you became disillusioned with Cainite society and its embrace of Watcher-taught arts, choosing to seek a different path.  
Ability Boosts Charisma, free  
Skill Training Diplomacy  
Lore Training Trade Routes Lore  
Skill Feat Bargain Hunter  
Additional Languages Two languages of your choice from any regional languages appropriate to trade networks  
Equipment Fine clothing suitable for a merchant, a set of merchant's scales, a ledger book, a signet ring identifying you as a member of your merchant house, and a pouch containing 25 gp  
Roleplaying the Merchant of Enoch  
As a Merchant of Enoch, you are pragmatic, resourceful, and skilled in the art of negotiation. You value relationships and reputation, knowing that your success depends on maintaining good standing with your contacts. You are comfortable in urban environments and may feel out of place in the wilderness or in small tribal settlements. Your perspective is shaped by your mercantile background—you tend to think in terms of value, exchange, and mutual benefit.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Merchant of Enoch:  
What specific goods or services does your merchant house specialize in? How does this specialization influence your knowledge and skills?  
What is your relationship with the ruling authorities of the city of Enoch? Are you loyal to them, independent, or opposed to their rule?  
How do you feel about the Watcher-taught arts that are prevalent in Cainite society? Do you embrace them, avoid them, or see them as just another commodity to be traded?  
What motivated you to leave the city of Enoch? Was it a voluntary choice, or were you forced out?  
How do you apply your mercantile skills to non-business situations? Do you try to negotiate or trade your way out of conflicts, or do you have other approaches?  
Beast Tamer  
"And out of the ground the LORD God formed every beast of the field, and every fowl of the air; and brought them unto Adam to see what he would call them." — Genesis 2:You have a special connection with animals, a gift for understanding and communicating with them that sets you apart from ordinary people. This ability may be innate, something you were born with, or it may be a skill you developed through years of experience living and working with beasts. Whatever its origin, your talent for taming and training animals has made you valuable to your community and has shaped your understanding of the world.  
Your relationship with animals goes beyond mere utility. You see them as companions, allies, and sometimes even friends. You understand their behaviors, their needs, and their ways of communicating. You can calm a frightened horse, train a hunting dog, or even befriend wild creatures that would attack others. This connection has given you insight into the natural world and has taught you lessons about loyalty, instinct, and survival that you apply to your interactions with people as well.  
You may have worked as a herdsman, caring for livestock and protecting them from predators. You might have been a hunter, using trained animals to track and bring down game. You could have served as a guardian, using fierce beasts to protect your settlement from threats. Or you might simply have been someone who found solace and purpose in the company of animals when human society failed you.  
Your bond with beasts has likely drawn you away from settled life and into the wilderness, where you feel most at home. You are comfortable living outdoors, surviving on what the land provides, and traveling with animal companions at your side. The call of civilization holds little appeal for you compared to the freedom and simplicity of life among the creatures of the wild.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Nature  
Lore Training Animal Behavior Lore  
Skill Feat Train Animal  
Additional Languages Sylvan or Druidic (reflecting your connection to the natural world)  
Equipment A set of wilderness survival gear, a trained animal companion (such as a dog, hawk, or horse), a whistle for signaling your companion, and a pouch containing 10 gp  
Roleplaying the Beast Tamer  
As a Beast Tamer, your perspective is shaped by your relationship with animals. You may find human society confusing or frustrating at times, preferring the straightforward nature of beasts. You are patient, observant, and attuned to non-verbal communication. You may be more comfortable expressing yourself through actions than words, and you may have developed a quiet, unassuming demeanor that belies your inner strength.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Beast Tamer:  
What is your primary animal companion, and how did you come to be bonded with it? What is your relationship like?  
Do you have a special affinity for a particular type of animal (predators, herd animals, birds, etc.)? Why are you drawn to these creatures?  
How do other people react to your ability to communicate with animals? Do they see it as a gift, a curiosity, or something to be feared?  
What have you learned from animals that you apply to your interactions with people? Are there lessons from the natural world that guide your decisions?  
How do you feel about the domestication and use of animals by human society? Do you see it as a natural relationship, exploitation, or something else?  
Star Reader  
"And Baraqel taught astrology, and the constellations, and the course of the moon, and the course of the sun." — 1 Enoch 8:You are a student of the heavens, an astronomer and astrologer who reads the stars for guidance, prophecy, and understanding. Your knowledge comes from the teachings of Baraqel, one of the fallen Watchers who revealed the secrets of celestial observation to humanity. Though this knowledge is forbidden and carries the risk of corruption, you believe that understanding the patterns of the heavens can provide insight into the will of the Most High and the fate of the world.  
Your studies have given you expertise in identifying constellations, tracking the movements of planets and stars, predicting celestial events, and interpreting the symbolic meanings of astronomical phenomena. You keep detailed records of your observations on clay tablets or parchment, creating star charts and calendars that help you understand the cycles of time and the influences of the heavens on earthly affairs.  
You may use your knowledge for various purposes. You might provide guidance to tribal leaders about the best times for planting, harvesting, or going to war. You could offer personal readings to individuals seeking insight into their futures. You might search for signs of the coming judgment in celestial events. Or you might simply pursue astronomical knowledge for its own sake, driven by a desire to understand the cosmos and humanity's place within it.  
Your work requires patience, precision, and long hours of observation, often at night. You are accustomed to solitude and contemplation, and you may seem distant or preoccupied to those who do not share your interests. Your connection to the heavens has given you a broad perspective on the world, but it may have also made you somewhat detached from everyday concerns.  
Ability Boosts Intelligence, free  
Skill Training Occultism  
Lore Training Astronomy Lore  
Skill Feat Dubious Knowledge (applied to astrological interpretations)  
Additional Languages Celestial (reflecting your study of the heavens)  
Equipment A set of astronomical instruments (astrolabe, compass, measuring tools), a collection of star charts on clay tablets, a journal for recording observations, and a pouch containing 12 gp  
Roleplaying the Star Reader  
As a Star Reader, your worldview is shaped by your understanding of celestial patterns and their influences. You tend to think in terms of cycles, signs, and long-term consequences rather than immediate concerns. You may seem mysterious or aloof to others, especially those who do not share your interest in the heavens. Your knowledge of forbidden arts may create tension with righteous characters, but you believe that the stars themselves are not corrupt—only the intentions of those who use this knowledge.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Star Reader:  
What specific aspect of astronomy or astrology most interests you (predicting events, understanding influences, mapping the heavens, etc.)? How does this focus shape your approach to your studies?  
How do you reconcile your use of forbidden knowledge with your moral beliefs? Do you see yourself as walking a dangerous line, or do you believe your intentions justify the risks?  
What is the most significant celestial event or pattern you have observed, and what did it reveal to you?  
How do you respond when your astrological predictions prove incorrect? Do you question your methods, your interpretations, or the reliability of celestial guidance?  
What do you hope to achieve through your study of the stars? Are you seeking personal enlightenment, practical guidance, prophetic insight, or something else?  
Herb Cutter  
"And Semyaza taught enchantments, and root-cuttings." — 1 Enoch 8:You are a master of herbalism and plant lore, skilled in identifying, harvesting, and preparing plants for various purposes. Your knowledge encompasses both beneficial and harmful applications—you can create healing poultices and remedies, but you also understand the properties of poisonous plants and the arts of creating toxins and venoms. This dual nature of your craft reflects its origin in the teachings of Semyaza, one of the fallen Watchers who revealed the secrets of root-cutting to humanity.  
Your expertise extends beyond simple identification of plants. You understand their growth cycles, their habitats, their interactions with other plants and animals, and the optimal times and methods for harvesting them. You know how to prepare herbs through drying, crushing, boiling, fermenting, or other techniques to extract their properties. You can create salves, tinctures, poultices, teas, and powders for medicinal, poisonous, or ritual purposes.  
Your work as a herb cutter has likely made you valuable to your community. You may serve as a healer, treating injuries and illnesses with your herbal remedies. You might work as a poisoner, creating toxins for hunting, warfare, or more sinister purposes. You could be a ritualist, using herbs in ceremonies and magical practices. Or you might simply be a gatherer and preparer of plants, selling your products to those who need them.  
Your connection to the plant world has given you a deep appreciation for the natural cycles of growth, decay, and renewal. You are patient and methodical, understanding that some processes cannot be rushed. You are also aware of the dual nature of many plants—the same herb that can heal in one context can harm in another, depending on preparation, dosage, and intent.  
Ability Boosts Intelligence or Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Nature  
Lore Training Herbalism Lore  
Skill Feat Specialty Crafting (alchemical items)  
Additional Languages Sylvan or Druidic (reflecting your connection to the plant world)  
Equipment A herb cutter's toolkit (knife, mortar and pestle, drying racks, containers), a collection of dried herbs and plants, a book of herbal recipes, and a pouch containing 8 gp  
Roleplaying the Herb Cutter  
As a Herb Cutter, your perspective is shaped by your understanding of the natural world and the dual nature of your craft. You are practical and resourceful, with a deep knowledge of the properties and uses of plants. You may be cautious about sharing your knowledge, aware of the potential for misuse. Your work requires patience and attention to detail, qualities that influence your approach to other aspects of life as well.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Herb Cutter:  
Do you focus more on healing herbs or poisonous plants? What draws you to this aspect of your craft?  
How do you decide when to use your knowledge for healing versus harm? Are there ethical guidelines that guide your choices?  
What is your relationship with other healers, poisoners, or herbalists? Do you share knowledge freely, guard your secrets jealously, or seek to learn from others?  
How do you feel about the forbidden origins of your craft? Do you see it as tainted, or do you believe that knowledge itself is neutral and depends on how it is used?  
What is the most valuable or dangerous plant you have ever worked with, and what did you learn from the experience?  
Survivor of the Flood Plains  
"And God said unto Noah, The end of all flesh is come before me; for the earth is filled with violence through them; and, behold, I will destroy them with the earth." — Genesis 6:You come from a region that has already experienced the early signs of the coming judgment—flooding, storms, and environmental upheaval that foreshadow the Great Flood to come. Your homeland, once fertile and prosperous, has been devastated by rising waters, torrential rains, and violent weather. You have witnessed the destruction of settlements, the loss of crops and livestock, and the displacement of entire communities.  
Your survival in these harsh conditions has taught you valuable skills in wilderness survival, resource management, and adaptation to changing environments. You know how to find food and water in flooded landscapes, how to build shelters that can withstand storms, how to navigate by landmarks that are constantly shifting due to erosion and deposition, and how to predict weather patterns based on subtle signs that others might miss.  
You have also learned hard lessons about human nature in times of crisis. You have seen communities come together in mutual aid and support, but you have also witnessed desperation, violence, and betrayal as people compete for scarce resources. You understand the fragility of civilization and the resilience of the human spirit. You carry the trauma of loss and displacement, but also the strength that comes from having survived against overwhelming odds.  
Your experiences have given you a unique perspective on the prophecies of judgment that are spreading through the antediluvian world. While others may dismiss these warnings as fear-mongering or superstition, you have seen firsthand the signs that something is profoundly wrong with the world. You may be more receptive to messages of repentance and preparation, or you may be cynical about the possibility of avoiding the inevitable.  
Ability Boosts Constitution, free  
Skill Training Survival  
Lore Training Weather Patterns Lore  
Skill Feat Forager  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from any regional language appropriate to your homeland  
Equipment A set of weather-resistant clothing, a waterskin, a fishing net, a knife, a tarp for shelter, and a pouch containing 5 gp  
Roleplaying the Survivor of the Flood Plains  
As a Survivor of the Flood Plains, your worldview is shaped by your experiences of environmental catastrophe and human resilience. You are pragmatic and resourceful, with a deep understanding of the natural world and its dangers. You may be pessimistic about the future, having seen the destructive power of nature firsthand, or you may be optimistic about humanity's ability to adapt and survive. Your experiences have likely made you cautious about forming attachments, aware of how quickly everything can be taken away.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Survivor of the Flood Plains:  
What specific region did you come from, and what was it like before the flooding began? What do you miss most about your homeland?  
How did you survive when others did not? What skills, knowledge, or luck enabled you to endure?  
Have you lost family members or close friends to the flooding? How has this loss affected you?  
How do you respond to people who have not experienced environmental catastrophe? Do you try to warn them, envy their innocence, or avoid discussing your experiences?  
What do you believe is causing the flooding and environmental changes? Do you see it as divine judgment, natural cycles, or something else?  
Nephilim Offspring  
"There were giants in the earth in those days; and also after that, when the sons of God came in unto the daughters of men, and they bare children to them, the same became mighty men which were of old, men of renown." — Genesis 6:You are the offspring of a Nephilim or other giant and a human parent, a living reminder of the forbidden unions that have corrupted the world. Your heritage places you in an ambiguous position—you are not fully human, but you are also not a full giant. You may appear mostly human with subtle giant characteristics (unusual height, strength, or features), or you may be more obviously hybrid in appearance, marking you as different from both humans and giants.  
Your mixed heritage has shaped your life in profound ways. If your giant parent was powerful or respected, you may have grown up with certain privileges and advantages, but also with the burden of expectations and the resentment of those who oppose giant rule. If your human parent was from a righteous lineage, you may have been raised to reject your giant heritage and embrace the righteous path, struggling with the conflicting aspects of your nature. If you were born out of violence or exploitation, you may carry the trauma of your origins and the stigma of your parentage.  
Your experiences have given you unique insights into both human and giant societies. You understand the perspectives, motivations, and behaviors of both groups, and you may be able to move between them in ways that pure humans or pure giants cannot. This ability to bridge worlds can be a valuable asset, but it can also make you feel like you don't truly belong anywhere.  
Your physical and mental characteristics reflect your mixed heritage. You may have enhanced strength, size, or durability compared to pure humans. You might possess unusual abilities or senses inherited from your giant parent. You could have a longer lifespan than humans but shorter than giants. Your appearance may mark you as different, making you stand out in human society and potentially causing fear or hostility.  
Ability Boosts Strength or Charisma, free  
Skill Training Intimidation  
Lore Training Giant Kin Lore  
Skill Feat Group Coercion  
Additional Languages Giant (inherited from your giant parent)  
Equipment Clothing that helps conceal your hybrid nature (if desired), a token from your giant parent (if you have one), a weapon appropriate to your size and strength, and a pouch containing 10 gp  
Roleplaying the Nephilim Offspring  
As a Nephilim Offspring, your identity is shaped by the tension between your human and giant heritage. You may struggle with questions of belonging, loyalty, and self-acceptance. You might feel torn between the values and perspectives of your two lineages, or you might have chosen to embrace one side of your heritage over the other. Your experiences of prejudice, privilege, or ambiguity have likely made you resilient and adaptable, but also potentially guarded or conflicted.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Nephilim Offspring:  
What is the nature of your parentage? Was your birth the result of love, violence, exploitation, or something else? How does this origin story affect your self-image?  
Do you know both of your parents, or only one? What is your relationship with them like?  
How do you feel about your giant heritage? Do you embrace it, reject it, or have a complex relationship with it?  
How do humans and giants react to you? Do you face prejudice, curiosity, fear, or acceptance from each group?  
What unique insights or abilities have you gained from your mixed heritage? How do you use these to your advantage?  
Keeper of the Scrolls  
"And all the days of Enoch were three hundred sixty and five years: And Enoch walked with God: and he was not; for God took him." — Genesis 5:23-You are a scholar and scribe, dedicated to preserving knowledge in a world where much is being lost to corruption and violence. Your work involves copying, studying, and safeguarding written records—genealogies, histories, laws, prophecies, and other texts that contain the wisdom and experiences of past generations. You may serve in a library, scriptorium, or archive maintained by a righteous community, or you may be an independent scholar traveling to collect and preserve texts from endangered sources.  
Your skills encompass not only reading and writing but also the physical arts of creating and maintaining written materials. You know how to prepare clay tablets, parchment, or papyrus for writing. You understand the properties of different inks and pigments and how to make them last. You can repair damaged texts and create protective containers for valuable scrolls. You are also skilled in the organization and retrieval of information, able to find specific passages or references quickly in large collections.  
Your knowledge extends beyond mere literacy. You have studied the content of the texts you preserve, gaining expertise in history, theology, law, astronomy, medicine, and other subjects. You understand the context and significance of different writings and can interpret obscure references or symbolic language. You may also be familiar with multiple writing systems and languages, enabling you to access texts from different cultures and periods.  
Your role as a keeper of knowledge carries both privilege and responsibility. You have access to information that most people never see, but you also bear the burden of ensuring that this knowledge is not lost, corrupted, or misused. You may face difficult choices about which texts to preserve, which to destroy, and which to share with others. You may also encounter dangers—from those who want to suppress certain knowledge, from those who want to exploit it for corrupt purposes, or from the general chaos of a world descending into judgment.  
Ability Boosts Intelligence, free  
Skill Training Society  
Lore Training Ancient Texts Lore  
Skill Feat Multilingual  
Additional Languages Two languages of your choice from Ancient, Celestial, or any other languages appropriate to scholarly study  
Equipment A scribe's toolkit (stylus, ink, writing surfaces), a collection of scrolls or tablets, a book chest, a magnifying lens, and a pouch containing 15 gp  
Roleplaying the Keeper of the Scrolls  
As a Keeper of the Scrolls, your identity is defined by your dedication to knowledge and preservation. You are methodical, detail-oriented, and patient, with a deep respect for the written word and the wisdom it contains. You may seem bookish or academic to others, more comfortable with texts than with people. Your perspective is shaped by your study of history and ancient wisdom, giving you a long-term view of events and a sense of continuity with the past.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Keeper of the Scrolls:  
What specific types of texts do you specialize in preserving (genealogies, prophecies, laws, histories, etc.)? Why are these texts particularly important to you?  
What is the most valuable or significant text in your collection, and why is it so important?  
How do you decide which texts to preserve and which to let go? Are there ethical guidelines that guide your choices?  
What dangers or challenges have you faced in your work as a keeper of knowledge? How have you overcome them?  
How do you balance your duty to preserve knowledge with the potential risks of that knowledge falling into the wrong hands?  
Hunter of Abominations  
"And the angels, the children of the heaven, saw and lusted after them, and they said to one another: 'Come, let us choose us wives from among the children of men and beget us children.'" — 1 Enoch 6:You are a dedicated hunter of corrupted beings—Nephilim, demons, possessed humans, and other abominations that threaten the righteous and corrupt the world. Your calling may be religious, driven by a conviction that these creatures are an affront to the Most High and must be eliminated. It may be personal, motivated by the loss of loved ones to giant oppression or demonic possession. Or it may be pragmatic, based on the recognition that these threats must be dealt with for humanity to survive.  
Your training has made you an expert in tracking, identifying, and combating supernatural threats. You know the signs of corruption to look for in people and places. You understand the strengths and weaknesses of different types of abominations. You have developed specialized weapons, tactics, and techniques for dealing with these foes. You are skilled in stealth and ambush, knowing that direct confrontation with powerful enemies is often suicidal.  
Your work has taken you to dark and dangerous places—Nephilim strongholds, haunted ruins, corrupted settlements, and other locations where abominations gather or prey upon the innocent. You have seen things that would break ordinary people, and you have developed a grim determination and a certain detachment from the horrors you face. You may struggle with the moral weight of your calling, questioning whether your methods are justified or whether you are becoming as corrupted as the creatures you hunt.  
Despite the darkness of your work, you are driven by a sense of purpose and a belief that your efforts make a difference. You may see yourself as a protector of the innocent, a purifier of corruption, or an agent of divine judgment. Your experiences have given you a unique perspective on the struggle between righteousness and corruption, and you may serve as a guide or mentor to others who face similar threats.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Survival  
Lore Training Aberrations Lore  
Skill Feat Hunter's Lore  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from Abyssal, Giant, or any other language useful for understanding your prey  
Equipment A specialized weapon for hunting abominations (such as a blessed spear or a silver-tipped arrow), a set of tracking tools, protective charms or amulets, a journal for recording your hunts, and a pouch containing 12 gp  
Roleplaying the Hunter of Abominations  
As a Hunter of Abominations, your worldview is shaped by your experiences confronting corruption and supernatural evil. You are vigilant, suspicious, and prepared for danger, with a keen eye for signs of corruption or threat. You may be grim and serious, having seen too much darkness to maintain a lighthearted outlook. Your sense of purpose drives you forward, but it may also isolate you from others who do not share your calling or understand your methods.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Hunter of Abominations:  
What motivated you to become a hunter of abominations? Was it a personal loss, a religious calling, a practical necessity, or something else?  
What is your most significant victory as a hunter, and what did it cost you?  
What is your greatest failure or regret, and how has it affected your approach to your work?  
How do you maintain your own righteousness and avoid becoming corrupted by the darkness you fight? Do you have specific practices or beliefs that help you stay grounded?  
What is your ultimate goal as a hunter? Do you hope to eliminate all abominations, protect specific people or places, prepare for the coming judgment, or something else?  
Penitent Cultist  
"And the giants, who were produced from them, devoured the labor of men, and when men could no longer sustain them, the giants turned against them and devoured mankind." — 1 Enoch 7:You were once a member of a cult that worshipped the Watchers, Nephilim, or other corrupted beings, but you have since renounced your former beliefs and seek redemption for your past actions. Your time in the cult exposed you to forbidden knowledge, dark rituals, and practices that you now recognize as corrupt and destructive. You may have participated in these activities willingly, been coerced into them, or joined the cult out of ignorance before realizing its true nature.  
Your experiences in the cult have left you with both scars and skills. You understand the beliefs, practices, and organizational structures of corrupted groups. You may know secret signs, passwords, or rituals that can help you infiltrate or oppose similar groups. You have insights into the psychology of corruption and the methods used to recruit and control members. You may also have knowledge of forbidden arts or dark magic that you learned during your time in the cult, though you now struggle with the temptation to use this power.  
Your journey toward redemption is ongoing and often difficult. You may face suspicion and distrust from righteous communities who are wary of your past. You may struggle with guilt, shame, or trauma from your experiences. You might encounter former cult members who seek to punish you for your betrayal or try to lure you back into their fold. You may also face internal conflicts as you work to overcome the conditioning and beliefs you absorbed during your time in the cult.  
Despite these challenges, you are committed to making amends for your past and using your knowledge to oppose the corruption you once served. You may work to expose and dismantle cults, rescue current members, or warn others about the dangers of forbidden paths. Your unique perspective as a former insider can be invaluable in these efforts, though it also makes you a target for those you oppose.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Deception  
Lore Training Cult Practices Lore  
Skill Feat Lie to Me  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from Abyssal, Celestial, or any other language associated with your former cult  
Equipment Clothing that helps you blend in with ordinary people (concealing any cult markings), a token of your repentance (such as a symbol of the Most High), a journal documenting your experiences and insights, and a pouch containing 7 gp  
Roleplaying the Penitent Cultist  
As a Penitent Cultist, your identity is shaped by your journey from corruption to redemption. You carry the weight of your past actions and the determination to make amends. You may be cautious about trusting others, aware of how easily people can be manipulated or deceived. You might struggle with self-doubt or feelings of unworthiness, questioning whether you can ever truly atone for your past. At the same time, your experiences have given you valuable insights and a deep appreciation for the importance of righteousness.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Penitent Cultist:  
What specific cult were you a member of, and what were its beliefs and practices? What initially drew you to it?  
What caused you to leave the cult and seek redemption? Was it a gradual realization, a specific event, or the influence of someone else?  
What is the worst thing you did as a cult member, and how do you cope with the guilt or trauma associated with it?  
How do you respond when you encounter current cult members or corrupted individuals? Do you try to convert them, avoid them, or confront them?  
What does redemption mean to you? How will you know if you have achieved it?  
Desert Nomad  
"And the sons of God saw the daughters of men that they were fair; and they took them wives of all which they chose." — Genesis 6:You are a member of a nomadic people who roam the deserts and arid regions of the antediluvian world, following seasonal patterns and seeking out scarce resources in harsh environments. Your people have developed specialized skills and knowledge for surviving in these challenging conditions—finding water in seemingly barren landscapes, navigating by stars and landmarks, protecting themselves from extreme temperatures and sandstorms, and making use of the limited plants and animals that can thrive in desert environments.  
Your nomadic lifestyle has shaped your worldview and values. You are self-reliant and adaptable, accustomed to dealing with uncertainty and change. You have a deep connection to the land and its rhythms, understanding the subtle signs that indicate the presence of water, the approach of storms, or the movements of game. You value community and cooperation, knowing that survival in the desert depends on mutual support and shared knowledge. You may have a spiritual connection to the desert itself, seeing it as a place of testing, purification, or revelation.  
Your people may have various relationships with settled communities. You might trade with them, exchanging desert goods (such as salt, rare plants, or crafted items) for agricultural products and manufactured goods. You might serve as guides or scouts for travelers crossing the desert. You might raid settlements when resources are scarce. Or you might avoid contact with settled peoples altogether, maintaining your independence and traditional ways of life.  
Your experiences have given you a unique perspective on the antediluvian world. You have seen places and things that settled people never encounter. You have developed skills and knowledge that are rare among those who live in cities or agricultural communities. You may have insights into the movements of Nephilim warbands, the locations of hidden oases or ruins, or the patterns of desert wildlife that could be valuable to others.  
Ability Boosts Constitution, free  
Skill Training Survival  
Lore Training Desert Survival Lore  
Skill Feat Terrain Expertise (desert)  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from any regional language spoken by desert-dwelling peoples  
Equipment Desert-appropriate clothing (light, loose-fitting, protective from sun), a waterskin, a sunshade or head covering, a knife, a small tent or shelter, and a pouch containing 8 gp  
Roleplaying the Desert Nomad  
As a Desert Nomad, your perspective is shaped by your experiences of harsh environments, self-reliance, and community interdependence. You are practical, resourceful, and attuned to the natural world. You may seem reserved or cautious to outsiders, but you are fiercely loyal to those you consider part of your community. Your values emphasize survival, adaptability, and respect for the land and its resources.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Desert Nomad:  
What specific desert region do you come from, and what are its characteristics and challenges?  
What is your role within your nomadic community (hunter, guide, healer, elder, etc.)? How did you come to have this role?  
How does your community make decisions and resolve conflicts? What values or traditions guide your social interactions?  
What is your relationship with settled communities? Do you trade with them, avoid them, raid them, or have some other interaction?  
What is the most valuable lesson the desert has taught you, and how does it influence your approach to life?  
River Fisher  
"And out of the ground the LORD God made to grow every tree that is pleasant to the sight, and good for food; the tree of life also in the midst of the garden, and the tree of knowledge of good and evil." — Genesis 2:You are a fisher who makes your living along the rivers, lakes, and coastal waters of the antediluvian world. Your skills encompass not only fishing but also boat handling, navigation, weather prediction, and knowledge of aquatic ecosystems. You understand the behaviors and habitats of different fish species, the best techniques and tools for catching them, and the methods for preserving and preparing your catch. You are also familiar with the dangers of water travel—storms, currents, submerged obstacles, and aquatic predators.  
Your life is intimately connected to the waterways you depend on. You know the seasonal patterns of fish migrations and water levels. You can read the signs of weather changes in the water's surface, the behavior of birds and other animals, and the patterns of clouds and wind. You understand the geography of river systems—the locations of rapids, deep pools, tributaries, and landing sites. You may also have knowledge of trade routes and transportation networks that follow waterways, giving you insights into the movement of goods and people across the region.  
Your community may be a small fishing village, a family group that travels along the waterways, or part of a larger settlement that depends on fishing for sustenance and trade. Your status within your community depends on your skill and success as a fisher, as well as your willingness to share your knowledge and resources with others. You may also have relationships with traders who buy your catch or with other communities that rely on your expertise for transportation or guidance.  
Your experiences on the water have given you a unique perspective on the world. You have seen things that land-dwellers never encounter—distant shores, hidden coves, unusual wildlife, and the ever-changing moods of the water. You have developed patience, resilience, and a certain philosophical outlook shaped by the rhythms of nature and the uncertainties of fishing. You may also have a spiritual connection to the water and its creatures, seeing them as gifts from the Creator or as manifestations of divine power.  
Ability Boosts Wisdom, free  
Skill Training Athletics  
Lore Training Aquatic Ecosystems Lore  
Skill Feat Underwater Marauder  
Additional Languages Aquan or one language of your choice from any regional language spoken by riverside communities  
Equipment Fishing gear (nets, lines, hooks, traps), a small boat or raft, a paddle or oar, waterproof containers, a knife, and a pouch containing 10 gp  
Roleplaying the River Fisher  
As a River Fisher, your worldview is shaped by your connection to the water and its rhythms. You are patient, observant, and attuned to natural patterns and signs. You may seem calm and steady to others, with a certain philosophical acceptance of the uncertainties of life. Your values emphasize self-sufficiency, community cooperation, and respect for the natural world. You may have a practical, no-nonsense approach to problems, preferring direct solutions over complicated schemes.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your River Fisher:  
What specific waterway do you primarily fish (a great river, a lake, a coastal area, etc.)? What are its characteristics and challenges?  
What fishing techniques and tools do you specialize in? How did you learn these skills?  
How does your community organize its fishing activities? Is it cooperative, competitive, or some combination?  
What is your relationship with other communities that depend on the same waterway? Do you cooperate, compete, or have some other interaction?  
What is the most memorable or significant experience you've had while fishing? How did it affect you?  
Stone Mason  
"And Cain went out from the presence of the LORD, and dwelt in the land of Nod, on the east of Eden." — Genesis 4:You are a skilled craftsman who works with stone, creating structures, tools, monuments, and artworks from the raw materials of the earth. Your expertise encompasses quarrying, cutting, shaping, and assembling stone, as well as understanding the properties and uses of different types of rock. You may specialize in architectural stonework (building walls, foundations, temples, or other structures), sculptural work (creating statues, reliefs, or decorative elements), or practical stonework (making tools, weapons, or containers).  
Your craft requires strength, precision, and patience. You understand how to select appropriate stone for different purposes, how to work with the natural grain and structure of the material, and how to create durable, functional, and aesthetically pleasing results. You are familiar with the tools of your trade—hammers, chisels, wedges, levers, and other implements—and you know how to maintain and use them effectively. You may also have knowledge of related skills such as mortar-making, foundation-laying, or structural engineering.  
Your work has likely brought you into contact with various aspects of antediluvian society. You may have contributed to the construction of Cainite cities, Nephilim fortresses, Sethite temples, or other significant structures. You might have created monuments to commemorate important events or people. You could have made tools and weapons for hunters, warriors, or other craftsmen. Your skills are valuable in many contexts, and your reputation as a stonemason may open doors or create opportunities in different communities.
