Your connection to stone and the earth has given you a unique perspective on the world. You understand the patience and persistence required to shape hard materials. You appreciate the durability and permanence of stone, seeing your work as something that may outlast you and serve future generations. You may have a spiritual or philosophical connection to the earth and its materials, viewing your craft as a form of collaboration with the natural world rather than domination over it.  
Ability Boosts Strength or Intelligence, free  
Skill Training Crafting  
Lore Training Stoneworking Lore  
Skill Feat Specialty Crafting (stoneworking)  
Additional Languages Terran or one language of your choice from any regional language appropriate to your work locations  
Equipment A stonemason's toolkit (hammers, chisels, wedges, measuring tools), a set of protective gloves, a water flask, a sketchbook for designs, and a pouch containing 12 gp  
Roleplaying the Stone Mason  
As a Stone Mason, your perspective is shaped by your work with durable, permanent materials. You are patient, methodical, and detail-oriented, with an appreciation for craftsmanship and quality. You may seem steady and reliable to others, with a certain solidity that reflects your connection to stone. Your values emphasize durability, functionality, and the creation of things that will last. You may have a practical, grounded approach to problems, preferring solutions that are solid and dependable over those that are flashy or temporary.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Stone Mason:  
What type of stonework do you specialize in (architecture, sculpture, tools, etc.)? What draws you to this specialty?  
What is the most significant or challenging project you have worked on? What did you learn from the experience?  
How do you approach the design and creation of your work? Do you follow traditional patterns, innovate new techniques, or adapt to specific needs and contexts?  
What is your relationship with other craftsmen and builders? Do you collaborate, compete, or work independently?  
How do you feel about the permanence of your work? Do you see it as a legacy, a responsibility, or something else?  
Tribal Scout  
"And the sons of God saw the daughters of men that they were fair; and they took them wives of all which they chose." — Genesis 6:You are a scout for your tribe, responsible for ranging ahead of the main group to gather information, identify threats, and find resources. Your skills encompass wilderness survival, tracking, stealth, observation, and communication. You know how to move quietly through different terrains, read signs of recent activity (tracks, broken branches, campfire remains), identify potential dangers (predators, hostile groups, environmental hazards), and locate valuable resources (water, game, edible plants, shelter).  
Your role requires independence, self-reliance, and quick thinking. You often operate alone or in small teams, far from the support of your tribe. You must be able to make decisions quickly based on limited information, adapt to changing circumstances, and handle unexpected challenges without panicking. You need to be physically fit and mentally resilient, capable of enduring long periods of travel, exposure to the elements, and the psychological strain of isolation and uncertainty.  
Your knowledge of the land and its inhabitants is extensive. You understand the geography of your tribe's territory and the surrounding regions. You know the locations of landmarks, water sources, game trails, and potential hazards. You are familiar with the behaviors and territories of local wildlife. You may also have information about neighboring tribes, Nephilim settlements, or other groups that inhabit the area, including their numbers, movements, and intentions.  
Your relationship with your tribe is based on trust and mutual dependence. Your people rely on you to provide accurate information and early warnings of danger. In return, you depend on them for support, supplies, and a place to return to after your scouting missions. You may have a close bond with the tribal leadership, who value your insights and recommendations. You might also serve as a guide for hunting parties, trading expeditions, or relocation efforts.  
Your experiences as a scout have given you a unique perspective on the antediluvian world. You have seen places and things that most people never encounter. You have developed a keen awareness of your surroundings and a heightened sensitivity to subtle signs and patterns. You may have insights into the movements and activities of various groups that could be valuable to others. Your independence and self-reliance have shaped your character, making you resourceful, observant, and somewhat reserved.  
Ability Boosts Dexterity, free  
Skill Training Stealth  
Lore Training Regional Geography Lore  
Skill Feat Terrain Stalker  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from any regional language spoken by neighboring groups or tribes  
Equipment Scout's gear (light clothing, waterskin, rations, rope, flint and steel), a knife, a shortbow with arrows, a journal for recording observations, and a pouch containing 8 gp  
Roleplaying the Tribal Scout  
As a Tribal Scout, your worldview is shaped by your experiences of independence, observation, and connection to the land. You are alert, perceptive, and self-reliant, with a keen awareness of your surroundings and a tendency to notice details that others miss. You may seem quiet or reserved to others, preferring to observe and listen rather than speak. Your values emphasize vigilance, preparedness, and the importance of accurate information. You may have a practical, no-nonsense approach to problems, preferring direct observation and action over speculation or debate.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Tribal Scout:  
What specific territory does your tribe inhabit, and what are its characteristics and challenges?  
What is your relationship with the tribal leadership? How much influence do your reports and recommendations have on tribal decisions?  
How do you handle the isolation and uncertainty of scouting missions? Do you have specific practices or routines that help you cope?  
What is the most significant threat or discovery you have encountered while scouting? How did you respond?  
How do you balance your independence as a scout with your loyalty and responsibility to your tribe?  
Sacred Dancer  
"And his brother's name was Jubal: he was the father of all such as handle the harp and organ." — Genesis 4:You are a performer who uses dance as a form of worship, ritual, storytelling, or celebration. Your art is deeply connected to the spiritual and cultural life of your community. You may serve in a temple or sacred space, performing dances that honor the Most High, commemorate important events, or accompany rituals and ceremonies. You might be part of a traveling troupe that brings performances to different communities, sharing stories, traditions, and spiritual messages through movement. Or you might be a community dancer who participates in local celebrations, festivals, and gatherings.  
Your training has given you expertise in various aspects of dance—technique, choreography, expression, rhythm, and performance. You understand how to use your body to convey emotions, tell stories, and connect with audiences. You may specialize in specific styles or traditions of dance, such as ritual dances, narrative dances, celebratory dances, or meditative dances. You are also skilled in related arts such as music (singing, playing instruments), costume design, and stagecraft.  
Your role as a sacred dancer carries both artistic and spiritual significance. Your performances are not merely entertainment but expressions of faith, culture, and community values. You may use dance to teach moral lessons, preserve historical narratives, invoke divine presence, or create communal experiences of joy and unity. Your art requires discipline, dedication, and a deep understanding of the meanings and purposes behind the movements you perform.  
Your experiences as a dancer have shaped your perspective on the world. You understand the power of non-verbal communication and the ability of movement to express things that words cannot. You appreciate the beauty and expressiveness of the human body. You may have a spiritual or meditative connection to your art, seeing dance as a form of prayer, meditation, or communion with the divine. Your performances have likely brought you into contact with diverse communities and traditions, giving you insights into different ways of understanding and expressing spirituality.  
Ability Boosts Dexterity or Charisma, free  
Skill Training Performance  
Lore Training Ritual Dance Lore  
Skill Feat Fascinating Performance  
Additional Languages One language of your choice from any regional language appropriate to the communities you perform for  
Equipment Performance clothing and accessories (costumes, props, instruments), a journal for recording choreography, a water flask, and a pouch containing 10 gp  
Roleplaying the Sacred Dancer  
As a Sacred Dancer, your identity is shaped by your art and its spiritual significance. You are expressive, disciplined, and attuned to non-verbal communication. You may seem passionate or intense to others, with a deep emotional connection to your performances. Your values emphasize beauty, expression, and the power of art to convey meaning and connect people. You may have a holistic approach to life, seeing the physical, emotional, and spiritual aspects of experience as interconnected.  
Consider the following questions when roleplaying your Sacred Dancer:  
What specific tradition or style of sacred dance do you practice? What are its characteristics and purposes?  
What is the most meaningful or challenging performance you have given? What made it significant?  
How do you approach the creation and interpretation of your dances? Do you follow traditional forms, innovate new expressions, or adapt to specific contexts?  
What is your relationship with the communities you perform for? How do they respond to your art?  
How do you see the connection between your dance and your spiritual beliefs? Is dance a form of worship, meditation, teaching, or something else?  

Chapter 4: Classes  
Introduction  
In the antediluvian world, classes represent more than mere professions or combat specialties—they embody the fundamental roles that individuals assume in a society teetering on the brink of divine judgment. Unlike standard fantasy settings where classes might reflect medieval guilds or arcane traditions, the seven core classes of Nephilim Wars are shaped by the unique pressures of the pre-Flood era: the oppressive presence of Nephilim giants, the forbidden knowledge taught by fallen Watchers, the struggle for survival in a corrupted world, and the looming certainty of coming judgment.  
These classes differ from their Pathfinder 2e counterparts in significant ways. While they utilize the same mechanical chassis for balance and familiarity, each has been thoroughly reimagined to fit the bronze-age aesthetic and thematic concerns of the setting. The Warrior is not a knight in shining armor but a tribal fighter mastering bronze weapons and shield walls against titanic foes. The Magi does not study abstract arcane theory but wields the dangerous, forbidden arts taught by specific Watchers, risking spiritual corruption with every spell. The Priest channels divine power not through institutional religion but through direct connection to the Most High, preserving the righteous line in an age of increasing darkness.  
The seven classes presented in this chapter offer distinct approaches to surviving and thriving in the antediluvian world:  
Warrior — The tribal combat specialist, mastering bronze-age weaponry and defensive tactics to stand against giant oppression. Warriors form the backbone of human resistance, protecting their communities through martial discipline and tactical expertise.  
Gibbor — The mighty hero of giant bloodline, wielding oversized weapons with supernatural strength. Designed for characters with giant heritage, the Gibbor channels the power of their Nephilim ancestors while struggling with the corruption inherent in their bloodline.  
Hunter — The wilderness expert and ranged specialist, excelling at tracking, survival, and precision strikes from a distance. Hunters serve as scouts, pathfinders, and ambush specialists, using their knowledge of the land to overcome physically superior foes.  
Magi — The Watcher-taught sorcerer, wielding forbidden elemental magic at the cost of spiritual corruption. Magi represent the dangerous allure of forbidden knowledge, offering immense power tempered by the ever-present risk of gaining Corruption Points.  
Priest — The divine miracle-worker, channeling the power of the Creator to heal, protect, and turn back the forces of corruption. Priests preserve the righteous line and serve as spiritual guides in an increasingly dark world.  
Artisan — The master of Tubal-Cain's legacy, specializing in bronze-working, trap-making, and mechanical innovation. Artisans represent human ingenuity and craftsmanship, creating tools and weapons that allow mortals to challenge titanic foes.  
Scribe — The keeper of antediluvian knowledge, wielding lore and tactical analysis as weapons. Scribes preserve ancient wisdom, decipher forbidden texts, and provide strategic guidance to their companions.  
When choosing a class, consider not only your preferred playstyle but also your character's place in the world. Are you a frontline defender standing against Nephilim tyranny? A cunning survivor using stealth and precision to overcome superior numbers? A wielder of forbidden power walking the razor's edge between salvation and damnation? Your class choice defines your capabilities, your role in the party, and your relationship to the central themes of the setting. Choose wisely, for in the age before the Flood, every decision carries eternal weight.  
Warrior  
The Warrior stands as the bulwark against chaos, the tribal fighter who has mastered the arts of bronze-age combat to protect their people from the threats that surround them. In a world dominated by Nephilim giants and corrupted beings, Warriors represent humanity's resilience and determination to survive through martial discipline and tactical expertise. They are not knights in the medieval sense, nor are they mercenaries fighting for coin—they are defenders of their communities, whether tribal elders training the next generation, veteran fighters who have faced giants and lived to tell the tale, or young champions rising to meet the challenges of their age.  
Warriors come from all walks of life but share a common dedication to martial excellence. Some are Sethite protectors, sworn to defend the righteous line with weapons blessed through prayer and sacrifice. Others are Cainite city guards, maintaining order in the bustling metropolises built under giant patronage. Wanderer scouts become Warriors through necessity, learning to fight in the harsh wilderness against predators and raiders. Even some Sorcerer Clans produce Warriors, though these individuals typically focus on the martial teachings of Azazel rather than the more corrupting magical arts.  
In society, Warriors hold positions of respect and authority. They train the youth in weapons handling, lead hunting parties and patrols, and form the core of any resistance against Nephilim oppression. Their expertise extends beyond mere combat—they understand tactics, fortification, and the psychology of warfare. They know when to stand and fight, when to retreat and regroup, and when to use terrain and preparation to overcome superior numbers or strength. This wisdom makes them valuable advisors to tribal leaders and community elders, even when they do not hold formal positions of power.  
The Warrior's approach to combat reflects the bronze-age setting. They master weapons forged by Tubal-Cain's descendants: bronze swords that can pierce scaled hide, spears that keep giants at bay, axes that can sever limbs, and shields that can turn aside titanic blows. They fight in formation when possible, using shield walls and coordinated attacks to maximize their effectiveness against larger foes. They understand the importance of preparation, studying their enemies' weaknesses and exploiting environmental advantages. Most importantly, they know that direct confrontation with Nephilim is often suicide—they rely on ambush, harassment, and strategic withdrawal rather than seeking glorious but fatal duels with titans.  
Class Statistics  
Key Ability: Strength or Dexterity  
Hit Points: 10 plus your Constitution modifier  
Initial Proficiencies:  
Perception: Trained  
Saving Throws: Fortitude Expert, Reflex Trained, Will Trained  
Skills: Trained in Athletics and four other skills of your choice  
Attacks: Trained in simple weapons, martial weapons, and unarmed attacks  
Defenses: Trained in all armor types and shields  
Class DC: Trained in Fortitude DC and Reflex DC  
Class Features  
Levels 1-5: At 1st level, Warriors gain Fighting Style, choosing between Defensive Stance (bonus to AC when wielding a shield), Two-Weapon Fighting (reduced penalties when wielding two weapons), or Power Attack (bonus damage at the cost of accuracy). At 2nd level, they gain Tactical Awareness, adding their Wisdom modifier to Initiative rolls. At 3rd level, Weapon Specialization grants \+2 damage with all weapons they are proficient with. At 4th level, Combat Flexibility allows them to change their Fighting Style once per day during a short rest. At 5th level, Extra Attack enables two attacks when using the Attack action.  
Levels 6-10: At 6th level, Warriors gain Defensive Training, adding their proficiency bonus to AC when unarmored or wearing light armor. At 7th level, Tribal War Cry (once per day) grants all allies within 30 feet a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws for 1 minute. At 8th level, Improved Critical expands their weapon's critical range by 1\. At 9th level, Combat Mastery allows them to make an additional attack when using the Attack action, though this third attack takes a \-5 penalty. At 10th level, Resilient Defense grants resistance 5 to all damage when wielding a shield.  
Levels 11-15: At 11th level, Warriors gain Legendary Resistance, allowing them to reroll a failed saving throw once per day. At 12th level, Master of Arms lets them treat any martial weapon as if they had the Weapon Specialization feat for it. At 13th level, Unbreakable grants immunity to the frightened and fatigued conditions. At 14th level, Battlefield Control allows them to use the Aid action as a single action instead of two actions. At 15th level, Titan's Bane adds \+1d6 damage against creatures larger than Medium.  
Levels 16-20: At 16th level, Warriors gain Indomitable Will, adding their proficiency bonus to Will saving throws. At 17th level, War Leader allows them to grant an ally within 30 feet an additional action on their turn once per day. At 18th level, Perfect Defense grants resistance 10 to all damage when wielding a shield. At 19th level, Martial Mastery allows them to make four attacks when using the Attack action, with the fourth attack taking a \-10 penalty. At 20th level, Legendary Warrior grants a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls and damage rolls, and they can use their Tribal War Cry ability twice per day.

Class Feats  
Levels 1-5:  
Shield Master \[Feat 1\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, proficiency with shields  
You have mastered the art of fighting with a shield. When you Raise a Shield, you gain the shield's AC bonus until the start of your next turn instead of until the beginning of your next turn. Additionally, you can use the Shield Block reaction even if you didn't Raise a Shield on your previous turn, though you still need to be wielding a shield.  
Weapon Focus \[Feat 1\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class  
Choose one weapon group (axes, swords, spears, etc.). You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to attack rolls with weapons from that group. You can take this feat multiple times, each time choosing a different weapon group.  
Defensive Stance \[Feat 2\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Fighting Style feature  
When you use the Take Cover action while wielding a shield, you gain a \+4 circumstance bonus to AC instead of \+2. Additionally, enemies take a \-2 penalty to attack rolls against you while you are in Defensive Stance.  
Power Strike \[Feat 4\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Strength 14+  
When you make a melee Strike, you can take a \-2 penalty to the attack roll to deal an additional 1d6 damage. If you have the Power Attack fighting style, this damage increases to 2d6.  
Levels 6-10:  
Improved Shield Master \[Feat 6\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Shield Master feat  
Your mastery of shield combat has reached new heights. When you use the Shield Bash action, you deal an additional 1d6 bludgeoning damage. Additionally, you can use Shield Bash as a reaction when an enemy misses you with a melee attack, provided you are wielding a shield.  
Tactical Formation \[Feat 8\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Tribal War Cry feature  
When you use your Tribal War Cry ability, you can choose to grant the bonus only to allies who are adjacent to you and each other, forming a defensive formation. These allies gain a \+2 status bonus to AC instead of the normal \+1 bonus to attack rolls and saving throws.  
Weapon Specialization (Advanced) \[Feat 8\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Weapon Focus feat  
The circumstance bonus from your Weapon Focus feat increases to \+2. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to damage rolls with weapons from your chosen weapon group.  
Combat Reflexes \[Feat 10\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Dexterity 14+  
You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Reflex saving throws. Additionally, when an enemy within your reach makes a move action, you can make a single melee Strike against them as a reaction.  
Levels 11-15:  
Master Shield Fighter \[Feat 12\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Improved Shield Master feat  
Your shield has become an extension of your arm. When you Raise a Shield, you gain the shield's AC bonus until the beginning of your next turn, and you can use the Shield Block reaction twice per round instead of once. Additionally, your shield's Hardness increases by 2\.  
Legendary Weapon Focus \[Feat 14\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Weapon Specialization (Advanced) feat  
The circumstance bonus from your Weapon Focus feat increases to \+3. Additionally, you ignore the first 5 points of resistance that a target has against your weapon attacks. If you have the Power Strike feat, the additional damage increases to 3d6.  
Unbreakable Formation \[Feat 14\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Tactical Formation feat  
When you use your Tribal War Cry ability to create a defensive formation, allies in the formation gain resistance 5 to all damage in addition to the \+2 status bonus to AC. This resistance increases to 10 if you are also in the formation.  
Titan Slayer \[Feat 16\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Titan's Bane feature  
Your expertise in fighting giants has reached its peak. The additional damage from your Titan's Bane feature increases to 2d6. Additionally, when you score a critical hit against a creature larger than Medium, you can attempt to Trip or Shove the creature as a free action.  
Levels 16-20:  
Perfect Shield Mastery \[Feat 18\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Master Shield Fighter feat  
Your shield provides near-perfect protection. When you Raise a Shield, you gain the shield's AC bonus until the beginning of your next turn, and you can use the Shield Block reaction three times per round instead of twice. Additionally, your shield's Hardness increases by 4 (for a total increase of 6 from normal).  
Weapon Legend \[Feat 18\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Legendary Weapon Focus feat  
The circumstance bonus from your Weapon Focus feat increases to \+4. Additionally, you ignore the first 10 points of resistance that a target has against your weapon attacks. Your weapon attacks are treated as magical for the purpose of overcoming resistances. If you have the Power Strike feat, the additional damage increases to 4d6.  
Indomitable Formation \[Feat 20\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Unbreakable Formation feat  
Your defensive formations are now virtually impregnable. When you use your Tribal War Cry ability to create a defensive formation, allies in the formation gain resistance 10 to all damage and a \+4 status bonus to AC. Additionally, enemies take a \-4 penalty to attack rolls against allies in the formation. You can use this ability twice per day.  
Legendary Titan Slayer \[Feat 20\]  
Warrior  
Prerequisites Warrior class, Titan Slayer feat  
Your mastery over giant-slaying techniques is complete. The additional damage from your Titan's Bane feature increases to 3d6. Additionally, when you hit a creature larger than Medium with a melee weapon attack, you can spend 1 RP to deal an additional 4d6 damage of the weapon's type. You can use this ability once per round.  
Example Build  
Kedar, Sethite Tribal Defender — A Sethite Warrior who has dedicated his life to protecting his community from Nephilim raiders. With high Strength and Constitution, he wields a bronze greatsword and heavy shield, using the Defensive Stance fighting style to protect his allies. His Tribal War Cry inspires his fellow tribesmen, while his Titan's Bane feature ensures he can contribute meaningful damage against the giants that threaten his people. Kedar begins with 2 RP from his Sethite heritage, which he spends to reroll critical failures and power his divine connection. His goal is to train the next generation of defenders before the coming judgment arrives.  
Gibbor  
The Gibbor stands as a living bridge between humanity and the titanic Nephilim, a mighty hero whose bloodline grants them supernatural strength and martial prowess. Unlike the pure-blooded giants who view humans with contempt, the Gibbor understands both worlds—the resilience and cunning of humanity, and the raw power and martial traditions of their giant ancestors. They are the "Mighty Men of Old" mentioned in Genesis 6:4, warriors of renown who carved out their own place in a world dominated by their larger kin.  
Gibborim typically arise from unions between Nephilim or other giant lineages and human partners, though some are born to Gibborim parents, creating dynasties of mighty warriors. Their appearance varies depending on the strength of their giant blood—some are nearly indistinguishable from exceptionally large humans, while others bear clear giant characteristics like elongated limbs, unusual eye colors, or celestial markings on their skin. Regardless of their specific appearance, all Gibborim possess enhanced physical capabilities that set them apart from pure humans.  
In society, Gibborim occupy a unique and often precarious position. They are valued as elite warriors, bodyguards, and military leaders, capable of matching the strength of true giants while maintaining the tactical intelligence and adaptability of humans. Many serve as captains in Nephilim armies, commanders of Anakim household guards, or champions in Rephaim warbands. Others carve out independent paths as mercenaries, selling their services to the highest bidder regardless of bloodline or allegiance. Some Gibborim use their unique position to protect human communities from giant oppression, acting as intermediaries or enforcers who can stand against titanic foes.  
The Gibbor's combat style reflects their hybrid nature. They master weapons that are too heavy for ordinary humans but too small for true giants—bronze greatswords that would require two hands for a human can be wielded one-handed by a Gibbor. They excel at close-quarters combat, using their enhanced strength to deliver devastating blows and their tactical intelligence to exploit weaknesses in larger opponents. Their signature ability, Giant's Grip, allows them to wield two-handed weapons in one hand, freeing their other hand for shields, grappling, or secondary attacks. This versatility makes them formidable opponents in any combat situation.  
Unlike the rage-fueled Barbarian archetype they're based on, the Gibbor's power comes from controlled fury and ancestral memory rather than mindless berserker rage. They channel the strength of their giant ancestors through focused discipline, calling upon their heritage in moments of greatest need. This approach reflects the Gibbor's struggle to balance their dual nature—they must harness the power of their giant blood without succumbing to the corruption and brutality that often accompanies it.  
Class Statistics  
Key Ability: Strength  
Hit Points: 12 plus your Constitution modifier  
Initial Proficiencies:  
Perception: Trained  
Saving Throws: Fortitude Expert, Reflex Trained, Will Trained  
Skills: Trained in Athletics and three other skills of your choice  
Attacks: Trained in simple weapons, martial weapons, and unarmed attacks  
Defenses: Trained in light armor, medium armor, and shields  
Class DC: Trained in Fortitude DC  
Class Features  
Levels 1-5: At 1st level, Gibborim gain Giant's Grip, allowing them to wield two-handed weapons in one hand (though they still take a \-2 item penalty to attack rolls unless they have the appropriate feat). They also gain Ancestral Strength, adding their Strength modifier to damage rolls twice instead of once when making melee attacks. At 2nd level, Mighty Blow allows them to deal an additional 1d6 damage of the weapon's type once per round. At 3rd level, Heroic Resilience grants a \+1 status bonus to Fortitude saving throws. At 4th level, Powerful Build allows them to count as one size larger when determining carrying capacity and the amount they can push, drag, or lift. At 5th level, Extra Attack enables two attacks when using the Attack action.  
Levels 6-10: At 6th level, Gibborim gain Tireless Rage, allowing them to enter a state of ancestral fury as a single action. While in this state (which lasts for 1 minute), they gain a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls and resistance 5 to all damage, but they take a \-1 penalty to AC. They can use this ability once per day. At 7th level, Improved Giant's Grip removes the \-2 item penalty when wielding two-handed weapons in one hand. At 8th level, Devastating Blow increases the additional damage from Mighty Blow to 2d6. At 9th level, Combat Mastery allows them to make an additional attack when using the Attack action, though this third attack takes a \-5 penalty. At 10th level, Legendary Reputation grants a \+1 status bonus to Intimidation checks and Diplomacy checks when dealing with creatures that have the giant trait.  
Levels 11-15: At 11th level, Gibborim gain Ancestral Fury, allowing them to use their Tireless Rage ability twice per day. While in this state, the bonuses increase to \+3 status bonus to damage rolls and resistance 10 to all damage. At 12th level, Titanic Blow allows them to deal an additional 3d6 damage of the weapon's type once per round. At 13th level, Unbreakable grants immunity to the clumsy and enfeebled conditions. At 14th level, Giant's Reach increases their reach by 5 feet when making melee attacks. At 15th level, Heroic Endurance allows them to heal 1d8+their level Hit Points as a single action once per day.  
Levels 16-20: At 16th level, Gibborim gain Legendary Rage, allowing them to use their Tireless Rage ability three times per day. While in this state, the bonuses increase to \+4 status bonus to damage rolls and resistance 15 to all damage, and the AC penalty is removed. At 17th level, Colossal Blow allows them to deal an additional 4d6 damage of the weapon's type once per round. At 18th level, Titan's Grip allows them to wield weapons meant for Large creatures without penalty. At 19th level, Martial Mastery allows them to make four attacks when using the Attack action, with the fourth attack taking a \-10 penalty. At 20th level, Legendary Gibbor grants a \+4 status bonus to all Strength-based skill checks and damage rolls, and they can use their Tireless Rage ability at will (though they still need to rest for 10 minutes between uses).

Class Feats  
Levels 1-5:  
Ancestral Weapon Training \[Feat 1\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class  
You gain proficiency with one advanced weapon type (such as bronze greatswords, giant mauls, or bronze glaives). Additionally, you can wield this weapon type with only one hand, though you still take a \-2 item penalty to attack rolls unless you have the Improved Giant's Grip feature.  
Heroic Feat \[Feat 1\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class  
Once per day, when you fail an attack roll, skill check, or saving throw, you can reroll it. You must use the result of the second roll, even if it is worse. This represents your heroic heritage refusing to accept failure.  
Giant's Resilience \[Feat 2\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class  
You gain resistance 2 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. This represents your enhanced durability from your giant heritage. This resistance increases to 5 at 10th level and 10 at 18th level.  
Power Attack \[Feat 4\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Strength 14+  
When you make a melee Strike, you can take a \-2 penalty to the attack roll to deal an additional 1d6 damage. If you have the Mighty Blow feature, you can combine this with Mighty Blow, though the penalties stack.  
Levels 6-10:  
Ancestral Memory \[Feat 6\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Heroic Feat feat  
Once per day, when you or an ally within 30 feet is reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can restore them to 1 Hit Point and grant them temporary Hit Points equal to your level. Additionally, you can immediately use your Heroic Feat ability.  
Improved Power Attack \[Feat 8\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Power Attack feat  
The additional damage from your Power Attack feat increases to 2d6. Additionally, you can take a \-4 penalty to the attack roll to deal an additional 3d6 damage instead of the normal \-2 penalty for 1d6 damage.  
Titanic Resilience \[Feat 8\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Giant's Resilience feat  
The resistance from your Giant's Resilience feat increases to 5 against bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, you gain resistance 2 to piercing and slashing damage from non-magical attacks.  
War Leader \[Feat 10\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Legendary Reputation feature  
Once per day, as a single action, you can inspire your allies. For 1 minute, you and all allies within 30 feet gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and a \+1 status bonus to AC. Additionally, you can use the Aid action as a single action instead of a two-action activity.  
Levels 11-15:  
Ancestral Awakening \[Feat 12\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Ancestral Memory feat  
Once per day, as a single action, you can fully awaken the power of your giant ancestors. For 1 minute, you gain a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws, and you gain resistance 10 to all damage except force. Additionally, you can use any of your Gibbor class feats without expending their daily uses.  
Devastating Critical \[Feat 14\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Titanic Blow feature  
When you score a critical hit with a melee weapon, you can deal an additional 3d6 damage of the weapon's type. Additionally, when you use Titanic Blow on a critical hit, the additional damage is doubled (as per normal critical hit rules).  
Colossal Resilience \[Feat 14\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Titanic Resilience feat  
You gain resistance 10 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, you are immune to the clumsy and enfeebled conditions. This represents your mastery over your giant heritage and your inability to be easily hindered.  
Master Tactician \[Feat 16\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, War Leader feat  
Once per day, as a two-action activity, you can become a master of battlefield tactics. For 10 minutes, you and all allies within 60 feet gain a \+3 status bonus to attack rolls, a \+2 status bonus to AC, and a \+2 status bonus to saving throws. Additionally, when an ally within 60 feet rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll, they can immediately reroll it.  
Levels 16-20:  
Ancestral Ascension \[Feat 18\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Ancestral Awakening feat  
Once per week, as a two-action activity, you can temporarily ascend to the full power of your giant ancestors. For 10 minutes, you gain a \+6 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws, and you gain resistance 20 to all damage except force. Additionally, you can use any of your Gibbor class feats without expending their daily uses, and you can cast one spell of up to 7th level from your ancestral tradition (Nephilim warfare magic, Anakim enchantments, or Rephaim necromancy) without needing to prepare it or have it in your repertoire.  
Legendary Blow \[Feat 18\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Devastating Critical feat  
When you score a critical hit with a melee weapon, you can deal an additional 5d6 damage of the weapon's type. Additionally, when you use Colossal Blow on a critical hit, the additional damage is tripled instead of doubled. Finally, creatures hit by your critical hits must attempt a DC 25 Fortitude save or be stunned until the end of their next turn.  
Indestructible \[Feat 20\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Colossal Resilience feat  
You gain resistance 15 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from all sources (including magical attacks). Additionally, you are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious. When you would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level. You can use this ability once per day.  
Legendary Commander \[Feat 20\]  
Gibbor  
Prerequisites Gibbor class, Master Tactician feat  
Once per week, as a three-action activity, you achieve legendary status as a battlefield commander. For 1 hour, you and all allies within 120 feet gain a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls, a \+3 status bonus to AC, and a \+3 status bonus to saving throws. Additionally, when an ally within 120 feet rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll, they can immediately reroll it, and when an ally within 120 feet scores a critical hit, they deal an additional 2d6 damage of the weapon's type. You can use the Master Tactician ability twice per day instead of once per day.  
Example Build  
Rahab, Anakim-Blooded Champion — A Gibbor with strong Anakim heritage, Rahab stands nearly eight feet tall with the regal bearing of her giant ancestors. She wields a massive bronze greatsword one-handed, using her free hand to carry a heavy shield. Her Tireless Rage ability allows her to channel her ancestral fury in battle, while her Legendary Reputation helps her command respect from both human allies and giant foes. Rahab walks a difficult path, torn between her loyalty to her human companions and the pull of her giant bloodline. She begins with 2 CP from her Anakim heritage but works to maintain her honor and avoid further corruption. Her goal is to prove that Gibborim can be heroes rather than tyrants, using their strength to protect the weak rather than oppress them.

continue

Hunter  
The Hunter stands as the wilderness expert and ranged specialist of the antediluvian world, a master of tracking, survival, and precision strikes from a distance. In a world where direct confrontation with Nephilim giants often means certain death, Hunters represent humanity's adaptability and cunning—using stealth, terrain, and patience to overcome physically superior foes. They are the scouts who range ahead of tribal migrations, the pathfinders who guide caravans through treacherous wilderness, and the ambush specialists who strike at giant warbands from the shadows before disappearing into the landscape.  
Hunters come from diverse backgrounds but share a deep connection to the natural world and its rhythms. Many are Wanderers who learned to survive in the harsh wilderness after rejecting both the righteous path of Seth and the city-building ways of Cain. Others are tribal scouts from Sethite communities, trained to protect their people from threats that lurk beyond the settlement walls. Some Hunters are former thralls who escaped Nephilim servitude and now use their intimate knowledge of giant behavior to hunt their former masters. A few are even Gibborim or Elioud who have chosen to use their enhanced abilities not for direct combat but for the more subtle arts of tracking and ranged warfare.  
In society, Hunters occupy a respected but often solitary position. They are valued for their skills in finding game, identifying safe paths, and warning of approaching dangers, but their independent nature and comfort with solitude can make them seem distant or mysterious to more community-oriented individuals. Hunters often serve as intermediaries between settled communities and the wilderness, bringing back not only game and resources but also knowledge of what lies beyond the familiar territories. They may work as guides for merchants traveling between Cainite cities, as scouts for Sethite enclaves seeking new homes, or as trackers hired to find lost travelers or pursue raiders.  
The Hunter's approach to combat reflects their wilderness expertise and preference for ranged engagement. They master weapons that allow them to strike from a distance—composite longbows that can penetrate scaled hide, slings that can deliver stones with deadly accuracy, and javelins that can be thrown with lethal force. They understand the importance of positioning, using terrain features for cover and concealment while denying their enemies the same advantages. They excel at ambush tactics, striking quickly and then retreating before their opponents can bring their superior strength to bear. Most importantly, they know when to fight and when to flee—a pragmatic approach that has allowed many Hunters to survive encounters that would have killed more reckless warriors.  
Class Statistics  
Key Ability: Dexterity  
Hit Points: 8 plus your Constitution modifier  
Initial Proficiencies:  
Perception: Trained  
Saving Throws: Fortitude Trained, Reflex Expert, Will Trained  
Skills: Trained in Survival and four other skills of your choice  
Attacks: Trained in simple weapons, martial weapons, and unarmed attacks  
Defenses: Trained in light armor and medium armor  
Class DC: Trained in Reflex DC  
Class Features  
Levels 1-5: At 1st level, Hunters gain Favored Terrain, choosing one type of terrain (forest, desert, mountains, plains, or underground). They gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Survival checks and Stealth checks in their favored terrain. At 2nd level, Hunter's Mark allows them to mark a creature within 60 feet as a single action. For 1 minute, they gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls against the marked creature and can deal an additional 1d6 damage of the weapon's type with the first Strike against the marked creature each round. At 3rd level, Natural Explorer grants them a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about wilderness environments, plants, animals, and weather patterns. At 4th level, Precise Shot allows them to ignore the concealed condition when making ranged attacks. At 5th level, Extra Attack enables two attacks when using the Attack action.  
Levels 6-10: At 6th level, Hunters gain Improved Favored Terrain, allowing them to choose a second favored terrain type. The circumstance bonus in favored terrains increases to \+2. At 7th level, Hunter's Quarry enhances their Hunter's Mark ability—when they reduce a marked creature to 0 Hit Points, they can immediately mark a new creature within 60 feet as a free action. At 8th level, Terrain Mastery allows them to move through difficult terrain in their favored terrains without penalty. At 9th level, Combat Mastery allows them to make an additional attack when using the Attack action, though this third attack takes a \-5 penalty. At 10th level, Deadly Mark increases the additional damage from Hunter's Mark to 2d6.  
Levels 11-15: At 11th level, Hunters gain Master Hunter, allowing them to use their Hunter's Mark ability twice per day instead of once per day. At 12th level, Perfect Shot allows them to ignore the hidden condition when making ranged attacks, though the target still has the standard bonuses to AC and saving throws against hidden creatures. At 13th level, Unerring Aim grants them a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls with ranged weapons. At 14th level, Terrain Expert allows them to use the Hide and Sneak actions as a single action instead of two actions while in their favored terrains. At 15th level, Hunter's Precision allows them to reroll one damage die when dealing damage to a marked creature.  
Levels 16-20: At 16th level, Hunters gain Legendary Hunter, allowing them to use their Hunter's Mark ability three times per day instead of once per day. At 17th level, Supreme Mark increases the additional damage from Hunter's Mark to 3d6, and the status bonus to attack rolls increases to \+2. At 18th level, Master of the Wild grants them a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about wilderness environments and a \+2 circumstance bonus to Survival checks. At 19th level, Martial Mastery allows them to make four attacks when using the Attack action, with the fourth attack taking a \-10 penalty. At 20th level, Legendary Hunter grants a \+2 status bonus to all attack rolls with ranged weapons, and they can use their Hunter's Mark ability at will (though they can only have one marked creature at a time).

Class Feats  
Levels 1-5:  
Beast Companion \[Feat 1\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class  
You gain a loyal animal companion that fights alongside you. Choose a creature of 1st level or lower with the animal trait. Your companion gains the minion trait and can use 2 actions per turn (though it can't use reactions). It gains a number of Hit Points equal to your level plus its Constitution modifier. You can command your companion as a single action, which allows it to Stride or Strike. Your companion levels up with you, gaining additional abilities at 5th, 10th, 15th, and 20th levels.  
Quick Draw \[Feat 1\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Dexterity 14+  
You can draw a weapon and make a Strike with it as a single action. This allows you to make ranged attacks more quickly, especially when switching between different weapons or when your current weapon is out of ammunition.  
Terrain Stealth \[Feat 1\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Favored Terrain feature  
You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks in your favored terrain. Additionally, you can use the Hide action even when you are only partially concealed by natural features in your favored terrain (such as foliage, rocks, or shadows).  
Manyshot \[Feat 4\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Precise Shot feature  
When you make a ranged Strike with a bow or crossbow, you can fire multiple projectiles at once. Make one attack roll and compare it to the AC of up to two adjacent creatures. If the attack hits, both creatures take damage. If the attack misses, both creatures avoid the attack. This ability can only be used once per round.  
Levels 6-10:  
Improved Beast Companion \[Feat 6\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Beast Companion feat  
Your animal companion grows stronger and more capable. It gains the advanced template (increasing its level by 2), and it can now use 3 actions per turn. Additionally, you can command your companion to use any action it has available, not just Stride or Strike. Your companion also gains resistance 5 to all damage.  
Rapid Shot \[Feat 6\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Manyshot feat  
When you use the Manyshot feat, you can target up to three adjacent creatures instead of two. Additionally, you can use Manyshot twice per day instead of once per day.  
Terrain Advantage \[Feat 8\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Terrain Mastery feature  
While in your favored terrain, you gain a \+2 status bonus to AC against attacks from creatures that are not in your favored terrain. Additionally, you can use the Cover action without needing physical cover, provided you are in your favored terrain with natural features that provide tactical advantages.  
Hunter's Trap \[Feat 10\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Survival trained  
You can create simple traps using natural materials found in your favored terrain. As a 10-minute activity, you can create a trap that affects a 10-foot square area. Creatures entering the trapped area must attempt a Reflex save (DC \= 10 \+ your level \+ your Dexterity modifier) or take 2d6 damage of a type appropriate to the trap (piercing from sharpened stakes, bludgeoning from falling rocks, etc.). You can have up to three active traps at a time.  
Levels 11-15:  
Master Beast Companion \[Feat 12\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Improved Beast Companion feat  
Your animal companion becomes a formidable ally. It gains the advanced template again (increasing its level by 2 more, for a total of \+4), and it can now use 4 actions per turn. Additionally, your companion gains a \+2 status bonus to all attack rolls and saving throws, and its resistance to all damage increases to 10\. You can also command your companion to use reactions.  
Deadly Manyshot \[Feat 14\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Rapid Shot feat  
When you use the Manyshot feat, the damage dealt to each target increases by 1d6. Additionally, you can target up to four adjacent creatures instead of three. Finally, if you score a critical hit with Manyshot, all targets affected by the attack take double damage.  
Terrain Master \[Feat 14\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Terrain Advantage feat  
While in your favored terrain, you gain a \+4 status bonus to AC against attacks from creatures that are not in your favored terrain. Additionally, you can use the Cover action as a free action, and you gain a \+2 status bonus to Stealth checks in your favored terrain. You can also create difficult terrain in a 20-foot burst centered on a point you can see within 60 feet as a single action. This difficult terrain lasts for 1 minute.  
Hunter's Ambush \[Feat 16\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Hunter's Trap feat  
When you make a Strike against a flat-footed creature, you deal an additional 2d6 precision damage. Additionally, when you successfully Sneak, you can make a ranged Strike against the flat-footed creature as part of the same action. Finally, creatures that start their turn in your traps take damage at the start of their turn, not just when they enter the area.  
Levels 16-20:  
Legendary Beast Companion \[Feat 18\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Master Beast Companion feat  
Your animal companion reaches its peak potential. It gains the advanced template again (increasing its level by 2 more, for a total of \+6), and it can now use 5 actions per turn. Additionally, your companion gains a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls and saving throws, and its resistance to all damage increases to 15\. Your companion also gains the ability to use your Hunter's Mark ability once per day, marking a creature it attacks.  
Supreme Manyshot \[Feat 18\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Deadly Manyshot feat  
When you use the Manyshot feat, the damage dealt to each target increases by 2d6 (for a total increase of 3d6). Additionally, you can target up to five adjacent creatures instead of four. Finally, if you score a critical hit with Manyshot, all targets affected by the attack are stunned until the end of their next turn.  
Terrain Lord \[Feat 20\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Terrain Master feat  
You become a master of your favored terrain. While in your favored terrain, you gain a \+6 status bonus to AC against attacks from creatures that are not in your favored terrain. Additionally, you can use the Cover action as a free action, and you gain a \+4 status bonus to Stealth checks in your favored terrain. You can also create difficult terrain in a 40-foot burst centered on a point you can see within 120 feet as a single action. This difficult terrain lasts for 10 minutes. Finally, you can change your favored terrain types once per day during a short rest.  
Hunter's Vengeance \[Feat 20\]  
Hunter  
Prerequisites Hunter class, Hunter's Ambush feat  
Once per day, when you reduce a marked creature to 0 Hit Points, you can immediately make a ranged Strike against another creature within 60 feet as a free action. This Strike deals an additional 4d6 precision damage. Additionally, when you use your Hunter's Mark ability, you can mark up to three creatures simultaneously, though you can only deal the additional damage to one marked creature per round.  
Example Build  
Tamar, Wanderer Scout — A Wanderer Hunter who has spent her life roaming the wilderness between Nephilim territories. With high Dexterity and Wisdom, she wields a composite longbow with deadly accuracy, using her Favored Terrain (forest) to move unseen and strike from concealment. Her Hunter's Mark ability allows her to focus fire on giant leaders, while her Beast Companion (a loyal wolf named Asher) distracts enemies and provides early warnings of approaching threats. Tamar's survival skills have kept her alive in the harsh wilderness, and her knowledge of giant behavior makes her invaluable to any group traveling through dangerous territories. Her goal is to map safe routes for human refugees fleeing Nephilim oppression, creating a network of hidden paths and safe houses that can help others escape the coming judgment.  
Magi  
The Magi stands as the wielder of forbidden knowledge in the antediluvian world, a practitioner of the dangerous arts taught by the fallen Watchers. Unlike the divine miracles of the Priest or the natural abilities of other classes, the Magi's power comes from stolen celestial secrets—knowledge that was never meant for mortal minds. This makes the Magi both feared and respected, for their abilities can accomplish feats that seem miraculous to ordinary humans, but every use of this power carries the risk of spiritual corruption and divine judgment.  
Magi trace their lineage back to the teachings of specific Watchers, each tradition offering different types of power. Followers of Azazel master the arts of warfare and metallurgy, creating weapons and armor of supernatural quality and wielding fire and force in combat. Students of Semyaza learn enchantments and root-cutting, manipulating minds and creating potions with magical properties. Disciples of Baraqel study astrology and divination, reading the stars for prophecy and manipulating fate. Each tradition offers unique abilities, but all share the common thread of forbidden knowledge and the corruption that accompanies it.  
In society, Magi occupy an ambiguous and often dangerous position. Sethite communities view them with suspicion or outright hostility, seeing their powers as evidence of corruption and rebellion against the Creator. Cainite cities may welcome them for their useful abilities but keep them under close watch, aware of the dangers they represent. Nephilim warlords sometimes employ Magi as advisors or weapons, valuing their power while remaining wary of their independence. Wanderer tribes may seek out Magi for their healing abilities or divinatory powers, though they too recognize the spiritual cost of such knowledge.  
The Magi's approach to magic reflects their bronze-age setting and forbidden origins. They do not study abstract arcane theory in ivory towers but learn practical applications of Watcher-taught arts—how to enchant a bronze sword to strike with supernatural force, how to create poisons that can fell a giant, how to read the stars for guidance in battle. Their spellcasting often involves physical components—herbs and roots for Semyaza's followers, star charts and celestial observations for Baraqel's students, blood and metal for Azazel's disciples. This tangible connection to their power makes their magic feel immediate and dangerous, not abstract or academic.  
The central tension of the Magi class is the Corruption Risk mechanic. Every time a Magi casts a spell, especially powerful ones, they risk gaining Corruption Points (CP). This represents the spiritual degradation that accompanies the use of forbidden knowledge. While the power is undeniably useful—able to turn the tide of battle, heal grievous wounds, or reveal hidden truths—the cost is eternal. Magi must constantly weigh the immediate benefits of their magic against the long-term consequences for their souls. This creates meaningful roleplaying opportunities and strategic decisions, as Magi players must decide when to use their power and when to rely on more mundane means.  
Class Statistics  
Key Ability: Intelligence  
Hit Points: 6 plus your Constitution modifier  
Initial Proficiencies:  
Perception: Trained  
Saving Throws: Fortitude Trained, Reflex Trained, Will Expert  
Skills: Trained in Occultism and four other skills of your choice  
Attacks: Trained in simple weapons and unarmed attacks  
Defenses: Trained in unarmored defense  
Class DC: Trained in Spell DC  
Class Features  
Levels 1-5: At 1st level, Magi gain Forbidden Lore, choosing one Watcher tradition (Azazel's Warfare, Semyaza's Enchantments, Baraqel's Divination, Kokabiel's Illusions, Penemue's Knowledge, or Kasdeja's Necromancy). They gain access to a spell list associated with their chosen tradition and can cast spells from that list using spell slots. They start with two 1st-level spell slots and a number of cantrips equal to their Intelligence modifier. At 2nd level, Elemental Affinity allows them to choose one damage type (fire, cold, electricity, or force) associated with their tradition. They gain a \+1 status bonus to spell attack rolls and spell DCs for spells that deal that damage type. At 3rd level, Corruption Risk introduces the mechanic where casting spells of 2nd level or higher requires a flat check (DC 11 \+ spell level). On a failure, they gain 1 Corruption Point. At 4th level, Watcher's Gift allows them to reroll one damage die when casting a spell from their tradition. At 5th level, they gain a 2nd-level spell slot and can cast 2nd-level spells from their tradition.  
Levels 6-10: At 6th level, Magi gain Advanced Forbidden Lore, allowing them to learn one additional spell from their tradition for each spell level they can cast. At 7th level, Improved Elemental Affinity increases their status bonus to spell attack rolls and spell DCs for their chosen damage type to \+2. At 8th level, Corruption Resistance grants them a \+1 circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points. At 9th level, they gain a 3rd-level spell slot and can cast 3rd-level spells from their tradition. At 10th level, Watcher's Power allows them to cast one additional spell per day from their tradition without expending a spell slot, though this spell still carries the normal Corruption Risk.  
Levels 11-15: At 11th level, Magi gain Master of Forbidden Lore, allowing them to learn two additional spells from their tradition for each spell level they can cast. At 12th level, Perfect Elemental Affinity increases their status bonus to spell attack rolls and spell DCs for their chosen damage type to \+3. At 13th level, Advanced Corruption Resistance increases their circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points to \+2. At 14th level, they gain a 4th-level spell slot and can cast 4th-level spells from their tradition. At 15th level, Watcher's Mastery allows them to cast two additional spells per day from their tradition without expending spell slots, though these spells still carry the normal Corruption Risk.  
Levels 16-20: At 16th level, Magi gain Legendary Forbidden Lore, allowing them to learn three additional spells from their tradition for each spell level they can cast. At 17th level, Supreme Elemental Affinity increases their status bonus to spell attack rolls and spell DCs for their chosen damage type to \+4. At 18th level, Perfect Corruption Resistance increases their circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points to \+3, and they can reduce their Corruption Points by 1 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation. At 19th level, they gain a 5th-level spell slot and can cast 5th-level spells from their tradition. At 20th level, Watcher Avatar allows them to cast three additional spells per day from their tradition without expending spell slots, and they can choose to ignore the Corruption Risk for one spell per day.

Class Feats  
Levels 1-5:  
Blood Magic \[Feat 1\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class  
You can sacrifice your own vitality to fuel your magical abilities. As a single action, you can take 1d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 1 Focus Point. You can use this ability only if you have a focus pool. The bleed damage can be stopped by a successful DC 15 flat check or by receiving healing that restores Hit Points. This ability represents the dangerous nature of forbidden magic, where power comes at a personal cost.  
Extended Spell \[Feat 1\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class  
When you cast a spell with a duration of 1 minute or less, you can increase its duration to 10 minutes. If the spell's duration is already 10 minutes or more, you can double its duration. This allows you to maintain beneficial effects for longer periods, though it also extends the duration of any Corruption Risk associated with the spell.  
Quickened Spell \[Feat 1\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class  
You can cast a spell that normally requires two actions as a single action. This allows you to cast spells more quickly in combat, though it doesn't reduce the Corruption Risk associated with the spell. You can use this ability once per day.  
Spell Penetration \[Feat 4\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Intelligence 14+  
Your spells are particularly effective at overcoming magical resistance. When you cast a spell that allows a saving throw, you can increase the DC of that saving throw by 1\. Additionally, when you cast a spell that deals damage to a target with resistance to that damage type, you can ignore the first 5 points of that resistance. This represents your deep understanding of how magic works and how to make it more effective.  
Levels 6-10:  
Advanced Blood Magic \[Feat 6\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Blood Magic feat  
You can sacrifice more of your vitality for greater power. As a single action, you can take 2d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 2 Focus Points. Alternatively, you can take 1d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 1 Focus Point and increase the DC of your next spell cast before the end of your turn by 1\. The bleed damage from this ability can be stopped only by magical healing or by succeeding at two consecutive DC 15 flat checks.  
Heightened Spell \[Feat 6\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Extended Spell feat  
When you cast a spell, you can heighten it to a higher level than its base level, up to your highest available spell slot level. This allows you to make your spells more powerful without needing to learn higher-level versions. The Corruption Risk for the spell is based on the heightened level, not the base level.  
Empowered Spell \[Feat 8\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Spell Penetration feat  
When you cast a spell that deals damage, you can increase the damage by rolling one additional damage die. This represents your ability to channel more power into your spells, making them more devastating. The Corruption Risk for the spell increases by 1 when you use this ability.  
Metamagic Mastery \[Feat 10\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Quickened Spell feat  
You can use your Quickened Spell ability twice per day instead of once per day. Additionally, you can combine Quickened Spell with Extended Spell or Heightened Spell, allowing you to cast heightened or extended spells as a single action. This represents your growing mastery over the forbidden arts and your ability to manipulate magic in ways that would be impossible for less experienced practitioners.  
Levels 11-15:  
Life Essence \[Feat 12\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Advanced Blood Magic feat  
You can sacrifice a significant portion of your life essence for immense power. As a single action, you can take 4d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 3 Focus Points. Alternatively, you can take 2d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 2 Focus Points and increase the DC of your next spell cast before the end of your turn by 2\. The bleed damage from this ability can be stopped only by magical healing of 5th level or higher, or by succeeding at three consecutive DC 20 flat checks. Additionally, your maximum Hit Points are reduced by 10 until you complete a full week of downtime.  
Perfect Spell \[Feat 14\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Empowered Spell feat  
When you cast a spell that allows a saving throw, you can increase the DC of that saving throw by 2 instead of 1\. Additionally, when you cast a spell that deals damage to a target with resistance to that damage type, you can ignore the first 10 points of that resistance instead of 5\. Finally, when you use the Empowered Spell feat, you can roll two additional damage dice instead of one. This represents your near-perfect understanding of magical principles and your ability to make your spells almost impossible to resist.  
Corruption Mastery \[Feat 14\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Advanced Corruption Resistance feat  
Your circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points increases to \+3. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from casting a spell, you gain 1 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 2 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation. This represents your growing ability to resist the corrupting influence of forbidden knowledge, though you can never fully escape it.  
Watcher's Blessing \[Feat 16\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Watcher's Mastery feature  
Once per day, you can call upon your patron Watcher for assistance. Choose one of the following effects:  
Gain a \+2 status bonus to one attack roll, skill check, or saving throw  
Restore 1 Focus Point  
Gain resistance 5 to one damage type (acid, cold, electricity, fire, or sonic) for 1 minute  
Gain a \+2 status bonus to your spell DCs for 1 minute  
This ability represents the direct intervention of your Watcher patron, granting you power beyond what you can normally access through your own studies.  
Levels 16-20:  
Sacrifice of Power \[Feat 18\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Life Essence feat  
You can sacrifice a significant portion of your life force for godlike power. As a single action, you can take 8d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 5 Focus Points. Alternatively, you can take 4d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 3 Focus Points and increase the DC of your next spell cast before the end of your turn by 3\. The bleed damage from this ability can be stopped only by magical healing of 7th level or higher, or by succeeding at four consecutive DC 25 flat checks. Additionally, your maximum Hit Points are reduced by 20 until you complete a full month of downtime, and you permanently lose 1 point of Constitution (this reduction can be restored only by a Wish spell or similar miracle).  
Perfect Metamagic \[Feat 18\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Metamagic Mastery feat  
You can use your Quickened Spell ability three times per day instead of twice per day. Additionally, you can combine Quickened Spell with Extended Spell, Heightened Spell, and Empowered Spell simultaneously, allowing you to cast empowered, heightened, extended spells as a single action. Finally, when you use the Extended Spell feat, you can increase the duration of a spell to 1 hour instead of 10 minutes. This represents your complete mastery over the manipulation of magical energy, allowing you to bend spells to your will in ways that would be impossible for lesser practitioners.  
Corruption Immunity \[Feat 20\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Corruption Mastery feat  
Your circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points increases to \+4. Additionally, when you gain Corruption Points from casting a spell, you gain 2 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). Finally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 3 once per day by spending 10 minutes in meditation. You also gain the ability to sense the Corruption Points of other creatures within 30 feet—you can determine whether a creature has 0, 1–2, 3–5, 6–10, or 11+ Corruption Points. This represents your near-immunity to corruption, though you can never be completely free of it as long as you continue to use forbidden magic.  
Watcher Avatar \[Feat 20\]  
Magi  
Prerequisites Magi class, Watcher's Blessing feat  
Once per week, you can temporarily become an avatar of your patron Watcher. For 1 hour, you gain the following benefits:  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws  
You restore 5 Focus Points  
You gain resistance 20 to all damage except force  
You gain a \+4 status bonus to your spell DCs  
You can cast spells without needing to Refocus  
You can cast spells of up to 8th level without needing to prepare them or have them in your repertoire (you still need the appropriate spell slots)  
You can cast one additional spell of up to 8th level before needing to Refocus  
This ability represents the ultimate expression of your connection to your Watcher patron, allowing you to wield power that approaches the divine. However, using this ability carries a significant Corruption Risk—you automatically gain 5 Corruption Points when the duration ends, representing the spiritual cost of channeling such immense power.  
Example Build  
Keturah, Semyaza's Disciple — A Sorcerer Clan Magi who has dedicated her life to mastering the enchantments and root-cutting arts taught by Semyaza. With high Intelligence and Charisma, she wields spells of charm and manipulation, using her powers to influence both human and giant minds. Her Corruption Resistance feat helps her mitigate the spiritual cost of her magic, while her Blood Magic ability allows her to push beyond her normal limits when necessary. Keturah walks a dangerous path, constantly balancing the immediate benefits of her forbidden knowledge against the long-term consequences for her soul. She begins with 2 CP from her Sorcerer Clan heritage and must carefully manage her Corruption Points to avoid divine judgment. Her goal is to use her powers to protect her community from Nephilim oppression, though she fears that her methods may ultimately corrupt the very people she seeks to save.  
\[PAUSED \- Chapter 4: Priest Class\]  
continue

Priest  
The Priest stands as the divine miracle-worker of the antediluvian world, a channeler of the Creator's power in an age of increasing darkness and corruption. Unlike the forbidden magic of the Magi or the natural abilities of other classes, the Priest's power flows directly from the Most High, granted through faith, righteousness, and covenant relationship. They are the keepers of the original faith, the preservers of divine truth in a world being consumed by the teachings of fallen Watchers.  
Priests trace their lineage back to the righteous line of Seth, though some come from other backgrounds who have turned to the Creator in faith. They serve as spiritual leaders in Sethite communities, offering sacrifices, teaching the ways of righteousness, and calling upon divine power to heal the sick and protect the faithful. Some Priests are wandering prophets like Enoch, receiving direct revelations and visions that guide their path. Others serve as temple attendants in hidden sanctuaries where the faithful gather to worship away from the corruption of Nephilim-dominated cities.  
In society, Priests occupy a position of spiritual authority and moral leadership. They are respected not for their martial prowess or political power, but for their connection to the divine and their commitment to righteousness. They mediate disputes, offer counsel, perform sacred rituals, and intercede on behalf of their communities. Their words carry weight because they speak not from personal opinion but from divine revelation. Even those who do not share their faith often seek their wisdom, recognizing that their connection to the Most High grants them insight beyond ordinary human understanding.  
The Priest's approach to divine power reflects the bronze-age setting and the intimate relationship between the Creator and humanity in the antediluvian age. They do not cast spells through abstract formulae or complex rituals but through prayer, sacrifice, and righteous living. Their miracles often involve tangible, immediate interventions—fire from heaven consuming sacrifices, healing waters restoring the sick, protective barriers shielding the faithful from harm. This direct connection to divine power makes them invaluable allies in the struggle against corruption and giant oppression, though it also makes them targets for those who serve the fallen Watchers.  
The central tension of the Priest class is the Righteousness mechanic. Unlike the Magi's Corruption Risk, the Priest's power is fueled by Righteousness Points (RP), which represent their spiritual connection to the divine. Using powerful miracles requires spending RP, and maintaining a high RP total grants various benefits. However, RP can only be gained through righteous actions—acts of mercy, self-sacrifice, resisting corruption, and keeping sacred covenants. This creates meaningful roleplaying opportunities as Priest players must constantly choose between the immediate benefits of their power and the long-term spiritual health of their character.  
Class Statistics  
Key Ability: Wisdom  
Hit Points: 8 plus your Constitution modifier  
Initial Proficiencies:  
Perception: Expert  
Saving Throws: Fortitude Trained, Reflex Trained, Will Expert  
Skills: Trained in Religion and four other skills of your choice  
Attacks: Trained in simple weapons and unarmed attacks  
Defenses: Trained in light armor, medium armor, and shields  
Class DC: Trained in Will DC  
Class Features  
Levels 1-5: At 1st level, Priests gain Divine Miracles, allowing them to cast divine spells from the Priest spell list using spell slots. They start with two 1st-level spell slots and a number of cantrips equal to their Wisdom modifier. At 2nd level, Turn Unholy allows them to repel spirits and corrupted beings. As a single action, they can present their holy symbol and command undead creatures and corrupted beings (those with 3 or more CP) to flee. Affected creatures must attempt a Will save (DC \= 10 \+ your level \+ your Wisdom modifier). On a failure, the creature is frightened 1 and must move away from you on its next turn. At 3rd level, Righteous Aura creates a protective field around the Priest. Allies within 30 feet gain a \+1 status bonus to saving throws against fear effects and possession attempts. At 4th level, Sanctuary allows them to create a protected area. As a single action, they can designate a 20-foot burst centered on a point they can see within 60 feet. For 1 minute, corrupted beings (those with 3 or more CP) cannot enter the area, and undead creatures take a \-2 penalty to attack rolls while in the area. At 5th level, they gain a 2nd-level spell slot and can cast 2nd-level spells from the Priest spell list.  
Levels 6-10: At 6th level, Priests gain Divine Favor, allowing them to spend 1 RP to reroll any failed saving throw or skill check. They can use this ability once per day. At 7th level, Improved Turn Unholy increases the range of their Turn Unholy ability to 60 feet, and creatures that fail their Will save are frightened 2 instead of frightened 1\. At 8th level, Enhanced Righteous Aura increases the status bonus from their Righteous Aura to \+2, and the aura now also grants resistance 5 to negative damage to allies within 30 feet. At 9th level, they gain a 3rd-level spell slot and can cast 3rd-level spells from the Priest spell list. At 10th level, Divine Shield allows them to protect an ally from harm. As a reaction when an ally within 30 feet is hit by an attack, they can interpose divine energy. The ally gains resistance 10 to all damage from the triggering attack.  
Levels 11-15: At 11th level, Priests gain Master of Miracles, allowing them to cast one additional spell per day from the Priest spell list without expending a spell slot. This spell still requires the normal RP cost if applicable. At 12th level, Powerful Turn Unholy allows them to use their Turn Unholy ability twice per day instead of once per day. Additionally, creatures that fail their Will save are frightened 3 instead of frightened 2\. At 13th level, Supreme Righteous Aura increases the status bonus from their Righteous Aura to \+3, and the aura now also grants resistance 10 to negative damage and immunity to the frightened condition to allies within 30 feet. At 14th level, they gain a 4th-level spell slot and can cast 4th-level spells from the Priest spell list. At 15th level, Divine Intervention allows them to call upon the Most High for direct assistance. Once per week, as a single action, they can restore an ally within 60 feet to full Hit Points and remove all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious.  
Levels 16-20: At 16th level, Priests gain Legendary Priest, allowing them to cast two additional spells per day from the Priest spell list without expending spell slots. These spells still require the normal RP cost if applicable. At 17th level, Perfect Turn Unholy allows them to use their Turn Unholy ability three times per day instead of once per day. Additionally, creatures that fail their Will save are frightened 4 and stunned until the end of their next turn. At 18th level, Perfect Righteous Aura increases the status bonus from their Righteous Aura to \+4, and the aura now also grants resistance 15 to all damage except force and immunity to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious to allies within 30 feet. At 19th level, they gain a 5th-level spell slot and can cast 5th-level spells from the Priest spell list. At 20th level, Avatar of Righteousness allows them to temporarily become an avatar of divine judgment. For 1 minute, they gain a \+4 status bonus to all attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws, resistance 20 to all damage except force, and the ability to cast any Priest spell without expending spell slots or RP. They can use this ability once per month.
