Intervene: The party confronts Keturah about her corruption, offering help and support for repentance. This may strain relationships but could save her soul.  
Ignore it: The party ignores the corruption, focusing on immediate goals. Keturah's corruption may worsen, eventually threatening the party.  
Expel her: The party expels Keturah for her corruption, protecting themselves but abandoning a companion to damnation.  
Consequences:  
Intervention risks conflict but offers hope for redemption  
Ignoring it maintains party unity but risks greater corruption  
Expulsion protects the party but may create an enemy  
Scenario 4: The Sacred Oath  
Situation: The party swears an oath to protect a Sethite enclave from Nephilim attacks. Later, they discover that fulfilling this oath will prevent them from completing a lucrative mission that could provide resources for their own community.  
Player Choices:  
Keep the oath: The party honors their promise, gaining 1 RP each but losing the lucrative mission. The Sethite enclave is grateful and may provide future aid.  
Break the oath: The party abandons the Sethite enclave, gaining resources but 2 CP each for breaking a sacred oath. The enclave may be destroyed, and righteous NPCs will distrust the party.  
Compromise: The party seeks a third option, such as finding allies to protect the enclave while they complete the mission. This is difficult and time-consuming but avoids both corruption and loss.  
Consequences:  
Keeping the oath maintains righteousness but sacrifices resources  
Breaking the oath gains resources but spiritual cost  
Compromising seeks balance but requires creativity and effort  
Integration with Pathfinder 2e Systems  
Action Economy  
The Soul Economy integrates seamlessly with Pathfinder 2e's three-action economy. Spending RP or gaining CP typically occurs as part of other actions, not as separate actions themselves.  
Spending RP:  
Rerolling a failed roll: Free action (declared after the roll but before the result is finalized)  
Healing an ally: Single action (in addition to the normal action cost of healing)  
Resisting corruption: Reaction (triggered by the corruption effect)  
Powering divine miracles: Included in the action cost of the miracle itself  
Gaining CP:  
Using forbidden magic: Occurs automatically after casting the spell (no action required)  
Committing corrupt acts: Occurred during the action that constitutes the corrupt act  
Breaking oaths: Occurred at the moment the oath is broken  
Proficiency System  
The Soul Economy interacts with Pathfinder 2e's proficiency system (Trained, Expert, Master, Legendary) in several ways:  
Divine Skill Checks: Characters with high Net Righteousness may add their proficiency bonus to certain skill checks related to divine matters:  
Religion checks to understand righteous teachings or sacred texts  
Diplomacy checks when dealing with righteous NPCs  
Will saves against corruption effects  
Corruption Skill Checks: Characters with high Corruption Points may add their proficiency bonus to certain skill checks related to corrupt matters:  
Occultism checks to understand forbidden knowledge or corrupted texts  
Intimidation checks when dealing with corrupted NPCs  
Fortitude saves against physical effects of corruption (such as disease or poison)  
Conditions  
The Soul Economy introduces several new conditions that interact with Pathfinder 2e's existing condition system:  
Blessed (Net Righteousness \+5 or higher):  
Effects: \+1 status bonus to attack rolls, skill checks, and saving throws; resistance 2 to all damage from corrupted sources  
Removal: Lost when Net Righteousness drops below \+Marked for Judgment (5+ CP):  
Effects: When you roll a natural 1 on any d20 roll, attempt a Will save (DC 15 \+ 1 per 2 CP over 5). On a failure, suffer a minor divine judgment effect  
Removal: Lost when CP drops below Condemned (10+ CP):  
Effects: Marked for Judgment effects trigger on natural rolls of 1-2; DC increases by 5; effects are more severe  
Removal: Lost when CP drops below Corrupted (Net Righteousness \-1 or lower):  
Effects: \-1 status penalty to saving throws against divine effects; vulnerable 2 to radiant damage  
Removal: Lost when Net Righteousness rises above Possessed (failed save against possession):  
Effects: Controlled by possessing entity; may act against your will  
Removal: Successful save against possession; divine exorcism; removal of CP that made you vulnerable  
Feats and Class Features  
Many feats and class features interact with the Soul Economy, providing ways to gain, spend, or mitigate RP and CP. These are detailed in their respective chapters, but common interactions include:  
RP-Gaining Feats:  
Divine Favor (Sethite ancestry): Reroll failed saves, potentially avoiding situations that would cause CP gain  
Righteous Heritage (Sethite ancestry): Start with additional RP  
Teaching the Righteous (Priest class): Grant RP to allies  
CP-Mitigating Feats:  
Corruption Resistance (Sorcerer Clan ancestry): Reduce CP gain from forbidden magic  
Watcher's Blood (Nephilim ancestry): Reduce CP gain from ancestry-specific actions  
Spiritual Resilience (Priest class): Resist corruption effects  
RP-Spending Abilities:  
Divine Miracles (Priest class): Spend RP to fuel divine spells  
Righteous Strike (Warrior class): Spend RP to deal additional radiant damage  
Divine Guidance (Hunter class): Spend RP to gain bonuses to skill checks  
Multiclassing  
When multiclassing, your Soul Economy status is determined by your total character level, not your level in any specific class. However, certain class features may provide bonuses or penalties to RP gain or CP mitigation:  
Divine Classes (Priest, certain Warrior builds):  
\+1 bonus to RP gained from righteous actions  
\-1 penalty to CP gained from borderline actions  
Forbidden Classes (Magi, certain Scribe builds):  
\+1 bonus to CP gained from using class abilities  
\-1 penalty to RP gained from righteous actions  
Neutral Classes (Hunter, Artisan, Gibbor):  
No modifiers to RP or CP gain  
May have abilities that allow spending RP or gaining CP in unique ways  
Downtime Activities  
The Soul Economy integrates with Pathfinder 2e's downtime system, providing additional activities related to spiritual development:  
RP-Gaining Downtime:  
Prayer and Meditation (1 day): Attempt a DC 15 Religion check. On success, gain 1 RP.  
Righteous Service (1 week): Serve a righteous community or cause. Gain 1 RP automatically, or 2 RP on a successful DC 12 Diplomacy check.  
Teaching the Righteous (1 week): Instruct others in righteous ways. Gain 1 RP, and each student gains 1 RP.  
CP-Removing Downtime:  
Repentance (1 week): Attempt a DC 15 Will save. On success, remove 1 CP.  
Pilgrimage (1 month): Travel to a sacred site. Remove 1 CP automatically, or 2 CP on a successful DC 15 Religion check.  
Atonement (1 month): Make restitution for past wrongs. Remove 1 CP automatically, or 2 CP if the restitution is particularly meaningful.  
CP-Gaining Downtime:  
Forbidden Study (1 day): Study forbidden texts or practices. Gain 1 CP, but gain a \+1 item bonus to a specific skill check related to the study.  
Corrupt Alliance (1 week): Form alliances with corrupted beings. Gain 1 CP, but gain a \+1 status bonus to checks when dealing with corrupted NPCs.  
Dark Ritual (1 month): Participate in a dark ritual or ceremony. Gain 2 CP, but gain a powerful temporary ability or item.  
Game Master Guidelines  
Awarding and Tracking RP/CP  
As the Game Master, you are responsible for awarding RP and tracking CP. This requires judgment and consistency to ensure the system feels fair and meaningful.  
Awarding RP:  
Be generous but not automatic—RP should feel earned, not guaranteed  
Consider the context and motivation behind actions, not just the action itself  
Reward genuine sacrifice and difficult choices, not just successful outcomes  
Provide opportunities for RP gain in every session, but don't force them  
Tracking CP:  
Be consistent with CP risk checks for forbidden magic  
Track CP gained from actions, but don't penalize players for forgetting to track them  
Provide warnings when characters are approaching dangerous CP thresholds  
Allow opportunities for CP removal, but don't make it too easy  
Using the Soul Economy Narratively  
The Soul Economy is not just a mechanical system—it's a narrative tool that can enhance your campaign's themes and drama.  
Character Arcs:  
Use RP/CP changes to reflect character development and moral choices  
Create storylines around redemption, corruption, or spiritual struggle  
Allow characters to influence each other's spiritual states through their actions and relationships  
Campaign Themes:  
Emphasize the tension between power and righteousness  
Create moral dilemmas that force players to choose between immediate benefits and long-term consequences  
Use the approaching Flood as a ticking clock that makes spiritual choices more urgent  
NPC Interactions:  
Have NPCs react to characters' Net Righteousness in meaningful ways  
Create NPCs who are struggling with their own spiritual states, providing mirrors for the players  
Use righteous and corrupted NPCs as guides, mentors, or antagonists based on their spiritual alignment  
Balancing the System  
The Soul Economy should enhance gameplay, not hinder it. Balance is key to ensuring the system feels fair and engaging.  
Avoiding Punishment:  
Don't use CP as a punishment for player choices—use it as a consequence of character choices  
Provide meaningful benefits for high RP, not just penalties for high CP  
Allow players to recover from corruption without feeling permanently damaged  
Maintaining Agency:  
Give players meaningful choices about when to gain CP or spend RP  
Don't force players into situations where they must gain CP to succeed  
Provide multiple paths to success, some righteous and some corrupt  
Scaling with Level:  
As characters level up, their RP and CP totals will naturally increase  
Adjust the significance of RP/CP thresholds to maintain tension at higher levels  
Introduce new RP/CP mechanics at higher tiers to keep the system engaging  
Common Issues and Solutions  
Issue: Players hoard RP and never spend it  
Solution: Create situations where spending RP is necessary or highly beneficial; introduce time limits on RP storage; provide incentives for spending RP  
Issue: Players accumulate CP too quickly  
Solution: Provide more opportunities for CP removal; reduce CP risk for borderline actions; introduce mechanics that allow converting CP to RP through redemption  
Issue: The system feels too punitive  
Solution: Emphasize the benefits of high RP, not just the penalties of high CP; provide clear paths to redemption; allow players to make meaningful choices without severe consequences  
Issue: Tracking becomes cumbersome  
Solution: Use a shared tracking system (such as a whiteboard or digital sheet); assign a player to help track RP/CP; simplify tracking by using Net Righteousness instead of separate RP/CP totals  
Is this where we should clear some context space, or are you clear to continue?

This chapter will adapt the Pathfinder 2e skill system to the bronze-age setting of Nephilim Wars, modifying skill descriptions, adding setting-specific uses, and integrating the Soul Economy where appropriate. I'll maintain the same detailed, comprehensive approach we've used for previous chapters.  
Proceeding with Chapter 6 now...  
Chapter 6: Skills  
Introduction to Skills in the Antediluvian World  
In the age before the Flood, skills represent more than mere proficiencies—they embody the practical knowledge, survival instincts, and specialized expertise necessary to navigate a world dominated by giants, corrupted by forbidden knowledge, and shadowed by impending judgment. Unlike medieval fantasy settings where skills might reflect guild training or courtly education, the skills of Nephilim Wars are forged in the crucible of bronze-age survival, tribal necessity, and spiritual struggle.  
Every skill check carries the weight of consequence. A failed Survival check doesn't just mean hunger—it could mean becoming prey for Nephilim scouts. A failed Religion check might lead to misinterpreting a divine warning about the coming Flood. A failed Occultism check while studying forbidden texts could result in gaining Corruption Points or attracting the attention of disembodied spirits (Refa'im).  
This chapter adapts the Pathfinder 2e skill system to the antediluvian setting, providing setting-specific applications, modified descriptions, and Soul Economy interactions for all 26 core skills. Additionally, it introduces specialized Lore categories unique to the world of Nephilim Wars and explains how skills function within the bronze-age technological and social context.  
Core Skill Adaptations  
Acrobatics  
Description: Your ability to move with agility, balance, and grace—essential for navigating treacherous terrain, avoiding giant stomps, and escaping tight situations. In a world without plate armor (which doesn't exist yet), physical agility is often the difference between life and death.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Tumble Through Giant Legs: When a Nephilim moves through your space, you can attempt an Acrobatics check (DC \= 10 \+ the giant's level) to avoid being trampled. Success means you slip between their legs unharmed; failure means you take bludgeoning damage equal to the giant's level.  
Balance on Narrow Surfaces: Traverse fallen logs over raging rivers, narrow canyon ledges, or the curved roofs of Cainite buildings. Failure might mean falling into water infested with river monsters or onto sharp rocks below.  
Escape Grapples: Break free from Nephilim grabs or Anakim chain weapons. The DC is typically 15 \+ the creature's level.  
Soul Economy Interaction: None directly, though using Acrobatics to escape a situation where you could have stood and fought righteously might be considered cowardice (GM discretion for CP gain).  
Arcana  
Description: Knowledge of magical principles, magical creatures, and supernatural phenomena—but in the antediluvian world, this primarily means understanding the forbidden arts taught by the Watchers. True arcane magic as known in later ages does not yet exist; what passes for "arcana" is either divine power (handled by Religion) or corrupted knowledge (handled by Occultism).  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Identify Forbidden Magic: Recognize spells cast by Magi or Sorcerer Clans as belonging to specific Watcher traditions (Azazel's fire magic, Semyaza's enchantments, etc.). Success reveals the spell's effects and potential Corruption Risk.  
Understand Magical Items: Identify artifacts created through Watcher-taught arts, such as Azazel's bronze weapons or Baraqel's star charts. Failure might result in accidentally triggering a corrupting effect.  
Detect Magical Auras: Sense the presence of magical energy in an area, which could indicate a hidden Watcher shrine, a cursed location, or a place of divine power.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Studying forbidden magical texts or items requires a flat check (DC 11 \+ item's level). On a failure, you gain 1 CP. Using Arcana to identify divine miracles (rather than forbidden magic) grants 1 RP on a critical success.  
Athletics  
Description: Your physical strength, endurance, and prowess in feats of raw power—climbing, jumping, swimming, and breaking objects. In a world where giants can crush stone with their bare hands, human strength must be augmented by technique, leverage, and determination.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Climb Giant Structures: Scale the massive walls of Nephilim fortresses, the towering gates of Cainite cities, or natural cliff faces to escape pursuit. DC varies by surface (mud brick: DC 15, smooth stone: DC 25, giant-carved handholds: DC 20).  
Break Bonds: Snap ropes, break wooden bars, or bend bronze manacles. DC equals 15 \+ the material's Hardness. Success frees you; failure might alert guards.  
Swim Against Currents: Navigate flooded plains, raging rivers, or storm-tossed lakes. Failure means being swept away or drowning. Particularly relevant as environmental chaos increases before the Flood.  
High Jump/Long Jump: Leap across chasms created by earthquakes, over Nephilim footprints (which can be 3 feet deep), or onto the backs of smaller giants for a killing blow.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Athletics to perform a feat of strength that protects the innocent (such as holding up a collapsing roof to let villagers escape) grants 1 RP on success.  
Crafting  
Description: Your skill in creating, repairing, and modifying physical objects—from bronze weapons and pottery to shelters and tools. This skill embodies Tubal-Cain's legacy, the foundation of antediluvian civilization and humanity's primary defense against giant oppression.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Forge Weapons/Armor: Create bronze swords, spears, shields, and scale mail. Higher results produce masterwork items with better critical ranges or AC bonuses. Legendary results might create weapons capable of harming Nephilim more effectively.  
Repair Equipment: Fix damaged weapons, broken shields, or torn clothing. Critical failures might ruin the item beyond repair.  
Create Traps: Build mechanical traps using wood, stone, and bronze—pit traps with sharpened stakes, falling rock triggers, or snare nets. DC varies by trap complexity.  
Build Structures: Construct temporary shelters, reinforce village walls, or create siege engines (battering rams, siege towers). Time required scales with structure size and complexity.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Crafting weapons or armor specifically designed to protect the righteous (such as shields for a Sethite enclave) grants 1 RP upon completion. Crafting items for corrupted purposes (such as weapons for a Nephilim warlord) grants 1 CP.  
Deception  
Description: Your ability to lie, bluff, disguise yourself, and manipulate others through words and actions. In a world where being identified as a Sethite could mean death at the hands of Nephilim collaborators, or where hiding your Sorcerer Clan heritage is essential for survival, deception is often a necessary tool.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Blend In: Pass as a member of another lineage (e.g., an Elioud passing as human, a Sethite hiding among Cainites). DC depends on scrutiny level and physical differences.  
Lie to Authorities: Convince Nephilim guards, Cainite officials, or corrupted priests that you belong somewhere you don't. Failure might mean imprisonment, interrogation, or worse.  
Create Diversions: Distract guards by shouting "Fire\!" or "Nephilim attack\!" to create an opportunity for escape or infiltration.  
Feign Death: Convince enemies you're dead to avoid being finished off. Requires a successful check opposed by observers' Perception.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Lying to protect the innocent (such as hiding Sethite refugees from Nephilim patrols) does not incur CP. Lying for personal gain, to betray allies, or to serve corrupted interests grants 1 CP on success. Critical failures when lying to righteous NPCs might grant 1 RP to those NPCs.  
Diplomacy  
Description: Your skill in negotiating, persuading, gathering information, and making impressions through honest communication. In tribal societies where reputation and word carry immense weight, diplomacy can prevent bloodshed, forge alliances, and preserve communities.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Make an Impression: Gain the trust of tribal elders, Cainite merchants, or even reasonable giant-kin (such as some Gibborim). Success might secure safe passage, trade agreements, or military aid.  
Request: Ask NPCs for assistance, information, or resources. A successful check might convince a Wanderer tribe to share their water sources or a Cainite smith to repair your weapons.  
Gather Information: Learn about local threats (Nephilim patrols, corrupted sorcerers), safe routes, or hidden communities. Higher results reveal more detailed or secret information.  
Negotiate Peace: Mediate disputes between tribes, prevent raids, or broker truces between human settlements and giant factions. Failure might escalate tensions.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Successfully using Diplomacy to prevent violence, protect the innocent, or promote righteousness grants 1 RP. Using Diplomacy to manipulate others into corrupt actions (such as convincing a village to surrender to Nephilim rule) grants 1 CP. Righteous NPCs grant bonuses to Diplomacy checks; corrupted NPCs impose penalties based on your Net Righteousness.  
Intimidation  
Description: Your ability to coerce, demoralize, and command through threats, displays of power, or sheer presence. In a brutal world where strength is respected and fear is a tool of control, intimidation can be as effective as diplomacy—but at a spiritual cost.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Coerce: Force information from prisoners, make merchants lower prices, or compel guards to look the other way. DC depends on the target's resolve and your leverage.  
Demoralize: Shake an enemy's confidence before or during combat. Success makes them frightened, potentially causing them to flee or fight less effectively.  
Command: Issue orders to subordinates, thralls, or even lower-status giants (such as Elioud serving a Nephilim). Success depends on your relative status and their willingness to obey.  
Interrogate: Extract information through threats of violence or other consequences. Higher results yield more accurate or detailed information.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Intimidation to protect the innocent (such as threatening a Nephilim scout to leave a village alone) does not incur CP. Using Intimidation for cruelty, oppression, or personal gain grants 1 CP on success. Characters with high CP gain bonuses to Intimidation checks against corrupted NPCs.  
Medicine  
Description: Your knowledge of healing, anatomy, diseases, and poisons—essential in a world without modern medicine, where infections can be fatal and giant-inflicted wounds are catastrophic. This skill encompasses both practical first aid and spiritual healing practices.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Treat Wounds: Stabilize dying allies, stop bleeding, set broken bones, and treat infections. Success restores Hit Points; critical success provides additional benefits (removing conditions, preventing complications).  
Treat Disease: Identify and treat illnesses, from common fevers to supernatural afflictions caused by corrupted beings. DC varies by disease severity.  
Treat Poison: Neutralize toxins from giant venoms, corrupted plants, or assassin's blades. Success reduces poison stages or duration.  
Perform Surgery: Remove arrowheads, amputate gangrenous limbs, or extract parasitic spirits. Requires specialized tools and a high DC; failure causes additional damage.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Medicine to heal the innocent without expectation of reward grants 1 RP. Using Medicine to heal corrupted beings who will continue harming others (without securing their repentance) grants 1 CP. Healing a righteous NPC grants additional RP equal to their level divided by 5 (minimum 1).  
Nature  
Description: Your understanding of the natural world—animals, plants, weather, and wilderness survival. For Wanderers, Sethite shepherds, and wilderness scouts, this skill is as vital as any weapon, providing food, shelter, and early warnings of danger.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Recall Knowledge: Identify plants (edible, medicinal, poisonous), animals (predators, prey, omens), weather patterns (approaching storms, seasonal changes), and natural phenomena (earthquakes, floods).  
Sense Direction: Navigate without landmarks, using stars, sun position, wind patterns, or animal behavior. Critical failure might lead you dangerously off course.  
Track: Follow creatures or people through wilderness terrain. Higher results reveal more details about the quarry (size, condition, direction, time since passing).  
Handle Animal: Calm, train, or command beasts—from hunting dogs and horses to wild predators. Success might gain a temporary animal ally; failure might provoke an attack.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Nature to protect innocent life (such as leading a herd away from a Nephilim hunting party) grants 1 RP. Using Nature to harm the innocent (such as poisoning a village's water source) grants 1 CP. Characters with high RP gain bonuses to Nature checks involving sacred groves or divinely blessed locations.  
Occultism  
Description: Your knowledge of forbidden arts, supernatural entities, and corrupted practices—the dangerous legacy of the Watchers. This skill covers astrology (Baraqel's teachings), enchantments (Semyaza's arts), root-cutting (herbalism and poisons), and other forbidden knowledge that risks spiritual corruption.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Identify Forbidden Magic: Recognize spells and effects from specific Watcher traditions. Success reveals the spell's origin, effects, and Corruption Risk. Critical success might reveal weaknesses or counters.  
Understand Corrupted Entities: Identify types of disembodied spirits (Refa'im), their origins, and their vulnerabilities. Success might reveal how to banish or avoid them.  
Decipher Forbidden Texts: Read and understand scrolls, tablets, or inscriptions containing Watcher-taught knowledge. Failure might result in gaining CP or attracting spiritual attention.  
Detect Corruption: Sense the presence of Corruption Points in people, places, or objects. Higher results reveal the amount and nature of corruption.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Critical Mechanic: Every use of Occultism to study forbidden knowledge requires a flat check (DC 11 \+ the knowledge's level). On a failure, you gain 1 CP. On a critical failure, you gain 2 CP and suffer a minor corruption effect (such as being sickened 1 for 1 hour). Using Occultism to identify divine miracles or righteous practices grants 1 RP on success.  
Performance  
Description: Your ability to entertain, inspire, and influence through music, dance, storytelling, or other artistic expression. In oral cultures where stories preserve history and music unites communities, performance is both art and necessity.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Perform: Entertain crowds in Cainite cities, tribal gatherings, or Nephilim courts. Success might earn coin, favor, or information. Critical success might inspire listeners to righteous action or reveal hidden emotions.  
Fascinate: Captivate an audience with your performance, making them temporarily oblivious to their surroundings. Useful for creating diversions or calming hostile crowds.  
Storytelling: Share tales of ancient heroes, divine judgments, or warnings about the Flood. Success might preserve cultural knowledge, inspire hope, or warn of dangers.  
Musical Communication: Use instruments or songs to send coded messages to allies (common among Wanderer tribes or resistance groups).  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Performance to spread righteous teachings, preserve sacred history, or inspire hope grants 1 RP. Using Performance to glorify corruption, spread false teachings, or entertain corrupted beings grants 1 CP. Performing in sacred spaces (with permission) grants additional RP.  
Religion  
Description: Your knowledge of divine truths, righteous practices, sacred texts, and the will of the Most High. This skill represents the preserved wisdom of the Sethite line—the antidote to the corruption spreading through Watcher-taught arts.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Recall Knowledge: Understand divine commandments, righteous history, prophecies (such as Enoch's warnings), and the nature of the Creator. Success might reveal weaknesses of corrupted beings or guidance for righteous action.  
Perform Rituals: Conduct sacrifices, prayers, blessings, or other sacred ceremonies. Success might grant temporary divine favor; failure might offend the divine or attract negative attention.  
Identify Divine Effects: Recognize miracles, blessings, curses, or other divine interventions. Success reveals the effect's nature and origin.  
Sense Sacredness: Detect the presence of divine power in places, objects, or people. Higher results reveal the strength and nature of the sacred presence.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Religion to perform righteous acts (such as blessing a community, interpreting prophecies correctly, or turning away corruption) grants 1 RP. Using Religion to justify corrupt actions, blaspheme, or desecrate sacred things grants 1 CP. Characters with high RP gain bonuses to Religion checks; characters with high CP suffer penalties.  
Society  
Description: Your knowledge of cultures, laws, politics, history, and social structures—the complex web of human (and giant) civilization. In a world of tribal societies, Cainite cities, and Nephilim courts, understanding social dynamics is essential for navigation and influence.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Recall Knowledge: Understand tribal customs, Cainite trade laws, Nephilim court protocols, or the history of conflicts between groups. Success might prevent social blunders or reveal opportunities.  
Gather Information: Learn about political tensions, upcoming events, powerful figures, or hidden agendas in settlements. Higher results reveal secrets or confidential information.  
Decipher Writing: Read non-magical texts—clay tablets, scrolls, inscriptions, or correspondence. Success reveals the content; critical success might uncover hidden meanings or codes.  
Understand Social Hierarchies: Navigate complex social structures, from tribal elder councils to Nephilim noble houses. Success might grant access to restricted areas or influential people.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Society to promote righteousness, protect the innocent, or expose corruption grants 1 RP. Using Society to manipulate social structures for corrupt purposes, betray communities, or serve oppressive regimes grants 1 CP. Righteous communities grant bonuses to Society checks; corrupted societies impose penalties based on your Net Righteousness.  
Stealth  
Description: Your ability to move unseen, unheard, and unnoticed—essential for scouts, hunters, spies, and anyone who must avoid detection by giants, enemies, or supernatural threats. In a world where being seen by a Nephilim patrol often means death, stealth is a survival skill.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Hide: Conceal yourself behind natural cover (rocks, trees, ruins) or in shadows. Success makes you hidden; critical success makes you undetected. Giants' poor Perception might make this easier, but their tremorsense or other abilities could complicate things.  
Sneak: Move while hidden without revealing your position. Success allows you to reposition, approach targets, or escape. Failure reveals your location.  
Create a Diversion: Use noise, thrown objects, or minor illusions to draw attention away from yourself or allies. Success creates an opportunity for others to Hide or Sneak.  
Track Without Being Tracked: Follow a quarry while minimizing signs of your own passage. Success prevents others from tracking you; failure leaves a trail that can be followed.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Stealth to protect the innocent (such as sneaking medicine to imprisoned Sethites) grants 1 RP. Using Stealth for theft, assassination, or other corrupt purposes grants 1 CP. Characters with high RP might find it harder to be stealthy in sacred places (divine light reveals them); characters with high CP might find it easier to hide in corrupted areas.  
Survival  
Description: Your expertise in wilderness living—finding food and water, building shelters, avoiding hazards, and reading environmental signs. For Wanderers, tribal scouts, and anyone traveling beyond settled lands, this skill is the difference between survival and death.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Subsist: Find enough food and water to survive in wilderness environments. Success feeds you and possibly allies; critical success finds extra resources or particularly nutritious food. Failure means hunger, thirst, or consuming something harmful.  
Navigate: Find your way through trackless wilderness, avoiding natural hazards and finding safe paths. Success reaches your destination; critical success finds shortcuts or avoids dangers. Failure leads you astray or into danger.  
Track: Follow creatures or people through natural terrain. Higher results reveal more details about the quarry. Essential for hunting, pursuing enemies, or finding lost companions.  
Predict Weather: Anticipate storms, temperature changes, or other weather events. Success allows preparation; failure might leave you exposed to dangerous conditions. Increasingly important as environmental chaos grows before the Flood.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Survival to help others survive (such as leading refugees to safety) grants 1 RP. Using Survival to abandon others to die or to exploit natural disasters for personal gain grants 1 CP. Characters with high RP gain bonuses to Survival checks in divinely blessed lands; characters with high CP gain bonuses in corrupted or blighted areas.  
Thievery  
Description: Your skill in picking locks, disarming traps, stealing items, and performing other illicit activities. In a world of fortified settlements, guarded treasures, and oppressive regimes, thievery can be a tool of resistance—or corruption.  
Antediluvian Applications:  
Palm an Object: Secretly take small items from people or places. Success means no one notices; failure might alert guards or owners.  
Pick a Lock: Open locked doors, chests, or gates using lockpicks (bone, bronze, or wood). DC varies by lock quality. Critical failure might break your tools or trigger an alarm.  
Disable a Device: Disarm traps, sabotage machinery, or disable security mechanisms. DC varies by device complexity. Critical failure might trigger the trap or alert guards.  
Steal: Take items directly from people or guarded locations. Opposed by targets' Perception. Success means you get the item undetected; failure means you're caught.  
Soul Economy Interaction: Using Thievery to steal from the corrupt to give to the innocent (such as taking food from a Nephilim granary to feed starving villagers) does not incur CP and may grant 1 RP. Using Thievery for personal gain, to harm the innocent, or to serve corrupt interests grants 1 CP. Stealing sacred objects grants 2 CP; returning stolen sacred objects grants 2 RP.  
Specialized Skill Applications  
Skill Synergies  
Certain skill combinations have enhanced effects in the antediluvian world:  
Survival \+ Nature: When tracking in wilderness, add your Nature modifier to your Survival check. Critical success reveals not just the path but the quarry's condition, intentions, and vulnerabilities.  
Religion \+ Society: When interacting with religious communities, add your Religion modifier to your Society check. Critical success might reveal hidden righteous networks or sacred safe houses.  
Stealth \+ Acrobatics: When moving through tight spaces while hidden, add your Acrobatics modifier to your Stealth check. Critical success allows you to move through spaces normally too small without penalty.  
Crafting \+ Lore (Metallurgy): When crafting bronze weapons or armor, add your Metallurgy Lore modifier to your Crafting check. Critical success creates masterwork items with special properties.  
Environmental Modifiers  
The antediluvian world's unique environments impose modifiers on skill checks:  
Nephilim Territory: \-2 circumstance penalty to Stealth, Diplomacy, and Deception checks (constant surveillance, giant patrols, corrupted informants).  
Sacred Sethite Enclave: \+2 circumstance bonus to Religion, Medicine, and Survival checks; \-2 penalty to Occultism and Thievery checks (divine protection, but heightened scrutiny of corrupt actions).  
Cainite City: \+2 circumstance bonus to Society, Crafting, and Diplomacy checks; \-2 penalty to Survival and Nature checks (urban advantages, but wilderness skills are less applicable).  
Flood Plains (Environmental Chaos): \-2 circumstance penalty to Survival, Acrobatics, and Perception checks (unpredictable weather, flooding, unstable terrain); \+2 bonus to Religion checks to interpret divine warnings about the coming judgment.  
Watcher Ruins: \+2 circumstance bonus to Occultism and Arcana checks; \-2 penalty to Religion and Will saves (strong corruption aura); risk of gaining 1 CP per hour spent there.  
Lore Skills  
Lore skills represent specialized knowledge in specific fields, cultures, or topics. In Nephilim Wars, Lore skills are crucial for navigating the complex world of lineages, forbidden knowledge, and antediluvian history. Characters gain Lore skills through backgrounds, class features, or skill increases.  
Common Lore Categories  
Lineage Lore  
Sethite Lineage Lore: Knowledge of righteous genealogies, sacred traditions, prophetic lineages, and the history of the faithful line.  
Cainite Lineage Lore: Understanding of Cainite city politics, trade networks, metallurgical innovations, and cultural practices.  
Nephilim Lineage Lore: Knowledge of giant bloodlines, Nephilim warlords, their territories, tactics, and weaknesses.  
Anakim Lineage Lore: Understanding of Anakim noble houses, chain traditions, court protocols, and internal politics.  
Rephaim Lineage Lore: Knowledge of death cults, burial practices, spirit communication, and Rephaim warbands.  
Gibborim Lineage Lore: Understanding of hybrid culture, mercenary companies, and the social dynamics between giant-kin and humans.  
Horim Lineage Lore: Knowledge of underground settlements, cave systems, stone-working traditions, and Horim clan structures.  
Sorcerer Clan Lore: Understanding of forbidden traditions, Watcher teachings, secret societies, and corruption risks.  
Setting Lore  
Watcher Teachings Lore: Specialized knowledge of specific Watcher traditions—Azazel's warfare arts, Semyaza's enchantments, Baraqel's astrology, etc.  
Nephilim Tactics Lore: Understanding of giant military strategies, siege techniques, patrol patterns, and command structures.  
Flood Prophecies Lore: Knowledge of Enoch's writings, divine warnings about the coming judgment, and signs of the approaching Deluge.  
Ancient Texts Lore: Ability to read, interpret, and preserve clay tablets, scrolls, and inscriptions from antediluvian civilizations.  
Star Reading Lore: Specialized knowledge of constellations, celestial events, and astrological interpretations taught by Baraqel.  
Root Cutting Lore: Expertise in herbalism, poisons, and plant-based remedies as taught by Semyaza (carries Corruption Risk).  
Bronze Working Lore: Deep understanding of metallurgy, alloy creation, tempering techniques, and weapon/armor crafting.  
Tribal Customs Lore: Knowledge of specific tribal traditions, rituals, leadership structures, and inter-tribal relations.  
Sacred Sites Lore: Understanding of holy places, altars, groves, and other locations of divine significance.  
Corruption Signs Lore: Ability to identify symptoms of spiritual corruption in people, places, and objects.  
Using Lore Skills  
Lore skills function like other skills but are highly specialized:  
Recall Knowledge: Make a Lore check to remember information about your specialty. DC varies by obscurity (common knowledge: DC 15, obscure: DC 20, secret: DC 25+).  
Identify Magic: Certain Lore skills (Watcher Teachings, Ancient Texts) can substitute for Arcana or Occultism when identifying related magical effects.  
Create Advantage: Use Lore knowledge to gain tactical advantages—knowing a Nephilim warlord's weakness, understanding a Cainite merchant's motivations, or recognizing signs of an approaching storm.  
Example: Kedar the Warrior has Nephilim Tactics Lore. When facing a Nephilim patrol, he makes a DC 18 check. Success reveals that this particular warlord always attacks from the high ground and is vulnerable to flank attacks. The GM grants the party a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls against this warlord.  
Soul Economy and Lore  
Righteous Lore: Using Lore skills related to righteousness (Sethite Lineage, Flood Prophecies, Sacred Sites) to aid righteous causes grants 1 RP on success.  
Corrupt Lore: Using Lore skills related to corruption (Watcher Teachings, Root Cutting, Corruption Signs) risks gaining CP. Each use requires a flat check (DC 11 \+ Lore's level). On failure, gain 1 CP.  
Forbidden Knowledge: Studying certain Lore topics (such as specific Watcher rituals) automatically grants 1 CP per session of study, regardless of check results.  
Skill Increases and Proficiency  
The Pathfinder 2e proficiency system applies normally in Nephilim Wars, with the following setting-specific considerations:  
Trained: You have basic competence in the skill, sufficient for everyday tasks in your culture. A trained Survival check can find food for yourself; a trained Religion check can perform basic prayers.  
Expert: You have notable skill, recognized within your community. An expert Crafting check can create masterwork weapons; an expert Religion check can interpret complex prophecies.  
Master: You are renowned for your skill, sought after for your expertise. A master Occultism check can safely study high-level forbidden texts (with reduced CP risk); a master Survival check can lead entire communities through wilderness.  
Legendary: You are a living legend, your skill approaching mythical status. A legendary Crafting check can create artifacts; a legendary Religion check can receive direct divine revelations.  
Skill Feats  
All standard Pathfinder 2e skill feats are available in Nephilim Wars, with the following setting-specific adaptations:  
New Skill Feats  
Watcher's Insight \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Requires Occultism trained  
When you use Occultism to study forbidden knowledge, the DC of the flat check to avoid gaining CP is reduced by 2\. Additionally, on a critical success when identifying forbidden magic, you learn a way to counter its effects.  
Righteous Resolve \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Requires Religion trained  
When you use Religion to perform a righteous ritual or interpret a divine sign, you gain 1 RP on a success (instead of only on critical success). Additionally, you can spend 1 RP to add your Religion modifier to a Will save against corruption effects.  
Giant's Bane \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Requires Survival trained  
When tracking or navigating in Nephilim territory, you ignore the \-2 circumstance penalty. Additionally, when you successfully Track a giant creature, you learn one of its vulnerabilities (such as a weak spot in its armor or a fear of certain terrain).  
Sacred Hands \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Requires Medicine trained  
When you use Medicine to Treat Wounds on a righteous creature (3+ RP), you restore additional Hit Points equal to your level. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP when Treating Wounds to also remove one condition (such as sickened or fatigued).  
Adapted Skill Feats  
Assurance works normally but cannot be used for skill checks that carry Corruption Risk (such as studying forbidden texts with Occultism).  
Dubious Knowledge can be used with Occultism or Religion checks, but using it with Occultism still carries the normal CP risk.  
Group Check is particularly valuable for Survival checks when leading groups through dangerous wilderness or for Religion checks when performing community rituals.  
Skill Challenges  
Nephilim Wars introduces formalized skill challenges for complex, multi-step tasks that require multiple skill uses and character cooperation. These are particularly useful for:  
Building a Refuge  
Crafting (DC 20): Construct shelters and fortifications  
Survival (DC 18): Find suitable location and resources  
Society (DC 16): Organize labor and resolve disputes  
Religion (DC 15): Bless the site for divine protection  
Success creates a safe haven for refugees; failure results in inadequate protection or internal conflict.  
Deciphering an Ancient Prophecy  
Religion (DC 22): Understand divine context and symbolism  
Society (DC 20): Translate archaic language and cultural references  
Occultism (DC 25): Identify corrupted interpolations or false additions (risk CP)  
Arcana (DC 18): Recognize magical seals or protections on the text  
Success reveals crucial information about the coming Flood; failure might misinterpret the prophecy or attract corrupted entities.  
Infiltrating a Nephilim Stronghold  
Stealth (DC 24): Avoid patrols and guards  
Thievery (DC 22): Pick locks and disable traps  
Deception (DC 20): Bluff past checkpoints or disguise as thralls  
Acrobatics (DC 18): Navigate narrow passages and avoid hazards  
Success allows access to the stronghold's interior; failure means capture, imprisonment, or death.  
Skill Challenge Rules:  
Each challenge requires 4 successful skill checks before 3 failures  
Different skills can be used for different steps  
Characters can Aid each other  
Critical successes count as 2 successes; critical failures count as 2 failures  
Success grants significant story benefits; failure creates complications but not necessarily total defeat
