Chapter 7: Feats  
Introduction to Feats in the Antediluvian World  
In the age before the Flood, feats represent more than mere mechanical advantages—they embody the specialized training, hard-won experience, and unique capabilities that distinguish heroes from ordinary mortals. Unlike medieval fantasy settings where feats might reflect knightly training or arcane academies, the feats of Nephilim Wars are forged in the crucible of bronze-age survival, spiritual struggle, and the constant shadow of giant oppression.  
Every feat carries the weight of consequence. A combat feat mastered through years of tribal training might mean the difference between life and death against a Nephilim raider. A skill feat developed through wilderness survival could lead refugees to safety as the waters rise. A general feat reflecting spiritual discipline might protect your soul from corruption when forbidden knowledge tempts you.  
This chapter presents feats adapted from Pathfinder 2e Core Rulebook with antediluvian flavor, plus new feats unique to the Nephilim Wars setting. Feats are organized by type (General, Skill) and level, with special attention to Soul Economy interactions, bronze-age combat applications, and setting-specific prerequisites. Class feats were covered in Chapter 4; Ancestry feats were covered in Chapter 2\.  
Important Note: Feats requiring technology, materials, or concepts that do not exist in the antediluvian world (plate armor, gunpowder, medieval feudalism) have been adapted or replaced. The GM may approve other PF2e feats on a case-by-case basis if they can be reasonably adapted to the bronze-age setting.  
General Feats  
General feats are available to any character who meets the prerequisites, regardless of class or ancestry. They represent universal capabilities developed through training, experience, or innate talent.  
Level 1 General Feats  
Alertness \[Feat 1\]  
You are always watchful for danger. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures and to Initiative checks. In the antediluvian world, this represents the constant vigilance required to survive in lands patrolled by Nephilim scouts and corrupted beasts.  
Armor Proficiency (Bronze Plate) \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites Proficiency with medium armor  
You become trained in bronze plate armor. This rare and heavy armor (AC 18, check penalty \-2, Speed penalty \-10 feet, Strength 16\) represents the pinnacle of Tubal-Cain's metallurgical legacy. Only the strongest warriors can wear it effectively, and it is typically reserved for champions facing titanic foes. Note: True steel plate armor does not exist yet; this is bronze.  
Bronze Age Combatant \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites Trained in Athletics  
You have mastered the unique combat styles of the antediluvian world. When wielding bronze weapons (sword, spear, axe, mace) or fighting unarmed, you deal an additional 1 damage on critical hits. Additionally, you gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, or Trip creatures larger than Medium. This feat represents specialized training against giant opponents.  
Diehard \[Feat 1\]  
When reduced to 0 Hit Points, you remain conscious and can continue to act until the end of your next turn. In the brutal world of Nephilim Wars, this represents the stubborn will to survive against overwhelming odds—a trait essential for those who face titanic foes.  
Quick Repair \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites Trained in Crafting  
You can make emergency repairs to damaged equipment. As a single action, you can restore an item to full Hit Points if it has the broken condition and is of common rarity or lower. For uncommon items or higher, you restore 10 Hit Points. This represents the practical necessity of maintaining weapons and tools in a world without easy replacement.  
Righteous Resolve \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites 1+ RP  
You can spend 1 RP to gain a \+2 status bonus to a Will saving throw against fear, possession, or corruption effects. This represents your spiritual fortitude bolstered by righteous living. You can use this ability once per day.  
Soul Guard \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites 0 CP  
Your pure heart provides spiritual protection. You gain a \+1 status bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. Additionally, when you would gain CP from a source other than your own actions (such as a corrupted area or possession attempt), you gain 1 fewer CP (minimum 0). This feat represents the divine protection granted to the uncorrupted.  
Toughness \[Feat 1\]  
You gain 5 additional Hit Points. In a world where a single blow from a Nephilim can shatter bones, physical resilience is not just advantageous—it's essential for survival.  
Watcher's Resistance \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites 1+ CP  
You have learned to resist the corrupting influence of forbidden knowledge. You gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to flat checks made to avoid gaining Corruption Points from using forbidden magic or studying corrupted texts. Additionally, when you gain CP from such sources, you gain 1 fewer CP than normal (minimum 0). This represents hard-won experience in handling dangerous knowledge.  
Weapon Specialization \[Feat 1\]  
Prerequisites Trained in a weapon group  
Choose a weapon group (axes, swords, spears, etc.). You deal an additional 2 damage with weapons from this group. In the antediluvian world, this represents mastery of bronze-age weaponry—knowing exactly where to strike a Nephilim's scaled hide or how to angle a spear to pierce giant armor.  
Level 5 General Feats  
Advanced Soul Guard \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Soul Guard feat, 3+ RP  
Your spiritual protection strengthens. The status bonus from Soul Guard increases to \+2. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP as a reaction when you would gain CP to reduce the amount gained by 2 (minimum 0). This represents deepening divine favor through continued righteousness.  
Armor Expertise (Bronze) \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Armor Proficiency (Bronze Plate) feat  
When wearing bronze armor (any type), you reduce the armor's check penalty by 1 and ignore the Speed penalty from bronze plate armor. This represents refined technique in moving efficiently while protected—a necessity when every second counts against giant opponents.  
Corruption Mastery \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Watcher's Resistance feat, 3+ CP  
Your resistance to corruption deepens. The circumstance bonus from Watcher's Resistance increases to \+2. Additionally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 1 once per day by spending 10 minutes in focused meditation (this does not require any specific faith—only disciplined willpower). This represents your growing ability to contain the corruption within you.  
Dreadful Mantle \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites 5+ CP  
Your corruption radiates outward, intimidating lesser beings. Enemies that are smaller than Large take a \-1 status penalty to attack rolls against you. Additionally, when you use the Demoralize action, you can target all enemies within 30 feet instead of just one. This represents the fearsome presence of a soul marked by corruption.  
Improved Righteous Resolve \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Righteous Resolve feat, 3+ RP  
The status bonus from Righteous Resolve increases to \+3. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP to gain this bonus to Fortitude or Reflex saving throws against effects that would increase your Corruption Points. This represents enhanced spiritual resilience through continued righteous living.  
Iron Will \[Feat 5\]  
You gain a \+1 status bonus to Will saving throws. In a world filled with disembodied spirits (Refa'im), demonic whispers, and possession attempts, mental fortitude is as vital as physical strength.  
Light Armor Expertise \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Trained in light armor  
When wearing light armor, you reduce the armor's check penalty by 1 and increase your Speed by 5 feet. For Wanderers, scouts, and others who rely on mobility over protection, this represents perfected movement techniques essential for survival in dangerous wilderness.  
Righteous Aura \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites 5+ RP  
You radiate divine protection. Allies within 30 feet gain a \+1 status bonus to saving throws against fear effects and possession attempts. This represents the inspiring presence of a truly righteous soul—a beacon of hope in dark times. The aura moves with you.  
Titan's Bane \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Bronze Age Combatant feat, Strength 16+  
Your training against giants reaches new heights. You deal an additional 1d6 damage against creatures larger than Medium. Additionally, when you score a critical hit against such creatures, you can attempt to Trip or Shove them as a free action. This represents specialized techniques for bringing down titanic foes.  
Watcher's Insight \[Feat 5\]  
Prerequisites Watcher's Resistance feat, Occultism trained  
When you use Occultism to study forbidden knowledge, the DC of the flat check to avoid gaining CP is reduced by 2\. Additionally, on a critical success when identifying forbidden magic, you learn a way to counter its effects. This represents deep understanding of corrupted arts that allows safer navigation of dangerous knowledge.  
Level 9 General Feats  
Advanced Corruption Mastery \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Corruption Mastery feat, 5+ CP  
The circumstance bonus from Corruption Mastery increases to \+3. Additionally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 2 once per day through meditation. You also gain the ability to sense the Corruption Points of other creatures within 30 feet—you can determine whether a creature has 0, 1–2, 3–5, 6–10, or 11+ CP. This represents near-mastery over your own corruption and heightened perception of spiritual decay in others.  
Armor Mastery (Bronze) \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Armor Expertise (Bronze) feat  
When wearing bronze armor, you ignore all check penalties and Speed penalties. Additionally, you gain resistance 2 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. This represents perfect integration with your armor—a state where protection becomes second nature.  
Divine Shield \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Righteous Aura feat, 7+ RP  
Your righteous aura strengthens into tangible protection. Allies within 30 feet gain resistance 5 to negative damage and a \+1 status bonus to AC against attacks from corrupted beings (creatures with 3+ CP). This represents divine energy actively shielding the righteous from corruption's effects.  
Greater Soul Guard \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Advanced Soul Guard feat, 5+ RP  
The status bonus from Soul Guard increases to \+3. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP as a reaction when you would gain CP to reduce the amount gained by 3 (minimum 0). Finally, you are immune to gaining CP from environmental sources (corrupted areas, ambient spiritual decay). This represents near-perfect divine protection for the steadfastly righteous.  
Improved Dreadful Mantle \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Dreadful Mantle feat, 7+ CP  
The status penalty from Dreadful Mantle increases to \-2. Additionally, enemies within 30 feet that are smaller than Large take a \-1 status penalty to saving throws against your fear effects. This represents the overwhelming aura of corruption that surrounds deeply fallen souls.  
Legendary Righteous Resolve \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Improved Righteous Resolve feat, 7+ RP  
The status bonus from Righteous Resolve increases to \+4. Additionally, when you succeed at a saving throw against a corruption effect or possession attempt, you can spend 1 RP to turn the success into a critical success. This represents spiritual fortitude so strong that even partial resistance becomes complete immunity.  
Master of Giants \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Titan's Bane feat  
The additional damage from Titan's Bane increases to 2d6. Additionally, when you reduce a creature larger than Medium to 0 Hit Points, you can immediately make a melee Strike against another creature within your reach as a free action. This represents perfected giant-slaying techniques that allow continuous assault on titanic foes.  
Perfect Watcher's Insight \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Watcher's Insight feat, 5+ CP  
The DC reduction from Watcher's Insight increases to 4\. Additionally, you can study forbidden knowledge without making the flat check to avoid gaining CP, though you still gain 1 CP per session of study (reduced from the normal risk). This represents such deep understanding of corrupted arts that you can navigate them with calculated precision rather than blind risk.  
Righteous Judgment \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Righteous Aura feat, 7+ RP  
Requirements You have 3+ RP  
You can spend 3 RP as a single action to mark a creature within 60 feet as anathema to the righteous. For 1 minute, all attacks against the marked creature deal an additional 1d6 radiant damage, and the creature takes a \-2 status penalty to saving throws against divine spells and effects. This represents divine judgment focused through your righteous will.  
Unbreakable Will \[Feat 9\]  
Prerequisites Iron Will feat  
You gain a \+2 status bonus to Will saving throws. Additionally, you are immune to the frightened and stupefied conditions. In a world where Nephilim radiate supernatural terror and corrupted beings wield fear as a weapon, this mental fortitude is invaluable.  
Level 13 General Feats  
Corruption Lord \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Advanced Corruption Mastery feat, 10+ CP  
The circumstance bonus from Corruption Mastery increases to \+4. Additionally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 3 once per day through meditation. You can also sense the exact number of CP possessed by any creature within 60 feet. Finally, you can attempt to drain CP from others—once per day, target a creature within 30 feet and attempt a spell attack roll (DC \= 10 \+ your level \+ your Intelligence or Charisma modifier). On a success, the target loses 1d4 CP and you gain half that amount (rounded down). This represents mastery over corruption to the point of wielding it as a weapon.  
Divine Avatar \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Divine Shield feat, 10+ RP  
Your connection to the divine becomes palpable. Allies within 30 feet gain resistance 10 to negative damage and a \+2 status bonus to AC against attacks from corrupted beings. Additionally, you can spend 2 RP as a single action to grant one ally within 30 feet the benefits of the Bless spell for 1 minute. This represents your role as a living conduit of divine protection.  
Indomitable Soul \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Greater Soul Guard feat, 10+ RP  
The status bonus from Soul Guard increases to \+4. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP as a reaction when you would gain CP to reduce the amount gained by 4 (minimum 0). You are immune to gaining CP from any source except your own voluntary actions (such as casting forbidden spells). This represents a soul so pure that corruption cannot touch it except by conscious choice.  
Master Dreadful Mantle \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Improved Dreadful Mantle feat, 10+ CP  
The status penalties from Dreadful Mantle increase to \-3 to attack rolls and \-2 to saving throws. Additionally, when an enemy within 30 feet rolls a natural 1 on an attack roll against you, that enemy becomes frightened 2\. This represents the terrifying presence of a soul so corrupted that its mere proximity induces dread.  
Righteous Champion \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Righteous Judgment feat, 10+ RP  
Requirements You have 5+ RP  
You can spend 5 RP as a single action to mark a creature within 60 feet. For 1 minute, all attacks against the marked creature deal an additional 2d6 radiant damage, and the creature is sickened 1\. Additionally, you and all allies within 30 feet gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls against the marked creature. This represents focused divine judgment that empowers all righteous warriors.  
Titan Slayer \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Master of Giants feat  
The additional damage from Titan's Bane increases to 3d6. Additionally, when you hit a creature larger than Medium with a melee weapon attack, you can spend 1 RP to deal an additional 4d6 damage of the weapon's type. You can use this ability once per round. This represents perfected techniques for delivering killing blows to the mightiest giants.  
Watcher King \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Perfect Watcher's Insight feat, 10+ CP  
You can study forbidden knowledge without gaining CP from the flat check risk. However, you still gain 1 CP per hour spent studying corrupted texts or practicing forbidden arts. Additionally, you can teach forbidden knowledge to others—any character who studies under you for at least 1 month gains the Sorcerer Clan ancestry and the Forbidden Lore feat. This represents mastery over corruption to the point of becoming a source of forbidden power for others.  
Unbreakable \[Feat 13\]  
Prerequisites Unbreakable Will feat, Constitution 18+  
You gain resistance 5 to all damage except force. Additionally, you are immune to the clumsy, enfeebled, and slowed conditions. This represents physical and mental resilience so profound that conventional hindrances cannot affect you—a necessity when facing opponents who can shatter stone with their bare hands.  
Level 17 General Feats  
Corruption God \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Corruption Lord feat, 15+ CP  
The circumstance bonus from Corruption Mastery increases to \+6. Additionally, you can reduce your Corruption Points by 5 once per day through meditation. You can sense the exact CP of any creature within 120 feet. Finally, you can drain CP from others three times per day instead of once, and the amount drained increases to 2d4 CP. This represents apotheosis through corruption—a state where you wield spiritual decay as a god wields lightning.  
Divine Incarnation \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Divine Avatar feat, 15+ RP  
Your divine connection reaches its peak. Allies within 60 feet gain resistance 15 to negative damage and a \+3 status bonus to AC against attacks from corrupted beings. Additionally, you can spend 3 RP as a single action to grant one ally within 60 feet the benefits of the Bless spell for 10 minutes. Finally, you can use your Righteous Judgment ability twice per day instead of once. This represents becoming a living embodiment of divine protection and judgment.  
Immortal Soul \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Indomitable Soul feat, 15+ RP  
The status bonus from Soul Guard increases to \+6. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP as a reaction when you would gain CP to reduce the amount gained by 6 (minimum 0). You are completely immune to gaining CP from any source except your own voluntary actions, and even then, you gain half the normal amount (rounded down). This represents a soul so pure and divinely protected that corruption cannot touch it—a state approaching angelic purity.  
Righteous Deity \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Righteous Champion feat, 15+ RP  
Requirements You have 7+ RP  
You can spend 7 RP as a single action to mark a creature within 120 feet. For 10 minutes, all attacks against the marked creature deal an additional 3d6 radiant damage, and the creature is sickened 2\. Additionally, you and all allies within 60 feet gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls against the marked creature, and the creature takes a \-2 status penalty to saving throws against your divine spells and effects. This represents the power of divine judgment focused through a nearly perfected righteous soul.  
Titan God \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Titan Slayer feat  
The additional damage from Titan's Bane increases to 4d6. Additionally, when you hit a creature larger than Medium with a melee weapon attack, you can spend 1 RP to deal an additional 6d6 damage of the weapon's type. You can use this ability twice per round. Finally, creatures larger than Medium take a \-2 status penalty to attack rolls against you. This represents mastery over giant-slaying to the point of becoming a mythic hero whose name strikes fear into the hearts of titans.  
Watcher Deity \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Watcher King feat, 15+ CP  
You can study forbidden knowledge without gaining any CP. Additionally, you can create new spells based on forbidden arts—once per week, spend 8 hours in research to create a new spell of up to 8th level. The GM determines exact effects based on your concept. Finally, you can establish a Sorcerer Clan enclave—any settlement where you reside for at least 1 year becomes a center of forbidden learning. This represents transcendence through forbidden knowledge—a state where you rival the Watchers themselves in power over corrupted arts.  
Unstoppable \[Feat 17\]  
Prerequisites Unbreakable feat  
You gain resistance 10 to all damage except force. Additionally, you are immune to all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious. When you would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, you can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to your level. You can use this ability once per day. This represents resilience so profound that you cannot be hindered, stopped, or destroyed by conventional means—a necessity when standing against the mightiest Nephilim warlords.  
Skill Feats  
Skill feats enhance your capabilities with specific skills. They represent specialized training, natural talent, or hard-won experience in particular areas of expertise. All standard PF2e skill feats are available in Nephilim Wars with the following setting-specific adaptations and additions.  
Acrobatics Skill Feats  
Acrobatic Strike \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Acrobatics trained  
When you Tumble Through an enemy's space, you can make a melee Strike against that enemy as part of the same action. In antediluvian combat, this represents the specialized technique of darting between a giant's legs to strike vulnerable spots before they can react.  
Giant's Bane Acrobatics \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Acrobatics expert, Bronze Age Combatant feat  
When you use Acrobatics to avoid being trampled by a creature larger than Medium, you can attempt to climb onto the creature as part of the same action. If you succeed at an Athletics check (DC \= 15 \+ the creature's level), you gain the grabbed condition but can make melee Strikes against the creature with a \+2 status bonus (representing attacks against vulnerable areas like eyes or neck). This represents the desperate but effective tactic of swarming giants.  
Sure Feet \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Acrobatics trained  
You rarely slip or fall. You automatically succeed at Acrobatics checks to Balance on narrow surfaces or uneven terrain. In a world of muddy trails, rocky canyons, and unstable ruins, this represents perfected balance essential for survival.  
Crafting Skill Feats  
Incredible Crafter \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Crafting expert  
You can Craft items of up to rare rarity without needing a formula. Additionally, the time required to Craft items is reduced by 50%. This represents mastery of Tubal-Cain's legacy—the ability to create exceptional weapons and tools with astonishing speed and precision.  
Master Armorer \[Skill Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites Crafting master, Bronze Working Lore  
You can Craft bronze armor of up to legendary rarity without needing a formula. Items you Craft have their Hardness increased by 4 and Hit Points increased by 20\. Additionally, you can add special properties to armor: once per day, spend 1 hour crafting to grant a suit of bronze armor one of the following properties for 24 hours:  
The armor grants resistance 2 to one damage type (bludgeoning, piercing, or slashing)  
The armor has the Fortification property (50% chance to negate critical hits)  
The armor allows the wearer to ignore difficult terrain caused by natural features  
This represents the pinnacle of antediluvian metallurgy—armor that can withstand titanic blows.  
Trap Finder \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Crafting trained  
You can detect mechanical traps with a Crafting check instead of Perception. Additionally, you gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Disable Devices. In a world where Nephilim fortresses and Cainite cities employ sophisticated traps, this represents specialized knowledge of mechanical security systems.  
Medicine Skill Feats  
Battle Medicine \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Medicine trained  
When you use the Treat Wounds action, you can do so as a single action instead of a three-action activity. In the brutal combat of the antediluvian world, where every second counts and allies can be crushed by giant blows, this represents emergency field medicine techniques essential for survival.  
Field Surgeon \[Skill Feat 6\]  
Prerequisites Medicine expert, Battle Medicine feat  
When you use Treat Wounds, you can also remove one of the following conditions from the target: clumsy, enfeebled, or sickened. Additionally, the Hit Points restored increase by an amount equal to your level. This represents advanced medical knowledge that can address both wounds and their debilitating effects—a rare skill in an age before modern medicine.  
Sacred Hands \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Medicine trained, 3+ RP  
When you use Medicine to Treat Wounds on a creature with 3+ RP, you restore additional Hit Points equal to your level. Additionally, you can spend 1 RP when Treating Wounds to also remove one condition (such as sickened or fatigued). This represents healing power enhanced by divine favor—a blessing granted to those who use their medical skills in service of righteousness.  
Occultism Skill Feats  
Forbidden Insight \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Occultism expert, Watcher's Resistance feat  
When you use Occultism to study forbidden knowledge, the DC of the flat check to avoid gaining CP is reduced by 4\. Additionally, on a critical success when identifying forbidden magic, you learn a way to counter its effects and can attempt to counteract it as if you had cast Dispel Magic. This represents deep understanding of corrupted arts that allows not just safer study but active resistance.  
Watcher's Disciple \[Skill Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites Occultism master, Forbidden Insight feat, 5+ CP  
You can study forbidden knowledge without making the flat check to avoid gaining CP. However, you still gain 1 CP per hour spent studying corrupted texts. Additionally, you can identify the specific Watcher who taught a particular forbidden art or practice with a successful check. This represents such deep immersion in corrupted knowledge that you understand its origins and structures intimately.  
Religion Skill Feats  
Divine Insight \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Religion expert, 3+ RP  
When you use Religion to identify divine effects or perform rituals, you gain 1 RP on a success (instead of only on critical success). Additionally, you can spend 1 RP to add your Religion modifier to a Will save against corruption effects. This represents enhanced spiritual sensitivity that strengthens your connection to the divine.  
Prophetic Understanding \[Skill Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites Religion master, Divine Insight feat, 5+ RP  
You can interpret prophecies and divine warnings with exceptional clarity. When you succeed at a Religion check to understand a prophecy about the coming Flood or other divine judgment, you gain 2 RP. On a critical success, you gain 3 RP and receive a specific, actionable warning about an upcoming danger. This represents the gift of prophetic insight granted to those who diligently study divine truths.  
Righteous Intercession \[Skill Feat 6\]  
Prerequisites Religion expert, 5+ RP  
Once per day, when an ally within 30 feet fails a saving throw against a corruption effect or possession attempt, you can use your reaction to grant them a \+2 status bonus to the save. If they succeed, they gain 1 RP. This represents your ability to intercede spiritually on behalf of others—a manifestation of righteous community.  
Stealth Skill Feats  
Giant's Shadow \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Stealth expert  
When hiding in areas with large structures (Nephilim fortresses, Cainite cities, natural canyons), you gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks. Additionally, you can Hide or Sneak even when you have cover or concealment from only part of your body (such as hiding behind a giant's discarded weapon or a massive stone block). This represents specialized training in using the oversized environment of the antediluvian world for concealment.  
Swift Sneak \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Stealth trained  
When you Sneak, you don't need to move at half your Speed. This represents perfected movement techniques essential for scouts and spies who must move quickly while avoiding detection by Nephilim patrols.  
Survival Skill Feats  
Hardy Survivalist \[Skill Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Survival expert  
You can subsist in environments that would normally be inhospitable (flood plains, scorched earth, corrupted lands) without taking the normal penalty for not being trained in Survival. Additionally, you need only half the normal amount of food and water to avoid the effects of starvation or dehydration. This represents exceptional wilderness survival skills honed in the increasingly hostile antediluvian world.  
Pathfinder \[Skill Feat 2\]  
Prerequisites Survival trained  
You can find a safe path through even the most treacherous wilderness. This functions as the Guidance spell, but it applies to Survival checks to Navigate and to avoid environmental hazards. The bonus lasts for 1 hour. In a world where getting lost could mean encountering Nephilim patrols or corrupted beasts, this represents essential navigation skills.  
Weather Watcher \[Skill Feat 6\]  
Prerequisites Survival master  
You can predict weather patterns with exceptional accuracy. As a single action, you can attempt a Survival check (DC 15\) to predict weather conditions for the next 24 hours. On a success, you learn about approaching storms, temperature changes, or other significant weather events. On a critical success, you also learn the best ways to prepare for or avoid the predicted conditions. This represents deep understanding of natural signs increasingly important as environmental chaos grows before the Flood.  
New Feats Specific to Nephilim Wars  
These feats are unique to the Nephilim Wars setting and have no direct equivalent in standard PF2e.  
Soul Economy Feats  
Corruption Channel \[Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites 3+ CP  
You can temporarily harness your corruption for power. As a single action, you can gain 2 CP to gain 1 Focus Point. You can use this ability once per day. This represents deliberately embracing corruption to fuel abilities—a dangerous but sometimes necessary choice. Warning: This feat accelerates your path toward damnation.  
Righteous Sacrifice \[Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites 3+ RP  
You can sacrifice your own righteousness to protect others. As a reaction when an ally within 30 feet would gain CP, you can gain that CP instead. Additionally, you can spend 2 RP as a single action to remove 1 CP from an ally within 30 feet. This represents the selfless choice to bear spiritual burden for others—a hallmark of true righteousness.  
Soul Balance \[Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites 5+ RP and 5+ CP  
You have learned to balance righteousness and corruption within your soul. You gain a \+1 status bonus to all saving throws. Additionally, when you would gain CP, you can spend 1 RP to prevent gaining that CP. Conversely, when you would gain RP, you can spend 1 CP to prevent gaining that RP. This represents the precarious equilibrium of a soul walking the razor's edge between salvation and damnation.  
Spiritual Transference \[Feat 12\]  
Prerequisites Soul Balance feat, 7+ RP and 7+ CP  
Your mastery over your spiritual state allows transfer between states. Once per day, you can spend 10 minutes in meditation to convert 2 CP into 1 RP, or 2 RP into 1 CP. This represents profound spiritual discipline—the ability to consciously reshape your soul's alignment through focused will. Note: This does not change your fundamental nature, only your current spiritual state.  
Bronze-Age Combat Feats  
Bronze Disarm \[Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Bronze Age Combatant feat  
When you hit a creature with a bronze weapon, you can attempt to Disarm them as a free action. The target must be wielding a weapon of bronze or lesser material (stone, wood, bone). This represents specialized techniques for knocking weapons from opponents' hands—a crucial tactic when facing armed Nephilim thralls or human enemies.  
Chain Fighter \[Feat 6\]  
Prerequisites Anakim ancestry or Chain Master feat  
You have mastered the unique combat style of the Anakim nobility. When wielding a chain weapon with the reach trait, you can use it to Grapple creatures up to 15 feet away. Additionally, when you successfully Grapple a creature with a chain weapon, you can deal 1d6 bludgeoning damage to the creature at the start of each of its turns while grappled. This represents the distinctive fighting style of the "Long-Necked Ones."  
Giant's Weak Spot \[Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites Titan's Bane feat, Perception expert  
You can identify vulnerabilities in giant physiology. As a single action, you can attempt a Perception check (DC \= 15 \+ the target's level) to study a creature larger than Medium. On a success, your attacks against that creature ignore the first 5 points of its resistance to physical damage for 1 minute. On a critical success, this increases to 10 points of resistance ignored. This represents specialized knowledge of where to strike titanic foes for maximum effect.  
Shield Wall \[Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Trained in shields  
You can form a defensive formation with allies. When you and at least two allies are adjacent and all wielding shields, you each gain a \+1 status bonus to AC. If you have the Raise a Shield action available, you can use it as a reaction when an ally in the formation is hit by an attack, granting them the shield's AC bonus against that attack. This represents the coordinated defensive tactics essential for human warriors facing giant opponents.  
Wilderness and Survival Feats  
Flood Survivor \[Feat 6\]  
Prerequisites Survival expert, Swim trained  
You have learned to survive in increasingly flooded environments. You gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Swim and to Survival checks to navigate flood plains. Additionally, you can hold your breath for a number of rounds equal to twice your Constitution modifier (minimum 2\) before needing to attempt checks to avoid drowning. This represents adaptation to the environmental changes preceding the Great Flood.  
Giant Track \[Feat 4\]  
Prerequisites Survival trained, Nature trained  
You can track creatures larger than Medium with exceptional skill. When Tracking a giant creature, you gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to your Survival check. Additionally, you can determine the creature's size category, approximate Hit Points remaining, and whether it is injured or healthy from its tracks. This represents specialized knowledge essential for hunters and scouts who must avoid or prepare for encounters with titanic foes.  
Sacred Grove \[Feat 8\]  
Prerequisites Nature master, 5+ RP  
You can identify and utilize places of divine blessing in the wilderness. As a 10-minute activity, you can designate a 20-foot burst area as a sacred grove. For 1 hour, the area provides the following benefits:  
Allies within the area gain a \+1 status bonus to saving throws against fear effects and corruption  
The area cannot be entered by undead creatures or corrupted beings with 5+ CP  
Plants within the area provide fresh water and edible fruits sufficient to feed up to six Medium creatures for the duration  
This represents your ability to find and activate places where the Creator's presence still lingers strongly—a rare sanctuary in a corrupted world.  
Feat Progression and Integration  
Standard Feat Acquisition  
Characters gain feats according to the standard Pathfinder 2e progression:  
Level 1: 1 General Feat  
Level 2: 1 Class Feat (covered in Chapter 4\)  
Level 3: 1 General Feat  
Level 4: 1 Skill Feat  
Level 5: 1 General Feat  
Level 6: 1 Class Feat  
Level 7: 1 General Feat  
Level 8: 1 Skill Feat  
Level 9: 1 General Feat  
Level 10: 1 Class Feat  
(Continues every level)  
Soul Economy and Feat Selection  
Your Net Righteousness Score influences which feats are available or beneficial:  
Blessed Tier (Net \+5+): Feats with RP prerequisites become easier to qualify for. Divine-themed feats provide enhanced benefits.  
Righteous Tier (Net \+1 to \+4): Standard feat availability and benefits.  
Balanced Tier (Net 0): Standard feat availability and benefits.  
Corrupted Tier (Net \-1 to \-4): Feats with CP prerequisites become easier to qualify for. Corruption-themed feats provide enhanced benefits.  
Condemned Tier (Net \-5+): Some righteous feats become unavailable or provide reduced benefits. Corruption-themed feats provide maximum benefits.  
Recommended Feat Paths by Role  
Giant Slayer (Warrior/Gibbor):  
Level 1: Bronze Age Combatant  
Level 3: Weapon Specialization (spears or axes)  
Level 5: Titan's Bane  
Level 7: Shield Wall  
Level 9: Master of Giants  
Level 11: Armor Mastery (Bronze)  
Level 13: Titan Slayer  
Level 15: Improved Critical (weapons)  
Level 17: Titan God  
Righteous Protector (Priest/Scribe):  
Level 1: Soul Guard  
Level 3: Righteous Resolve  
Level 5: Righteous Aura  
Level 7: Divine Shield  
Level 9: Greater Soul Guard  
Level 11: Righteous Judgment  
Level 13: Divine Avatar  
Level 15: Righteous Champion  
Level 17: Divine Incarnation  
Corrupted Scholar (Magi/Sorcerer Clan):  
Level 1: Watcher's Resistance  
Level 3: Occultism skill feats  
Level 5: Corruption Mastery  
Level 7: Watcher's Insight  
Level 9: Advanced Corruption Mastery  
Level 11: Perfect Watcher's Insight  
Level 13: Corruption Lord  
Level 15: Watcher King  
Level 17: Watcher Deity  
Wilderness Survivor (Hunter/Wanderer):  
Level 1: Pathfinder  
Level 3: Hardy Survivalist  
Level 5: Giant Track  
Level 7: Weather Watcher  
Level 9: Flood Survivor  
Level 11: Swift Sneak  
Level 13: Giant's Shadow  
Level 15: Sure Feet  
Level 17: Master Armorer (for crafting survival gear)  
Feat Restrictions  
Certain feats are restricted based on ancestry, class, or Soul Economy status:  
Divine feats (requiring RP) cannot be selected by characters with Net Righteousness below \-Corruption feats (requiring CP) cannot be selected by characters with Net Righteousness above \+Giant-specific feats (Titan's Bane, Giant's Weak Spot) are most effective when used against creatures larger than Medium  
Bronze Age Combatant and related feats only apply to bronze weapons and armor (not stone, wood, or iron)  
Creating New Feats  
GMs and players may create new feats following these guidelines:  
Setting Appropriateness: Feats must fit the bronze-age aesthetic and antediluvian themes  
Soul Economy Integration: Consider how the feat interacts with RP/CP  
Balance: Compare to existing feats of the same level  
Prerequisites: Ensure prerequisites are achievable and meaningful  
Flavor Text: Include antediluvian-themed description explaining the feat's origin and application  
Example custom feat:  
Ancestral Memory \[Feat 6\]  
Gibbor, Elioud  
Prerequisites Gibbor or Elioud ancestry  
You can temporarily channel the strength of your giant ancestors. Once per day, as a single action, you gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and damage rolls with Strength-based attacks for 1 minute. Additionally, you ignore the effects of difficult terrain caused by natural features. This represents the awakening of bloodline memories in moments of crisis.  
Chapter 8: Equipment  
Introduction to Antediluvian Equipment  
In the age before the Flood, equipment is not merely a collection of items—it is the tangible manifestation of humanity's struggle for survival against titanic foes, the legacy of forbidden knowledge taught by fallen Watchers, and the practical wisdom preserved by the righteous line. Unlike medieval fantasy settings where plate armor, steel weapons, and complex machinery are commonplace, the equipment of Nephilim Wars reflects the bronze-age reality of Genesis 4:22: "Tubal-Cain forged all kinds of tools out of bronze and iron."  
This chapter presents a complete arsenal of setting-appropriate equipment, from the bronze swords wielded by tribal warriors to the heavy chains worn by Anakim nobles. Every item has been carefully adapted to fit the antediluvian aesthetic, with historical accuracy balanced against gameplay functionality. Iron exists but is exceptionally rare and sacred—associated with Tubal-Cain's innovations but not yet widespread. Steel does not exist at all. Plate armor is primitive and uncommon, reserved for champions facing titanic foes. Most equipment is crafted from bronze, stone, wood, leather, bone, and other materials available in the pre-Flood world.  
Equipment in Nephilim Wars carries spiritual weight as well as practical utility. Weapons forged through righteous rituals may deal additional damage to corrupted beings. Armor blessed by priests might provide protection against spiritual attacks. Items created using forbidden Watcher knowledge could grant power at the cost of Corruption Points. The Soul Economy integrates with equipment through special properties, usage requirements, and crafting processes.  
This chapter covers weapons, armor, shields, tools, adventuring gear, and special setting-specific items. Pricing is provided in gold pieces (gp), though barter is the primary economic system in most communities. Trade goods values are included where appropriate. All equipment follows Pathfinder 2e rules with antediluvian adaptations.  
Weapons  
Weapon Categories  
Simple Weapons: Basic weapons that require minimal training—clubs, daggers, slings, and spears. These are the tools of hunters, farmers, and tribal militia.  
Martial Weapons: Weapons requiring specialized training—bronze swords, axes, maces, and war spears. These are wielded by professional warriors, tribal champions, and elite guards.  
Exotic Weapons: Unusual weapons requiring extensive training or specific cultural knowledge—Anakim chain weapons, Horim stone-bladed weapons, and Watcher-forged artifacts. These are rare and often tied to specific ancestries or traditions.  
Siege Weapons: Large weapons designed to breach fortifications or combat giants—battering rams, catapults, and ballistae. These are crew-operated and typically found in Cainite cities or Nephilim fortresses.

Armor  
Armor Categories  
Light Armor: Flexible protection that prioritizes mobility over defense—leather, hide, and padded garments. Favored by scouts, hunters, and those who rely on speed.  
Medium Armor: Balanced protection offering reasonable defense without sacrificing too much mobility—bronze scale, lamellar, and segmented armor. Used by most professional warriors.  
Heavy Armor: Maximum protection at the cost of mobility and stealth—bronze plate and reinforced scale. Reserved for champions facing titanic foes or guarding critical positions.  
Special Note: True plate armor as known in medieval times does not exist. "Bronze Plate" is primitive, consisting of large bronze plates sewn onto leather or fabric backing. It is heavy, expensive, and rare.

Special Armor Properties  
Blessed Armor (Rare): Armor blessed through righteous rituals. Grants resistance 2 to negative damage and a \+1 item bonus to saving throws against fear effects. Requires 3+ RP to attune. Crafting requires 1 RP per day of work.  
Corrupted Armor (Rare): Armor forged using forbidden Watcher knowledge. Grants resistance 2 to physical damage but imposes a \-1 item penalty to saving throws against corruption effects. Requires 3+ CP to attune. Crafting requires 1 CP per day of work.  
Giant-Forged Armor (Very Rare): Armor crafted by Nephilim smiths for their elite warriors. AC increases by 1, and the armor ignores difficult terrain caused by natural features. Requires Large size or Giant's Grip feat to wear. Only available through capture or special commission.  
Sacred Sethite Armor (Legendary): Armor crafted by Sethite elders using preserved divine knowledge. AC increases by 2, grants resistance 5 to negative damage, and radiates a 10-foot aura that imposes a \-1 status penalty on corrupted beings (3+ CP). Requires 5+ RP to attune. Only available through righteous quests or divine intervention.

Special Items  
Setting-Specific Magic Items  
Chains of the Anakim (Rare)  
Price: 100 gp  
These heavy bronze chains are worn across the shoulders and chest by Anakim nobles. When wielded as a weapon (Anakim Chain stats), they deal an additional 1d6 bludgeoning damage on critical hits. Additionally, once per day, the wearer can use the chains to Grapple a creature up to 15 feet away as a single action. The chains also serve as a status symbol—when dealing with Anakim or other giant-kin, the wearer gains a \+2 item bonus to Diplomacy and Intimidation checks.  
Watcher-Carved Staff (Uncommon)  
Price: 75 gp  
This staff is carved with forbidden celestial symbols that glow faintly in darkness. It functions as a magical weapon (1d8 force damage, Reach trait). Additionally, once per day, the wielder can cast Detect Magic as a divine innate spell. However, each use requires a DC 15 flat check. On a failure, the wielder gains 1 Corruption Point. The staff resonates with forbidden knowledge—when within 30 feet of corrupted beings or places, it vibrates subtly.  
Enochian Scrolls (Very Rare)  
Price: 250 gp  
These ancient parchment scrolls contain prophecies and warnings recorded by Enoch before his translation. When unrolled and read aloud (10-minute activity), they grant all listeners a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against fear effects and corruption for 1 hour. Additionally, once per week, the reader can attempt a DC 20 Religion check to receive a vague but helpful vision of an upcoming danger. The scrolls are sacred objects—destroying them or using them for corrupt purposes automatically grants 3 Corruption Points to the perpetrator.  
Star Charts of Baraqel (Rare)  
Price: 120 gp  
These detailed celestial maps chart the movements of stars and planets according to Baraqel's forbidden teachings. When used for navigation or astrology (10-minute activity), they grant a \+2 item bonus to Survival checks to Navigate or Occultism checks to predict celestial events. Additionally, once per day, the user can cast Guidance as a divine innate spell. However, each use requires a DC 13 flat check. On a failure, the user gains 1 Corruption Point. The charts are highly sought after by Sorcerer Clans and feared by righteous communities.  
Tears of the Righteous (Rare)  
Price: 80 gp  
These small clay vials contain water blessed by Sethite elders through prayer and sacrifice. When sprinkled on a creature (single action), the water deals 2d6 positive damage to undead creatures or corrupted beings (those with 5+ CP). Against other creatures, it restores 1d8 Hit Points. The vials are fragile—each contains 3 uses before shattering. Creating Tears of the Righteous requires 1 Righteousness Point per vial and a day of prayerful preparation.  
Blood of Azazel (Very Rare)  
Price: 200 gp  
This small bronze flask contains a dark, viscous liquid said to be distilled from Azazel's teachings on metallurgy and warfare. When consumed (single action), the drinker gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls for 1 minute. However, after the duration ends, the drinker gains 2 Corruption Points and is sickened 1 for 1 hour. The flask contains 3 doses. Using this item is considered a corrupt act—righteous NPCs will refuse to associate with those known to use it.  
Horn of the Wanderer (Uncommon)  
Price: 60 gp  
This ram's horn, wrapped in leather and bronze, is used by Wanderer tribes to signal across vast distances. When blown (single action), it can be heard up to 1 mile away. Additionally, once per day, the blower can attempt a DC 15 Performance check. On a success, all allies within 120 feet who can hear the horn gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws for 1 minute. The horn is a symbol of freedom—when dealing with Wanderer tribes, the bearer gains a \+2 item bonus to Diplomacy checks.  
Seal of Cain (Legendary)  
Price: 500 gp  
This ancient bronze medallion bears the mark placed upon Cain by the Creator. When worn visibly, it grants the wearer immunity to attacks from creatures that would normally gain vengeance for killing them (reflecting Genesis 4:15: "Therefore whosoever slayeth Cain, vengeance shall be taken on him sevenfold"). Additionally, once per week, when the wearer is reduced to 0 Hit Points, they are restored to 1 Hit Point and the attacker takes 3d6 divine damage. However, the seal carries a heavy spiritual burden—while wearing it, the wearer cannot gain Righteousness Points and gains 1 Corruption Point per day. Only those with 10+ CP can attune to the seal without suffering physical illness.

Livestock (ox)  
25 gp  
Used for plowing and transport.  
Grain (1 bushel)  
1 sp  
Staple food source.  
Wine (clay jar)  
2 gp  
Fermented grape juice. Common trade item.  
Incense  
5 gp  
Used in religious ceremonies.  
Dyes (set)  
15 gp  
Rare colors for fine clothing.  
Salt  
1 gp  
Essential for preservation.  
Timber (log)  
3 gp  
Building material.  
Crafting and Repair Rules  
Crafting Basics  
Creating items requires appropriate tools, raw materials, and time. The standard Crafting rules from Pathfinder 2e apply with the following antediluvian adaptations:  
Material Availability:  
Common materials (wood, leather, stone, bone): Available in most settlements. Cost equals item price.  
Bronze: Available in Cainite cities and near mining communities. Costs 50% more than standard material costs.  
Iron: Extremely rare. Only available through special quests, Nephilim caches, or Tubal-Cain's descendants. Costs 500% more than standard material costs.  
Special materials (obsidian, crystal, sacred wood): Location-dependent. May require quests to obtain.  
Crafting Time:  
Simple items (tools, basic weapons): 1 day per 2 gp of item price  
Complex items (armor, quality weapons): 1 day per gp of item price  
Masterwork items: Double normal time  
Artifacts: Weeks or months of work  
Crafting Checks:  
Standard DC equals 10 \+ item's level  
Critical success: Item gains additional property or reduced crafting time  
Failure: Materials wasted, time lost  
Critical failure: Materials wasted, time lost, and crafter gains 1 CP (if crafting corrupted item) or loses 1 RP (if crafting sacred item)  
Special Crafting Rules  
Righteous Crafting:  
When crafting items intended for righteous purposes (weapons for Sethite defenders, healing supplies for refugees), the crafter can spend 1 RP per day of work to gain a \+2 status bonus to Crafting checks. Items created this way are blessed and may have enhanced properties against corrupted foes.  
Corrupted Crafting:  
When crafting items using forbidden Watcher knowledge (Azazel's metallurgy, Semyaza's enchantments), the crafter must make a flat check (DC 11 \+ item's level) each day. On a failure, they gain 1 CP. Items created this way are corrupted and may have enhanced properties but carry spiritual risks.  
Giant-Forged Items:  
Items crafted by or for giants require special techniques. Characters without Large size or appropriate feats take a \-4 penalty to Crafting checks for giant-sized items. Giant-forged weapons deal additional damage against Large or larger creatures.  
Repairing Items:  
Broken condition: Repair requires successful Crafting check (DC \= 15 \+ item's level)  
Destroyed items: Cannot be repaired; must be replaced  
Sacred items: Repairing blessed items requires 1 RP per repair session  
Corrupted items: Repairing corrupted items requires 1 CP per repair session  
Sample Crafting Projects  
Bronze Longsword (Level 1, 15 gp):  
Materials: 7.5 gp worth of bronze and wood  
Time: 15 days  
DC: Special: Requires blacksmith's tools  
Bronze Scale Armor (Level 2, 25 gp):  
Materials: 12.5 gp worth of bronze and leather  
Time: 25 days  
DC: Special: Requires armorer's tools  
Enochian Scrolls (Level 5, 250 gp):  
Materials: 125 gp worth of parchment, ink, and sacred components  
Time: 250 days (can be reduced with assistance)  
DC: Special: Requires 5 RP to begin crafting; crafter must succeed at DC 20 Religion check to inscribe prophecies correctly  
Chains of the Anakim (Level 4, 100 gp):  
Materials: 50 gp worth of bronze and leather  
Time: 100 days  
DC: Special: Requires blacksmith's tools and Anakim heritage or Chain Master feat; crafter must succeed at DC 18 Crafting check to create functional chain weapon  
Trade and Barter System  
Economic Realities  
The antediluvian world lacks standardized currency across all regions. While gold, silver, and copper pieces exist (especially in Cainite cities), many communities rely on barter systems. The following guidelines help GMs handle trade:  
Currency Conversion:  
1 gold piece (gp) \= 10 silver pieces (sp) \= 100 copper pieces (cp)  
Trade Ratio: 1 gp value ≈ 1 day's skilled labor ≈ 10 days' unskilled labor ≈ 1 sheep  
Regional Variations:  
Sethite Enclaves: Primarily barter. Coin is viewed with suspicion as a Cainite innovation.  
Cainite Cities: Standardized currency widely accepted. Banking and credit systems developing.  
Wanderer Tribes: Barter only. Livestock, tools, and crafted goods are primary trade items.  
Nephilim Territories: Tribute system. Human settlements pay goods/labor to giant overlords.  
Horim Caves: Rare minerals and crystals serve as currency.  
Haggling Rules  
When buying or selling items, characters can attempt a Diplomacy check to negotiate better prices:  
Selling Items:  
Base value: 50% of item price  
Success: 75% of item price  
Critical success: 100% of item price  
Failure: 25% of item price  
Critical failure: Item damaged during negotiation (loses 10% value)  
Buying Items:  
Base price: 100% of item price  
Success: 75% of item price  
Critical success: 50% of item price  
Failure: 125% of item price  
Critical failure: Merchant refuses to sell or reports you to authorities  
Modifiers:  
\+2 circumstance bonus when trading in your home community  
\+1 circumstance bonus per 5 points by which your Diplomacy exceeds merchant's  
\-2 circumstance penalty when trading with hostile or suspicious merchants  
Righteous characters gain \+1 bonus when trading with Sethite communities  
Corrupted characters gain \+1 bonus when trading with Nephilim territories  
Special Trade Considerations  
Forbidden Items:  
Weapons, armor, and tools can be traded freely in most places. However, certain items face restrictions:  
Watcher-forged items: Banned in Sethite communities; confiscated if discovered  
Sacred items: Banned in Nephilim territories; destroyed if discovered  
Giant-sized equipment: Restricted to giant-kin or those with special permits  
Iron items: Highly regulated; require special licenses in most settlements  
Black Markets:  
In Cainite cities and Nephilim territories, black markets exist for forbidden items:  
Prices are 50% higher than normal  
Buyers must succeed at DC 15 Deception or Society check to find black market  
Transactions carry risk of betrayal, theft, or arrest  
Purchasing corrupted items may grant 1 CP  
Trade Routes:  
Major trade routes connect settlements but face dangers:  
Cainite Trade Road: Well-maintained but patrolled by Nephilim toll collectors  
Wanderer Paths: Hidden trails known only to nomadic tribes  
River Routes: Efficient but vulnerable to river monsters and pirates  
Mountain Passes: Dangerous but avoid Nephilim territories  
Characters with Trade Routes Lore gain \+2 circumstance bonus to Survival checks when navigating trade routes and to Diplomacy checks when negotiating with merchants familiar with those routes.  
Equipment by Lineage and Class  
Recommended Starting Equipment  
Warrior:  
Bronze Longsword (15 gp) or Warhammer (10 gp)  
Bronze Scale Armor (25 gp)  
Heavy Shield (10 gp)

Rations (10 days, 1 gp)  
Waterskin (5 cp)  
Total: \~60 gp

Bronze Greatsword (30 gp) or Giant's Maul (40 gp)

Anakim Chain (20 gp) \[if Anakim-blooded\]  
Bedroll (2 sp)  
Rations (10 days, 1 gp)

Spear (1 gp)

Fishing Kit (2 gp)

Padded Armor (2 gp)
