Hide Armor (10 gp)  
Holy Symbol (10 gp)  
Scribe's Tools (12 gp)  
Enochian Scrolls (250 gp) \[if Sethite\]  
Bedroll (2 sp)  
Total: \~290 gp (Priests often receive equipment from their community)  
Artisan:  
Battle Axe (12 gp)  
Studded Leather Armor (15 gp)  
Blacksmith's Tools (20 gp) or appropriate crafting tools  
Waterskin (5 cp)  
Rations (10 days, 1 gp)  
Total: \~50 gp  
Scribe:  
Staff (5 sp)  
Padded Armor (2 gp)  
Scribe's Tools (12 gp)  
Map Case (5 sp)  
Star Charts (15 gp) \[if astronomer\]  
Fine Clothing (15 gp)  
Total: \~45 gp  
Lineage-Specific Equipment Access  
Sethite:  
\+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when purchasing sacred items  
Can acquire Enochian Scrolls and Tears of the Righteous at 50% discount  
Cannot purchase corrupted items without gaining 1 CP  
Cainite:  
\+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks in Cainite cities  
Can acquire iron items (if available) and advanced tools  
Access to black markets for forbidden items (with risks)  
Wanderer:  
\+2 circumstance bonus to Barter checks with nomadic tribes  
Can acquire survival gear at 50% discount  
Horn of the Wanderer available at normal price  
Sorcerer Clan:  
Access to black markets for corrupted items  
Can acquire Watcher-Carved Staff and Star Charts of Baraqel  
Purchasing sacred items requires DC 15 Will save or gain 1 CP  
Nephilim:  
Can purchase giant-sized equipment at normal prices  
Access to Nephilim-forged weapons and armor  
Cannot purchase sacred items without gaining 1 CP per item  
Anakim:  
Chains of the Anakim available at 50% discount  
Access to Anakim Chain Shield and noble armor styles  
\+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks with giant-kin  
Rephaim:  
Access to Rephaim Scythe and death cult items  
Can acquire corrupted items at 25% discount  
Purchasing sacred items requires DC 18 Will save or gain 2 CP  
Horim:  
Horim Stone Blade available at normal price  
Access to underground trade networks for rare minerals  
\+2 circumstance bonus to Barter checks in subterranean communities  
Gibborim:  
Access to hybrid-sized equipment (between Medium and Large)  
Giant's Maul available at 25% discount  
\+1 circumstance bonus to Crafting checks for weapons/armor  
Elioud:  
Can purchase both human-sized and slightly oversized equipment  
\+1 circumstance bonus to Deception checks when purchasing restricted items  
Access to "passing" gear that helps conceal giant heritage  
Equipment Maintenance  
Wear and Tear  
Equipment in the harsh antediluvian world suffers wear and tear:  
Daily Maintenance:  
Weapons and armor require 10 minutes of cleaning and oiling per day  
Failure to maintain: \-1 item penalty to attack rolls or AC after 3 days neglect  
Environmental Damage:  
Flood Plains: Metal items rust; \-1 item penalty after 1 week without maintenance  
Desert: Leather dries and cracks; \-1 item penalty after 2 weeks without maintenance  
Underground: Moisture damages wood and leather; \-1 item penalty after 1 week without maintenance  
Corrupted Areas: Items gain faint corruption; sacred items lose properties after 1 week exposure  
Repairing Damage  
Minor Damage (item at half HP):  
Requires 1 hour of work and successful Crafting check (DC 15\)  
Restores item to full HP  
Major Damage (item at 0 HP, broken condition):  
Requires 1 day of work and successful Crafting check (DC 20\)  
Restores item to half HP; requires additional repair to fully restore  
Destroyed Items (HP reduced to negative maximum):  
Cannot be repaired; must be replaced  
Sacred items destroyed in corrupted areas cannot be replaced without divine intervention  
Blessing and Consecration  
Sacred Items:  
Can be blessed by priests (10-minute ritual)  
Blessing grants \+1 item bonus to saves against corruption  
Requires 1 RP from priest per blessing  
Blessing lasts 1 week or until item is used for corrupt purpose  
Corrupted Items:  
Can be consecrated by corrupted sorcerers (10-minute ritual)  
Consecration grants \+1 item bonus to damage against righteous beings  
Requires 1 CP from sorcerer per consecration  
Consecration lasts 1 week or until item is used for righteous purpose  
Purification:  
Corrupted items can be purified through righteous ritual (1 hour)  
Requires DC 20 Religion check and 2 RP  
Success removes corruption; failure destroys item  
Sacred items corrupted by exposure can be restored through purification  

Chapter 9: Spells and Miracles  
Introduction: Two Paths of Power  
In the antediluvian world, supernatural power flows from two opposing sources—the forbidden knowledge taught by the fallen Watchers and the divine grace granted by the Most High. These two paths represent the central spiritual conflict of the age: the temptation of stolen power versus the discipline of righteous faith. Unlike standard fantasy settings where magic is a neutral tool, every supernatural act in Nephilim Wars carries eternal weight. To wield forbidden arts is to risk spiritual corruption; to channel divine power is to bear the responsibility of righteousness.  
This chapter details both systems with complete, gameplay-ready mechanics. Forbidden Sorcery (used by Magi and Sorcerer Clans) draws from six Watcher traditions, each with unique spell lists and Corruption Point risks. Divine Miracles (used by Priests and righteous characters) channels the Creator's power through faith, requiring Righteousness Point expenditure and moral purity. Every spell includes full Pathfinder 2e stat blocks, Soul Economy costs, setting-appropriate flavor text, and tactical guidance for players and GMs.  
All spells follow standard PF2e formatting with antediluvian adaptations. Components reflect bronze-age materials (clay tablets instead of scrolls, bronze focuses instead of crystal). Descriptions avoid medieval or Renaissance terminology. Damage types align with setting lore (fire from Azazel's warfare arts, radiant from divine judgment). Crucially, every forbidden spell includes Corruption Risk mechanics, while every divine miracle includes Righteousness Cost mechanics.  
Forbidden Sorcery: The Watcher Traditions  
System Overview  
Forbidden Sorcery represents knowledge stolen from the celestial realm by the 200 Watchers. Each tradition corresponds to a specific fallen angel's teachings, granting access to distinct magical domains. Casting these spells risks spiritual corruption—every spell of 2nd level or higher requires a flat check to avoid gaining Corruption Points (CP). The risk increases with spell power and harmful intent.  
Corruption Risk Mechanics (Recap from Chapter 5):  
Spell Level CP Risk: DC \= 11 \+ spell level (failure gains 1 CP)  
Harmful Spells: \+2 to DC (curses, damage, control)  
Healing/Beneficial: No modifier  
Divination: \-2 to DC (knowledge itself isn't inherently corrupting)  
Necromancy: \+4 to DC (directly opposes divine life)  
Magi Class Feature: Corruption Resistance reduces DC by 1-3 based on level  
Sorcerer Clan Feat: Watcher's Resistance reduces DC by 1-Spellcasting Requirements:  
Focus Item: Required for all spells. Must be crafted from materials associated with your tradition:  
Azazel: Bronze weapon fragment or armor scale  
Semyaza: Dried herbs or poisoned thorn  
Baraqel: Star chart fragment or celestial observation tool  
Kokabiel: Obsidian shard or constellation carving  
Penemue: Clay tablet fragment or ink-stained stylus  
Kasdeja: Bone shard or shadow-touched stone  
Components: Verbal components involve chanting Watcher names or forbidden syllables. Somatic components mimic celestial gestures. Material components use bronze-age substances (clay, herbs, blood, starlight).  
Social Consequences: Casting forbidden magic in righteous communities (Sethite enclaves) risks immediate hostility. Visible effects (flames, shadows) may attract Nephilim patrols or disembodied spirits (Refa'im).  
Learning Spells:  
Magi learn spells automatically as they level  
Sorcerer Clans learn spells through dangerous study of forbidden texts (requires Occultism check; failure gains 1 CP)  
Stealing spells from other traditions requires finding ancient Watcher texts (adventure hook)  
Azazel's Warfare Arts  
Fire, force, weapon enhancement, battlefield control  
Azazel taught humanity the arts of war—forging swords, crafting shields, and creating armor. His magical tradition reflects this legacy: destructive fire magic, weapon enchantments, and battlefield domination. Spells often manifest as bronze weapons of pure energy, walls of fire shaped like fortifications, or armor forged from celestial light. Practitioners risk becoming consumed by violence and martial pride.  
Cantrips  
Azazel's Spark \[Cantrip\]  
Evocation Fire Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[bronze shard\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 creature  
Saving Throw Basic Reflex  
A flick of your wrist ignites a spark of divine fire. Deal 1d4 fire damage to the target.  
Corruption Risk: None (cantrip)  
Antediluvian Flavor: The spark appears as a tiny bronze ember that leaves no physical trace but burns with supernatural heat. Used by Watcher-taught smiths to test metal purity.  
Weapon Bond \[Cantrip\]  
Transmutation Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[drop of blood\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 weapon you're holding  
For 1 minute, the weapon glows with faint bronze light. You gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls with this weapon.  
Corruption Risk: None (cantrip)  
Antediluvian Flavor: The weapon hums with stolen celestial energy. Nephilim thralls recognize this glow and report users to their masters.  
Level 1 Spells  
Bronze Blade \[1\]  
Conjuration Force Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[bronze splinter\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 10 minutes  
A shimmering bronze greatsword manifests in the target's hand. This \+1 striking weapon deals 1d8 force damage and has the force, magical, and versatile P traits. It vanishes if the target is disarmed or drops it.  
Corruption Risk: DC 12 flat check (failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Essential for human-sized characters facing giants. The force damage bypasses some resistances.  
Antediluvian Flavor: The blade appears forged from solidified starlight, etched with Azazel's forbidden sigils. It feels unnaturally heavy.  
Wall of Bronze \[1\]  
Conjuration Earth Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[clay tablet\])  
Range 120 feet; Area 10-foot burst  
Duration 1 minute  
Bronze plates erupt from the ground, forming a 10-foot-high wall. The wall has AC 10, Hardness 10, HP 30, and BT 15\. Creatures can't see through it.  
Corruption Risk: DC 12 flat check (failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Creates instant cover against giant projectiles or to split enemy forces.  
Antediluvian Flavor: The plates bear Watcher carvings that glow faintly. Sethite priests consider this desecration of earth.  
Level 2 Spells  
Azazel's Flame \[2\]  
Evocation Fire Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[sulfur\])  
Range 120 feet; Area 20-foot burst  
Saving Throw Basic Reflex  
Celestial fire erupts in the area. Creatures take 4d6 fire damage. The fire doesn't spread or ignite objects.  
Corruption Risk: DC 15 flat check (+2 for harmful spell; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Devastating against clustered Nephilim thralls. Ineffective against fire-resistant giants.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Flames burn with unnatural bronze hue. Victims' shadows writhe independently for 1 round after extinguishing.  
GM Warning: Using this in wooden Cainite cities risks catastrophic fires. Righteous NPCs gain 1 CP just witnessing it.  
Level 3 Spells  
Titan's Bane \[3\]  
Transmutation Force Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[giant's tooth\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 weapon  
Duration 1 minute  
The weapon becomes supernaturally sharp. It ignores the first 5 points of resistance to physical damage and gains the deadly d10 trait against creatures larger than Medium.  
Corruption Risk: DC 16 flat check (+2 for harmful spell; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Critical against Nephilim and other giants. Combine with Bronze Blade for devastating effect.  
Antediluvian Flavor: The weapon's edge shimmers with heat haze. When striking giants, it emits a sound like shattering stone.  
Level 4 Spells  
Siege Engine \[4\]  
Conjuration Force Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[miniature bronze ram\])  
Range 60 feet; Targets 1 object or creature  
Saving Throw Basic Fortitude  
A massive spectral battering ram slams into the target. Deal 8d6 bludgeoning damage. On a critical failure, the target is pushed 15 feet and knocked prone.  
Corruption Risk: DC 17 flat check (+2 for harmful spell; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Designed to breach Nephilim fortress gates. Effective against large creatures' weak points.  
Antediluvian Flavor: The ram appears as a bronze-headed log wielded by ghostly giant hands. Leaves no physical damage on objects.  
Level 5 Spells  
Armory of Azazel \[5\]  
Conjuration Force Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[bronze scale\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets Up to 4 willing creatures  
Duration 10 minutes  
Each target gains spectral bronze armor. They gain a \+2 item bonus to AC and resistance 5 to physical damage.  
Corruption Risk: DC 16 flat check (no harmful modifier; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Transforms lightly armored parties into giant-slayers. Stacks with physical armor.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Armor appears as interlocking bronze plates etched with forbidden sigils. Makes faint clinking sounds when moving.  
Level 6+ Spells (Summary)  
Inferno of the First War \[6\]  
Evocation Fire Occult  
120-ft line, 10d6 fire damage (Reflex half). Corruption Risk: DC 19 (+2 harmful)  
Flavor: Recreates the celestial fire Azazel used in the war in heaven. Burns with bronze-and-crimson flames.  
Colossus Form \[7\]  
Transmutation Occult  
Target grows to Large size for 1 minute. +10-foot reach, +2 status to Strength checks. Corruption Risk: DC 20 (+2 harmful). Flavor: Body swells with stolen angelic power. Skin takes on metallic sheen. Risk of permanent size change on critical failure.  
Azazel's Final Judgment \[10\]  
Evocation Fire Occult  
30-ft burst, 20d6 fire damage (Reflex half). Ignores fire resistance. Corruption Risk: DC 23 (+2 harmful; failure gains 2 CP)  
Flavor: Summons the fire that will consume the earth at judgment. Casting this automatically marks the caster for divine retribution.  
Semyaza's Enchantments & Root-Cutting  
Charm, poison, herbalism, healing (corrupted)  
Semyaza, leader of the 200 Watchers, taught enchantments and root-cutting—the arts of herbalism, poisons, and mind manipulation. His tradition blends subtle mental influence with dangerous botanical knowledge. Spells manifest as glowing herbs, whispered compulsions, or toxic mists. Practitioners risk addiction to power and moral compromise through manipulation.  
Level 1 Spells  
Semyaza's Charm \[1\]  
Enchantment Mental Occult  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[rose petal\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 humanoid  
Saving Throw Will  
The target is fascinated by you for 1 minute. On a critical failure, they are charmed for 1 round.  
Corruption Risk: DC 13 flat check (+2 for harmful spell; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Essential for infiltrating Nephilim courts or calming hostile tribes.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Target's eyes briefly glow with the same color as the rose petal used. Sethites recognize this as soul-theft.  
Healing Draught \[1\]  
Necromancy Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[herbs\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 willing creature  
Restore 1d8 \+ your spellcasting ability modifier HP.  
Corruption Risk: DC 11 flat check (no modifier; failure gains 1 CP)  
Critical Warning: This "healing" draws vitality from nearby life. For every 5 HP restored, a plant within 10 feet withers and dies. Using this in sacred groves causes immediate divine backlash (GM discretion).  
Level 2 Spells  
Root of Binding \[2\]  
Abjuration Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[vine\])  
Range 60 feet; Targets 1 creature  
Saving Throw Reflex  
Thorny vines erupt from the ground, restraining the target. On a failure, the target is immobilized for 1 round.  
Corruption Risk: DC 14 flat check (+2 harmful; failure gains 1 CP)  
Antediluvian Flavor: Vines bear black thorns that drip sap smelling of decay. They vanish after use but leave thorn scars.  
Level 3 Spells  
Poison Cloud \[3\]  
Conjuration Poison Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[poisonous mushroom\])  
Range 120 feet; Area 20-foot burst  
Saving Throw Fortitude  
A greenish cloud fills the area. Creatures take 4d6 poison damage and are sickened 1 on a failure.  
Corruption Risk: DC 16 flat check (+2 harmful; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Effective against giants with poor Reflex saves. Wind disperses the cloud.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Cloud smells of rotting herbs. Victims vomit black bile.  
Level 4 Spells  
Blood Price \[4\]  
Necromancy Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[drop of your blood\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 1 minute  
Target gains \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and damage. At the spell's end, they take 2d6 persistent bleed damage.  
Corruption Risk: DC 15 flat check (+2 harmful; failure gains 1 CP)  
Antediluvian Flavor: Target's veins glow crimson beneath skin. The bleed damage cannot be stopped by normal means—requires Heal spell or 1 hour of rest.  
Level 5+ Spells (Summary)  
Soul Harvest \[5\]  
Necromancy Occult  
Drain life from a dying creature to heal yourself. Corruption Risk: DC 17 (+2 harmful)  
Flavor: Victim's shadow detaches and flows into caster. Using this on righteous beings (5+ RP) automatically gains 2 CP.  
Enthrallment \[6\]  
Enchantment Mental Occult  
Mass charm effect. Corruption Risk: DC 18 (+2 harmful)  
Flavor: Targets' eyes turn milky white. They obey commands but remember nothing after effect ends—causing psychological trauma.  
Semyaza's Final Bargain \[9\]  
Necromancy Occult  
Restore a dead creature to life as a thrall. Corruption Risk: DC 21 (+2 harmful; failure gains 2 CP)  
Flavor: The revived being has hollow eyes and moves with unnatural stiffness. They serve for 24 hours before crumbling to dust. Using this on Nephilim creates a Refa'im spirit.  
Baraqel's Star Reading  
Divination, fate manipulation, celestial phenomena  
Baraqel taught humanity astrology and the observation of stars. His tradition focuses on foresight, fate manipulation, and drawing power from celestial bodies. Spells manifest as starlight, constellations in the air, or visions of possible futures. Practitioners risk obsession with fate and detachment from mortal concerns.  
Level 1 Spells  
Starlight Guidance \[1\]  
Divination Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[star chart fragment\])  
Range Self; Targets You  
Duration 10 minutes  
You gain a \+1 status bonus to your next attack roll, skill check, or saving throw.  
Corruption Risk: DC 10 flat check (-2 for divination; failure gains 1 CP)  
Antediluvian Flavor: A single star glows above your head. Righteous characters feel uneasy under its light.  
Level 2 Spells  
Celestial Path \[2\]  
Divination Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[obsidian mirror\])  
Range 120 feet; Targets 1 creature  
Duration 1 minute  
You see the optimal path for the target. They ignore difficult terrain and gain a \+10-foot status bonus to Speed.  
Corruption Risk: DC 11 flat check (-2 divination; failure gains 1 CP)  
Tactical Note: Critical for escaping Nephilim patrols or navigating flood plains.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Target leaves faint starlight footprints that fade after 1 minute.  
Level 3 Spells  
Fate's Glimpse \[3\]  
Divination Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[clay tablet\])  
Range Self; Targets You  
Duration Instantaneous  
Roll a d20 and record the result. Within the next hour, you can replace any attack roll, skill check, or saving throw with this recorded roll.  
Corruption Risk: DC 12 flat check (-2 divination; failure gains 1 CP)  
Antediluvian Flavor: The rolled number appears etched on your palm in starlight. Fades after use.  
Level 4 Spells  
Constellation Shield \[4\]  
Abjuration Occult  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[constellation carving\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 1 minute  
Shimmering starlight forms a protective barrier. The target gains resistance 10 to all damage.  
Corruption Risk: DC 13 flat check (-2 divination; failure gains 1 CP)  
Antediluvian Flavor: The shield displays the Orion constellation. Sethite priests recognize this as stolen celestial order.  
Level 5+ Spells (Summary)  
Starfall \[5\]  
Evocation Force Occult  
Call down celestial energy. Corruption Risk: DC 14 (-2 divination)  
Flavor: Bronze-colored meteors streak from the sky. Leaves no crater but scorches the ground with star patterns.  
Fate Weaver \[7\]  
Divination Occult  
Reroll any d20 roll in the past minute. Corruption Risk: DC 16 (-2 divination)  
Flavor: Time briefly fractures. Onlookers experience déjà vu. Using this to alter divine judgments gains 3 CP automatically.  
Baraqel's Final Prophecy \[10\]  
Divination Occult  
See all possible futures for 1 minute. Corruption Risk: DC 19 (-2 divination; failure gains 2 CP)  
Flavor: Caster's eyes turn into swirling galaxies. After effect ends, they are permanently blinded in one eye. Reveals the Flood's inevitability.  
Kokabiel's Illusions, Penemue's Knowledge, Kasdeja's Shadows  
(Condensed for space—full spells follow same format)  
Kokabiel's Tradition (Illusions & Darkness):  
Level 1: Shadow Step (teleport through shadows)  
Level 3: Veil of Stars (create illusory terrain)  
Level 5: Nightmare Manifest (create tangible fear)  
Level 7: Event Horizon (create area of absolute darkness)  
Corruption Risk: Standard \+2 for harmful illusions  
Flavor: Illusions have faint star patterns. Darkness feels "hungry."  
Penemue's Tradition (Knowledge & Communication):  
Level 1: Clay Tablet Memory (perfect recall)  
Level 3: Tongues of Babel (speak any language)  
Level 5: Scroll of Truth (compel honesty)  
Level 7: Library of Eden (access ancestral knowledge)  
Corruption Risk: DC reduced by 2 for non-harmful spells  
Critical Warning: Reading forbidden knowledge (Watcher texts) automatically gains 1 CP per hour regardless of spell success.  
Kasdeja's Tradition (Necromancy & Shadows):  
Level 1: Shadow Touch (1d4 negative damage)  
Level 3: Spirit Bind (trap disembodied spirit)  
Level 5: Refa'im Summoning (call Nephilim spirit)  
Level 7: Soul Rend (permanent ability damage)  
Corruption Risk: DC increased by 4 for all spells  
Flavor: Spells manifest with weeping shadows. Using these near burial sites risks attracting multiple Refa'im.  
Divine Miracles: Power of the Most High  
System Overview  
Divine Miracles flow from faith in the Creator, channeled through righteous living and covenant relationship. Unlike forbidden sorcery, these powers cannot be stolen or learned through study—they are granted to those who maintain spiritual purity. Casting miracles requires spending Righteousness Points (RP), representing the spiritual capital earned through virtuous acts. Using miracles while corrupted (high CP) risks divine backlash.  
Righteousness Cost Mechanics:  
Minor Miracles (healing, protection): 0-1 RP  
Major Miracles (turning undead, judgment): 1-2 RP  
Legendary Miracles (resurrection, divine intervention): 3+ RP  
Corrupted Caster Penalty: If caster has 3+ CP, add \+1 RP cost per spell level. On critical failure, lose additional RP equal to spell level.  
Righteous Environment Bonus: Casting in sacred places (Sethite altars, holy groves) reduces RP cost by 1 (minimum 0).  
Spellcasting Requirements:  
Holy Symbol: Required focus. Must be crafted from natural materials (carved stone, woven reeds, bronze medallion with righteous symbols).  
Components: Verbal components involve prayers or scriptural quotes. Somatic components mimic sacred gestures (hands raised to heaven, blessing motions). Material components use pure substances (clean water, olive oil, unblemished grain).  
Social Effects: Casting miracles in righteous communities inspires hope (+1 status bonus to allies' next check). In corrupted areas, miracles shine with visible light, potentially attracting attention.  
Learning Miracles:  
Priests learn miracles automatically through prayer and study  
Righteous non-Priests may learn miracles through divine revelation (GM discretion)  
Studying miracles does not risk CP gain (unlike forbidden sorcery)  
Core Miracle List  
Cantrips (No RP Cost)  
Sacred Flame \[Cantrip\]  
Evocation Radiant Divine  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[holy symbol\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 creature  
Saving Throw Basic Reflex  
A flickering flame of divine light appears above the target's head. Deal 1d4 radiant damage.  
Antediluvian Flavor: Flame burns with pure white light that casts no shadows. Corrupted beings (3+ CP) feel searing pain even on a successful save.  
Tactical Note: Most effective against undead and Refa'im spirits. Ineffective against non-corrupted giants.  
Blessing \[Cantrip\]  
Abjuration Divine  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[olive oil\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 10 minutes  
The target gains a \+1 status bonus to their next attack roll or saving throw.  
Antediluvian Flavor: A faint golden light surrounds the target. Sethite elders use this before sending hunters into the wilderness.  
Level 1 Miracles  
Healing Touch \[1\]  
Necromancy Divine  
Cast \[A\] (somatic, material \[clean water\])  
Range Touch; Targets 1 willing creature  
Restore 1d8 \+ your spellcasting ability modifier HP.  
Righteousness Cost: 0 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: Wounds close with golden light. The healed creature smells of fresh rain.  
Tactical Note: Standard healing. More potent when cast by high-RP characters (add 1 HP per 5 RP the caster has).  
Shield of Faith \[1\]  
Abjuration Divine  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[holy symbol\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 1 minute  
A shimmering barrier of light surrounds the target. They gain a \+1 status bonus to AC.  
Righteousness Cost: 0 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: The shield displays faint images of the Garden of Eden. Corrupted beings cannot see it.  
Tactical Note: Essential protection against giant attacks. Stacks with physical shields.  
Level 2 Miracles  
Sanctuary \[2\]  
Abjuration Divine  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[holy symbol\])  
Range 30 feet; Targets 1 willing creature  
Duration 1 minute  
Divine protection surrounds the target. Attackers must succeed at a DC 5 flat check to target them with attacks or harmful spells.  
Righteousness Cost: 1 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: Target appears slightly translucent to hostile creatures. Sacred animals (doves, lambs) gather near them.  
Tactical Note: Critical for protecting non-combatants during Nephilim raids. Does not prevent area effects.  
Turn Unholy \[2\]  
Necromancy Divine  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[holy symbol\])  
Range 30 feet; Area 30-foot burst  
Saving Throw Will  
Undead creatures and corrupted beings (3+ CP) must flee for 1 minute. On a critical failure, they are destroyed (if undead) or gain 1d4 CP (if corrupted).  
Righteousness Cost: 1 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: A wave of golden light erupts from caster. Corrupted beings' shadows writhe in pain.  
Tactical Note: Primary defense against Refa'im spirits and corrupted thralls. Ineffective against uncorrupted giants.  
Level 3 Miracles  
Divine Judgment \[3\]  
Evocation Radiant Divine  
Cast \[A\] (verbal, material \[holy symbol\])  
Range 120 feet; Targets 1 corrupted creature (3+ CP)  
Saving Throw Basic Will  
Celestial fire strikes the target. Deal 6d6 radiant damage.  
Righteousness Cost: 2 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: A beam of sunlight pierces clouds to strike the target. Their corruption visibly burns away (smoke rises from their skin).  
Tactical Note: Devastating against corrupted sorcerers or possessed humans. Ineffective against non-corrupted targets.  
GM Warning: Using this on a target with 10+ CP may trigger divine backlash—GM may require caster to attempt DC 15 Will save or gain 1 CP from witnessing such deep corruption.  
Level 4 Miracles

Evocation Fire Divine

A pillar of divine fire erupts. Creatures take 8d6 fire damage. The fire doesn't spread or ignite objects.

Antediluvian Flavor: Fire burns with pure white light, not consuming fuel. Represents the pillar that guarded Eden.  
Tactical Note: Creates impassable barrier against Nephilim patrols. Corrupted beings take double damage.  
Critical Note: Unlike Azazel's Flame, this fire purifies—it heals righteous creatures (1d6 HP) who pass through it.

Necromancy Divine

Restore the target to life with 1 HP. They are fatigued for 24 hours.  
Righteousness Cost: 3 RP  
Antediluvian Flavor: Golden light flows into the body. The resurrected person smells of myrrh and speaks of "walking in a green valley."  
Critical Restrictions:  
Cannot resurrect creatures killed by divine judgment (e.g., struck by lightning from heaven)  
Cannot resurrect creatures with 10+ CP at time of death (their souls are bound for judgment)  
Caster permanently loses 1 point of Constitution (restored only by Wish or divine intervention)  
Antediluvian Lore: Only three documented resurrections in antediluvian history—each performed by prophets with 15+ RP.

Evocation Divine

Tactical Guidance for Players  
Magi Strategy Guide  
Low-Level Survival: Focus on Azazel's cantrips for weapon enhancement. Avoid high-CP-risk spells until you have Corruption Resistance feats.  
Giant Combat: Titan's Bane (Level 3\) is essential. Combine with Bronze Blade for reliable damage against Nephilim.  
Corruption Management: Keep CP below 5 to avoid Marked for Judgment effects. Use downtime to remove CP through repentance.  
Tradition Synergy: Azazel \+ Kokabiel for battlefield control (fire \+ illusions). Semyaza \+ Kasdeja for debuffing enemies (poison \+ necromancy).  
Critical Warning: Never cast high-level forbidden spells in Sethite communities. The social consequences (expulsion, attack) outweigh tactical benefits.  
Priest Strategy Guide  
RP Conservation: Use cantrips for routine healing/protection. Save RP for critical moments (turning Refa'im, healing after giant attacks).  
Giant Combat: Divine Judgment (Level 3\) is ineffective against uncorrupted giants. Focus on protecting allies with Shield of Faith and Sanctuary.  
Righteousness Farming: Perform miracles in sacred groves to reduce RP costs. Heal refugees after Nephilim raids to gain RP.  
Corruption Defense: Keep CP at 0\. Even 1 CP increases RP costs and risks divine backlash on critical failures.  
Critical Warning: Righteous Resurrection should be reserved for campaign-defining moments. The Constitution loss is permanent until post-Flood.  
Mixed Party Tactics  
Magi \+ Priest Combo: Magi uses Wall of Bronze to create cover; Priest casts Sanctuary on the wall to make it impassable to corrupted beings.  
Hunter \+ Magi Combo: Hunter marks target with Hunter's Mark; Magi casts Azazel's Flame on the marked area for guaranteed damage.  
Warrior \+ Priest Combo: Warrior uses Tribal War Cry; Priest spends 1 RP to extend duration to 10 minutes.  
Critical Synergy: When Priest casts Divine Judgment on a corrupted target, Magi can follow with Semyaza's Charm—the target's Will save is reduced by 2 due to spiritual vulnerability.  
GM Guidance: Spellcasting in Your Campaign  
Balancing Forbidden Power  
CP as Pacing Mechanic: Track CP carefully. When a Magi reaches 5 CP, introduce divine judgment effects (natural 1s trigger minor punishments).  
Corruption Consequences: At 7+ CP, have disembodied Refa'im spirits begin following the character, whispering temptations.  
Redemption Arcs: Allow CP removal through meaningful atonement (not just downtime). Example: Sacrificing a powerful forbidden item to save innocents removes 2 CP.  
Watcher Intervention: At 10+ CP, the character's patron Watcher may manifest in dreams, offering greater power for greater corruption.  
Divine Miracle Limitations  
Faith Requirements: Characters with 5+ CP cannot cast divine miracles. Attempts fail and cost double RP.  
Sacred Spaces: Create locations where miracles are enhanced (Sethite altars) or forbidden (Nephilim temples). This creates meaningful travel decisions.  
Divine Silence: During major corruption events (Nephilim sacrifices, Watcher rituals), miracles may fail entirely until the corruption is cleansed.  
Prophetic Restrictions: High-level miracles (Divine Intervention) should require fulfilling a divine quest first (e.g., "Bring water from the four rivers of Eden").  
Adventure Hooks Involving Magic  
Forbidden Text Recovery: The party must retrieve a stolen Watcher text before a Sorcerer Clan uses it to summon a Refa'im army. Moral dilemma: Destroy the text (losing its knowledge) or secure it (risking corruption)?  
Sacred Grove Defense: A Sethite sanctuary is under attack. The Priest must maintain Sanctuary while others defend the perimeter. If the grove is desecrated, all divine miracles cost \+1 RP for a month.  
Corrupted Miracle: A Priest has fallen to corruption but still casts miracles. Their Healing Touch now drains life from others. The party must decide: Redeem them or stop them?  
Watcher's Bargain: A bound Watcher offers power in exchange for freedom. The Magi who accepts gains access to 10th-level spells but starts at 10 CP. Campaign-altering choice.  
Spellcasting NPCs  
Righteous NPCs: Sethite elders cast low-level miracles freely (0 RP cost) but refuse high-cost spells without righteous cause. They can sense CP on others.  
Corrupted NPCs: Sorcerer Clan leaders have high CP but powerful magic. They may offer to remove a PC's CP... for a price (souls, betrayal, etc.).  
Giant Spellcasters: Nephilim sorcerers combine physical might with forbidden magic. Anakim nobles use Kokabiel's illusions to enhance their chain weapons.  
Divine Agents: Archangels (Michael, Gabriel) may appear to deliver miracles—but only to characters with 10+ RP and 0 CP. Their presence terrifies corrupted beings.
