Chapter 11: Game Mastering  
Introduction: Your Role as Storyteller  
As the Game Master (GM) of Nephilim Wars, you hold a sacred trust. You are not merely a referee of rules or a creator of challenges—you are a steward of one of humanity's oldest and most profound narratives. The antediluvian world is not a setting of graphic horror or gratuitous violence; it is a biblical landscape where moral choices carry eternal weight, where faith is tested against overwhelming odds, and where hope persists even as judgment approaches. Your role is to guide players through this spiritually significant era with reverence, wisdom, and narrative purpose.  
This chapter provides practical guidance for running Nephilim Wars campaigns that honor the biblical foundation while delivering compelling, playable adventures. Every recommendation emphasizes moral weight over graphic detail, spiritual stakes over visceral horror, and redemptive possibilities over despair. The Flood is not merely a catastrophe—it is divine judgment with purpose, and your campaign should reflect the profound theological significance of this pivotal moment in human history.  
Core Principles for Biblical Storytelling  
Focus on Moral Choices, Not Graphic Details  
When depicting conflict, emphasize the why over the how. Describe a Nephilim warlord's tyranny through its impact on communities ("The village granaries stand empty while the giant's thralls demand more tribute") rather than graphic violence. When characters face moral dilemmas, highlight the spiritual consequences: "Using this forbidden spell might save your friend, but you feel a cold darkness settle in your soul."  
Honor the Sacred Texts  
The setting draws from Genesis, 1 Enoch, Jubilees, and related apocryphal texts. Treat these sources with respect:  
Biblical figures (Enoch, Noah, Methuselah) should be portrayed with dignity and purpose  
Divine actions reflect justice and mercy, not arbitrary cruelty  
Prophecies and visions should align with scriptural themes of judgment and redemption  
Avoid adding speculative details that contradict core biblical narratives  
Emphasize Hope Amidst Judgment  
Even as corruption spreads and the Flood approaches, the righteous line persists. Every session should contain glimmers of hope:  
A Sethite elder sharing wisdom that strengthens faith  
A rescued refugee finding safety in a hidden enclave  
A moment of repentance that removes Corruption Points  
A vision of the rainbow covenant that will follow judgment  
Respect Player Agency in Spiritual Matters  
Never force players into specific religious beliefs. The Soul Economy tracks in-game spiritual alignment within the setting's framework, not players' personal faith. Frame choices as "within this world's context" rather than prescriptive morality:  
"Your character feels the weight of this choice" rather than "You should feel guilty"  
"The Most High's judgment is approaching" rather than theological assertions about real-world beliefs  
Provide multiple paths to righteousness (protection, mercy, truth-telling) rather than single "correct" choices  
Running the Antediluvian World  
Establishing Tone Without Graphic Content  
The antediluvian world is intense but need not be graphic. Use these techniques to convey stakes without explicit content:  
Show Impact, Not Violence  
Instead of: "The Nephilim's bronze sword cleaves the villager in two, spraying blood across the mud huts."  
Use: "The Nephilim's shadow falls over the cowering villager. A moment later, the giant turns away, leaving silence where a life once was. The other villagers bow their heads in grief."  
Focus on Emotional and Spiritual Consequences  
Instead of: Graphic descriptions of torture or suffering  
Use: "The prisoner returns to the village hollow-eyed, speaking of whispers that promised power if he would betray his people. He refuses to meet anyone's gaze, and the elders worry for his soul."  
Use Environmental Storytelling  
Instead of: Describing corpses or gore  
Use: "Abandoned homes stand with doors hanging open. A child's clay toy lies trampled in the dust. In the center of the village square, a single bronze chain—Anakim insignia visible—lies coiled like a serpent."  
Emphasize Divine Presence  
Instead of: Focusing solely on corruption and darkness  
Use: "As the party prays over the wounded, a gentle rain begins to fall—not the violent storms of judgment, but a cleansing shower that washes the dust from their faces. For a moment, the weight of the world feels lighter."  
Campaign Pacing: The Approach of Judgment  
The Flood is not a surprise—it is an inevitable culmination. Structure your campaign in four phases:  
Phase 1: The World as It Was (Levels 1-5)  
Focus: Establishing communities, introducing moral choices, building relationships  
Signs of Judgment: Minor environmental shifts (unseasonal rains, strange animal behavior), whispers of prophecies  
GM Guidance: Keep corruption manageable. Introduce Soul Economy mechanics gently. Focus on local threats (raiders, minor corrupted beings) rather than world-ending dangers.  
Phase 2: Shadows Lengthen (Levels 6-10)  
Focus: Rising tensions, moral compromises, expanding conflicts  
Signs of Judgment: Increased seismic activity, rivers running backward, celestial omens (comets, eclipses)  
GM Guidance: Introduce meaningful corruption risks. Create dilemmas where "right" choices have costs. Show how communities fracture under pressure.  
Phase 3: The Gathering Storm (Levels 11-15)  
Focus: Major conflicts, divine interventions, preparation for judgment  
Signs of Judgment: Widespread environmental chaos (flooded plains, drought-stricken lands), visible spiritual warfare (Refa'im spirits manifesting openly)  
GM Guidance: Make Soul Economy consequences significant. Introduce prophetic visions of the Ark. Create opportunities for redemption arcs.  
Phase 4: The Final Days (Levels 16-20)  
Focus: Climactic choices, legacy building, preparation for the Flood  
Signs of Judgment: Continuous rain, rivers overflowing, animals gathering in pairs  
GM Guidance: Focus on character legacies. Provide meaningful conclusions regardless of Net Righteousness. Emphasize hope beyond judgment (the covenant to come).  
Designing Encounters: Humans and Giants  
Scaling Challenges Appropriately  
Nephilim are meant to be formidable, not routinely defeatable. Use these principles:  
Avoid Direct Confrontation  
Design encounters where defeating giants requires:  
Environmental advantages (collapsing structures, narrow canyons)  
Strategic preparation (traps, poisoned weapons, divine blessings)  
Alliance building (recruiting other giant-kin, gaining divine aid)  
Non-combat solutions (negotiation, stealth, misdirection)  
Example Encounter Framework  
Situation: A Nephilim patrol (Level 8\) threatens a Sethite enclave  
Player Options:  
Stealth Approach: Use Survival/Stealth to guide refugees through hidden paths (DC 20\)  
Diplomatic Approach: Negotiate with the patrol's human thralls (Diplomacy DC 22\)  
Strategic Approach: Collapse a canyon entrance to block pursuit (Crafting DC 24\)  
Divine Approach: Perform a ritual to create a temporary sanctuary (Religion DC 26\)  
Combat as Last Resort: If players choose battle, emphasize retreat options and consequences (heavy casualties, corruption from desperate measures)  
Mixed-Size Party Dynamics  
When players include both human-sized and giant-kin characters:  
Positioning Matters  
Human-sized characters excel at flanking, targeting weak points, and using terrain  
Giant-kin characters control space, absorb damage, and create openings  
Design battlefields with features both sizes can use (boulders for cover, narrow passages for bottlenecks)  
Role Specialization  
Human-sized: Scouts, archers, spellcasters, trap-disablers  
Giant-kin: Frontline defenders, siege breakers, morale anchors  
Encourage complementary tactics: "While Rahab holds the canyon mouth, Tamar scales the cliff to rain arrows on the Nephilim's unprotected neck."  
Shared Vulnerabilities  
Both sizes suffer from environmental hazards (flood waters, earthquakes)  
Both need rest, food, and community  
Both face spiritual consequences for their choices  
Emphasize interdependence: "Your survival depends on each other's strengths."  
The Soul Economy in Practice  
Awarding and Tracking RP/CP  
Clear, Consistent Criteria  
Create a reference sheet for your table:  
Action Type  
RP Award  
CP Award  
Protecting innocents from harm  
1 RP  
—  
Showing mercy to surrendered foes  
1 RP  
—  
Keeping sacred oaths despite cost  
1 RP  
—  
Using forbidden magic (per spell)  
—  
1 CP (risk)  
Breaking vows for personal gain  
—  
1 CP  
Cruelty beyond necessity  
—  
1 CP  
Transparent Tracking  
Use a shared tracker visible to all players  
Announce RP/CP changes immediately with clear reasoning: "Because you chose to heal the wounded thrall instead of executing him, you gain 1 RP."  
At session end, review significant changes: "Keturah gained 2 CP from casting Azazel's Flame twice. Enoch gained 2 RP from protecting refugees."  
Narrative Integration of Soul States  
Blessed Tier (Net \+5+)  
Manifestations:  
Divine protection: Arrows veer away, paths clear unexpectedly  
Spiritual insight: Receive dreams guiding next steps  
Community trust: Sethite elders seek your counsel  
GM Guidance: Grant minor narrative advantages ("The storm parts just enough for you to see the safe path") but avoid making characters invincible. True righteousness faces greater tests.  
Corrupted Tier (Net \-5+)  
Manifestations:  
Spiritual isolation: Righteous NPCs avoid eye contact  
Environmental dissonance: Sacred groves feel cold to you  
Whispering doubts: "What if power is the only truth?"  
GM Guidance: Never remove player agency. Instead of "You feel evil," use "A voice whispers that mercy is weakness—do you listen?" Provide clear paths to redemption.  
Critical Thresholds  
At 5 CP: Introduce Marked for Judgment effects subtly (natural 1s trigger minor consequences like equipment failure or missed opportunities)  
At 10 CP: Increase frequency of judgment effects; righteous NPCs may refuse aid  
At 15 CP: Divine judgment becomes imminent; provide urgent redemption opportunities  
At \+10 RP: Divine favor becomes evident (prophecies mention your name, natural phenomena aid your path)  
Redemption Arcs  
Design meaningful redemption paths:  
Acknowledgment: Character must verbally recognize their corruption  
Restitution: Make amends to those harmed (GM sets specific task)  
Change: Demonstrate new behavior through actions  
Divine Grace: After completing steps, allow Will save (DC 15 \+ CP) to remove 1d4 CP  
Example: Keturah (8 CP) seeks redemption:  
Acknowledges her reliance on forbidden magic before Enoch  
Uses her knowledge to purify a corrupted spring harming a village  
Refuses to cast forbidden spells for three sessions  
Succeeds at Will save, removing 3 CP and gaining 1 RP for sincere repentance  
Campaign Frameworks  
Framework 1: The Righteous Remnant (Survival Focus)  
Premise: Players are members of a Sethite community preserving ancient truths as corruption spreads. Their mission: protect the righteous line, preserve knowledge, and prepare for judgment.  
Key Elements:  
Base of operations: Hidden enclave with sacred grove  
Primary threats: Nephilim patrols seeking tribute, corrupted humans infiltrating the community  
Moral challenges: Who to shelter? When to fight vs. flee? How to maintain faith amid despair?  
Progression:  
Levels 1-5: Defend enclave from local threats  
Levels 6-10: Establish safe routes between righteous communities  
Levels 11-15: Recover lost prophecies about the coming judgment  
Levels 16-20: Prepare final sanctuary as rains begin  
Session Zero Questions:  
What is your character's role in the community? (Elder, scout, healer, scribe)  
What knowledge or skill do you bring to preserve?  
What personal connection keeps you committed to righteousness?  
Sample Adventure: The Lost Scroll  
Hook: An elder reveals a fragment of Enoch's writings mentioning a hidden archive.  
Challenges:  
Navigate flooded plains (Survival DC 20\)  
Decipher celestial markers (Occultism DC 22\)  
Resist temptation to use forbidden knowledge found in the archive (Will DC 18\)  
Protect recovered scrolls from corrupted seekers  
Rewards: 2 RP per character, prophetic insight about the Flood's timing, community gratitude  
Framework 2: Chains of Freedom (Rebellion Focus)  
Premise: Players lead resistance against Nephilim oppression, freeing human communities from giant tyranny while avoiding corruption themselves.  
Key Elements:  
Base of operations: Mobile camp among Wanderer tribes  
Primary threats: Nephilim warlords, human collaborators, internal moral compromises  
Moral challenges: When does resistance become vengeance? How to avoid becoming what you fight?  
Progression:  
Levels 1-5: Free small villages from tribute demands  
Levels 6-10: Unite scattered resistance cells  
Levels 11-15: Confront a major Nephilim stronghold  
Levels 16-20: Create lasting freedom before the Flood  
Session Zero Questions:  
What personal experience drove you to resist?  
What line will you never cross, even to win?  
Who depends on your success?  
Sample Adventure: The Anakim's Chain  
Hook: A village is forced to provide children as thralls to an Anakim noble who wears sacred chains as trophies.  
Challenges:  
Infiltrate the noble's fortress (Stealth DC 24\)  
Free prisoners without triggering alarms  
Confront the noble: fight, negotiate, or expose his corruption to other giants  
Return the sacred chains to their rightful owners  
Rewards: 1 RP per character for protecting innocents, alliance with freed thralls, moral dilemma resolution  
Framework 3: The Prophet's Path (Prophecy Focus)  
Premise: Players serve as messengers and interpreters of divine warnings about the coming judgment, guiding communities toward righteousness.  
Key Elements:  
Base of operations: Mobile, following prophetic guidance  
Primary threats: Disbelief, corruption tempting "practical" solutions, spiritual attacks  
Moral challenges: How to deliver hard truths with compassion? When to intervene vs. let consequences teach?  
Progression:  
Levels 1-5: Interpret local signs and warnings  
Levels 6-10: Gather scattered righteous communities  
Levels 11-15: Receive visions of the Ark's construction  
Levels 16-20: Prepare final warnings as judgment approaches  
Session Zero Questions:  
How did you receive your prophetic calling?  
What personal doubt challenges your faith?  
Who is your anchor when visions become overwhelming?  
Sample Adventure: The Dream of Waters  
Hook: A community leader has nightmares of rising waters but dismisses them as fear.  
Challenges:  
Interpret the dream correctly (Religion DC 22\)  
Convince skeptical elders without causing panic (Diplomacy DC 24\)  
Guide community to higher ground before flash floods hit  
Comfort those who lost homes while affirming divine purpose  
Rewards: 2 RP per character, strengthened community faith, prophetic insight about larger patterns  
Framework 4: The Watcher's Shadow (Corruption Focus)  
Premise: Players begin with ties to forbidden knowledge but seek redemption. This framework requires mature players committed to redemption arcs.  
Key Elements:  
Base of operation: Hidden sanctuary with righteous mentor  
Primary threats: Former allies seeking to reclaim them, internal corruption struggles  
Moral challenges: Can forbidden knowledge be redeemed? How to atone for past actions?  
Progression:  
Levels 1-5: Resist corruption while using knowledge sparingly  
Levels 6-10: Confront former mentors/corrupted allies  
Levels 11-15: Purify corrupted sites using redeemed knowledge  
Levels 16-20: Achieve full redemption or face judgment  
Critical Safeguards:  
Session Zero must establish boundaries and redemption focus  
GM must provide clear, achievable redemption paths  
Never glorify corruption; always show its costs  
Balance corruption mechanics with hope  
Sample Adventure: The Cleansing Fire  
Hook: A former ally uses forbidden knowledge to control a village.  
Challenges:  
Confront ally without becoming what you oppose  
Free villagers from mental control (Will saves)  
Choose: Destroy the corrupted knowledge source or redeem it  
Help community heal spiritually  
Rewards: 1 RP per character for resisting corruption, redeemed knowledge item, ally's potential redemption  
Handling Divine Elements with Reverence  
Portraying the Most High  
Guidelines:  
Never give the Creator human flaws or limitations  
Divine actions reflect perfect justice and mercy  
Use intermediaries (angels, prophets, visions) rather than direct appearance  
Focus on divine effects rather than physical description: "A presence fills the grove—not visible, but felt in every heart"  
Divine Communication Methods:  
Dreams/Vision: Symbolic messages requiring interpretation  
Nature Signs: Unusual weather patterns, animal behavior  
Prophetic Words: Through righteous NPCs like Enoch  
Inner Conviction: Strong moral certainty during dilemmas  
Scriptural Truth: References to preserved ancient teachings  
Working with Biblical Figures  
Enoch the Prophet  
Portrayal: Wise elder with deep connection to the divine, burdened by visions of judgment but hopeful for righteousness  
Role: Guide and mentor, not combatant. Provides wisdom, not weapons.  
Key Quote: "I have walked with the Most High and seen what is to come. The path is narrow, but those who walk it will not be abandoned."  
Noah and His Family  
Portrayal: Faithful builders facing ridicule and danger, focused on their divine task  
Role: Symbol of hope and obedience. Players may protect them or deliver supplies.  
Critical Boundary: Never depict Noah as doubting or failing in his mission. His faith is unwavering.  
Methuselah and Lamech  
Portrayal: Elders preserving knowledge and righteousness across centuries  
Role: Living libraries of antediluvian wisdom, connecting players to ancient truths  
Depicting the Watchers Respectfully  
Focus on Their Role, Not Graphic Details  
Azazel: Emphasize his teachings on warfare as corruption of human ingenuity, not glorification of violence  
Semyaza: Frame enchantments as perversion of human connection, not romanticized magic  
Baraqel: Present astrology as misplaced trust in stars over Creator  
Never Depict:  
Sexual details of Watcher-human unions (state factually: "They took wives from among the daughters of men")  
Graphic descriptions of punishments (Azazel "bound in darkness" suffices)  
Sympathetic portrayal of rebellion against divine order  
Instead Emphasize:  
The tragedy of their fall from grace  
The consequences of their actions on humanity  
The hope of ultimate judgment and restoration  
Managing the Flood as Campaign Conclusion  
Theological Framework  
The Flood is not random destruction but purposeful judgment with redemptive purpose:  
Justice: Addresses pervasive corruption threatening creation's integrity  
Mercy: Preserves righteous remnant (Noah's family) and all living creatures  
Covenant: Establishes pattern of judgment followed by grace (rainbow covenant)  
Hope: Points toward ultimate restoration beyond judgment  
Campaign Conclusion Options  
For Righteous Characters (Net \+5+):  
Witness the Ark's completion  
Receive divine assurance of preservation (spiritual, not physical)  
Pass knowledge to post-Flood generations through visions  
Ascend like Enoch if particularly faithful  
For Balanced Characters (Net \-4 to \+4):  
Face judgment but receive mercy through intercession  
Experience purification rather than destruction  
Given final opportunity for repentance  
Legacy continues through influence on others  
For Corrupted Characters (Net \-5+):  
Confront consequences of choices with dignity  
Opportunity for last-minute repentance (Will save DC 25\)  
If unrepentant: "The waters rise around you. In your final moments, you understand the weight of your choices."  
Never depict eternal punishment graphically; focus on narrative closure  
Final Session Structure  
Gathering Storm (Session Start):  
Rain begins falling continuously  
Animals gather in pairs  
Righteous NPCs prepare final messages  
Final Choices (Middle):  
Each character faces one last moral decision  
Opportunities for redemption, sacrifice, or legacy-building  
Divine visions reveal personal significance  
The Waters Rise (Climax):  
Describe judgment with solemn reverence, not horror  
Focus on emotional/spiritual resolution, not physical details  
Righteous characters feel peace; corrupted face consequences  
Beyond Judgment (Conclusion):  
Vision of the rainbow covenant  
Legacy statements: "Your choices preserved knowledge that will guide generations"  
Hope for restoration: "Though this world passes, the promise remains"  
Transition to post-Flood campaign if desired  
Practical GM Tools  
Session Zero Checklist  
□ Establish campaign framework and tone expectations  
□ Review Soul Economy mechanics and tracking method  
□ Discuss boundaries regarding biblical content  
□ Confirm player comfort with moral/spiritual themes  
□ Set expectations for redemption arcs and consequences  
□ Agree on handling of divine elements  
□ Establish communication for discomfort during play  
Quick Reference: Soul Economy Triggers  
Situation  
RP Gain  
CP Gain  
Protecting innocents  
1 RP  
—  
Showing mercy  
1 RP  
—  
Keeping oaths  
1 RP  
—  
Using forbidden magic  
—  
Risk (DC 11+spell level)  
Breaking vows  
—  
1 CP  
Cruelty beyond need  
—  
1 CP  
Repenting corruption  
—  
Remove 1 CP (after restitution)  
Righteous service

—  
Adventure Creation Template  
1\. Divine Purpose  
What truth does this adventure reveal? (e.g., "Mercy requires courage")  
2\. Moral Choice  
What meaningful decision will players face? (e.g., "Spare a corrupted thrall or execute to prevent future harm?")  
3\. Environmental Storytelling  
How does the setting reflect the theme? (e.g., "Flooded fields show judgment's approach; a single blooming flower represents hope")  
4\. Soul Economy Integration  
How do choices affect RP/CP? (e.g., "Mercy grants 1 RP; execution grants 1 CP")  
5\. Hope Element  
Where is light in this darkness? (e.g., "The thrall's child offers a clay token of gratitude")  
Handling Sensitive Moments  
If a player feels uncomfortable:  
Pause the game immediately  
Ask privately: "Would you like to adjust or skip this scene?"  
Offer alternatives: "We can fade to black and describe the outcome"  
Never require participation in spiritually distressing content  
If theological questions arise:  
Acknowledge: "This is a profound question"  
Refocus: "Within our game's context..."  
Suggest: "This might be worth discussing further after the session"  
Respect boundaries: Never debate personal beliefs at the table  
Conclusion: Stewarding Sacred Stories  
As GM of Nephilim Wars, you are entrusted with more than game mechanics—you steward narratives that have shaped human understanding of justice, mercy, and divine purpose for millennia. Your table becomes a space where players can explore profound questions: What does righteousness require? How do we maintain hope in dark times? What legacy will we leave?  
Remember these guiding principles:  
Reverence over sensationalism: Honor the sacred sources  
Hope over despair: Even judgment contains purpose and promise  
Agency over coercion: Let players discover truth through choice  
Community over isolation: Build shared understanding at your table  
The antediluvian world is not defined by its corruption but by the righteous who preserved light within it. Your campaign should reflect this truth. When the final rains fall and the waters rise, may your players carry not just memories of adventures, but insights about courage, faith, and the enduring promise that follows even the deepest judgment.  
"The waters shall no more become a flood to destroy all flesh... While the earth remains, seedtime and harvest, cold and heat, summer and winter, day and night, shall not cease." — Genesis 8:21-22 (paraphrased for narrative context)  
Chapter 12: The Antediluvian World  
Introduction: A World Before the Flood  
The antediluvian world was not merely an earlier version of our own—it was a fundamentally different creation, where the boundaries between heaven and earth were thinner, where lifespans stretched for centuries, and where the consequences of rebellion against divine order manifested in tangible, world-altering ways. This was the world that existed from creation until the Great Flood, a period spanning over 1,600 years during which humanity multiplied, civilizations rose, and corruption spread until "every intent of the thoughts of \[humanity's\] heart was only evil continually" (Genesis 6:5).  
This chapter presents a comprehensive gazetteer of the antediluvian world as it existed in the generations immediately preceding the Flood. Drawing from biblical accounts, the Book of Enoch, the Book of Jubilees, and other ancient sources, this guide provides Game Masters and players with the geographical, cultural, and spiritual landscape in which their adventures will unfold. The world described here is not a fantasy construct but a respectful reconstruction of the biblical narrative's setting—a world of bronze-age technology, tribal societies, emerging urban centers, and the looming presence of Nephilim giants.  
Every location in this chapter serves the campaign's central themes: the struggle between righteousness and corruption, the weight of moral choices, and the approaching certainty of divine judgment. Regions are described not merely as backdrops but as active participants in the spiritual drama—places where characters can find sanctuary or temptation, community or isolation, hope or despair.  
The Geography of the Antediluvian World  
The Four Rivers of Eden  
According to Genesis 2:10-14, a single river flowed out of Eden and divided into four headwaters. These rivers formed the geographical heart of the antediluvian world and remained significant landmarks even after humanity's expulsion from the garden:  
Pishon — "The river that winds through the entire land of Havilah, where there is gold" (Genesis 2:11). This river flowed through regions rich in precious metals and gemstones, making it a center of early metallurgy and trade. By the time of the Nephilim Wars, the Pishon's banks were dotted with Cainite mining settlements and Nephilim fortresses controlling access to mineral wealth.  
Gihon — "The river that winds through the entire land of Cush" (Genesis 2:13). Flowing through fertile valleys and dense forests, the Gihon supported abundant wildlife and agricultural communities. Sethite enclaves often established themselves along its tributaries, where the land remained relatively untouched by giant influence.  
Tigris (Hiddekel) — "The river that flows east of Assyria" (Genesis 2:14). The Tigris became a major artery of civilization, with settlements clustering along its banks. By the antediluvian period's end, it flowed past the great Cainite city of Enoch and several Nephilim strongholds.  
Euphrates (Perath) — The fourth river, flowing through plains that would later become the cradle of post-Flood civilizations. During the Nephilim Wars, the Euphrates region was contested territory between human settlements and giant territories.  
GM Note: These rivers have not yet been joined by the catastrophic geological changes that would follow the Flood. They flow in patterns recognizable to students of ancient geography, but the surrounding landscapes remain pristine and unspoiled by millennia of human alteration.  
Major Regions  
The antediluvian world can be divided into several major regions, each with distinct characteristics, dominant populations, and spiritual atmospheres:  
The Land of Nod  
Location: East of Eden (Genesis 4:16)  
Dominant Population: Cainites, with Nephilim oversight  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Innovation mixed with spiritual distance; the birthplace of urban civilization but also of humanity's separation from divine presence  
After Cain's exile, he established the first permanent settlement in the land of Nod. This region became the cradle of Cainite civilization, where Jabal pioneered tent-making and herding, Jubal developed music and arts, and Tubal-Cain mastered metallurgy. The land of Nod features rolling plains suitable for grazing, mineral-rich hills supporting early mining, and defensible positions where the first cities arose.  
Key Features:  
Fertile valleys supporting agriculture and herding  
Mineral deposits of copper, tin, and occasional iron  
Defensible hills where early settlements clustered  
Trade routes connecting disparate communities  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Sethite elder seeks knowledge of Tubal-Cain's metallurgical techniques to better defend against Nephilim raids  
A musical instrument crafted by Jubal's descendants is said to calm even giant hearts—can it be recovered from a Nephilim warlord who stole it?  
The original site of Cain's first settlement is rumored to hold his mark in stone—what power or curse does it contain?  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Learning practical skills without embracing Cainite spiritual distance; protecting knowledge from corrupted uses  
CP Risks: Embracing innovation without moral consideration; serving Nephilim masters for technological advancement  
The Mountain of Hermon  
Location: Northern regions, where the Watchers descended (1 Enoch 6:6)  
Dominant Population: Initially Watchers and their offspring; later Nephilim strongholds and corrupted settlements  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Profound corruption; the site of humanity's greatest spiritual betrayal  
Mount Hermon stands as a stark reminder of the Watchers' rebellion. It was here that Semyaza and 199 other angels swore their oath to take human wives, initiating the chain of events that would corrupt the earth. The mountain itself bears the spiritual weight of this transgression—storms gather unnaturally around its peaks, and the very stone seems to reject righteous presence.  
Key Features:  
The Oath Site: A flat plateau where the 200 Watchers bound themselves to their decision. The ground remains barren despite surrounding fertility.  
Watcher Ruins: Stone circles and altars where forbidden teachings were first shared. These sites pulse with residual corruption.  
Nephilim Citadels: Massive fortresses carved into the mountainside, housing the first generation of giants and their descendants.  
Corrupted Valleys: The surrounding lands suffer environmental degradation—twisted vegetation, poisoned water sources, and unnatural wildlife.  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Sethite prophet receives a vision commanding the purification of the oath site before the Flood  
Ancient tablets hidden in the ruins contain prophecies about the coming judgment—can they be recovered without succumbing to corruption?  
A faction of Watchers seeks to break their bonds and return to the mountain—can they be stopped or redeemed?  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Purifying corrupted sites; recovering and destroying forbidden texts; rescuing prisoners from Nephilim citadels  
CP Risks: High corruption risk (DC 13 flat check per hour spent); studying ruins automatically gains 1 CP per session; righteous characters suffer \-2 penalty to all checks while on the mountain  
The Sethite Valleys  
Location: Scattered hidden valleys throughout the world, particularly near the Gihon river  
Dominant Population: Sethites and righteous humans  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Preservation and hope; communities maintaining ancient truths amid growing darkness  
While Cainites built cities and Nephilim established fortresses, the descendants of Seth withdrew into hidden valleys where they could preserve the knowledge and worship passed down from Adam. These communities are not primitive—they maintain sophisticated oral traditions, astronomical observations, and agricultural techniques—but they deliberately avoid the technological innovations that accompanied Cainite civilization.  
Key Features:  
Hidden Locations: Valleys accessible only through narrow passes known to community members  
Sacred Groves: Ancient trees where generations have worshipped and received divine guidance  
Memory Stones: Monuments inscribed with genealogies and key teachings, maintained by community elders  
Agricultural Terraces: Sustainable farming practices that work with the land rather than dominating it  
Watchtowers: Simple structures for spotting approaching threats, particularly Nephilim patrols  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Sethite community's sacred grove is threatened by a spreading corruption—can it be saved?  
An elder is compiling the final genealogies before the Flood; the party must protect them from those who would destroy this knowledge  
A young Sethite feels called to leave the valley and warn other communities of the coming judgment  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Abundant—teaching righteous ways, preserving knowledge, protecting the community, performing sacred rituals  
CP Risks: Minimal within the valleys themselves, but leaving on missions carries normal risks; bringing corrupted items or people into the valley risks community-wide spiritual contamination  
The Cainite Cities  
Location: Primarily in the land of Nod and along major river systems  
Dominant Population: Cainites, with Nephilim oversight and human thralls  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Ambition and innovation mixed with moral compromise; civilization advancing at spiritual cost  
Cainite cities represent humanity's first attempts at urban civilization. The greatest of these is Enoch, named for Cain's son and built as a monument to human achievement. These cities feature bronze-reinforced walls, organized marketplaces, specialized districts for different crafts, and emerging social hierarchies. They are centers of innovation where Tubal-Cain's metallurgical advances, Jubal's musical developments, and Jabal's agricultural techniques flourish.  
Key Features:  
Bronze Walls: Massive fortifications protecting against both wilderness threats and Nephilim demands  
Market Districts: Bustling centers of trade where goods from distant regions exchange hands  
Craftsmen Quarters: Areas dedicated to specific trades—metalworking, masonry, textiles, music  
Nephilim Precincts: Sections of the city controlled by giant overlords, where human thralls serve and tribute is collected  
Underground Networks: Secret passages and hidden chambers where resistance groups and Sorcerer Clans operate  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Cainite artisan has developed a new bronze alloy that could revolutionize weapon-making—but Nephilim overseers demand exclusive control  
A resistance cell seeks to free human thralls from Nephilim precincts without triggering city-wide reprisals  
Ancient texts hidden in the city's foundations contain knowledge predating the Watchers' fall—can they be recovered?  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Using innovation for righteous purposes; protecting the vulnerable within the city; preserving knowledge from corrupted uses  
CP Risks: High—daily exposure to corrupted practices; temptation to use forbidden knowledge for practical benefits; social pressure to conform to Nephilim demands  
The Wanderer Steppes  
Location: Vast grasslands between settled regions  
Dominant Population: Wanderer tribes, nomadic herders, and independent survivors  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Freedom and self-reliance; connection to the natural world without institutional religion  
The steppes represent the third path between Sethite withdrawal and Cainite urbanization. Here, nomadic tribes follow herds of livestock, moving with the seasons and maintaining independence from both giant overlords and settled civilizations. These communities value practical skills, oral traditions, and direct experience of the divine through nature rather than through formal worship.  
Key Features:  
Migratory Patterns: Tribes follow established routes between seasonal grazing lands  
Tribal Territories: Informal regions controlled by specific tribes, marked by natural landmarks rather than borders  
Meeting Grounds: Neutral sites where different tribes gather for trade, marriage arrangements, and conflict resolution  
Sacred Sites: Natural features (unusual rock formations, springs, groves) where tribes believe the divine is particularly present  
Hidden Valleys: Sheltered areas where tribes retreat during harsh weather or to avoid Nephilim patrols  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Wanderer tribe's sacred spring has been corrupted by a nearby Nephilim outpost—can it be purified?  
Different tribes are preparing for war over grazing rights; the party must mediate before bloodshed occurs  
A legendary Wanderer hero is said to be buried in a hidden valley, their weapons still waiting for a worthy successor  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Living in harmony with creation; protecting the vulnerable; maintaining freedom without exploiting others  
CP Risks: Moderate—temptation to use harsh survival methods; pressure to form alliances with corrupted groups for protection; isolation from righteous communities  
The Nephilim Territories  
Location: Strategic positions throughout the world—mountain passes, river crossings, fertile valleys  
Dominant Population: Nephilim and their giant-kin descendants, with human thralls and servants  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Oppression and corruption; the exercise of power without restraint  
Nephilim territories are not unified under a single ruler but consist of numerous warlord domains, each controlling key resources or strategic locations. These territories feature massive fortifications scaled to giant proportions, human settlements that exist only to serve giant needs, and landscapes scarred by giant activity. The spiritual atmosphere is heavy with corruption—the result of generations of tyranny, forbidden practices, and the lingering influence of Watcher teachings.  
Key Features:  
Giant Fortresses: Massive stone structures with walls 30 feet thick and gates requiring multiple humans to open  
Thrall Settlements: Human communities existing only to serve giant needs—farming, crafting, cleaning  
Tribute Collection Points: Locations where surrounding human settlements must deliver regular payments of food, goods, and sometimes people  
Watcher Ruins: Ancient sites where forbidden knowledge was first taught, now maintained as centers of corrupted power  
Training Grounds: Areas where Nephilim warriors practice combat and train human thralls as soldiers  
Adventure Hooks:  
A thrall settlement plans rebellion but needs outside assistance to succeed  
A Nephilim warlord possesses an ancient artifact that could turn the tide against giant oppression—if it can be stolen  
A faction of younger Nephilim questions their people's path and seeks contact with righteous humans  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Freeing thralls; sabotaging oppressive systems; protecting the vulnerable; offering mercy to repentant giants  
CP Risks: Extreme—constant exposure to corruption; temptation to use harsh methods against oppressors; risk of possession by disembodied spirits (Refa'im)  
The Underworld Caverns  
Location: Extensive cave systems beneath mountainous regions  
Dominant Population: Horim clans, with occasional Rephaim visitors  
Spiritual Atmosphere: Isolation and mystery; connection to death and the unseen  
The Horim, cave-dwelling giant-kin, established their communities in vast underground networks that few surface-dwellers ever see. These caverns feature natural wonders—crystal formations, underground rivers, massive chambers—and Horim adaptations like stone-carved dwellings, fungal farms, and sophisticated ventilation systems. The spiritual atmosphere reflects the Horim's connection to the underworld and their ambiguous relationship with death.  
Key Features:  
Crystal Caverns: Chambers filled with luminous crystals that provide light and are used for divination  
Underground Rivers: Water sources that sustain Horim communities and connect different cave systems  
Stone Cities: Horim settlements carved directly into cavern walls, with multi-level dwellings and communal spaces  
Burial Chambers: Sacred areas where Horim inter their dead, believing the underworld offers protection from surface corruption  
Echo Halls: Massive chambers where sound carries for miles, used for communication and ceremonial purposes  
Adventure Hooks:  
A Horim clan possesses ancient knowledge about the earth's structure that could predict seismic events preceding the Flood  
A Rephaim death cult seeks to desecrate Horim burial chambers to harness spiritual power  
An underground river is poisoning surface communities—can its source be found and purified?  
Soul Economy Considerations:  
RP Opportunities: Learning from Horim wisdom without embracing their death-focused spirituality; protecting underground ecosystems; recovering knowledge preserved from surface corruption  
CP Risks: High—proximity to death cults and Rephaim influence; temptation to use underworld knowledge for selfish purposes; isolation from righteous communities  
Major Settlements  
Enoch: The First City  
Location: Land of Nod, near the Tigris river  
Population: Approximately 5,000 Cainites, 200 Nephilim overseers, 1,000 human thralls  
Government: Council of master craftsmen led by a Nephilim governor  
Notable Features: Bronze-reinforced walls, central marketplace, Tubal-Cain's forge, Jubal's music hall, Nephilim citadel  
Enoch stands as humanity's first great urban achievement, a testament to Cainite ingenuity and organization. Named for Cain's son, the city represents both the heights of human civilization and its separation from divine presence. Within its walls, innovation flourishes—new metallurgical techniques, architectural advances, musical developments—but so does moral compromise as residents navigate life under Nephilim oversight.  
Districts:  
The Foundry District: Centered around Tubal-Cain's original forge, now expanded into a massive industrial complex producing weapons, tools, and construction materials  
The Artisan Quarter: Home to specialized craftspeople—potters, weavers, leatherworkers, and other trades supporting urban life  
The Market Circle: Central plaza where goods from across the region are bought and sold, surrounded by merchant stalls and counting houses  
The Nephilim Precinct: Walled section housing the giant overseers and their human administrators, featuring structures scaled to giant proportions  
The Music Quarter: Area dedicated to Jubal's legacy, with performance spaces, instrument makers, and schools teaching musical arts  
The Undercroft: Network of tunnels and chambers beneath the city where resistance groups, Sorcerer Clans, and other hidden elements operate  
Adventure Hooks:  
The Nephilim governor demands increased tribute, threatening to destroy the city if refused—can the council find another solution?  
A master smith has developed a new bronze alloy that could revolutionize warfare—but using it risks drawing unwanted Nephilim attention  
Ancient texts hidden in the city's foundations contain knowledge from before the Watchers' fall—can they be recovered without triggering Nephilim security?  
Soul Economy in Enoch:  
RP Opportunities: Using urban resources to help the vulnerable; preserving knowledge from corrupted uses; protecting the city's independence from excessive Nephilim control  
CP Risks: Constant exposure to compromised morality; temptation to use forbidden knowledge for practical benefits; social pressure to prioritize innovation over righteousness  
The Hidden Valley of Seth  
Location: Remote valley accessible only through a narrow pass known to community members  
Population: Approximately 300 Sethites  
Government: Council of elders led by a prophet  
Notable Features: Sacred grove, memory stones, agricultural terraces, watchtowers, library of clay tablets  
This secluded community represents the purest preservation of the righteous line in the antediluvian world. Here, descendants of Seth maintain the knowledge, worship practices, and moral teachings passed down from Adam through generations of faithful preservation. The valley's isolation has protected it from Nephilim influence and Cainite corruption, but this protection comes at the cost of limited resources and constant vigilance.  
Key Locations:  
The Sacred Grove: Ancient trees where generations have worshipped, received visions, and held important ceremonies. The grove is said to be especially receptive to divine presence.  
The Memory Stones: Large stones inscribed with genealogies, key teachings, and historical records. Elders regularly add new inscriptions to preserve current events for future generations.  
The Elder Council Chamber: Simple stone structure where community leaders meet to make decisions, settle disputes, and receive guidance.  
The Tablet Library: Collection of clay tablets containing preserved knowledge—genealogies, prophecies, agricultural techniques, medical knowledge, and spiritual teachings.  
The Watchtowers: Simple structures positioned at valley entrances and high points, manned by rotating guards watching for approaching threats.  
Adventure Hooks:  
A prophet receives a vision commanding the community to send messengers warning other settlements of the coming judgment  
The valley's sacred spring begins running dry—divine sign or natural disaster requiring investigation?  
A young Sethite feels called to leave the valley and seek knowledge that could help preserve their teachings through the coming Flood  
Soul Economy in the Hidden Valley:  
RP Opportunities: Abundant—daily worship, teaching younger generations, preserving knowledge, protecting the community, performing sacred rituals  
CP Risks: Minimal within the valley, but missions outside carry normal risks; bringing corrupted items or people into the valley risks community-wide spiritual contamination  
Mount Hermon Citadel  
Location: Summit and slopes of Mount Hermon  
Population: Approximately 100 Nephilim (first and second generation), 500 Rephaim warriors, 2,000 human thralls  
Government: Nephilim warlord claiming descent from Azazel  
Notable Features: Stone fortresses carved into mountainside, Watcher ruins, corrupted valleys, training grounds  
This massive complex represents the heart of Nephilim power and corruption in the northern regions. Built around the site where the 200 Watchers swore their oath, the citadel serves as both military stronghold and center of forbidden knowledge. The warlord ruling here claims direct descent from Azazel and uses this lineage to justify absolute authority over surrounding territories.  
Key Locations:  
The Oath Plateau: Flat area where the 200 Watchers bound themselves to their decision. The ground remains barren despite surrounding fertility, and spiritual energy is palpable.  
The Warlord's Fortress: Massive stone structure housing the Nephilim ruler, his family, and elite guards. Features throne room, war council chamber, and private quarters scaled to giant proportions.  
The Watcher Archives: Chambers containing ancient texts, star charts, and other materials from the original Watcher teachings. Heavily guarded and corrupted.  
The Thrall Quarters: Crowded living areas for human servants, featuring minimal comforts and constant surveillance.  
The Training Grounds: Expansive areas where Nephilim warriors practice combat and train human thralls as soldiers. Features weapon racks, obstacle courses, and sparring arenas.  
Adventure Hooks:  
The warlord possesses an ancient artifact that could be turned against Nephilim forces—if it can be stolen  
A faction of younger Nephilim questions their people's path and seeks contact with righteous humans  
The citadel's corruption is spreading to surrounding lands, poisoning water sources and twisting wildlife  
Soul Economy at Mount Hermon:  
RP Opportunities: Freeing thralls; sabotaging oppressive systems; recovering and destroying forbidden texts; offering mercy to repentant giants  
CP Risks: Extreme—automatic 1 CP per hour spent in the citadel; studying archives requires DC 15 flat check per hour (failure gains additional CP); righteous characters suffer \-2 penalty to all checks while within the citadel's influence  
Travel and Trade Routes  
Major Trade Routes  
The Cainite Trade Road  
Route: Enoch → Southern settlements → Eastern territories  
Distance: Approximately 300 miles  
Features: Well-maintained path with regular waystations, guarded by Cainite militia and Nephilim patrols  
Dangers: Nephilim toll collectors demanding tribute, bandits preying on merchants, occasional giant patrols  
Trade Goods: Bronze tools and weapons, musical instruments, crafted goods, information  
The Wanderer Paths  
Route: Network of trails connecting nomadic territories across the steppes  
Distance: Varies by specific path  
Features: Hidden trails known only to Wanderer tribes, marked by subtle natural signs  
Dangers: Harsh weather, predatory animals, territorial conflicts between tribes  
Trade Goods: Livestock, hides, dairy products, herbal remedies, oral news  
The River Route  
Route: Follows the Tigris and Euphrates rivers between settlements  
Distance: Approximately 200 miles of navigable waterway  
Features: Riverboats and rafts transporting goods, fishing communities along banks, natural harbors  
Dangers: River monsters, sudden storms, Nephilim river patrols demanding tribute  
Trade Goods: Grain, fish, pottery, clay tablets, perishable goods  
The Mountain Pass  
Route: Narrow paths through mountain ranges connecting isolated valleys  
Distance: Approximately 50 miles of difficult terrain  
Features: Treacherous paths requiring climbing skills, hidden caves for shelter, panoramic views for spotting threats  
Dangers: Landslides, falling rocks, mountain predators, Nephilim outposts controlling key passages  
Trade Goods: Rare minerals, mountain herbs, crystal formations, specialized knowledge  
Travel Times and Challenges  
Overland Travel (Normal Pace):  
Plains/Grasslands: 24 miles per day  
Forests: 18 miles per day  
Hills: 15 miles per day  
Mountains: 10 miles per day  
Swamps/Flood Plains: 8 miles per day  
River Travel:  
Downstream (with current): 30 miles per day  
Upstream (against current): 12 miles per day  
Still water (lakes): 20 miles per day  
Environmental Challenges:  
Weather: Storms can halt travel for days; extreme heat or cold reduces daily distance by 25%  
Seasonal Changes: Spring floods make rivers impassable; winter snow blocks mountain passes  
Nephilim Patrols: High-traffic routes may require stealth or payment of tribute to pass safely  
Corrupted Areas: Travel through spiritually contaminated regions requires Will saves (DC 15\) or gain 1 CP per day  
Communication Networks  
Messenger Systems:  
Cainite Cities: Professional runners carrying clay tablet messages between settlements  
Wanderer Tribes: Oral messages passed between tribes during seasonal gatherings  
Sethite Communities: Trusted travelers carrying sealed clay envelopes with important news  
Nephilim Territories: Human thralls running messages between outposts and fortresses  
Signal Methods:  
Smoke Signals: Used by Wanderer tribes to communicate across long distances  
Horn Calls: Specific patterns indicating different messages (danger, all clear, gathering)  
Fire Beacons: Chain of signal fires on high points to warn of approaching threats  
Star Signals: Horim use crystal formations to reflect starlight in coded patterns  
Cultural Practices and Daily Life  
Sethite Communities  
Daily Rhythms:  
Morning: Communal prayer and scripture recitation, followed by assigned work duties  
Midday: Shared meal with readings from preserved texts, rest period during hottest hours  
Afternoon: Continued work, with elders teaching younger generations  
Evening: Family time, storytelling, preparation for next day  
Night: Watch rotations, personal prayer and reflection  
Social Structure:  
Elders: Respected for wisdom and spiritual insight, responsible for teaching and decision-making  
Prophets: Individuals receiving divine visions and guidance, highly respected but not always understood  
Teachers: Dedicated to preserving and transmitting knowledge to younger generations  
Laborers: Farmers, herders, craftspeople supporting community needs  
Watchers: Those assigned to guard duties and scout surrounding areas  
Spiritual Practices:  
Sacrifice: Regular offerings of first fruits, livestock, or crafted items as thanksgiving and atonement  
Prayer: Individual and communal communication with the Most High, often at specific times of day  
Scripture Recitation: Regular reading and memorization of preserved teachings and genealogies  
Sabbath Observance: One day in seven set aside for rest, worship, and community gathering  
Pilgrimage: Periodic journeys to significant sites (sacred groves, memory stones, ancestral burial places)  
Cainite Urban Life  
Daily Rhythms:  
Early Morning: Artisans begin work, markets open, laborers report to assigned tasks  
Mid-Morning: Peak business activity, guild meetings, administrative functions  
Midday: Meal break, often taken at workplace or in market eateries  
Afternoon: Continued work, with merchants conducting business and craftspeople completing projects  
Evening: Social gatherings, entertainment (music, storytelling), family time  
Night: Guard rotations, some businesses remain open, wealthy districts host private events  
Social Structure:  
Master Craftsmen: Leaders of guilds and major workshops, wealthy and influential  
Merchants: Traders controlling flow of goods and information, often politically connected  
Artisans: Skilled craftspeople organized into guilds by trade  
Laborers: Unskilled workers performing essential but low-status tasks  
Thralls: Human servants under Nephilim control, performing menial tasks and serving giant needs  
Underclass: Criminals, outcasts, and those who have fallen from favor, surviving in shadows of society  
Cultural Practices:  
Guild Membership: Professional associations controlling training, standards, and business practices  
Market Days: Regular gatherings for trade, news exchange, and social interaction  
Festivals: Celebrations marking seasonal changes, successful harvests, or city achievements  
Music and Arts: Highly developed cultural expressions, particularly in Enoch where Jubal's legacy flourishes  
Innovation Competitions: Events where artisans showcase new techniques or inventions, with prizes and recognition  
Wanderer Tribal Life  
Daily Rhythms:  
Dawn: Breaking camp, preparing livestock for travel, morning meal  
Morning: Traveling with herds, scouts ranging ahead to find suitable grazing and water  
Midday: Rest during hottest hours, tending to animals, repairing equipment  
Afternoon: Continued travel, with hunters ranging out to supplement diet  
Evening: Setting up camp, evening meal, storytelling around fire  
Night: Guard rotations, stargazing, planning next day's route  
Social Structure:  
Chieftains: Leaders chosen for wisdom, strength, or spiritual insight, responsible for major decisions  
Elders: Respected for experience and knowledge of tribal history and traditions  
Warriors: Protectors of the tribe, skilled in combat and tracking  
Hunters: Providers supplementing herd products with wild game  
Healers: Those with knowledge of herbs, wounds, and spiritual ailments  
Storytellers: Keepers of oral history, genealogies, and cultural knowledge  
Cultural Practices:  
Seasonal Migration: Following established routes between grazing lands as seasons change  
Tribal Gatherings: Periodic meetings between related tribes for trade, marriage arrangements, and conflict resolution  
Rites of Passage: Ceremonies marking important life transitions (adulthood, marriage, leadership)  
Animal Husbandry: Deep connection to herds, with specific breeds developed for different purposes  
Star Navigation: Using celestial patterns for direction-finding and timekeeping  
Oral Tradition: Preserving history, laws, and knowledge through memorized stories and songs  
Nephilim Society  
Daily Rhythms:  
Morning: Military drills, inspection of thrall work, planning sessions  
Midday: Main meal, often featuring large quantities of meat and other resources  
Afternoon: Administrative duties, training of human soldiers, leisure activities  
Evening: Feasting, entertainment (often involving human performers), strategic discussions  
Night: Guard rotations, patrols, private meetings  
Social Structure:  
Warlords: Rulers of territories, often claiming descent from specific Watchers  
Nobles: High-ranking Nephilim serving as advisors, commanders, or administrators  
Warriors: Elite fighters forming the core of Nephilim military power  
Thrall Masters: Nephilim overseeing human servants and ensuring tribute collection  
Human Thralls: Servants performing essential tasks, from cooking and cleaning to skilled crafts  
Collaborators: Humans serving Nephilim willingly, often in administrative or supervisory roles  
Cultural Practices:  
Bloodlines: Emphasis on lineage and descent from specific Watchers as source of status and power  
Martial Prowess: High value placed on combat skill, physical strength, and military achievements  
Tribute System: Regular collection of resources from subject human settlements  
Forbidden Knowledge: Study and practice of Watcher teachings, particularly among nobility  
Giant Arts: Cultural expressions scaled to giant proportions—massive architecture, oversized weapons as art, booming music  
Human Sport: Using human thralls for entertainment—racing, fighting, performance  
The Changing World: Signs of the Coming Flood  
As the antediluvian period approaches its end, the world itself begins to show signs of the coming judgment. These changes are not merely environmental but spiritual, reflecting the corruption that has spread across creation:  
Environmental Changes  
Increasing Seismic Activity:  
Earthquakes become more frequent and severe  
New mountain ranges form; existing ones shift and crack  
Underground waters begin to rise, creating new springs and flooding lowlands  
Weather Patterns Shift:  
Storms become more violent and unpredictable  
Rain falls in unprecedented quantities, causing rivers to overflow  
Seasons become less distinct, with unseasonable weather becoming common  
Ecological Disruption:  
Animal behavior changes—predators become more aggressive, migration patterns shift  
Plant life shows signs of stress—crops fail, forests wither, new diseases appear  
Water sources become contaminated, requiring purification before use  
Spiritual Manifestations  
Increased Corruption Visibility:  
Corrupted areas show physical signs—twisted vegetation, discolored water, unnatural wildlife  
Disembodied spirits (Refa'im) manifest more frequently and boldly  
Forbidden rituals produce more powerful but unstable effects  
Divine Warnings:  
Prophets receive more frequent and detailed visions of the coming judgment  
Natural phenomena carry symbolic meaning—comets, eclipses, unusual celestial events  
Righteous individuals experience heightened spiritual sensitivity and guidance  
Moral Polarization:  
Communities become more clearly divided between righteous and corrupted  
Neutral positions become increasingly difficult to maintain  
Choices carry more immediate and visible consequences  
Timeline of Final Events  
50 Years Before the Flood:  
Environmental changes become noticeable but not alarming  
Prophetic warnings increase but are largely ignored  
Nephilim consolidation of power reaches its peak  
25 Years Before the Flood:  
Seismic activity and weather changes become disruptive  
Sethite communities begin preparing for judgment  
Noah receives initial instructions about the Ark  
10 Years Before the Flood:  
Environmental chaos becomes widespread  
Mass migrations begin as communities seek safety  
Ark construction begins, drawing ridicule and opposition  
5 Years Before the Flood:  
Continuous rain begins in some regions  
Animals begin gathering in pairs near Noah's location  
Final warnings issued to remaining communities  
1 Year Before the Flood:  
Rivers overflow their banks regularly  
Many settlements abandoned as uninhabitable  
Final preparations for the Ark completed  
The Flood:  
"All the springs of the great deep burst forth, and the floodgates of the heavens were opened" (Genesis 7:11)  
Forty days and nights of continuous rain  
Waters rise to cover even the highest mountains  
Adventure Seeds by Region  
Sethite Valleys  
The Last Genealogy  
An elder is compiling the final genealogies before the Flood, documenting the righteous line from Adam to Noah. Nephilim agents seek to destroy this record. The party must protect the elder and ensure the genealogies are completed and hidden safely.  
The Sacred Grove Threat  
The community's sacred grove—the site of generations of worship and divine encounters—begins to wither. Investigation reveals a corrupted spring feeding the grove's roots. The party must purify the spring without alerting nearby Nephilim patrols.  
The Prophet's Call  
A young Sethite receives a vision calling them to leave the valley and warn other communities of the coming judgment. The elders are divided—some see divine purpose, others fear for the youth's safety. The party must escort the prophet on their journey while protecting the valley from retaliation.  
Cainite Cities  
The Innovator's Dilemma  
A master smith has developed a bronze alloy that could revolutionize weapon-making, potentially allowing humans to stand against Nephilim more effectively. However, the Nephilim governor demands exclusive control of the innovation. The party must help the smith decide: surrender the knowledge, destroy it, or find a way to share it with righteous communities.  
The Resistance Cell  
A group of human thralls plans rebellion against their Nephilim overseers. They seek outside assistance to succeed without triggering city-wide reprisals that would endanger innocent residents. The party must help plan and execute the rebellion while minimizing collateral damage.  
The Ancient Archive  
Texts hidden in the city's foundations contain knowledge from before the Watchers' fall—agricultural techniques, medical knowledge, and spiritual teachings untainted by corruption. Recovering these texts could preserve crucial knowledge through the Flood, but the archive is guarded by Nephilim who recognize their value.  
Wanderer Steppes  
The Sacred Spring  
A Wanderer tribe's sacred spring—the source of their water and the site of their most important ceremonies—has been corrupted by a nearby Nephilim outpost's waste disposal. The tribe cannot relocate without losing their ancestral lands. The party must purify the spring or negotiate with the Nephilim for alternative solutions.  
Tribal Conflict  
Two Wanderer tribes prepare for war over grazing rights as environmental changes reduce available land. The conflict threatens to draw in Nephilim forces who would exploit the division. The party must mediate between the tribes and find a peaceful solution before bloodshed occurs.  
The Buried Hero  
Legend speaks of a Wanderer hero buried in a hidden valley, their weapons still waiting for a worthy successor. The weapons are said to be especially effective against Nephilim. Finding this burial site could provide crucial aid against giant oppression, but the valley is protected by natural hazards and possibly supernatural guardians.  
Nephilim Territories  
Thrall Rebellion  
A settlement of human thralls plans rebellion against their Nephilim masters. They have managed to hide weapons and coordinate their efforts, but they lack the military experience to succeed against trained Nephilim warriors. The party must train the thralls, plan the rebellion, and execute it with minimal casualties.  
The Reluctant Giant  
A younger Nephilim, disgusted by their people's tyranny and corruption, seeks contact with righteous humans. They possess valuable intelligence about Nephilim operations and weaknesses but fear retribution if discovered. The party must protect this informant while determining whether their conversion is genuine.  
The Stolen Artifact  
A Nephilim warlord possesses an ancient artifact that could turn the tide against giant oppression—a weapon blessed by early patriarchs or a tool that can breach Nephilim fortifications. Stealing this artifact would require infiltrating the warlord's fortress, avoiding detection, and escaping with the item before alarms are raised.  
Mount Hermon  
Purification Quest  
A Sethite prophet receives a vision commanding the purification of the oath site where the 200 Watchers swore their allegiance to corruption. The task seems impossible—the site is heavily guarded and saturated with spiritual contamination—but the prophet insists it must be attempted before the Flood.  
The Watcher Archives  
Ancient tablets hidden in the ruins contain prophecies about the coming judgment and instructions for surviving it. Recovering these texts could provide crucial guidance for righteous communities, but the archives are protected by corrupted guardians and filled with spiritual traps.  
The Bound Watcher  
Rumors persist that one of the original 200 Watchers remains bound near the mountain, unable to join his fellows in their imprisonment. This Watcher possesses unique knowledge about the coming Flood and how to prepare for it. Freeing or communicating with this being could provide invaluable insights—but at what spiritual cost?

Integrating the World into Your Campaign  
Starting Locations by Campaign Framework  
The Righteous Remnant:  
Begin in a Sethite valley, establishing the community and its values  
Early adventures involve protecting the valley from external threats  
Gradually expand to include contact with other righteous communities  
Chains of Freedom:  
Begin in a Nephilim-controlled settlement, experiencing oppression firsthand  
Early adventures involve small acts of resistance and building alliances  
Gradually expand to include coordinated rebellion efforts  
The Prophet's Path:  
Begin with a divine calling or vision, establishing the prophetic mission  
Early adventures involve interpreting signs and delivering warnings  
Gradually expand to include gathering scattered righteous communities  
The Watcher's Shadow:  
Begin with ties to forbidden knowledge but growing unease  
Early adventures involve using corrupted abilities for righteous purposes  
Gradually expand to include confrontation with former allies and redemption  
World Events Timeline  
Campaign Start (Year 1):  
Environmental changes begin but are not yet alarming  
Nephilim consolidation of power continues  
Righteous communities maintain isolation  
Mid-Campaign (Year 3-4):  
Seismic activity and weather changes become disruptive  
Prophetic warnings increase in frequency and detail  
Communities begin preparing for judgment  
Late Campaign (Year 5+):  
Environmental chaos becomes widespread  
Mass migrations begin as settlements become uninhabitable  
Final preparations for the Flood underway  
Adapting the World to Player Choices
