Develop detailed descriptions of their settlement  
Create NPCs representing different community roles  
Introduce challenges testing community values and resilience  
If Players Focus on Travel and Exploration:  
Create detailed descriptions of regions they visit  
Develop unique challenges and opportunities for each location  
Track changing conditions as environmental chaos increases  
If Players Focus on Spiritual Development:  
Create opportunities for divine encounters and revelations  
Develop moral dilemmas testing their faith and values  
Track their spiritual growth through RP gains and divine favor  
If Players Focus on Resistance Against Oppression:  
Develop detailed Nephilim territories and operations  
Create opportunities for sabotage, rescue, and rebellion  
Track the impact of their actions on oppressed communities  

# **Chapter 13: Bestiary**

## **Introduction: Creatures of the Antediluvian World**

The antediluvian world teems with life both familiar and fantastical—creatures shaped by a creation untouched by the Flood's cleansing waters, beings born of forbidden unions, and primordial entities that walked the earth before human civilization arose. Unlike standard fantasy bestiaries filled with medieval constructs, this collection presents creatures grounded in biblical and apocryphal texts, adapted to Pathfinder 2e mechanics with reverence for their source material and gameplay functionality.  
Every creature in this chapter includes:

* Complete PF2e Stat Block: Fully formatted with all mechanical details required for immediate use at your table  
* Lore Section: 2-3 paragraphs of setting-appropriate background drawn from Genesis, 1 Enoch, Jubilees, and related texts  
* Tactical Notes: Practical guidance for Game Masters on how to use the creature effectively in encounters  
* Soul Economy Integration: Where applicable, notes on how the creature interacts with Righteousness Points (RP) and Corruption Points (CP)

Creatures are organized by category with multiple variants to provide scaling options for parties of different levels. Named unique entities (such as Ohya or Behemoth) receive special attention with expanded lore and unique abilities reflecting their significance in antediluvian mythology.  
Important Note on Tone: These creatures represent spiritual realities within the biblical framework. Descriptions focus on their role in the cosmic struggle between righteousness and corruption, avoiding graphic violence or gratuitous horror. Even the most fearsome entities serve the narrative purpose of illustrating the consequences of rebellion against divine order and the hope of redemption.  
---

## **Giants (Nephilim and Kin)**

### **Nephilim Warrior**

NEPHILIM WARRIOR CREATURE 12  
UNIQUE LARGE CE GIANT HUMANOID  
Perception \+24; darkvision  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial  
Skills Athletics \+28, Intimidation \+26, Survival \+22, Warfare Lore \+24  
Str \+8, Dex \+3, Con \+7, Int \+2, Wis \+4, Cha \+5  
Items bronze greatsword, bronze scale armor, giant waterskin  
---

AC 32; Fort \+27, Ref \+19, Will \+22  
HP 220; Immunities disease, fear; Resistances radiant 10, precision 10  
Angelic Resistance The Nephilim warrior's celestial heritage grants resistance to radiant damage and precision damage.  
Insatiable Hunger The Nephilim warrior requires four times the normal amount of food and water. If it goes more than 24 hours without eating, it becomes fatigued and takes a \-2 penalty to all attack rolls and skill checks.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze greatsword \+28 (forceful, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d12+14 slashing  
Melee \[A\] fist \+26 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 2d8+12 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown boulder \+21 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 3d10+12 bludgeoning  
Crushing Blow \[A\] (attack) The Nephilim warrior makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target must attempt a DC 28 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is knocked prone and takes an additional 2d10 bludgeoning damage. On a critical failure, the target is also stunned until the end of its next turn.  
---

Titanic Presence (aura, emotion, fear, mental) 30-foot aura. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 26 Will save. On a failure, they are frightened 1; on a critical failure, they are frightened 2\. This is a fear effect.  
Earth-Shaking Step \[A\] (concentrate) The Nephilim warrior stomps the ground. Each creature within a 15-foot burst must attempt a DC 28 Reflex save. On a failure, the creature is knocked prone and takes 4d6 bludgeoning damage. On a critical failure, the creature is also stunned until the end of its next turn. The Nephilim warrior can use this ability once per minute.  
Watcher's Legacy When the Nephilim warrior reduces a creature to 0 Hit Points, it gains 1 Corruption Point. This represents the spiritual corruption inherent in its violent nature. Righteous characters (5+ RP) who witness this gain 1 RP for recognizing the corruption.

#### **Lore**

Nephilim Warriors stand 12 to 15 feet tall, their massive frames combining human features with subtle celestial characteristics—unusual eye colors (gold, silver, or deep crimson), hair that shimmers with unnatural hues, or skin bearing faint celestial markings. Born from the forbidden unions on Mount Hermon, these first-generation giants inherited both the martial prowess of their Watcher fathers and the mortal hunger of their human mothers. They established themselves as warlords and kings, building fortified strongholds and demanding tribute from human settlements. Their appetites were insatiable, consuming resources at an unsustainable rate until they turned to cannibalism, preying upon the very humanity that had given them birth. When slain, their immortal angelic souls cannot die—they become wandering demon spirits known as Refa'im, haunting the living and seeking bodies to possess.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Nephilim Warriors are designed to be formidable but not invincible opponents for mid-level parties. Their Crushing Blow and Earth-Shaking Step abilities create area control and battlefield disruption, forcing players to position carefully. Use their Titanic Presence aura to create psychological pressure before combat even begins. In encounters, pair them with human thralls or lesser giants to create layered challenges. Remember that their Insatiable Hunger provides roleplaying opportunities—perhaps they're raiding a village for food rather than malice, creating moral dilemmas for players. When defeated, describe their death not as mere physical destruction but as the release of a corrupted spirit that shrieks into the ether, becoming a Refa'im that may haunt the area.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Nephilim Warrior grants 1 RP per character for removing a source of corruption from the world. However, using excessive cruelty during the fight (executing a surrendered giant, desecrating the body) grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Nephilim Warriors automatically gain 1 CP when they kill a creature, representing their inherent corruption. This can be roleplayed as visible spiritual darkness gathering around them after violent acts.

---

### **Nephilim Scout**

NEPHILIM SCOUT CREATURE 8  
COMMON LARGE NE GIANT HUMANOID  
Perception \+20; darkvision, low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant  
Skills Acrobatics \+19, Stealth \+21, Survival \+22, Tracking Lore \+20  
Str \+6, Dex \+5, Con \+5, Int \+2, Wis \+4, Cha \+1  
Items bronze spear, hide armor, waterskin  
---

AC 26; Fort \+21, Ref \+21, Will \+18  
HP 150; Resistances precision 5  
Stealthy Giant The Nephilim scout's size would normally impose a penalty to Stealth checks, but its training negates this penalty. It can use the Hide and Sneak actions even when only partially concealed by natural features.  
---

Speed 30 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze spear \+22 (reach, versatile P), Damage 1d8+10 piercing  
Ranged \[A\] javelin \+21 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+9 piercing  
Trip Attack \[A\] (attack) The Nephilim scout makes a melee Strike with its spear. If it hits, the target must attempt a DC 24 Reflex save. On a failure, the target is knocked prone.  
---

Ambush Tactics When the Nephilim scout successfully Sneaks, its next attack against a flat-footed creature deals an additional 2d6 precision damage.  
Trackless Step The Nephilim scout leaves no trail and cannot be tracked by non-magical means. Magical tracking requires a DC 22 Survival check.  
Watcher's Eyes The Nephilim scout can see invisible creatures and objects within 60 feet. This represents the enhanced perception inherited from its Watcher heritage.

#### **Lore**

Nephilim Scouts serve as the eyes and ears of giant warbands, ranging far ahead of main forces to gather intelligence and identify vulnerable settlements. Standing 11 to 13 feet tall (slightly smaller than frontline warriors), they possess greater agility and cunning than their bulkier kin. Their role requires patience and observation rather than brute force, making them among the most intelligent of the first-generation Nephilim. They often work with human collaborators—Cainite guides or corrupted Wanderers—who help them navigate terrain and understand human settlements. Despite their tactical value, Scouts are still bound by the same insatiable hunger as other Nephilim, and their patrols frequently become raids when they encounter isolated communities.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Use Nephilim Scouts as encounter openers or reconnaissance threats. Their high Stealth and Tracking skills make them ideal for ambush scenarios or for creating tension as the party realizes they're being followed. Their Trip Attack and Ambush Tactics abilities make them dangerous to frontline characters, especially when fighting in terrain that provides cover. Pair them with human collaborators to create complex encounters where players must decide whether to show mercy to corrupted humans or treat them as enemies. Their Trackless Step ability means they can't be easily pursued, creating opportunities for recurring antagonists.

#### **Variants**

* Nephilim Tracker (Level 10): Gains the ability to cast *Locate* as a divine innate spell once per day. Its Trackless Step DC increases to 26\.  
* Nephilim Pathfinder (Level 6): Smaller (10-12 ft), with reduced stats but enhanced Survival skills (+24). Specializes in leading giant warbands through difficult terrain.

---

### **Nephilim Warlord**

NEPHILIM WARLORD CREATURE 16  
UNIQUE LARGE LE GIANT HUMANOID CHAMPION  
Perception \+30; darkvision, true seeing  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial, Abyssal  
Skills Athletics \+34, Command Lore \+30, Intimidation \+32, Warfare Lore \+30  
Str \+9, Dex \+4, Con \+8, Int \+5, Wis \+6, Cha \+7  
Items masterwork bronze greatsword, ornate bronze plate armor, ancestral chains of office  
---

AC 40; Fort \+33, Ref \+26, Will \+30  
HP 320; Immunities fear, mental; Resistances radiant 15, precision 15; Weaknesses righteous weapons 10  
Divine Defiance The Nephilim Warlord is immune to effects that would increase its Corruption Points. Its celestial heritage makes it resistant to spiritual corruption, though not to divine judgment.  
Righteous Vulnerability Weapons wielded by characters with 5+ RP ignore the Warlord's damage resistances. This represents the power of righteous faith against corrupted might.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork bronze greatsword \+34 (forceful, holy, sweep, two-handed), Damage 3d12+17 slashing plus 1d6 radiant  
Melee \[A\] fist \+32 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 2d10+15 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown boulder \+27 (range 40 ft, thrown), Damage 4d10+15 bludgeoning  
Titanic Cleave \[A\] (attack) The Warlord makes a melee Strike. If it hits, it deals damage to the target and all adjacent creatures.  
---

Commanding Presence (aura, emotion, mental) 60-foot aura. Allies within the aura gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 32 Will save or be frightened 1\.  
Earth-Shattering Stomp \[A\] (concentrate) The Warlord stomps the ground with such force that the earth cracks. Each creature within a 30-foot burst must attempt a DC 34 Reflex save. On a failure, the creature takes 8d6 bludgeoning damage and is knocked prone. On a critical failure, the creature is also stunned until the end of its next turn. The Warlord can use this ability once per hour.  
Watcher's Blessing \[A\] (concentrate) The Warlord calls upon its celestial heritage. For 1 minute, it gains a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls, and its melee Strikes deal an additional 2d6 force damage. This ability represents the direct intervention of its Watcher parent.  
Titan's Challenge \[A\] (concentrate) The Warlord points at a creature within 60 feet and issues a challenge. The target must attempt a DC 32 Will save. On a failure, the target is compelled to focus its attacks on the Warlord for 1 minute. The target can attempt a new save at the end of each of its turns.

#### **Lore**

Nephilim Warlords are the most powerful of the first-generation giants, standing 14 to 16 feet tall and commanding warbands of hundreds. They trace their lineage directly to specific Watchers—Azazel, Semyaza, or Baraqel—and wield authority through a combination of martial prowess, celestial heritage, and strategic brilliance. These warlords established the great Nephilim kingdoms that dominated antediluvian civilization, building massive fortresses and demanding tribute from human settlements across entire regions. Unlike common warriors, Warlords possess deeper understanding of their Watcher heritage, sometimes wielding fragments of forbidden knowledge as tactical advantages. They are fiercely proud of their lineage and view humans as little more than resources to be managed or consumed. When slain, their spirits become particularly powerful Refa'im that can influence events for generations.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Nephilim Warlords are campaign-defining antagonists for high-level parties. Their Commanding Presence aura creates dynamic battlefield control, while Earth-Shattering Stomp can reshape terrain and separate the party. Use their Watcher's Blessing ability sparingly for dramatic moments—perhaps when reduced to half HP or when a key ally falls. Their Titan's Challenge creates interesting tactical dilemmas, forcing melee characters to choose between attacking the Warlord or breaking free of the compulsion. Always pair Warlords with supporting forces (lesser Nephilim, human thralls, corrupted beasts) to create multi-layered encounters. Consider giving each Warlord a unique personality and goals beyond mere destruction—perhaps one seeks to build a lasting dynasty, another pursues forbidden knowledge, or a third believes it is fulfilling a divine mandate.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Nephilim Warlord grants 2 RP per character. However, if the Warlord offers surrender and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Nephilim Warlords automatically gain 1 CP per round when using Watcher's Blessing, representing the spiritual cost of calling upon corrupted celestial power. This can be roleplayed as visible darkness gathering around them during the ability's duration.

---

### **Ohya, Son of Semyaza**

OHYA, SON OF SEMYAZA CREATURE 15  
UNIQUE LARGE CE GIANT HUMANOID SORCERER  
Perception \+28; darkvision, see invisibility  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial, Abyssal, Ancient  
Skills Arcana \+28, Deception \+27, Intimidation \+26, Occultism \+30, Semyaza's Teachings Lore \+30  
Str \+6, Dex \+3, Con \+6, Int \+7, Wis \+4, Cha \+8  
Items enchanted bronze khopesh, ornate robes, star chart of Baraqel  
---

AC 36; Fort \+26, Ref \+23, Will \+30  
HP 260; Immunities mental; Resistances fire 15, precision 10  
Prophetic Dreams Ohya gains a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against effects that would affect his dreams or memories. He is immune to effects that would erase his memories of prophetic visions.  
Watcher's Blood Ohya's celestial heritage grants him resistance to fire damage. Additionally, when he casts a spell from Semyaza's tradition, the DC increases by 2\.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] enchanted bronze khopesh \+28 (disarm, finesse, magical, versatile S), Damage 2d8+12 slashing  
Arcane Spells DC 34, attack \+28; Spells Prepared (caster level 15th)  
*At Will* *detect magic, ghost sound, light, mage hand, message, read aura*  
*3/Day Each* *charm, dimension door, dispel magic, fly, invisibility, suggestion*  
*1/Day Each* *dominate, plane shift (to celestial or abyssal planes only), shadow blast*  
Semyaza's Enchantment \[A\] (arcane, enchantment, mental, occult) Ohya targets one creature within 30 feet. The target must attempt a DC 34 Will save. On a failure, the target is fascinated for 1 minute. On a critical failure, the target is charmed for 1 minute. This is a mental effect.  
---

Dream Walker Ohya can enter the dreams of sleeping creatures within 1 mile. While in a dream, he can communicate, plant suggestions (as the *suggestion* spell), or observe memories. A creature can attempt a DC 30 Will save to resist intrusion into its dreams.  
Prophecy of the Flood Ohya possesses fragmented visions of the coming judgment. Once per day, he can grant an ally within 30 feet a \+2 status bonus to their next saving throw against a water-based effect or environmental hazard. This represents his partial understanding of the Flood's nature.  
Watcher's Legacy When Ohya casts a spell from Semyaza's tradition, he must attempt a DC 16 flat check. On a failure, he gains 1 Corruption Point. This represents the spiritual cost of wielding forbidden knowledge.

#### **Lore**

Ohya stands as one of the most formidable of Semyaza's sons, a Nephilim warrior-poet whose dreams foretold the coming Flood. According to the Book of Giants, Ohya and his brother Hahya received visions of the destruction to come but remained trapped in their corrupted existence, unable to escape the consequences of their father's rebellion. Ohya's dreams showed him fragments of the judgment—the rising waters, the destruction of the great cities, the survival of a righteous remnant—but he interpreted these visions through the lens of his own corruption, believing he could find a way to preserve his lineage through the cataclysm. He became obsessed with forbidden knowledge, studying the star charts of Baraqel and the enchantments of Semyaza in hopes of discovering a means of survival. His presence is marked by an unsettling combination of poetic insight and brutal violence, as he alternates between reciting prophecies of doom and leading raids against human settlements.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Ohya functions as both a combatant and a spellcaster, using his enchanted khopesh for melee defense while casting debilitating enchantments from range. His Semyaza's Enchantment ability can remove key party members from combat, so use it strategically against the most dangerous opponents. His Dream Walker ability provides excellent roleplaying opportunities—perhaps he appears in the party's dreams to deliver cryptic warnings or taunts. His Prophecy of the Flood ability should be used sparingly, perhaps to save an ally from a water-based hazard during an environmental encounter. Remember that his Watcher's Legacy corruption risk creates tension—he may hesitate to use his most powerful spells if his CP is already high.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Learning from Ohya's prophecies (without embracing his corruption) grants 1 RP. Killing him grants 2 RP, but executing him after he surrenders grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Ohya begins encounters with 5 CP. Each time he fails his corruption check when casting spells, describe visible spiritual darkness gathering around him—a shadow that clings to his form and whispers temptations.

---

### **Hahya, Brother of Ohya**

HAHYA, BROTHER OF OHYA CREATURE 13  
UNIQUE LARGE NE GIANT HUMANOID CLERIC  
Perception \+25; darkvision, spirit sense 60 ft  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial, Ancient  
Skills Medicine \+26, Religion \+28, Survival \+24, Flood Prophecies Lore \+28  
Str \+5, Dex \+2, Con \+6, Int \+4, Wis \+8, Cha \+5  
Items ceremonial bronze mace, prayer beads of Enoch, waterskin  
---

AC 32; Fort \+25, Ref \+20, Will \+29  
HP 230; Immunities disease, fear; Resistances negative 10  
Spirit Sense Hahya can sense the presence of disembodied spirits (Refa'im) within 60 feet. He can also sense the Corruption Points of living creatures within 30 feet, determining whether they have 0, 1–5, 6–10, or 11+ CP.  
Righteous Seeker Hahya gains a \+2 status bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about prophecies, divine judgment, or righteous figures (such as Enoch or Noah).  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] ceremonial bronze mace \+26 (magical, versatile B), Damage 2d6+11 bludgeoning  
Divine Spells DC 31, attack \+25; Spells Prepared (caster level 13th)  
*At Will* *detect alignment (good only), guidance, light, read aura*  
*3/Day Each* *bless, heal, remove fear, sanctuary*  
*1/Day Each* *commune, dispel evil, holy cascade, restoration*  
Turn the Corrupted \[A\] (concentrate, divine, emotion, fear, mental) Hahya presents his prayer beads and commands corrupted beings to flee. Each undead creature and creature with 3+ CP within 30 feet must attempt a DC 31 Will save. On a failure, the creature is frightened 2 and must spend its actions moving away from Hahya. On a critical failure, the creature is frightened 3 and cannot attack Hahya or his allies for 1 minute.  
---

Interpreter of Dreams Hahya can interpret prophetic dreams with perfect accuracy. Once per day, he can grant an ally within 30 feet the benefits of the *dream message* spell, allowing them to receive guidance from divine sources.  
Warning of Judgment Hahya can sense impending environmental disasters related to the coming Flood (earthquakes, flash floods, landslides). He gains a \+4 status bonus to Perception checks to notice such events and can warn allies within 60 feet, granting them a \+2 status bonus to Reflex saves against these hazards.  
Seeker of Righteousness Hahya is drawn to characters with high RP. When within 30 feet of a character with 5+ RP, he gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. This represents his spiritual connection to righteousness.

#### **Lore**

Where Ohya embraced corruption and violence, Hahya turned inward, becoming the contemplative interpreter of his brother's prophetic dreams. Though born of the same forbidden union, Hahya developed a deep fascination with the righteous line of Seth and the prophecies of judgment circulating among human communities. He secretly studied the teachings of Enoch, memorizing genealogies and warnings about the coming Flood. Unlike his brother, Hahya recognized the spiritual danger of their lineage and sought ways to atone for his father's rebellion. He carries prayer beads carved with the names of righteous patriarchs and wears simple robes rather than the ornate armor of Nephilim nobility. Hahya's presence creates tension among his kin—he is respected for his wisdom but distrusted for his sympathy toward humans and his rejection of Nephilim tyranny.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Hahya serves as a potential ally or moral compass rather than a straightforward antagonist. His Turn the Corrupted ability makes him valuable against undead or highly corrupted foes. Use his Interpreter of Dreams and Warning of Judgment abilities to provide the party with crucial information or environmental advantages. In encounters with his brother Ohya, Hahya may attempt to reason with him or protect the party from Ohya's enchantments. His Seeker of Righteousness ability creates interesting dynamics—he may be more willing to cooperate with high-RP characters, creating opportunities for redemption arcs.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Gaining Hahya's trust and learning from his wisdom grants 1 RP. Protecting him from his corrupted kin grants an additional 1 RP.  
* For GMs: Hahya begins with 3 CP (from his Nephilim heritage) but actively works to reduce it. Each session where he aids righteous characters reduces his CP by 1\. When his CP reaches 0, he gains the Blessed 1 condition.

---

### **Mahway, The Winged**

MAHWAY, THE WINGED CREATURE 11  
UNIQUE LARGE CE GIANT HUMANOID MESSENGER  
Perception \+24; darkvision, low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial, Ancient  
Skills Acrobatics \+27, Diplomacy \+23, Religion \+25, Messenger Lore \+25  
Str \+5, Dex \+7, Con \+5, Int \+4, Wis \+6, Cha \+5  
Items bronze dagger, messenger's satchel, clay tablet with Enoch's seal  
---

AC 30; Fort \+23, Ref \+27, Will \+25  
HP 190; Immunities fall damage; Resistances precision 5  
Feathered Descent Mahway takes no damage from falls and can glide 5 feet horizontally for every 1 foot he descends vertically.  
Messenger's Speed Mahway's land Speed increases to 35 feet when carrying messages or traveling between settlements.  
---

Speed 25 ft, Fly 50 ft (average)  
Melee \[A\] bronze dagger \+25 (agile, finesse, magical, thrown 20 ft), Damage 1d6+9 piercing  
Ranged \[A\] thrown dagger \+27 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+9 piercing  
Sky Dive \[A\] (attack) Mahway dives from the air to strike a target. He makes a melee Strike with a \+2 status bonus. If the Strike hits, the target must attempt a DC 26 Reflex save or be knocked prone.  
---

Celestial Messenger Mahway can travel between the material plane and the celestial realms once per day, though this journey takes 1 hour of subjective time. He uses this ability to carry messages between Enoch and the archangels.  
Enoch's Seal Mahway carries a clay tablet bearing Enoch's seal. When presented to righteous characters (5+ RP), it grants them a \+2 status bonus to their next saving throw against corruption effects. The seal loses its power after being used three times.  
Winged Scout Mahway can fly at double his normal Speed (100 ft) when scouting or delivering urgent messages. During this time, he gains a \+2 status bonus to Perception checks to notice threats or landmarks.  
Voice of Warning Mahway can project his voice to be heard clearly up to 1 mile away. He often uses this ability to warn settlements of approaching dangers.

#### **Lore**

Mahway is among the most unique of the Nephilim—born with vestigial wings that, though too small for true flight, grant him enhanced agility and the ability to glide from great heights. According to the Book of Giants, Mahway served as a messenger between the Nephilim and Enoch the Prophet, carrying questions and pleas for mercy to the righteous patriarch. Unlike his warlike kin, Mahway developed a deep respect for Enoch's wisdom and the divine warnings he delivered. He became a reluctant intermediary, torn between loyalty to his Nephilim kin and recognition of the coming judgment. Mahway's wings are not fully functional—he cannot sustain powered flight for long periods—but they allow him to descend safely from heights and move with unusual grace for a giant. He carries clay tablets inscribed with prophecies and wears simple robes rather than armor, marking his role as messenger rather than warrior.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Mahway is designed as a non-combatant NPC who can provide crucial information or assistance to the party. His Fly Speed and Winged Scout ability make him ideal for reconnaissance missions. Use his Voice of Warning ability to deliver plot-critical information across distances. His Enoch's Seal provides valuable protection against corruption, creating incentives for the party to protect him. In rare combat situations, Mahway prefers to flee rather than fight, using his Sky Dive ability only as a last resort to escape. Consider having him appear at key moments to deliver warnings or guidance, particularly when the party faces moral dilemmas related to the coming Flood.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Accepting Mahway's guidance and heeding his warnings grants 1 RP. Protecting him from those who would silence his messages grants an additional 1 RP.  
* For GMs: Mahway begins with 2 CP (from his Nephilim heritage) but actively works to reduce it through righteous service. Each time he successfully delivers a warning that saves lives, he reduces his CP by 1\.

### **Gilgamesh, Hero-King**

GILGAMESH, HERO-KING CREATURE 14  
UNIQUE LARGE LN GIANT HUMANOID CHAMPION  
Perception \+28; darkvision, low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant, Ancient, Sumerian  
Skills Athletics \+32, Diplomacy \+26, Intimidation \+28, Leadership Lore \+28, Survival \+26  
Str \+8, Dex \+4, Con \+7, Int \+3, Wis \+5, Cha \+6  
Items Uruk's Blade (masterwork bronze greatsword), lion-pelt cloak, crown of Uruk  
---

AC 36; Fort \+31, Ref \+24, Will \+28  
HP 270; Immunities fear; Resistances precision 10  
Two-Thirds Divine Gilgamesh's mixed heritage grants him enhanced resilience. He gains a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against effects that would control his actions or cloud his mind.  
Heroic Legacy Gilgamesh gains a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls for each adjacent ally. This represents his ability to inspire those who fight alongside him.  
---

Speed 30 ft  
Melee \[A\] Uruk's Blade \+32 (forceful, holy, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d12+15 slashing plus 1d6 radiant  
Melee \[A\] fist \+30 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 2d8+13 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown crown \+25 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+11 bludgeoning  
Heroic Strike \[A\] (attack) Gilgamesh makes a melee Strike. If it hits, he deals an additional 2d10 damage of the weapon's type. If the target is a corrupted creature (3+ CP), this additional damage increases to 3d10.  
---

King of Uruk (aura, emotion, mental) 30-foot aura. Allies within the aura gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 30 Will save or be frightened 1\.  
Epic Resilience When Gilgamesh is reduced to 0 Hit Points, he can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to his level (14). He can use this ability once per day. This represents his legendary endurance.  
Quest for Immortality Gilgamesh can spend 10 minutes in meditation to regain all Hit Points and remove all conditions except dying, doomed, and unconscious. This ability represents his partial success in seeking eternal life, though it falls short of true immortality. He can use this ability once per week.  
Righteous Judgment \[A\] (concentrate) Gilgamesh calls upon his connection to divine justice. For 1 minute, his melee Strikes deal an additional 1d6 radiant damage, and he gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls against corrupted creatures (3+ CP).

#### **Lore**

Gilgamesh stands as the most famous of the antediluvian heroes—a king of Uruk whose legend survived the Flood to inspire post-diluvian civilizations. According to apocryphal traditions that bridge Genesis and Mesopotamian mythology, Gilgamesh was two-thirds divine and one-third human, a mighty ruler who sought immortality after the death of his friend Enkidu. Unlike the purely corrupted Nephilim, Gilgamesh walked a complex path—building great cities and establishing just laws while also pursuing forbidden knowledge and challenging divine authority. His epic quest took him across the antediluvian world, from the cedar forests guarded by Humbaba to the distant shores where Utnapishtim (Noah) survived the Flood. Gilgamesh's legacy is one of profound contradiction: a tyrant who became a wise ruler, a seeker of forbidden knowledge who ultimately accepted mortal limits, a giant-kin who protected humanity despite his own corrupted heritage.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Gilgamesh functions as a potential ally, rival, or mentor depending on the campaign's needs. His King of Uruk aura makes him valuable in large-scale battles, while his Epic Resilience and Quest for Immortality abilities ensure he survives encounters that would fell lesser giants. Use his Righteous Judgment ability selectively—perhaps when fighting alongside high-RP characters or against particularly corrupted foes. His Heroic Legacy ability creates interesting tactical considerations, encouraging the party to fight near him. Consider giving Gilgamesh a personal quest that intersects with the party's goals—perhaps he seeks knowledge about surviving the Flood or wishes to atone for past tyrannies.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Earning Gilgamesh's respect and fighting alongside him grants 1 RP. Learning from his hard-won wisdom about mortality and righteousness grants an additional 1 RP.  
* For GMs: Gilgamesh begins with 4 CP (from his giant heritage and past tyrannies) but actively works to reduce it. Each session where he acts righteously (protecting the innocent, accepting mortal limits, seeking wisdom rather than power) reduces his CP by 1\. When his CP reaches 0, he gains the Blessed 1 condition and his weapons gain the holy trait.

### **Rephaim Warrior**

REPHAIM WARRIOR CREATURE 8  
COMMON LARGE CE GIANT HUMANOID  
Perception \+18; darkvision, spirit sight 60 ft  
Languages Common, Giant, Abyssal  
Skills Athletics \+22, Intimidation \+20, Necrology Lore \+18, Survival \+16  
Str \+6, Dex \+2, Con \+5, Int \+1, Wis \+3, Cha \+4  
Items bronze war club, bronze scale armor, death mask  
---

AC 24; Fort \+21, Ref \+16, Will \+19  
HP 140; Immunities disease, fear; Resistances negative 5, precision 5  
Deathly Aura (aura, emotion, fear, mental) 15-foot aura. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 21 Will save. On a failure, they are frightened 1\. This is a fear effect.  
Spirit Sight The Rephaim warrior can see invisible creatures and objects, and can see into the Ethereal Plane.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze war club \+22 (shove), Damage 2d8+11 bludgeoning  
Melee \[A\] fist \+20 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 1d10+9 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown war club \+17 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 2d8+9 bludgeoning  
Spirit Strike \[A\] (attack, necromancy, negative) The Rephaim warrior makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 1d6 negative damage and must attempt a DC 21 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is sickened 1 until the end of its next turn.  
---

Death Gaze \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, fear, mental, necromancy) The Rephaim warrior fixes its gaze on a living creature within 30 feet. The target must attempt a DC 21 Will save. On a failure, the creature is frightened 1 until the end of the Rephaim warrior's next turn. On a critical failure, the creature is frightened 2\.  
Grave Digger The Rephaim warrior gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Subsist in wilderness environments and to checks to Recall Knowledge about burial sites, tombs, and other death-related locations.  
Undead Resistance The Rephaim warrior gains resistance 5 to negative damage and a \+2 circumstance bonus to saving throws against effects that would create undead creatures or control undead.

#### **Lore**

Rephaim Warriors stand 9 to 11 feet tall, their gaunt, angular features giving them a skeletal appearance that reflects their association with death and the underworld. The name "Rephaim" means "shades" or "ghosts" in Hebrew, a fitting designation for these second-generation giants who inherited their power from the first-generation Nephilim. Unlike their titanic parents who were direct offspring of Watchers and humans, Rephaim were born from unions between Nephilim and human women, resulting in slightly smaller but still formidable warriors. Their pale or ashen skin, deep-set shadowed eyes, and hair the color of dust or bone mark them as beings caught between life and death. Many Rephaim developed a fascination with mortality and the afterlife, perhaps sensing their own eventual fate as disembodied spirits (Refa'im) after death. They established themselves as regional warlords, ruling from fortified strongholds built near burial grounds and battlefields—places where death's presence was strongest. Some Rephaim even formed "death cults" that worshipped mortality itself or sought to commune with the spirits of the dead, practicing necromancy and other forbidden arts that further corrupted their already tainted bloodline.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Rephaim Warriors excel at psychological warfare, using their Deathly Aura and Death Gaze abilities to weaken enemies before physical combat begins. Their Spirit Strike ability makes them particularly dangerous to living creatures, dealing additional negative damage and potentially sickening targets. Use their Grave Digger trait to create encounters in burial grounds or ancient battlefields where they have tactical advantages. Pair them with undead minions or other corrupted beings to create layered encounters that test both physical and spiritual resilience. Their Undead Resistance trait means they work well alongside undead allies, creating synergistic combinations. In combat, position them to maximize their aura effect while protecting them from ranged attacks. Their Spirit Sight ability means they cannot be easily ambushed by invisible or ethereal creatures.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Rephaim Warrior grants 1 RP per character for removing a source of death-worship from the world. However, desecrating their bodies or using their death magic against them grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Rephaim Warriors automatically gain 1 CP when they kill a living creature, representing their spiritual connection to death and corruption. This can be roleplayed as their eyes glowing with darker intensity after each kill.

---

### **Rephaim Champion**

REPHAIM CHAMPION CREATURE 12  
UNIQUE LARGE CE GIANT HUMANOID CHAMPION  
Perception \+24; darkvision, spirit sight 120 ft, see invisibility  
Languages Common, Giant, Abyssal, Necril  
Skills Athletics \+28, Intimidation \+26, Command Lore \+24, Necrology Lore \+26  
Str \+7, Dex \+3, Con \+6, Int \+2, Wis \+5, Cha \+6  
Items masterwork bronze flail, ornate bronze plate armor, death crown  
---

AC 32; Fort \+29, Ref \+22, Will \+27  
HP 210; Immunities disease, fear, mental; Resistances negative 10, precision 10  
Death Lord's Aura (aura, emotion, fear, mental) 30-foot aura. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 28 Will save. On a failure, they are frightened 1; on a critical failure, they are frightened 2\. This is a fear effect.  
Spirit Mastery The Rephaim Champion can see invisible creatures and objects, can see into the Ethereal Plane, and can communicate telepathically with undead creatures within 120 feet.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork bronze flail \+28 (disarm, finesse, sweep, trip), Damage 2d8+13 bludgeoning  
Melee \[A\] fist \+26 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 2d6+11 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown flail head \+23 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 2d8+11 bludgeoning  
Death Strike \[A\] (attack, necromancy, negative) The Champion makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 2d6 negative damage and must attempt a DC 28 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is sickened 2 until the end of the Champion's next turn. On a critical failure, the target is also drained 1\.  
---

Command the Dead \[A\] (concentrate, necromancy) The Champion targets one undead creature within 60 feet. The target must attempt a DC 28 Will save. On a failure, the Champion gains control of the undead creature for 1 minute. This is a mental effect.  
Death Master The Champion can cast *Animate Dead* as a 5th-level divine innate spell once per day. Any undead created remain under the Champion's control for 1 hour.  
Grave Lord (aura, necromancy) 60-foot aura. Undead creatures within the aura cannot be turned or destroyed by Turn Undead effects. Living creatures within the aura take a \-2 status penalty to saving throws against death effects and negative damage.  
Spirit Hunter The Champion gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to attack rolls against undead creatures and creatures with the incorporeal trait. Additionally, the Champion can deal damage to incorporeal creatures with unarmed attacks and non-magical weapons as if they were magical.

#### **Lore**

Rephaim Champions are the elite warriors and death cult leaders among the Rephaim, standing 10 to 12 feet tall and wielding authority over both the living and the dead. These formidable giants have delved deeply into necromantic practices, learning to command undead servants and commune with disembodied spirits. Their appearance is even more deathly than common Rephaim—pale skin stretched tight over angular bones, eyes that burn with cold fire, and voices that echo with the whispers of the grave. Many Champions wear ornate armor adorned with bones, skulls, and other death-related motifs, and they carry weapons designed not just to kill but to corrupt the soul. They establish their strongholds in places saturated with death energy—ancient burial mounds, battlefields where thousands perished, or catacombs filled with the remains of past civilizations. Some Rephaim Champions claim descent from specific Nephilim warlords known for their brutality, using this lineage to justify their authority over lesser Rephaim and human thralls. When slain, their spirits become particularly powerful Refa'im that can influence the living for centuries.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Rephaim Champions are designed as major antagonists for mid-to-high level parties. Their Death Lord's Aura creates significant psychological pressure, while their Command the Dead and Death Master abilities allow them to control undead minions. Use their Grave Lord aura to create challenging encounters where the party must deal with both the Champion and supporting undead. Their Spirit Hunter trait makes them dangerous opponents for ghostly or ethereal enemies. Position them to maximize their aura effects while using terrain for cover against ranged attacks. Their Death Strike ability should be used strategically against key party members to debilitate them. Consider giving each Champion a unique personality and goals—perhaps one seeks to build an army of the dead, another pursues forbidden knowledge about immortality, or a third believes death worship is the only truth in a corrupt world.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Rephaim Champion grants 2 RP per character. However, using their necromantic abilities or keeping their death-related items grants 2 CP.  
* For GMs: Rephaim Champions begin encounters with 5 CP and automatically gain 1 CP per round when using necromantic abilities. This corruption manifests as visible darkness gathering around them, with whispers of the dead becoming audible to those nearby.

---

### **Rephaim Death Speaker**

REPHAIM DEATH SPEAKER CREATURE 10  
UNIQUE LARGE NE GIANT HUMANOID CLERIC  
Perception \+22; darkvision, spirit sense 120 ft  
Languages Common, Giant, Abyssal, Necril  
Skills Medicine \+22, Occultism \+24, Religion \+23, Necrology Lore \+24  
Str \+4, Dex \+2, Con \+5, Int \+3, Wis \+7, Cha \+4  
Items ceremonial bronze dagger, death robes, spirit focus  
---

AC 26; Fort \+23, Ref \+18, Will \+28  
HP 170; Immunities disease, fear; Resistances negative 10  
Spirit Sense The Death Speaker can sense the presence of undead creatures and disembodied spirits within 120 feet. They can also sense the Corruption Points of living creatures within 60 feet, determining whether they have 0, 1–5, 6–10, or 11+ CP.  
Death Lore The Death Speaker gains a \+2 status bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about death, undeath, the afterlife, and burial customs.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] ceremonial bronze dagger \+20 (agile, finesse, magical), Damage 1d4+8 piercing plus 1d6 negative  
Divine Spells DC 29, attack \+23; Spells Prepared (caster level 10th)  
*At Will* *detect undead, ghost sound, read aura, stabilize*  
*3/Day Each* *animate dead, command undead, harm, speak with dead*  
*1/Day Each* *circle of death, create undead, spirit blast*  
Death Touch \[A\] (attack, divine, necromancy, negative) The Death Speaker makes a melee Strike with their dagger. If it hits, the target takes an additional 3d6 negative damage and must attempt a DC 29 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is drained 1\. On a critical failure, the target is drained 2\.  
---

Command Undead \[A\] (concentrate, divine, mental, necromancy) The Death Speaker targets one undead creature within 60 feet. The target must attempt a DC 29 Will save. On a failure, the Death Speaker gains control of the undead creature for 10 minutes. This is a mental effect.  
Spirit Communion \[A\] (concentrate, divine, necromancy) The Death Speaker communes with disembodied spirits (Refa'im) within 120 feet. For 1 minute, the Death Speaker can ask the spirits questions and receive answers as if using the *commune* spell. The spirits are compelled to answer truthfully but may provide cryptic or incomplete information.  
Death Ward \[A\] (concentrate, divine) The Death Speaker grants resistance 10 to negative damage to themselves and all allies within 30 feet for 1 minute.  
Grave Keeper The Death Speaker can use the *Treat Wounds* action without needing to be trained in Medicine, though they still take the normal penalty for not being trained. Additionally, they can restore Hit Points to undead creatures using the Treat Wounds action.

#### **Lore**

Rephaim Death Speakers are the spiritual leaders and necromancers of Rephaim society, giants who have dedicated their lives to understanding and manipulating the boundary between life and death. Standing 9 to 11 feet tall with gaunt features and eyes that seem to look beyond the material world, these mystics serve as intermediaries between the living Rephaim and the disembodied spirits of their Nephilim ancestors. Unlike the brutal Warriors and Champions, Death Speakers pursue knowledge rather than conquest, though their methods are no less corrupted. They establish themselves in ancient burial grounds, catacombs, or other places where the veil between worlds is thin, building libraries of forbidden texts and conducting rituals to commune with the dead. Many Death Speakers believe that death is not an end but a transformation, and they seek to master this transformation to achieve a form of immortality. Some even claim to have spoken with the spirits of the original 200 Watchers, learning secrets that were lost when the fallen angels were bound beneath the earth. Their connection to the spirit world makes them valuable advisors to Rephaim warlords, though their true loyalties often lie with the dead rather than the living.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Death Speakers function primarily as spellcasters and support characters rather than frontline combatants. Their Command Undead ability makes them dangerous opponents when paired with undead minions. Use their Spirit Communion ability to provide plot-relevant information or cryptic warnings to the party. Their Death Ward ability makes them valuable support for undead-heavy encounters. In combat, keep them protected behind Warrior or Champion allies while they cast debilitating spells and command undead. Their Death Touch ability should be reserved for key targets, particularly those with healing abilities. Consider having Death Speakers serve as information sources—their communion with spirits might provide the party with crucial knowledge about ancient prophecies, hidden locations, or the weaknesses of powerful enemies.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Learning from a Death Speaker's knowledge (without embracing their necromancy) grants 1 RP. Destroying their spirit focus and preventing them from communing with corrupted spirits grants an additional 1 RP.  
* For GMs: Death Speakers begin with 6 CP and automatically gain 1 CP per day from their communion with disembodied spirits. This corruption manifests as visible spiritual energy clinging to them—a faint glow that pulses with the whispers of the dead.

---

### **Anakim Noble**

ANAKIM NOBLE CREATURE 9  
COMMON LARGE LE GIANT HUMANOID  
Perception \+20; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial  
Skills Diplomacy \+22, Intimidation \+21, Society \+19, Chain Weapon Lore \+20  
Str \+6, Dex \+1, Con \+5, Int \+2, Wis \+3, Cha \+5  
Items bronze greatsword, heavy bronze chains (worn as armor and weapon), noble's robes  
---

AC 26; Fort \+23, Ref \+17, Will \+21  
HP 160; Resistances precision 5  
Chain Master The Anakim Noble is trained in the use of chain weapons. When wielding a chain weapon with the reach trait, they can use it to Grapple creatures up to 15 feet away instead of the normal 10-foot reach.  
Noble Bearing The Anakim Noble gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when dealing with creatures that have the giant trait or are of Large size or larger.  
Long-Necked The Anakim Noble's elongated neck gives them enhanced vision. They gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures or objects.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze greatsword \+23 (forceful, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d6+12 slashing  
Melee \[A\] chain weapon \+22 (disarm, finesse, reach 15 ft, trip), Damage 1d8+10 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown chain \+17 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d8+8 bludgeoning  
Chain Strike \[A\] (attack) The Anakim Noble makes a melee Strike with a chain weapon. If it hits, they can attempt to Trip the target as a free action. The target must be their size or smaller.  
---

Heavy Chains The Anakim Noble can wear heavier chains than normal without penalty. When wearing chains as armor (using the rules for improvised armor), they gain a \+2 item bonus to AC instead of the normal \+0. Additionally, they gain resistance 5 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks while wearing chains.  
Noble Command \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, mental) The Anakim Noble issues a command to one creature within 30 feet. The target must attempt a DC 23 Will save. On a failure, the target is compelled to follow the Noble's command for 1 round. This is a mental effect.  
Chain of Office \[A\] (concentrate) The Anakim Noble invokes the authority of their noble house. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to Diplomacy and Intimidation checks, and creatures with the humanoid trait that are not their enemies treat them as friendly. This ability does not work on creatures that are already hostile or on creatures that are not influenced by social status.

#### **Lore**

Anakim Nobles stand 10 to 13 feet tall, their distinctive elongated necks giving them a regal bearing that sets them apart from other giant lineages. Known as the "Long-Necked Ones," the Anakim trace their ancestry back to the early unions between Watchers and human women, though their bloodline has been diluted through generations of intermarriage with both Nephilim and humans. Unlike the brutal Nephilim or death-obsessed Rephaim, the Anakim cultivated a sophisticated warrior culture centered around honor, lineage, and martial prowess. They established fortified city-states where they ruled as noble houses, each family distinguished by the unique patterns and weights of the bronze chains they wore as symbols of status and power. These chains were not merely decorative—they served as weapons, symbols of office, and markers of social rank. The heaviest chains belonged to the ruling families, and to wear them required immense strength and endurance. Anakim society was hierarchical and deeply concerned with bloodlines and honor. Noble houses competed for prestige through martial tournaments, territorial expansion, and strategic marriages. They kept detailed genealogies tracing their ancestry back to specific Watchers, and these lineages determined social standing. Anakim nobles often took human wives or concubines, producing offspring like the Gibborim who served as elite warriors and administrators in their courts.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Anakim Nobles excel at battlefield control and social manipulation. Their Chain Strike and Heavy Chains abilities make them dangerous in melee combat, particularly against smaller opponents. Use their Noble Command ability to create tactical challenges—perhaps compelling a party member to attack an ally or drop their weapon. Their Chain of Office ability makes them valuable in social encounters, able to sway neutral NPCs to their side. Position them to maximize their reach advantage while using their chains to control the battlefield. Pair them with human thralls or lesser giants to create layered encounters where the Noble commands while others fight. Their Long-Necked trait means they have excellent vision, making them difficult to ambush. Consider giving each Noble a unique personality and house affiliation—perhaps one is honorable but rigid, another is cunning and manipulative, or a third is proud but fair.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating an Anakim Noble grants 1 RP per character. However, executing a surrendered Noble or desecrating their ancestral chains grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Anakim Nobles begin with 2 CP from their diluted giant heritage. They automatically gain 1 CP when they use their Noble Command ability to compel others against their will, representing the corruption of using power to control others.

---

### **Anakim Chain-Bearer**

ANAKIM CHAIN-BEARER CREATURE 11  
UNIQUE LARGE LN GIANT HUMANOID FIGHTER  
Perception \+23; low-light vision, chain sense 30 ft  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial  
Skills Athletics \+27, Intimidation \+24, Warfare Lore \+23, Chain Weapon Lore \+25  
Str \+7, Dex \+2, Con \+6, Int \+3, Wis \+4, Cha \+5  
Items masterwork bronze greatsword, ancestral chains of Azazel, noble armor  
---

AC 30; Fort \+28, Ref \+20, Will \+24  
HP 190; Resistances precision 10, bludgeoning 10 (from chains)  
Chain Mastery The Chain-Bearer can use chain weapons with exceptional skill. When wielding a chain weapon with the reach trait, they can use it to Grapple or Trip creatures up to 20 feet away. Additionally, they can make Attacks of Opportunity with their chain weapon against creatures that move within their reach.  
Ancestral Chains The Chain-Bearer's ancestral chains are masterwork items that grant a \+2 item bonus to AC when worn as armor. The chains also have the Returning property when used as a thrown weapon.  
Warrior's Blood The Chain-Bearer gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork bronze greatsword \+27 (forceful, holy, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d12+14 slashing plus 1d6 radiant  
Melee \[A\] ancestral chains \+26 (disarm, finesse, reach 20 ft, returning, trip), Damage 2d6+12 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown chains \+21 (range 30 ft, returning, thrown), Damage 2d6+10 bludgeoning  
Chain Whirlwind \[A\] (attack) The Chain-Bearer spins their chains in a devastating arc. Make a melee Strike against each creature within the chain's reach (20 feet). Each Strike takes a \-2 penalty, and the Chain-Bearer can apply any weapon specializations for only the first Strike.  
---

Crushing Chains When the Chain-Bearer successfully Grapples a creature using their chain weapon, they can deal 2d6 bludgeoning damage to the creature as a free action. Additionally, the creature is restrained until the Grapple ends.  
Noble Presence (aura, emotion, mental) 30-foot aura. Enemies that are smaller than Large take a \-1 status penalty to attack rolls against the Chain-Bearer. Additionally, when the Chain-Bearer uses the Demoralize action, they can target all enemies within 30 feet instead of just one enemy.  
Chain of Azazel \[A\] (concentrate) The Chain-Bearer invokes the power of their Watcher ancestor. For 1 minute, their chain attacks deal an additional 1d6 fire damage, and they gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls with chain weapons. This ability represents the direct intervention of Azazel, the Watcher who taught warfare and metallurgy.  
Ancestral Authority \[A\] (concentrate) The Chain-Bearer commands respect through their noble bearing. For 1 minute, they gain a \+3 status bonus to Diplomacy and Intimidation checks, and allies within 30 feet gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws.

#### **Lore**

Anakim Chain-Bearers are the elite warriors and house champions of Anakim noble families, giants who have mastered the distinctive chain-fighting style that defines their culture. Standing 12 to 14 feet tall with the regal bearing of their lineage, these formidable warriors wear ancestral chains that have been passed down through generations—each link representing a century of rule, each pattern telling a story of battles won and enemies defeated. The most prestigious chains are those said to have been forged by Azazel himself or his direct descendants, imbued with celestial fire that burns with righteous fury against the corrupt. Chain-Bearers serve as personal guards to Anakim lords, commanders of household troops, and champions in martial tournaments that determine social standing and territorial rights. Unlike common Anakim warriors who may serve out of obligation or fear, Chain-Bearers are bound by honor and ancestral duty, viewing their chains not as weapons of oppression but as symbols of responsibility and protection. Some Chain-Bearers have even developed a code of conduct that emphasizes protecting the weak and upholding justice, though this noble ideal often conflicts with the harsh realities of giant society where power is the ultimate currency.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Chain-Bearers are designed as elite combatants who excel at battlefield control and area denial. Their Chain Whirlwind ability allows them to attack multiple enemies simultaneously, while their Crushing Chains and Chain Mastery traits make them dangerous grapplers. Use their Noble Presence aura to create psychological pressure, and their Chain of Azazel ability for dramatic moments when they need to turn the tide of battle. Position them to maximize their reach advantage while using terrain for cover against ranged attacks. Pair them with lesser Anakim warriors or human thralls to create layered encounters where the Chain-Bearer controls the battlefield while others provide support. Their Ancestral Authority ability makes them valuable leaders, able to inspire allies and command respect. Consider giving each Chain-Bearer a unique ancestral chain with special properties or history—perhaps one chain was forged from a meteorite, another contains the names of every house member who has borne it, or a third is said to have bound a Watcher during the ancient wars.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Chain-Bearer who fights honorably grants 1 RP. However, if the Chain-Bearer offers surrender and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Chain-Bearers begin with 3 CP from their giant heritage and connection to Azazel. Each time they use their Chain of Azazel ability, they must attempt a DC 14 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of calling upon corrupted celestial power. This corruption manifests as their chains glowing with darker fire and their eyes burning with increased intensity.

### **Gibborim Champion**

GIBBORIM CHAMPION CREATURE 7  
COMMON MEDIUM NG HUMANOID  
Perception \+17; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant  
Skills Athletics \+20, Intimidation \+16, Survival \+15, Warfare Lore \+17  
Str \+5, Dex \+2, Con \+4, Int \+1, Wis \+3, Cha \+2  
Items bronze greatsword, bronze scale armor, shield  
---

AC 24; Fort \+19, Ref \+15, Will \+16  
HP 120; Resistances precision 5  
Powerful Build The Gibborim Champion counts as one size larger when determining carrying capacity and the amount they can push, drag, or lift.  
Heroic Blood The Champion gains a \+1 circumstance bonus to saving throws against fear effects.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze greatsword \+20 (forceful, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d6+9 slashing  
Melee \[A\] shield bash \+18 (agile), Damage 1d4+7 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] javelin \+15 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+7 piercing  
Mighty Blow \[A\] (attack) The Champion makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 1d6 damage of the weapon's type.  
---

Heroic Feat \[A\] (concentrate) The Champion channels their ancestral strength. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. They can use this ability once per day.  
Giant's Grip The Champion can wield two-handed weapons in one hand, though they still take a \-2 item penalty to attack rolls unless they have the appropriate feat.  
Warrior's Training The Champion is trained in all martial weapons. Additionally, they can use the Fighter class's weapon specialization feature with any weapon they are proficient with, even if they are not a Fighter.  
Tactical Formation When the Champion uses the Aid action, they can grant a \+3 status bonus instead of \+2. Additionally, they can use Aid as a single action instead of two actions once per day.

#### **Lore**

Gibborim Champions are the elite warriors of the hybrid lineage, standing 6½ to 8 feet tall with powerful, well-proportioned builds that combine human agility with residual giant strength. These mighty heroes serve as captains in Nephilim armies, commanders of Anakim household guards, or champions in Rephaim warbands. Their unique position as bridge-beings between humanity and giant-kin makes them valuable intermediaries and formidable combatants. Unlike pure giants who rely solely on overwhelming power, Gibborim Champions combine supernatural strength with tactical intelligence and battlefield awareness. They master weapons that are too heavy for ordinary humans but too small for true giants—bronze greatswords that would require two hands for a human can be wielded one-handed by a Gibborim. Their hybrid nature grants them enhanced endurance—they can fight longer and harder than pure humans, yet they don't suffer from the insatiable appetites that plague their giant kin. Many Gibborim Champions struggle with their dual heritage, torn between the righteous path of their human ancestry and the martial pride of their giant bloodline.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Gibborim Champions excel at frontline combat and battlefield leadership. Their Mighty Blow and Heroic Feat abilities make them dangerous damage dealers, while their Tactical Formation ability allows them to support allies effectively. Use their Giant's Grip trait to create interesting combat scenarios—perhaps they wield a massive two-handed weapon in one hand while using a shield or grappling with the other. Position them to maximize their Aid ability, supporting both melee and ranged allies. Pair them with human soldiers or lesser giants to create layered encounters where the Champion serves as both damage dealer and support. Their Powerful Build trait means they can interact with oversized objects and environments in ways that pure humans cannot. Consider giving each Champion a unique background and motivation—perhaps one seeks to prove that hybrid heritage can be a strength rather than a weakness, another fights to protect human communities from giant oppression, or a third serves a Nephilim warlord out of loyalty or necessity.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Gibborim Champion who fights honorably grants 1 RP. However, if the Champion offers surrender and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Gibborim Champions begin with 1 CP from their giant heritage. Each time they use their Heroic Feat ability, they must attempt a DC 12 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of channeling their giant ancestry. This corruption manifests as their eyes glowing with increased intensity and their voice taking on deeper, more resonant tones.

### **Gibborim Elite**

GIBBORIM ELITE CREATURE 9  
COMMON MEDIUM NG HUMANOID CHAMPION  
Perception \+20; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial  
Skills Athletics \+24, Intimidation \+20, Command Lore \+20, Warfare Lore \+22  
Str \+6, Dex \+3, Con \+5, Int \+2, Wis \+4, Cha \+3  
Items masterwork bronze greatsword, ornate bronze scale armor, ancestral shield  
---

AC 28; Fort \+24, Ref \+19, Will \+21  
HP 150; Resistances precision 10  
Heroic Resilience The Elite gains a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against death effects and negative damage.  
Ancestral Strength The Elite adds their Strength modifier to damage rolls twice instead of once when making melee attacks.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork bronze greatsword \+24 (forceful, holy, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d8+11 slashing plus 1d6 radiant  
Melee \[A\] shield bash \+22 (agile), Damage 1d6+9 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] javelin \+19 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+9 piercing  
Devastating Blow \[A\] (attack) The Elite makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 2d6 damage of the weapon's type.  
---

Tireless Rage \[A\] (concentrate) The Elite enters a state of ancestral fury. For 1 minute, they gain a \+3 status bonus to damage rolls and resistance 10 to all damage, but they take a \-1 penalty to AC. They can use this ability once per day.  
Improved Giant's Grip The Elite can wield two-handed weapons in one hand without taking the \-2 item penalty to attack rolls.  
War Leader \[A\] (concentrate) The Elite inspires their allies. For 1 minute, they and all allies within 30 feet gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and a \+1 status bonus to AC. They can use this ability once per day.  
Heroic Endurance The Elite can heal 1d8+9 Hit Points as a single action once per day.

#### **Lore**

Gibborim Elites represent the pinnacle of hybrid martial prowess, warriors who have mastered both their human tactical intelligence and their giant ancestral strength. Standing 7 to 8½ feet tall with the regal bearing of their giant kin and the strategic minds of their human heritage, these champions serve as field commanders, personal guards to Nephilim warlords, or leaders of independent mercenary companies. Their appearance combines the best features of both lineages—broad shoulders and powerful limbs from their giant ancestry, refined features and intense eyes from their human side. Many Elites bear visible marks of their heritage—unusual eye colors, celestial patterns on their skin, or hair that shimmers with unnatural hues. They are respected by both humans and giants, though never fully accepted by either. Some Elites use their unique position to mediate between the two worlds, while others embrace their hybrid nature as a source of strength and independence. Their weapons and armor are masterwork items that reflect their dual heritage—bronze greatswords with human-style hilts but giant-scale blades, shields bearing both human symbols and giant sigils.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Gibborim Elites are designed as elite combatants and battlefield leaders. Their Tireless Rage ability creates dramatic moments when they need to turn the tide of battle, while their War Leader ability makes them valuable support for groups of allies. Use their Devastating Blow ability strategically against key targets to maximize damage output. Position them to maximize their aura effect while using terrain for cover against ranged attacks. Pair them with human soldiers, lesser giants, or other Gibborim to create layered encounters where the Elite serves as both damage dealer and commander. Their Improved Giant's Grip trait means they can wield massive weapons with one hand while using shields or performing other actions with their free hand. Consider giving each Elite a unique personality and background—perhaps one leads a company of hybrid warriors seeking acceptance, another serves a Nephilim warlord out of loyalty to their giant heritage, or a third fights to protect human communities while struggling with their own corrupted bloodline.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Gibborim Elite who fights honorably grants 2 RP. However, if the Elite offers surrender and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Gibborim Elites begin with 2 CP from their giant heritage. Each time they use their Tireless Rage ability, they must attempt a DC 14 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of channeling their giant ancestry. This corruption manifests as their weapons glowing with darker fire and their eyes burning with increased intensity. When their CP reaches 5 or higher, they gain the Marked for Judgment condition.

### **Horim Cave-Dweller**

HORIM CAVE-DWELLER CREATURE 5  
COMMON MEDIUM N HUMANOID  
Perception \+14; darkvision 120 ft  
Languages Common, Giant, Terran  
Skills Acrobatics \+13, Athletics \+12, Stealth \+13, Stonework Lore \+14  
Str \+3, Dex \+4, Con \+3, Int \+2, Wis \+3, Cha \+0  
Items stone dagger, hide armor, climbing tools  
---

AC 20; Fort \+14, Ref \+15, Will \+12  
HP 85; Resistances bludgeoning 5 (from stone skin)  
Stone Skin The Cave-Dweller's skin has the toughness of stone. They gain resistance 5 to bludgeoning damage from non-magical attacks.  
Sunlight Sensitivity The Cave-Dweller is dazzled while in sunlight. Additionally, they take a \-1 status penalty to attack rolls and Perception checks that require sight while in direct sunlight.  
---

Speed 25 ft, Climb 20 ft  
Melee \[A\] stone dagger \+13 (agile, finesse, versatile S), Damage 1d4+5 piercing  
Melee \[A\] fist \+12 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 1d4+4 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown stone \+15 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+5 bludgeoning  
Stone Strike \[A\] (attack) The Cave-Dweller makes a melee Strike with a stone weapon. If it hits, the target takes an additional 1d4 bludgeoning damage.  
---

Earth Sense The Cave-Dweller can sense vibrations and movements through stone. They gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden creatures or objects in underground environments. Additionally, they can detect the presence of creatures within 30 feet of them that are in contact with the ground.  
Tunnel Fighter The Cave-Dweller excels at combat in confined spaces. They gain a \+1 circumstance bonus to attack rolls when fighting in narrow tunnels, caves, or other confined environments. Additionally, they can use the High Ground action without needing to be on higher elevation, provided they are fighting in an underground environment with natural features that provide tactical advantages.  
Stone Cunning The Cave-Dweller gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about stonework, geology, and underground environments. Additionally, they can sense the presence of stone and earth within 30 feet of them, allowing them to detect hidden passages, structural weaknesses, and mineral deposits.

#### **Lore**

Horim Cave-Dwellers are the smallest of the giant lineages, standing 6 to 7 feet tall with lean, wiry builds adapted for navigating narrow tunnels and climbing rock faces. Their skin is pale, almost translucent in some cases, with visible blue veins tracing patterns across their bodies. Their hair is typically white, silver, or pale blonde, and their eyes are large and luminous, capable of seeing in absolute darkness. The Horim retreated into the earth after the Watchers' fall, seeking refuge from the corruption and violence that plagued the surface world. They established extensive cave networks and underground settlements, carving their homes from living rock and developing sophisticated systems of tunnels, chambers, and ventilation shafts. Their society is organized around extended family clans, each controlling a specific cave system or underground region. Leadership is based on age, wisdom, and skill in stoneworking and survival. Horim settlements are hidden and well-defended, with multiple entrances and escape routes known only to clan members. They are master stoneworkers, carving intricate patterns and symbols into cave walls, creating beautiful crystalline formations, and crafting weapons and tools from the finest stone and bronze.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Horim Cave-Dwellers excel at ambush tactics and underground combat. Their Earth Sense ability makes them difficult to surprise in subterranean environments, while their Tunnel Fighter trait gives them advantages in confined spaces. Use their Stone Strike ability to deal additional damage in melee combat, particularly against armored foes. Position them in underground environments where their darkvision and terrain advantages are maximized. Pair them with other Horim or underground creatures to create layered encounters that test the party's ability to navigate and fight in subterranean environments. Their Stone Cunning trait means they can detect hidden passages and structural weaknesses, making them valuable guides or dangerous ambushers. Consider giving each Cave-Dweller a unique clan affiliation and specialty—perhaps one is a master stoneworker who can collapse tunnels, another is a crystal-seer who can predict enemy movements, or a third is a tunnel-rat who can navigate the most treacherous underground passages.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Horim Cave-Dweller grants 1 RP per character for removing a potential threat to surface communities. However, desecrating their underground homes or sacred crystal formations grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Horim Cave-Dwellers begin with 1 CP from their giant heritage. They automatically gain 1 CP per week spent on the surface world, representing their spiritual disconnection from their underground home. This corruption manifests as their skin becoming paler and their eyes losing their luminous quality.

---
