Earth Glide \[A\] (primal) The Stone-Shaper moves through solid earth or stone as if it were air. They can travel up to 30 feet through natural, unworked earth or stone. They cannot end their movement inside solid material, and they emerge at the nearest open space. This movement does not trigger reactions. They can use this ability once per day.  
Crystal Ward \[A\] (concentrate, primal) The Stone-Shaper creates a protective barrier of crystalline energy. For 1 minute, they gain resistance 10 to all damage from the triggering attack. If the attack is a critical hit, this resistance increases to 20\. They can use this ability once per day.  
Stone Form \[A\] (concentrate, primal) The Stone-Shaper's body transforms into living stone. For 1 minute, they gain resistance 15 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. They are immune to precision damage and critical hits. Their Speed is reduced by 10 feet, and they cannot use abilities that require flexibility or fine manipulation. They can use this ability once per day.  
Tremorsense The Stone-Shaper can sense vibrations through the ground, allowing them to pinpoint the location of creatures in contact with the earth within 30 feet. This sense is blocked by 5 feet of earth or 1 foot of solid stone.

#### **Lore**

Horim Stone-Shapers are the master artisans and spiritual leaders of Horim society, individuals who have dedicated their lives to understanding and shaping the stone that forms their world. Standing 6½ to 7½ feet tall with the lean, wiry builds of their people, these mystics possess an almost supernatural connection to the earth. Their skin bears faint crystalline patterns that glow with soft light in darkness, and their eyes contain flecks of mineral that sparkle when they use their powers. Stone-Shapers serve as architects, crafting the intricate cave networks and underground settlements that house Horim clans. They are also spiritual guides, interpreting the patterns in crystal formations and the vibrations in stone as messages from the earth itself. Some Stone-Shapers develop the ability to communicate with earth elementals and other subterranean spirits, forming alliances that protect their communities from surface threats. Their workshops are sacred spaces filled with half-finished sculptures, glowing crystals, and tools of exquisite craftsmanship. The most powerful Stone-Shapers can shape living rock with their bare hands, creating fortifications that grow like plants or weapons that are extensions of the earth itself.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Stone-Shapers are designed as support casters and area controllers rather than frontline combatants. Their Earth Glide ability makes them difficult to pin down, while their Crystal Ward and Stone Form abilities provide significant defensive capabilities. Use their Crystal Strike ability to restrain key party members, creating tactical advantages for supporting forces. Position them in underground environments where their tremorsense and terrain advantages are maximized. Pair them with Horim warriors, earth elementals, or other subterranean creatures to create layered encounters that test the party's ability to deal with both physical and magical threats. Their Master Stoneworker trait means they can detect and exploit structural weaknesses, potentially collapsing tunnels or creating barriers during combat. Consider giving each Stone-Shaper a unique specialty and crystal focus—perhaps one specializes in defensive fortifications, another in offensive crystal weapons, or a third in spiritual communion with earth spirits.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Learning from a Stone-Shaper's knowledge of the earth (without exploiting their people) grants 1 RP. Destroying their sacred crystal formations or forcing them to use their powers for corrupt purposes grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Horim Stone-Shapers begin with 2 CP from their giant heritage. Each time they use their Earth Glide or Stone Form abilities, they must attempt a DC 13 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of manipulating the earth's essence. This corruption manifests as their crystalline patterns glowing with darker light and their connection to the earth becoming more predatory than harmonious.

---

### **Elioud Deceiver**

ELIOUD DECEIVER CREATURE 4  
COMMON MEDIUM CE HUMANOID  
Perception \+12; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant  
Skills Deception \+15, Diplomacy \+13, Stealth \+14, Disguise Lore \+15  
Str \+2, Dex \+4, Con \+2, Int \+3, Wis \+1, Cha \+4  
Items bronze dagger, fine clothing, disguise kit  
---

AC 18; Fort \+10, Ref \+14, Will \+9  
HP 65; Resistances precision 5  
Diluted Blood The Deceiver can attempt to pass as a human of exceptional size and strength. When they do so, creatures that would normally be hostile to giants or giant-kin must succeed at a DC 15 Perception check to notice their true heritage.  
Ancestral Echo Once per day, the Deceiver can channel the strength of their giant ancestors. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks.  
---

Speed 30 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze dagger \+14 (agile, finesse, versatile S), Damage 1d4+5 piercing  
Ranged \[A\] thrown dagger \+16 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d4+5 piercing  
Deceptive Strike \[A\] (attack) The Deceiver makes a melee or ranged Strike. If the target is flat-footed against the Deceiver, the Strike deals an additional 1d6 precision damage.  
---

Master of Disguise The Deceiver gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to Deception checks to pass as a human and to Stealth checks to avoid detection. Additionally, when they are in a crowd of humans, they can use the Hide action even when they are observed.  
Silver Tongue The Deceiver can use their Charisma modifier instead of their Strength modifier for Intimidation checks. Additionally, they gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when dealing with creatures that are not already hostile.  
Blending In Once per day, when the Deceiver would be detected while using Stealth, they can attempt a DC 15 flat check. On a success, they remain hidden and can continue their movement or actions as if undetected.  
Ancestral Memory Once per day, when the Deceiver or an ally within 30 feet is reduced to 0 Hit Points, they can restore them to 1 Hit Point and grant them temporary Hit Points equal to their level. Additionally, they can immediately use their Ancestral Echo ability.

#### **Lore**

Elioud Deceivers are the third-generation offspring of giant lineages, standing 6'2" to 7 feet tall with features that are almost indistinguishable from exceptionally large humans. Their diluted blood allows them to pass as human when necessary, making them valuable spies, infiltrators, and intermediaries between giant and human societies. Deceivers are masters of disguise and deception, using their near-human appearance to move freely in human settlements while maintaining connections to their giant kin. Some Deceivers use their unique position to protect human communities from giant oppression, serving as double agents who warn of impending raids or negotiate truces. Others embrace their corrupted heritage, using their abilities to manipulate and exploit both humans and giants for personal gain. Their appearance varies widely depending on their specific ancestry—some bear subtle giant characteristics like broader shoulders or unusual eye colors, while others are virtually indistinguishable from large humans except for their exceptional strength and endurance. Deceivers often dress in practical clothing that helps them blend into human society, though some proudly display symbols of their giant heritage when among their own kind.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Elioud Deceivers excel at infiltration, ambush, and social manipulation. Their Master of Disguise and Blending In abilities make them difficult to detect, while their Deceptive Strike ability rewards them for attacking flat-footed targets. Use their Silver Tongue ability to create social challenges—perhaps they can talk their way out of combat or manipulate NPCs into serving their interests. Position them to maximize their stealth advantages, using crowds or urban environments for cover. Pair them with human collaborators, giant allies, or other Deceivers to create layered encounters that test both combat and social skills. Their Ancestral Memory ability makes them valuable support characters, able to save allies from death and immediately boost their combat effectiveness. Consider giving each Deceiver a unique motivation and allegiance—perhaps one works to protect human communities from giant oppression, another serves a Nephilim warlord as a spy, or a third seeks to build bridges between the two worlds for personal profit.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating an Elioud Deceiver who uses their abilities for corrupt purposes grants 1 RP. However, if the Deceiver was working to protect innocent lives and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Elioud Deceivers begin with 1 CP from their giant heritage. Each time they use their Master of Disguise ability to infiltrate a righteous community or their Silver Tongue ability to manipulate righteous NPCs, they must attempt a DC 12 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of deception and manipulation. This corruption manifests as their human disguise becoming less convincing and their true nature becoming more apparent to those with spiritual insight.

---

### **Elioud Champion**

ELIOUD CHAMPION CREATURE 7  
UNIQUE MEDIUM N HUMANOID WARRIOR  
Perception \+16; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Giant, Celestial  
Skills Athletics \+20, Diplomacy \+15, Intimidation \+18, Warfare Lore \+17  
Str \+5, Dex \+3, Con \+4, Int \+2, Wis \+3, Cha \+3  
Items masterwork bronze greatsword, bronze scale armor, ancestral amulet  
---

AC 23; Fort \+19, Ref \+16, Will \+16  
HP 110; Resistances precision 5  
Powerful Build The Champion counts as one size larger when determining carrying capacity and the amount they can push, drag, or lift.  
Mighty Heritage The Champion gains a \+1 circumstance bonus to Athletics checks to Grapple, Shove, and Trip.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork bronze greatsword \+20 (forceful, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d6+10 slashing  
Melee \[A\] fist \+18 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 1d6+8 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] javelin \+16 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+8 piercing  
Ancestral Strike \[A\] (attack) The Champion makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 1d8 damage of the weapon's type.  
---

Heroic Feat \[A\] (concentrate) The Champion channels their ancestral strength. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to Strength-based skill checks and a \+2 status bonus to damage rolls with Strength-based attacks. They can use this ability once per day.  
Diplomatic Presence The Champion can use their Charisma modifier instead of their Strength modifier for Intimidation checks. Additionally, they gain a \+2 circumstance bonus to Diplomacy checks when dealing with creatures that have the giant trait.  
Ancestral Memory Once per day, when the Champion or an ally within 30 feet is reduced to 0 Hit Points, they can restore them to 1 Hit Point and grant them temporary Hit Points equal to their level. Additionally, they can immediately use their Heroic Feat ability.  
Champion's Aura (aura, emotion, mental) 20-foot aura. Allies within the aura gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 18 Will save or be frightened 1\.

#### **Lore**

Elioud Champions are the elite warriors and diplomats of the third-generation giant lineage, individuals who have mastered the art of bridging the gap between human and giant societies. Standing 6'6" to 7'2" tall with powerful builds that combine human agility with residual giant strength, these champions serve as mediators, bodyguards, and military leaders in mixed communities. Their appearance is almost entirely human, with only subtle signs of their giant heritage—unusually broad shoulders, exceptional height, or eyes that occasionally flash with celestial light when they channel their ancestral power. Elioud Champions are respected by both humans and giants for their unique perspective and abilities. They understand the strengths and weaknesses of both lineages, allowing them to navigate complex social situations and mediate conflicts that would otherwise escalate into violence. Some Champions use their position to protect human communities from giant oppression, while others serve giant warlords as trusted advisors and enforcers. A few rare Champions seek to build lasting peace between the two worlds, establishing mixed communities where humans and giants can coexist. Their weapons and armor reflect their dual heritage—masterwork bronze items that are sized for large humans but bear subtle giant sigils or celestial patterns.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Elioud Champions excel at both combat and social situations. Their Ancestral Strike and Heroic Feat abilities make them dangerous damage dealers, while their Diplomatic Presence and Champion's Aura abilities provide valuable support for allies. Use their Ancestral Memory ability to create dramatic moments when allies are in danger. Position them to maximize their aura effect while using terrain for cover against ranged attacks. Pair them with human soldiers, giant allies, or other Elioud to create layered encounters that test both combat and social skills. Their Powerful Build trait means they can interact with oversized objects and environments in ways that pure humans cannot. Consider giving each Champion a unique background and motivation—perhaps one leads a mixed community seeking peace, another serves a Nephilim warlord as a trusted lieutenant, or a third works as a mercenary selling their services to the highest bidder.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating an Elioud Champion who fights honorably grants 1 RP. However, if the Champion was working to protect innocent lives and is executed, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Elioud Champions begin with 1 CP from their giant heritage. Each time they use their Heroic Feat ability, they must attempt a DC 13 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of channeling their giant ancestry. This corruption manifests as their eyes flashing with darker light and their voice taking on deeper, more resonant tones when they speak of their heritage.

### **Cainite Warrior**

CAINITE WARRIOR CREATURE 3  
COMMON MEDIUM N HUMANOID  
Perception \+9; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Ancient  
Skills Athletics \+10, Intimidation \+8, Society \+7, Warfare Lore \+9  
Str \+3, Dex \+2, Con \+2, Int \+1, Wis \+1, Cha \+2  
Items bronze sword, bronze scale armor, shield  
---

AC 18; Fort \+11, Ref \+9, Will \+8  
HP 50; Resistances precision 5  
Mark of Cain When a creature reduces the Cainite Warrior to 0 Hit Points, the attacker must attempt a DC 15 Will save. On a failure, the attacker is marked by divine retribution and takes 1d6 divine damage at the start of each of their turns for 1 minute. This represents the protection granted to Cain by the Most High.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] bronze sword \+10 (versatile P), Damage 1d8+4 slashing  
Melee \[A\] shield bash \+8 (agile), Damage 1d4+3 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] javelin \+8 (range 30 ft, thrown), Damage 1d6+3 piercing  
Shield Block \[R\] (reaction) Trigger: The Cainite Warrior is hit by a melee attack. Effect: The Warrior raises their shield, reducing the damage from the triggering attack by 5\.  
---

City Training The Cainite Warrior gains a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about urban environments, crafts, trades, and city politics.  
Builder's Heritage The Cainite Warrior is trained in one type of artisan's tools (blacksmith's, carpenter's, mason's, or leatherworker's). They can use these tools to Aid themselves or others on skill checks related to their chosen craft.  
Urban Survivor The Cainite Warrior gains a \+1 circumstance bonus to Stealth checks in urban environments and a \+1 circumstance bonus to Perception checks to notice hidden dangers or ambushes in cities and towns.

#### **Lore**

Cainite Warriors are the defenders and enforcers of the first human civilization, descendants of Cain who built the city of Enoch and established the foundations of urban life. Unlike the nomadic Wanderers or the righteous Sethites, Cainites embraced city-building, metallurgy, and the arts taught by Jubal and Tubal-Cain. Cainite Warriors serve as city guards, mercenary companies, and personal protectors for Cainite merchants and artisans. They are skilled in the use of bronze weapons and armor, trained in formation fighting and urban tactics. Their society values practical knowledge, artistic expression, and material prosperity over spiritual concerns. While they bear the Mark of Cain—a visible sigil that protects them from vengeance—they are not inherently evil. Many Cainite Warriors are honorable defenders of their communities, though they may be morally compromised by their acceptance of Nephilim oversight and their use of Watcher-taught technologies.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Cainite Warriors excel at urban combat and formation fighting. Their Shield Block reaction makes them durable frontline fighters, while their City Training and Urban Survivor traits give them advantages in city environments. Use them as guards, mercenaries, or city militia in encounters set in Cainite settlements. Pair them with other Cainite Warriors to create shield walls or tactical formations. Their Mark of Cain ability creates interesting consequences for players who kill them—attacking a Cainite Warrior carries spiritual risk. Consider giving each Warrior a unique background and motivation—perhaps one is a loyal city guard protecting their home, another is a mercenary fighting for coin, or a third is a conflicted warrior questioning their society's alliance with Nephilim overlords.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Cainite Warrior who fights honorably grants 0 RP/CP. However, if the Warrior was protecting innocent civilians and is executed after surrendering, each character gains 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Cainite Warriors begin with 1 CP from their society's moral compromises. They do not gain additional CP during encounters unless they commit specific corrupt acts (such as executing prisoners or betraying allies).

---

### **Baal-Priest**

BAAL-PRIEST CREATURE 6  
UNIQUE MEDIUM CE HUMANOID CLERIC  
Perception \+14; darkvision  
Languages Common, Ancient, Abyssal  
Skills Deception \+15, Intimidation \+14, Occultism \+16, Forbidden Lore \+16  
Str \+2, Dex \+3, Con \+3, Int \+4, Wis \+2, Cha \+5  
Items sacrificial bronze dagger, dark robes, blood chalice  
---

AC 22; Fort \+13, Ref \+14, Will \+15  
HP 90; Immunities mental; Resistances negative 10  
Blood Magic The Baal-Priest can sacrifice their own vitality to fuel their dark rituals. As a single action, they can take 1d6 persistent bleed damage to gain 1 Focus Point. They can use this ability only if they have a focus pool.  
Dark Rituals The Baal-Priest gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Perform Rituals involving blood sacrifice, necromancy, or demonic summoning.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] sacrificial bronze dagger \+14 (agile, finesse, magical), Damage 1d4+5 piercing plus 1d6 negative  
Ranged \[A\] thrown dagger \+16 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d4+5 piercing plus 1d6 negative  
Blood Curse \[A\] (attack, divine, necromancy, negative) The Baal-Priest makes a melee or ranged Strike with their dagger. If it hits, the target takes an additional 3d6 negative damage and must attempt a DC 21 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is drained 1\. On a critical failure, the target is drained 2\.  
---

Dark Spells DC 23, attack \+17; Spells Prepared (caster level 6th)  
*At Will* *chill touch, detect magic, ghost sound, read aura*  
*3/Day Each* *animate dead, harm, vampiric touch*  
*1/Day Each* *circle of death, create undead, finger of death*  
Sacrificial Ritual \[A\] (concentrate, divine, necromancy) The Baal-Priest performs a dark ritual requiring the sacrifice of a living creature. If a willing or helpless creature is sacrificed, the Baal-Priest heals 4d8 Hit Points and gains temporary Hit Points equal to their level. If an unwilling creature is sacrificed against its will, the Baal-Priest instead gains 2 Corruption Points and the sacrificed creature's soul is damned.  
Demonic Communion \[A\] (concentrate, divine) The Baal-Priest communes with demonic entities. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to spell attack rolls and spell DCs. However, each use of this ability requires a DC 16 flat check. On a failure, the Baal-Priest gains 1 Corruption Point as demonic influence seeps into their soul.

#### **Lore**

Baal-Priests are the corrupted religious leaders of Cainite cities who have abandoned worship of the Most High in favor of demonic entities and forbidden Watcher teachings. The name "Baal" (meaning "lord" or "master") represents their devotion to fallen powers rather than the Creator. These priests conduct dark rituals involving blood sacrifice, necromancy, and demonic summoning in hidden temples or underground chambers. They wear dark robes adorned with forbidden symbols and carry sacrificial daggers used to spill blood in service of their dark masters. Baal-Priests often serve as advisors to Cainite city leaders, using their dark knowledge to maintain power and control over the population. Some have formed pacts with disembodied Nephilim spirits (Refa'im) or lesser demons, gaining supernatural power at the cost of their souls. Their presence corrupts entire communities, turning once-prosperous cities into centers of darkness and oppression.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Baal-Priests function primarily as spellcasters and ritualists rather than frontline combatants. Their Blood Curse ability makes them dangerous in melee, while their Dark Spells provide versatile offensive and control options. Use their Sacrificial Ritual ability sparingly for dramatic moments—perhaps when reduced to half HP or when a key ally falls. Their Demonic Communion ability creates tension—they may hesitate to use it if their CP is already high. Position them behind Cainite Warriors or other defenders while they cast debilitating spells. Pair them with undead minions, demonic allies, or corrupted human followers to create layered encounters. Their Blood Magic ability means they can fuel powerful spells even when out of Focus Points, but at great personal cost. Consider giving each Baal-Priest a unique demonic patron and specific ritual specialties—perhaps one specializes in necromancy, another in blood magic, or a third in demonic summoning.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Baal-Priest grants 2 RP per character for removing a source of corruption from the world. However, using their dark rituals or keeping their sacrificial items grants 2 CP.  
* For GMs: Baal-Priests begin encounters with 8 CP and automatically gain 1 CP per round when using dark rituals or demonic communion. This corruption manifests as their eyes glowing with red fire and their voice taking on demonic echoes. When their CP reaches 15 or higher, they gain the Condemned condition and may be possessed by their demonic patrons.

### **Lamech the Avenger**

LAMECH THE AVENGER CREATURE 10  
UNIQUE MEDIUM CE HUMANOID WARRIOR  
Perception \+20; low-light vision  
Languages Common, Ancient  
Skills Athletics \+22, Intimidation \+21, Survival \+18, Warfare Lore \+20  
Str \+6, Dex \+4, Con \+5, Int \+2, Wis \+3, Cha \+4  
Items Tubal-Cain's Blade (masterwork bronze greatsword), ornate bronze plate armor, avenger's cloak  
---

AC 28; Fort \+24, Ref \+20, Will \+21  
HP 160; Immunities fear; Resistances precision 10  
Seventy-and-Sevenfold When Lamech is reduced to 0 Hit Points, he does not die immediately. Instead, his attacker is cursed with divine vengeance. The attacker must attempt a DC 26 Will save. On a failure, the attacker takes 7d10 divine damage and is marked for vengeance for 24 hours. During this time, any creature that attacks the marked attacker gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and deals an additional 1d6 divine damage. This represents Lamech's infamous declaration: "If Cain shall be avenged sevenfold, truly Lamech seventy and sevenfold" (Genesis 4:24).  
Avenger's Fury Lamech gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls against creatures that have killed his allies or family members.  
---

Speed 30 ft  
Melee \[A\] Tubal-Cain's Blade \+24 (forceful, holy, sweep, two-handed), Damage 2d12+13 slashing plus 1d6 radiant  
Melee \[A\] fist \+22 (agile, nonlethal), Damage 1d10+11 bludgeoning  
Ranged \[A\] thrown greatsword \+19 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 2d12+11 slashing  
Avenger's Strike \[A\] (attack) Lamech makes a melee Strike. If it hits, the target takes an additional 3d8 damage of the weapon's type. If the target has killed one of Lamech's allies or family members, this additional damage increases to 4d8.  
---

Legendary Resistance Lamech can reroll a failed saving throw once per day. He must use the result of the second roll, even if it is worse.  
Tribal War Cry \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, mental) Lamech lets out a mighty war cry that inspires his allies and terrifies his enemies. For 1 minute, Lamech and all allies within 30 feet gain a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. Enemies within 30 feet must attempt a DC 24 Will save or be frightened 1\. Lamech can use this ability once per day.  
Unbreakable Lamech is immune to the clumsy, enfeebled, and slowed conditions. When he would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, he can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to his level. He can use this ability once per day.  
Avenger's Legacy Lamech gains a \+4 status bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase his Corruption Points. This represents his fierce protection of his family line despite his moral compromises.

#### **Lore**

Lamech the Avenger stands as one of the most infamous figures of the antediluvian world—a seventh-generation descendant of Cain known for his violent nature and his declaration of seventy-sevenfold vengeance. According to Genesis 4:23-24, Lamech boasted to his wives about killing a young man who had injured him, declaring that if Cain's killer would be avenged sevenfold, his own killer would be avenged seventy-sevenfold. This declaration established a pattern of escalating violence that would characterize the pre-Flood world. Lamech was also the father of Noah (through his wife Naamah), making him a pivotal figure in the genealogy leading to the Flood survivor. His life represents the tragic contradiction of the Cainite line—capable of great violence and moral compromise, yet also producing the one who would preserve humanity through judgment. Lamech's weapon, Tubal-Cain's Blade, is said to have been forged by his ancestor Tubal-Cain himself, imbued with the metallurgical knowledge that defined Cainite civilization. His armor bears the marks of countless battles, and his cloak is woven from the hides of beasts he has slain in vengeance.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Lamech the Avenger is designed as a campaign-defining antagonist or tragic anti-hero. His Seventy-and-Sevenfold ability creates dramatic consequences for players who attempt to kill him—defeating him may curse the entire party with divine vengeance. His Avenger's Strike and Avenger's Fury abilities make him particularly dangerous against those who have harmed his allies or family. Use his Tribal War Cry ability to inspire supporting forces and create battlefield control. His Unbreakable ability ensures he survives encounters that would fell lesser warriors. Position him as a central figure in Cainite society—perhaps as a warlord protecting his city, a mercenary leader selling his services to the highest bidder, or a conflicted father struggling to protect his son Noah from the corruption surrounding them. His Avenger's Legacy ability reflects his complex moral position—he is corrupted by violence yet fiercely protective of his bloodline.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating Lamech the Avenger grants 3 RP per character for removing a source of violent corruption from the world. However, if Lamech was protecting innocent family members (including young Noah) and is executed, each character gains 2 CP.  
* For GMs: Lamech begins with 7 CP from his violent nature and moral compromises. Each time he uses his Avenger's Strike ability to kill a creature, he must attempt a DC 17 flat check. On a failure, he gains 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of his vengeance. This corruption manifests as his eyes burning with red fire and his weapon glowing with darker light. Despite his high CP, his Avenger's Legacy ability reflects his protective nature toward his family line.

### **Watcher Cultist**

WATCHER CULTIST CREATURE 2  
COMMON MEDIUM CE HUMANOID

Languages Common, Ancient  
Skills Deception \+8, Occultism \+9, Stealth \+7, Forbidden Lore \+9  
Str \+1, Dex \+3, Con \+2, Int \+3, Wis \+1, Cha \+2  
Items ritual dagger, dark robes, forbidden scroll  
---

AC 16; Fort \+8, Ref \+9, Will \+7  
HP 35; Resistances precision 5  
Forbidden Knowledge The Cultist gains a \+1 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about forbidden arts, Watcher teachings, and corrupted practices.  
Cult Training The Cultist is trained in the use of ritual daggers and dark robes that provide concealment in shadowy environments.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] ritual dagger \+8 (agile, finesse, magical), Damage 1d4+3 piercing plus 1d4 negative  
Ranged \[A\] thrown dagger \+10 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d4+3 piercing plus 1d4 negative  
Dark Whisper \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, mental, occult) The Cultist whispers forbidden knowledge to a creature within 30 feet. The target must attempt a DC 15 Will save. On a failure, the target is fascinated for 1 round. On a critical failure, the target is fascinated for 1 minute and gains 1 Corruption Point from exposure to forbidden knowledge.  
---

Occult Spells DC 17, attack \+11; Spells Prepared (caster level 2nd)  
*At Will* *detect magic, ghost sound, message*  
*1/Day Each* *charm, illusory disguise, unseen servant*  
Blood Ritual \[A\] (concentrate, divine, necromancy) The Cultist performs a minor blood ritual, cutting themselves to fuel dark magic. They take 1d4 persistent bleed damage and gain 1 Focus Point. This represents their willingness to sacrifice their own vitality for forbidden power.  
Cultist's Zeal When the Cultist reduces a creature to 0 Hit Points, they gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls until the end of their next turn. This represents their fanatical devotion to their dark masters.

#### **Lore**

Watcher Cultists are humans who have willingly embraced the forbidden teachings of the fallen Watchers, forming secret societies dedicated to preserving and expanding corrupted knowledge. These cults operate in hidden chambers beneath Cainite cities, in abandoned Watcher ruins, or in secluded wilderness locations where they can practice their dark arts without interference from righteous communities. Cultists wear dark robes adorned with forbidden symbols and carry ritual daggers used in blood sacrifices and dark ceremonies. They study ancient scrolls containing Watcher teachings on warfare, enchantments, astrology, and necromancy, often at great spiritual cost. Some cults worship specific Watchers as gods, building altars and performing rituals to honor their fallen masters. Others seek to resurrect or free bound Watchers, believing that their return will grant humanity even greater power. Watcher Cultists are often recruited from the Sorcerer Clans or from Cainite society, drawn by the promise of forbidden knowledge and supernatural power.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Watcher Cultists excel at ambush tactics and psychological warfare. Their Dark Whisper ability can remove key party members from combat, while their Occult Spells provide versatile control options. Use their Blood Ritual ability sparingly—they may hesitate to use it if already injured. Position them in shadowy environments where their dark robes provide concealment. Pair them with other cultists, corrupted beasts, or minor demons to create layered encounters that test both combat and spiritual resilience. Their Cultist's Zeal ability makes them more dangerous after securing kills, encouraging them to focus fire on weakened targets. Consider giving each cult a unique focus and patron Watcher—perhaps one specializes in Azazel's warfare magic, another in Semyaza's enchantments, or a third in Baraqel's divinations.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Watcher Cultist grants 1 RP per character for removing a source of corruption from the world. However, studying their forbidden scrolls or using their ritual items grants 1 CP.  
* For GMs: Watcher Cultists begin with 3 CP from their voluntary embrace of forbidden knowledge. Each time they use their Dark Whisper ability or cast a spell from their Occult Spells list, they must attempt a DC 13 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of wielding corrupted power. This corruption manifests as their eyes glowing with darker light and their whispers becoming more seductive and dangerous.

---

### **Watcher Cult Leader**

WATCHER CULT LEADER CREATURE 5  
UNIQUE MEDIUM CE HUMANOID SORCERER  
Perception \+12; darkvision, see invisibility  
Languages Common, Ancient, Celestial, Abyssal  
Skills Deception \+14, Intimidation \+13, Occultism \+15, Forbidden Lore \+15  
Str \+2, Dex \+3, Con \+3, Int \+4, Wis \+2, Cha \+5  
Items masterwork ritual dagger, ornate dark robes, Watcher's Seal  
---

AC 20; Fort \+11, Ref \+12, Will \+13  
HP 75; Immunities mental; Resistances negative 10, precision 5  
Watcher's Seal The Cult Leader bears a magical seal representing their patron Watcher. This seal grants them a \+2 status bonus to saving throws against effects that would increase their Corruption Points. Additionally, they can use the seal to identify other cult members or corrupted beings within 60 feet.  
Forbidden Mastery The Cult Leader gains a \+2 circumstance bonus to checks to Recall Knowledge about forbidden arts, Watcher teachings, and corrupted practices. They can also cast spells from their patron Watcher's tradition without gaining Corruption Points, provided the spell is of 3rd level or lower.  
---

Speed 25 ft  
Melee \[A\] masterwork ritual dagger \+13 (agile, finesse, magical), Damage 1d4+4 piercing plus 1d6 negative  
Ranged \[A\] thrown dagger \+15 (range 20 ft, thrown), Damage 1d4+4 piercing plus 1d6 negative  
Corrupting Touch \[A\] (attack, divine, necromancy, negative) The Cult Leader makes a melee Strike with their dagger. If it hits, the target takes an additional 2d6 negative damage and must attempt a DC 19 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target gains 1 Corruption Point. On a critical failure, the target gains 2 Corruption Points.  
---

Watcher Spells DC 21, attack \+15; Spells Prepared (caster level 5th)  
*At Will* *detect magic, ghost sound, light, mage hand, message, read aura*  
*3/Day Each* *charm, darkness, dispel magic, fear, invisibility, suggestion*  
*1/Day Each* *animate dead, dominate, shadow blast, vampiric touch*  
Watcher's Gift \[A\] (concentrate, divine) The Cult Leader calls upon their patron Watcher for assistance. For 1 minute, they gain a \+2 status bonus to one of the following: attack rolls, skill checks, saving throws, or spell DCs. They can use this ability once per day.  
Blood Sacrifice \[A\] (concentrate, divine, necromancy) The Cult Leader performs a major blood ritual, sacrificing a willing or helpless creature. If a creature is sacrificed, the Cult Leader heals 3d8 Hit Points and gains temporary Hit Points equal to their level. If an unwilling creature is sacrificed against its will, the Cult Leader instead gains 2 Corruption Points and the sacrificed creature's soul is damned.  
Cult Leader's Aura (aura, emotion, mental) 20-foot aura. Allies within the aura gain a \+1 status bonus to attack rolls and saving throws. Enemies that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 19 Will save or be frightened 1\. This represents the Cult Leader's fanatical influence over their followers.

#### **Lore**

Watcher Cult Leaders are the high priests and masters of forbidden knowledge within Watcher-worshipping cults. These powerful sorcerers have dedicated their lives to studying and practicing the arts taught by specific fallen Watchers—Azazel's warfare magic, Semyaza's enchantments, Baraqel's astrology, or other corrupted traditions. They wear ornate dark robes adorned with their patron Watcher's symbols and carry masterwork ritual daggers used in major blood sacrifices and dark ceremonies. Cult Leaders often establish hidden temples or sanctuaries where they train new cultists, perform complex rituals, and commune with their patron Watchers or disembodied Nephilim spirits (Refa'im). Some Cult Leaders claim direct descent from specific Watchers or Nephilim, using this lineage to justify their authority over lesser cultists. Others have formed pacts with bound Watchers, receiving visions and power in exchange for working toward their masters' eventual release. Their presence corrupts entire regions, turning once-peaceful settlements into centers of darkness and forbidden knowledge.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Watcher Cult Leaders function as powerful spellcasters and ritualists. Their Corrupting Touch ability makes them dangerous in melee, while their Watcher Spells provide versatile offensive, control, and support options. Use their Watcher's Gift ability to enhance their most dangerous spells or abilities. Their Blood Sacrifice ability should be used sparingly for dramatic moments—perhaps when reduced to half HP or when a key ally falls. Position them behind cultist followers or other defenders while they cast debilitating spells. Pair them with Watcher Cultists, corrupted beasts, undead minions, or minor demons to create layered encounters that test both combat and spiritual resilience. Their Cult Leader's Aura creates battlefield control, inspiring allies while intimidating enemies. Consider giving each Cult Leader a unique patron Watcher and specific ritual specialties—perhaps one specializes in Azazel's warfare magic and blood rituals, another in Semyaza's enchantments and mind control, or a third in Baraqel's divinations and celestial magic.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating a Watcher Cult Leader grants 2 RP per character for removing a major source of corruption from the world. However, studying their forbidden texts or using their ritual items grants 2 CP.  
* For GMs: Watcher Cult Leaders begin with 6 CP from their deep immersion in forbidden knowledge. Each time they use their Blood Sacrifice ability or cast a spell of 4th level or higher from their Watcher Spells list, they must attempt a DC 15 flat check. On a failure, they gain 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of wielding powerful corrupted magic. This corruption manifests as their eyes glowing with intense red or black fire, their voice taking on supernatural echoes, and their shadow moving independently of their body. When their CP reaches 10 or higher, they gain the Marked for Judgment condition and may be possessed by their patron Watcher's spirit.

---

### **Refa'im Spirit**

REFA'IM SPIRIT CREATURE 3  
COMMON SMALL CE INCORPOREAL UNDEAD  
Perception \+10; darkvision, spirit sense 60 ft  
Languages Common, Giant (understands but cannot speak)  
Skills Stealth \+12, Survival \+9, Spirit Lore \+10  
Str \-5, Dex \+4, Con \+2, Int \+1, Wis \+3, Cha \+2  
Items none  
---

AC 17; Fort \+7, Ref \+11, Will \+10  
HP 40; Immunities bleed, death effects, disease, paralyzed, poison, precision, unconscious; Resistances all damage 5 (except force and positive)  
Incorporeal The Refa'im Spirit has no physical body. It can move through solid objects, though it cannot end its movement inside them. It takes double damage from force and positive damage.  
Spirit Sense The Refa'im Spirit can sense the presence of living creatures within 60 feet, even through solid objects.  
---

Speed 20 ft, fly 20 ft (hover)  
Melee \[A\] spectral touch \+12 (agile, finesse, negative, nonlethal), Damage 1d6+3 negative  
Possession Attempt \[A\] (attack, divine, mental, necromancy) The Refa'im Spirit attempts to possess a living creature within 10 feet. The target must attempt a DC 16 Will save. On a failure, the Spirit possesses the target's body. While possessed, the target is controlled by the Spirit and gains the Refa'im Spirit's immunities and resistances. The possession ends if the Spirit is reduced to 0 Hit Points or if the target succeeds at a new Will save at the end of each of its turns.  
---

Ethereal Jaunt \[A\] (concentrate, transmutation) The Refa'im Spirit shifts between the Material Plane and the Ethereal Plane. It can use this ability once per round.  
Whisper of Corruption \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, mental, occult) The Spirit whispers corrupted thoughts to a living creature within 30 feet. The target must attempt a DC 16 Will save. On a failure, the target gains 1 Corruption Point and is sickened 1 until the end of the Spirit's next turn. On a critical failure, the target gains 2 Corruption Points.  
Spiritual Drain When the Refa'im Spirit reduces a living creature to 0 Hit Points, it heals 1d8 Hit Points.

#### **Lore**

Refa'im (meaning "shades" or "ghosts" in Hebrew) are the disembodied spirits of dead Nephilim and other giant-kin that refused to pass peacefully into the afterlife. According to the Book of Enoch, when the Nephilim perished in the Flood, their physical bodies were destroyed but their angelic spirits could not die. These spirits became "evil spirits" that wander the earth, seeking to possess living bodies and continue their corrupt influence. Refa'im are not true demons in the later theological sense but rather the restless souls of the antediluvian giants—beings caught between life and death, unable to find rest because of their corrupted nature. They appear as faint, shimmering humanoid forms with elongated limbs and hollow eyes, their outlines shifting like smoke. They are drawn to places of death, corruption, and spiritual darkness—battlefields, burial grounds, Watcher ruins, and the territories of their living kin. Refa'im seek to possess living hosts to experience physical sensation again and to continue their work of corruption. They particularly target those with giant bloodlines or high Corruption Points, as these souls are more receptive to their influence.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Refa'im Spirits excel at psychological warfare and possession tactics. Their incorporeal nature makes them difficult to harm with non-magical weapons, while their Possession Attempt ability can turn party members against each other. Use their Whisper of Corruption ability to gradually corrupt the party during extended encounters. Position them to maximize their spirit sense advantage—they can attack from unexpected angles through walls or floors. Pair them with other undead, corrupted beings, or living allies who can protect them from positive damage. Their Ethereal Jaunt ability makes them difficult to pin down—they may retreat to the Ethereal Plane to heal or reposition. Consider using Refa'im Spirits as recurring antagonists—they may escape possession attempts only to return later with reinforcements. Their Spiritual Drain ability means they become more dangerous as the encounter progresses, especially against weakened party members.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Destroying a Refa'im Spirit grants 1 RP per character for cleansing a corrupted spirit from the world. However, allowing a possessed ally to be killed rather than exorcised grants 1 CP per character.  
* For GMs: Refa'im Spirits automatically gain 1 CP per day they remain unbound, representing their growing corruption and desperation. This corruption manifests as their forms becoming darker and more defined, their whispers becoming more seductive, and their possession attempts becoming more frequent.

---

### **Refa'im Wraith**

REFA'IM WRAITH CREATURE 7  
UNIQUE MEDIUM CE INCORPOREAL UNDEAD

Languages Common, Giant, Abyssal (understands but cannot speak)  
Skills Intimidation \+17, Stealth \+18, Spirit Lore \+16  
Str \-5, Dex \+5, Con \+3, Int \+2, Wis \+4, Cha \+5  
Items none  
---

AC 23; Fort \+13, Ref \+17, Will \+16  
HP 90; Immunities bleed, death effects, disease, paralyzed, poison, precision, unconscious; Resistances all damage 10 (except force and positive)  
Incorporeal The Refa'im Wraith has no physical body. It can move through solid objects, though it cannot end its movement inside them. It takes double damage from force and positive damage.  
Spirit Mastery The Wraith can sense the Corruption Points of living creatures within 60 feet, determining whether they have 0, 1–5, 6–10, or 11+ CP. It gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls against creatures with 3+ CP.  
---

Speed 30 ft, fly 30 ft (hover)  
Melee \[A\] wraith touch \+18 (agile, finesse, negative, nonlethal), Damage 2d6+6 negative  
Possession Mastery \[A\] (attack, divine, mental, necromancy) The Wraith attempts to possess a living creature within 15 feet. The target must attempt a DC 22 Will save. On a failure, the Wraith possesses the target's body. While possessed, the target is controlled by the Wraith and gains the Wraith's immunities and resistances. The possession ends if the Wraith is reduced to 0 Hit Points or if the target succeeds at a new Will save at the end of each of its turns. On a critical failure, the target cannot attempt new saves to end the possession for 1 minute.  
---

Ethereal Shift \[A\] (concentrate, transmutation) The Wraith shifts between the Material Plane and the Ethereal Plane. It can use this ability as a single action and can remain on either plane indefinitely.  
Corrupting Whisper \[A\] (concentrate, emotion, mental, occult) The Wraith whispers corrupted thoughts to all living creatures within 30 feet. Each target must attempt a DC 22 Will save. On a failure, the target gains 1 Corruption Point and is frightened 1 until the end of the Wraith's next turn. On a critical failure, the target gains 2 Corruption Points and is frightened 2\.  
Soul Drain When the Wraith reduces a living creature to 0 Hit Points, it heals 2d8 Hit Points and gains temporary Hit Points equal to the creature's level.  
Spirit Command \[A\] (concentrate, divine, mental) The Wraith commands all Refa'im Spirits within 60 feet. Each Spirit gains a \+2 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls for 1 minute.

#### **Lore**

Refa'im Wraiths are the most powerful and ancient of the disembodied Nephilim spirits—chieftains, warlords, or particularly corrupted individuals who retained exceptional power after death. These wraiths appear as towering, semi-transparent figures with the distinctive features of their giant lineages (elongated necks for Anakim, gaunt features for Rephaim, etc.), their forms wreathed in swirling darkness that seems to absorb light. Unlike common Refa'im Spirits that drift aimlessly, Wraiths maintain purpose and intelligence, often seeking to rebuild their power bases or complete unfinished business from their mortal lives. Some Wraiths establish domains in places saturated with death energy—ancient battlefields, mass graves, or Watcher ruins—where they gather lesser spirits and corrupted beings into armies of the dead. Others serve as advisors to living Nephilim warlords, whispering strategies and forbidden knowledge from beyond the grave. A few rare Wraiths have achieved such mastery over their spiritual form that they can manifest physical effects—chilling the air, moving objects, or leaving frost patterns on surfaces. Their whispers carry the weight of centuries of corruption, and their possession attempts are nearly impossible to resist without divine protection.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Refa'im Wraiths are designed as major antagonists for mid-level parties. Their Possession Mastery ability can remove key party members from combat, while their Corrupting Whisper affects multiple targets simultaneously. Use their Spirit Command ability to coordinate attacks with lesser Refa'im Spirits. Position them to maximize their spirit sense advantage—they can attack from unexpected angles and target the most corrupted party members first. Pair them with Refa'im Spirits, undead minions, or corrupted living allies to create layered encounters that test both physical and spiritual resilience. Their Ethereal Shift ability makes them extremely difficult to pin down—they may retreat to heal, reposition, or set up devastating ambushes. Consider giving each Wraith a unique personality and unfinished business—perhaps one seeks to rebuild its Nephilim warband, another pursues forbidden knowledge it couldn't obtain in life, or a third guards the burial site of its giant kin.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Destroying a Refa'im Wraith grants 2 RP per character for cleansing a powerful source of corruption from the world. However, allowing a possessed ally to be killed rather than exorcised grants 2 CP per character.  
* For GMs: Refa'im Wraiths begin with 10 CP and automatically gain 1 CP per hour they remain unbound. This corruption manifests as their forms becoming more solid and menacing, their whispers becoming irresistible to highly corrupted targets, and their possession attempts gaining additional effects (such as draining RP from possessed hosts). When their CP reaches 15 or higher, they gain the ability to possess multiple targets simultaneously.

---

### **Lilith, Night Mother**

LILITH, NIGHT MOTHER CREATURE 15  
UNIQUE MEDIUM CE FEY HUMANOID  
Perception \+28; darkvision, low-light vision, true seeing  
Languages Common, Ancient, Abyssal, Sylvan  
Skills Acrobatics \+30, Deception \+32, Stealth \+32, Night Lore \+30  
Str \+3, Dex \+8, Con \+5, Int \+4, Wis \+6, Cha \+9  
Items shadow-woven robes, moonstone amulet, silver comb  
---

AC 38; Fort \+27, Ref \+34, Will \+31  
HP 240; Immunities fear, mental, sleep; Resistances cold 15, electricity 15, precision 10  
Shadow Blend Lilith has camouflage in dim light or darkness. She can use the Hide action even when observed, provided she is within dim light or darkness.  
Moon Vulnerability Lilith takes a \-2 status penalty to all checks and DCs while in bright light from the sun or a full moon.  
---

Speed 30 ft, fly 40 ft (perfect)  
Melee \[A\] shadow claws \+32 (agile, finesse, magical, nonlethal), Damage 2d8+11 slashing plus 2d6 negative  
Ranged \[A\] moonbeam \+29 (divine, light, range 120 ft), Damage 3d6+8 radiant (Lilith takes half this damage herself)  
Midnight Talons \[A\] (attack, divine, negative) Lilith makes two melee Strikes with her shadow claws. If both hit the same target, the target must attempt a DC 32 Fortitude save. On a failure, the target is drained 2 and takes 4d6 negative damage. On a critical failure, the target is drained 3 and falls unconscious for 1 minute.  
---

Night Terror \[A\] (concentrate, divine, emotion, fear, mental, occult) Lilith projects nightmares into the minds of all sleeping creatures within 1 mile. Each target must attempt a DC 32 Will save. On a failure, the target wakes up frightened 2 and gains 1 Corruption Point from the corrupted dreams. On a critical failure, the target is frightened 3 and gains 2 Corruption Points. This ability can be used once per night.  
Shadow Step \[A\] (concentrate, transmutation) Lilith teleports up to 120 feet to an unoccupied space she can see that is in dim light or darkness. This movement does not trigger reactions.  
Lunar Blessing \[A\] (concentrate, divine) Lilith calls upon the power of the moon. For 1 minute, she gains a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls, and her shadow claws deal an additional 2d6 negative damage. However, she takes 2d6 radiant damage at the start of each of her turns while this ability is active. She can use this ability once per hour.  
Mother of Nightmares (aura, divine, emotion, fear, mental) 60-foot aura. Creatures that start their turn in the aura must attempt a DC 32 Will save. On a failure, they are frightened 1\. On a critical failure, they are frightened 2 and have visions of their deepest fears. This is a fear effect.

#### **Lore**

Lilith stands as one of the most enigmatic figures of the antediluvian world—a being of disputed origin who became the "Night Mother" to outcasts, wanderers, and those who dwell in darkness. According to some traditions, she was Adam's first wife who refused to submit to him and fled Eden, later becoming a demoness who preys on infants and seduces men in their sleep. Other accounts describe her as a primordial spirit of the night, older than humanity itself, who found purpose in protecting those rejected by society. In the Nephilim Wars setting, Lilith is a complex figure—neither wholly evil nor wholly good, but fiercely independent and protective of those who live on the margins. She appears as a beautiful woman with long dark hair, pale skin that seems to absorb moonlight, and eyes that shift color with the phases of the moon. She wears robes woven from shadow and starlight, and carries a silver comb said to have been a gift from the moon itself. Lilith dwells in hidden valleys, deep forests, and other places untouched by civilization, offering sanctuary to Wanderers, outcasts, and those fleeing Nephilim oppression. She teaches survival skills, herbal lore, and the arts of stealth and deception to those she deems worthy. However, her protection comes with a price—those who seek her aid must embrace the night, reject societal constraints, and accept the darkness within themselves. Her relationship with the Watchers is ambiguous; some say she opposed their corruption of humanity, while others claim she learned secrets from them that she now guards jealously.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Lilith is designed as a potential ally, antagonist, or neutral power depending on the campaign's needs. Her Night Terror ability creates campaign-wide consequences—she may haunt the party's dreams for nights before a major encounter. Her Midnight Talons and Lunar Blessing abilities make her a dangerous combatant, but her Moon Vulnerability creates strategic weaknesses. Use her Shadow Step ability to create dramatic entrances and exits. Position her in nighttime encounters where her Shadow Blend ability maximizes her stealth advantages. Pair her with nocturnal creatures, shadow fey, or outcast followers to create layered encounters. Her Mother of Nightmares aura creates psychological pressure even before combat begins. Consider giving Lilith a unique motivation and relationship with the party—perhaps she protects a community of outcasts the party must approach, she possesses knowledge crucial to their quest, or she offers sanctuary in exchange for a dangerous favor. Her moral ambiguity creates rich roleplaying opportunities—she may help the party while simultaneously tempting them toward darkness.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Gaining Lilith's aid without embracing her darkness grants 1 RP. However, accepting her protection while rejecting righteous communities grants 1 CP. Learning her survival skills without adopting her philosophy grants no RP/CP.  
* For GMs: Lilith begins with 5 CP from her association with darkness and outcasts, but she actively resists further corruption. Each time she uses her Lunar Blessing ability, she must attempt a DC 20 flat check. On a failure, she gains 1 CP, representing the spiritual cost of drawing power from corrupted sources. This corruption manifests as her shadow becoming more independent and her eyes glowing with darker light. Despite her CP, she is not inherently evil—her actions are driven by protection of the marginalized rather than malice. Characters with high RP who approach her with respect and humility may earn her trust without corruption.

BEHEMOTH CREATURE 18

Languages understands Common but cannot speak  
Skills Athletics \+38, Survival \+28, Wilderness Lore \+30  
Str \+12, Dex \+0, Con \+10, Int \-4, Wis \+4, Cha \+2

AC 42; Fort \+40, Ref \+28, Will \+32  
HP 450; Immunities fear, mental; Resistances precision 20  
Colossal Size Behemoth takes up a 30-foot square and has reach 25 feet.  
Primordial Beast Behemoth is a remnant of the pre-Fall world, a creature of immense size and strength that has survived since the dawn of creation. It is immune to effects that would increase its Corruption Points, as it exists outside the moral framework of humanity's fall.  
---

Speed 40 ft  
Melee \[A\] trample \+38 (reach 25 ft), Damage 4d12+20 bludgeoning  
Melee \[A\] tail sweep \+36 (reach 25 ft), Damage 3d12+18 bludgeoning  
Melee \[A\] bite \+38 (reach 25 ft), Damage 4d10+20 piercing  
Earthquake Stomp \[A\] (attack) Behemoth stomps the ground with such force that the earth shakes. Each creature within a 60-foot burst must attempt a DC 40 Reflex save. On a failure, the creature takes 10d6 bludgeoning damage and is knocked prone. On a critical failure, the creature is also stunned until the end of its next turn. The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain for 1 minute.  
---

Primordial Roar \[A\] (concentrate, auditory, emotion, fear) Behemoth unleashes a roar that echoes across the landscape. Each creature within 120 feet must attempt a DC 38 Will save. On a failure, the creature is frightened 2\. On a critical failure, the creature is frightened 3 and must spend its actions moving away from Behemoth for 1 minute.  
Unstoppable Behemoth is immune to the clumsy, enfeebled, and slowed conditions. When it would be reduced to 0 Hit Points, it can instead be reduced to 1 Hit Point and gain temporary Hit Points equal to its level (18). It can use this ability once per day.  
Living Mountain Behemoth's hide is as tough as stone. It gains resistance 20 to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from non-magical attacks. Additionally, it can use the Cover action without needing physical cover, provided it is in a natural environment.  
Guardian of the Wild Behemoth is drawn to protect pristine wilderness areas from corruption and destruction. When fighting to defend its territory, it gains a \+4 status bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls.

#### **Lore**

Behemoth is one of the primordial creatures mentioned in the Book of Job (40:15-24)—a beast of immense size and strength that represents the untamed power of creation itself. Unlike the corrupted beings spawned by the Watchers' rebellion, Behemoth is a remnant of the original, unspoiled world—a creature that existed before humanity's fall and has survived through the antediluvian age as a guardian of the wild places. It dwells in remote, untouched regions—deep valleys hidden between mountain ranges, vast forests untouched by civilization, or plains where the earth remains pristine. Behemoth is not inherently evil or corrupted; it is a force of nature, neither good nor evil but simply *other*. Its appearance is described in Job as having bones like bronze tubes, limbs like iron bars, and a tail that moves like a cedar tree. It feeds on the grasses of the field and drinks from mountain rivers, living in harmony with the natural world. Behemoth does not seek conflict with humanity but will defend its territory with overwhelming force against those who threaten the wilderness—whether Nephilim warbands clearing forests for fortresses, Cainite miners scarring the mountains, or corrupted sorcerers twisting the land with forbidden magic. Some righteous communities view Behemoth as a divine guardian, a living reminder that creation retains its original goodness despite humanity's corruption.

#### **Tactical Notes**

Behemoth is designed as a campaign-defining encounter for high-level parties—a creature of mythic proportions that should only be faced in the most dire circumstances or as a last resort. Its Earthquake Stomp ability can reshape entire battlefields, while its Primordial Roar creates psychological pressure on a massive scale. Use its Living Mountain ability to make it nearly invulnerable to conventional attacks—parties must find creative solutions or divine assistance to harm it. Position it in a pristine wilderness environment where its Guardian of the Wild ability activates. Behemoth should not be a random encounter but a pivotal moment in the campaign—perhaps the party must cross its territory to reach safety, or they must convince it to allow passage rather than fighting it. Consider giving Behemoth intelligence beyond that of a normal animal—it may communicate through dreams, visions, or environmental signs, testing the party's wisdom and respect for creation before resorting to violence. Its Primordial Beast trait means it cannot be corrupted or turned to evil purposes, making it a potential ally against truly corrupted threats.

#### **Soul Economy Interaction**

* For Players: Defeating Behemoth grants no RP or CP—it is a neutral force of nature. However, killing it unnecessarily (when peaceful alternatives existed) grants 2 CP per character for destroying a guardian of creation. Convincing it to allow passage or earning its trust grants 2 RP per character for respecting the integrity of creation.  
* For GMs: Behemoth begins with 0 CP and cannot gain CP. It is completely neutral in the spiritual conflict between righteousness and corruption. However, its presence has a purifying effect on the land—corrupted areas within 1 mile of its territory gradually heal over time (1 CP removed per day for creatures resting there). This represents its role as a guardian of creation's original goodness.

---


# **Chapter 14: Treasures and Artifacts**

## **Introduction: Sacred Objects in a Corrupted World**

In the antediluvian world, treasures are not merely valuable commodities—they are vessels of spiritual significance, physical manifestations of divine blessing or forbidden knowledge, and tangible connections to humanity's origins. Unlike medieval fantasy settings where magic items are common loot, artifacts in Nephilim Wars carry profound weight. A bronze dagger blessed by Sethite elders might preserve a community through famine. A star chart inscribed by Baraqel could reveal the timing of divine judgment. A single seed from Eden's garden might represent the last hope for restoring corrupted lands.  
This chapter presents treasures grounded in biblical and apocryphal tradition, adapted to Pathfinder 2e mechanics with reverence for their sacred context. Every item includes:

* Complete PF2e Stat Block: Fully formatted with activation requirements, effects, and limitations  
* Biblical/Apocryphal Basis: Clear sourcing from Genesis, 1 Enoch, Jubilees, or related texts  
* Lore Section: 2-3 paragraphs of setting-appropriate background explaining the item's origin and significance  
* Soul Economy Integration: Specific RP/CP interactions reflecting the item's spiritual nature  
* Adventure Hooks: Practical ways to incorporate the item into campaigns without trivializing its significance

Items are categorized by origin and spiritual alignment. Divine Relics originate from righteous sources and require RP to use effectively. Forbidden Artifacts stem from Watcher teachings and carry CP risks. Cainite Innovations represent advanced but non-magical craftsmanship. Natural Wonders are gifts from unspoiled creation. All items respect the bronze-age setting—no steel, plate armor, or anachronistic technology.  
Critical Design Principle: No item trivializes the Flood's theological significance or the spiritual stakes of the setting. Artifacts provide hope and aid but cannot prevent divine judgment. Their true value lies not in power but in how they help characters fulfill their purpose before the waters rise.  
---

## **Divine Relics**

### **Enoch's Prayer Shawl**

ENOSH'S PRAYER SHAWL ITEM 12  
UNIQUE DIVINE INVESTED RELIC  
Price —; Bulk L  
This simple wool shawl bears faint celestial patterns visible only in starlight. It smells of desert winds and ancient parchment.  
---

Activate \[A\] (command, divine)  
Frequency once per day  
Effect You wrap the shawl around yourself or an ally within 30 feet. For 10 minutes, the target gains a \+3 status bonus to Will saves against fear, possession, and corruption effects. Additionally, while wearing the shawl, the target gains 1 RP at the end of each hour spent in prayer or righteous service.  
Sustained Activation While sustaining the shawl's activation, you can use an additional action to extend its protection. The target gains resistance 10 to negative damage and all damage from corrupted sources (creatures with 3+ CP).  
---

Lore  
According to the Book of Jubilees, Enoch walked so closely with the Most High that his very garments absorbed divine presence. This shawl, woven by his wife Edna from wool blessed during his heavenly journeys, became a vessel of that connection. After Enoch's translation (Genesis 5:24), the shawl passed to Methuselah, then Lamech, serving as a tangible link to the prophet's righteousness. It does not grant power but amplifies the wearer's existing faith—those with high RP find its protection stronger, while the corrupted feel only uncomfortable warmth. The shawl has preserved righteous communities through Nephilim raids and spiritual assaults, its celestial patterns glowing softly when corruption approaches. It cannot be destroyed by ordinary means; fire cleanses it, water renews it, and time only deepens its blessing.  
Soul Economy Interaction

* To Activate: User must have 5+ RP. Attempting activation with fewer RP causes the shawl to fall inert, and the user gains 1 CP for misusing sacred objects.  
* While Worn: Wearer gains \+1 RP per day spent in righteous service. If wearer commits a corrupt act while wearing it, the shawl dims for 24 hours and wearer gains 2 CP.  
* Destruction: Cannot be destroyed by mortal means. If deliberately desecrated (e.g., used in a corrupted ritual), it vanishes in a flash of light, and all witnesses gain 3 CP.

Adventure Hooks

* The shawl is entrusted to the party by a dying Sethite elder with instructions: "Take this to Noah. He will know its purpose."  
* A Nephilim warlord seeks the shawl to corrupt it into a weapon against righteous communities.  
* During a vision, Enoch appears to a party member and requests the shawl be used to bless a hidden valley where future generations will find refuge.

---

### **Seth's Seed Pouch**

SETH'S SEED POUCH ITEM 8  
UNIQUE DIVINE INVESTED RELIC  
Price —; Bulk L  
A small leather pouch containing seven seeds of unusual size and luster. The seeds glow faintly with inner light and never decay.  
---

Activate \[A\] (command, divine)  
Frequency once per week  
Effect You plant one seed in corrupted or barren soil. Within 1 hour, a tree sprouts and matures to full size. This Tree of Restoration has the following properties:

* Purification: The area within a 60-foot radius gradually purifies. Corrupted water becomes drinkable, blighted plants regain health, and spiritual contamination lessens (creatures resting here reduce CP by 1 per day).  
* Sanctuary: The tree radiates a 30-foot aura identical to the *Sanctuary* spell (DC 24). Corrupted beings (3+ CP) cannot enter this area.  
* Sustenance: The tree bears fruit each dawn. Each fruit restores 2d8+8 HP when consumed and removes the fatigued condition. Up to seven creatures can benefit per day.  
  The tree remains for 7 days before its leaves turn gold and fall, leaving behind seven new seeds.

---

Lore  
After Abel's murder, Seth preserved seeds from the garden tended by his righteous brother—a living memorial to innocence lost. According to the Book of Jubilees, these seeds carried residual blessing from Eden itself, capable of restoring small patches of creation to their original state. Passed down through generations of the righteous line, the pouch now contains the last seven seeds. They respond only to pure intent; planted by corrupted hands, they wither instantly. The trees they produce are not the original Tree of Life but echoes of its healing power—temporary sanctuaries in a corrupted world. Righteous communities guard these seeds as their most precious treasure, using them only in direst need. The trees' golden leaves at death symbolize hope beyond judgment—the promise that creation will be restored.  
Soul Economy Interaction

* To Activate: Planter must have 3+ RP and spend 10 minutes in prayer while planting. Failure (due to insufficient RP or corrupt intent) causes the seed to wither, and planter gains 1 CP.  
* While Active: Each day a creature rests under the tree, it may attempt a DC 18 Will save to reduce CP by 1 (success) or gain 1 RP (critical success).  
* Harvesting Seeds: Gathering the new seeds requires a DC 20 Religion check. Success yields seven seeds; failure yields none, and the harvester gains 1 CP for greed.

Adventure Hooks

* A Sethite enclave's last seed is stolen by a desperate Wanderer tribe seeking to purify their poisoned water source.  
* Noah requests the pouch to grow trees for the Ark's construction, but using it for this purpose consumes all seven seeds permanently.  
* A corrupted sorcerer seeks to poison the seeds, creating "Trees of Corruption" that spread spiritual blight.

---

### **Abel's Altar Stone**

ABEL'S ALTAR STONE ITEM 10  
UNIQUE DIVINE INVESTED RELIC  
Price —; Bulk 2  
A smooth river stone, warm to the touch, bearing a faint crimson stain that never fades.  
---

Activate \[A\] (command, divine)  
Frequency once per day  
Effect You place the stone on the ground and speak words of sacrifice. For 10 minutes, it functions as a sacred altar:

* Divine Focus: Divine spellcasters gain a \+2 status bonus to spell attack rolls and DCs while within 30 feet.  
* Purification Ritual: You can perform a 10-minute ritual to cleanse a corrupted item or location. The target must attempt a DC 26 Will save. On failure, all CP gained from the target are removed. On critical failure, the corruption is destroyed.  
* Righteous Gathering: Allies within 30 feet gain temporary HP equal to your level and a \+1 status bonus to saves against corruption.

Sustained Activation While sustaining the stone's activation, you can use an additional action to call upon divine judgment. One corrupted creature (5+ CP) within 60 feet must attempt a DC 26 Will save. On failure, it is stunned for 1 round and gains 1d4 CP from spiritual backlash.  
---

Lore  
This stone is the very rock upon which Abel offered his righteous sacrifice (Genesis 4:4), accepted by the Most High with fire from heaven. The crimson stain is not blood but the mark of divine approval—a permanent testament to faithfulness. After Cain's murder of Abel, Seth recovered the stone, and it became the focal point for righteous worship throughout the antediluvian age. Unlike Cainite altars of carved stone or bronze, this humble river stone represents worship offered in spirit and truth. It responds only to sincere sacrifice—not of animals, but of self: humility, mercy, and obedience. When placed on corrupted ground, the stone causes the earth to tremble until purity is restored. Righteous prophets have used it to consecrate hidden enclaves, purify poisoned wells, and strengthen communities facing Nephilim oppression. Its warmth intensifies near those of pure heart and grows cold near corruption.  
Soul Economy Interaction

* To Activate: User must spend 1 RP and speak a sincere prayer of sacrifice. Attempting activation without RP or with corrupt intent causes the stone to grow cold, and user gains 2 CP.  
* While Active: Each ally who performs a righteous act (healing the wounded, protecting the innocent) while within the aura gains 1 RP.  
* Divine Judgment: Using the judgment ability against a creature with 10+ CP grants 2 RP to all allies. Using it against a creature with fewer than 5 CP grants 1 CP to the user for misusing divine power.

Adventure Hooks

* The stone is hidden in a Sethite enclave under Nephilim siege. The party must protect it during evacuation.  
* A Cainite artisan offers vast wealth for the stone to "improve" Cainite altars—but his true intent is to corrupt it.  
* During a vision, Abel appears and instructs a party member to place the stone on Mount Ararat, where it will remain until after the Flood.
